## 1. Overview

Ecom Central is a single-server operations hub that unifies order management, shipping/courier tracking, delivery-performance analytics, warehouse operations, marketing analytics, and automated reporting for The Element's D2C business.

**Core responsibilities:**

- Aggregate orders from **Shopify** and enrich them with courier tracking from **RapidShyp** (primary 3PL aggregator) and **DocPharma** (secondary shipping platform).
- Track every shipment's full journey (dispatch → attempts → NDR → delivered/RTO) and compute delivery KPIs: FASR (First-Attempt Strike Rate), RTO rate, NDR recovery, TAT.
- Detect and escalate courier problems: silent RTOs (returned with no delivery attempt → freight claims), late deliveries (promise date broken), fake delivery attempts (manually flagged, AI-polished escalation emails).
- Run scheduled warehouse/ops reports into **Microsoft Teams** channels (native Adaptive Cards) with inbound keyword triggers via Microsoft Graph.
- Automate **Amazon review requests** — eligible orders are sent unattended each morning and the result is posted to Teams.
- Provide marketing analytics (Meta ad sets / ads / ranking) and profitability views.
- Multi-user access with per-dashboard, server-enforced permissions.

---

## 2. Tech Stack

| Layer | Technology |
|---|---|
| Backend | Node.js, Express 4 |
| Database | Supabase (PostgreSQL 17) via `@supabase/supabase-js` (service-role key, server-side only) |
| Frontend | Single-page vanilla JS (`app/static/app.js`) + one HTML template (`app/templates/index.html`) |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS **3.4** — statically compiled (no CDN/JIT at runtime) |
| Charts | Chart.js (self-hosted at `app/static/vendor/chart.min.js`) |
| Auth | JWT (`jsonwebtoken`), scrypt password hashing (Node crypto, no external dep) |
| Email | Nodemailer (SMTP send) + imapflow/mailparser (IMAP reply reading); portal-configurable settings, AES-256-GCM-encrypted password |
| Scheduling | node-cron (all times **Asia/Kolkata**) |
| AI | Google Gemini (native `generateContent`) or any OpenAI-compatible provider — used for escalation-email polish |
| Reports/Exports | ExcelJS, PDFKit |
| Process manager | pm2 (production) |

## 3. Architecture

```
                      ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                      │                 server.js (Express)            │
                      │  auth gate → RBAC gate → routers → crons       │
                      └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                         │            │             │            │
        ┌────────────────┤            │             │            └───────────────┐
        ▼                ▼            ▼             ▼                            ▼
  app/templates/   app/api/*.js   node-cron   app/api/teams.js         app/api/teams_listener.js
  index.html       (routers +     (scheduled  (outbound Adaptive       (inbound keyword triggers
  app/static/*     business       reports &   Cards via Power           via MS Graph delegated
  (SPA frontend)   logic)         syncs)      Automate webhooks)        token, 20s poll)
        │                │
        ▼                ▼
   Browser SPA      Supabase (PostgreSQL)  ◄── single source of truth
                         ▲
     ┌───────────────────┼──────────────────────┬───────────────┐
     ▼                   ▼                      ▼               ▼
  Shopify API      RapidShyp API          DocPharma API    EasyEcom API
  (orders)         (track_order,          (fetch-details,  (B2C orders,
                   shipment_details,      partner portal)  on-hold)
                   serviceability)
```

**Design principles**

- **DB-first reads:** dashboards read from synced Supabase tables; live courier APIs are called only to fill gaps (then cached back).
- **Single journey pipeline:** `app/api/delivery_journey.js` is the one parser/writer for `shipment_journey_ecom`; webhooks, backfills and crons all flow through it. Three delivery **sources** feed it: `rapidshyp` (webhook + scan timeline), `docpharma` (portal pull, milestones only), `kwikship` (nightly pull, `status_history` timeline — see below). All share the `saveJourney()` writer + `source` column; `PLATFORM_LABEL`/`platformLabel()` map source → display name.
- **Re-allocation cleanup (`supersedeStaleJourneys`):** the journey table is keyed by **AWB**, so re-allocating an order to a new AWB/aggregator (e.g. RapidShyp → deassign → Kwikship) would leave the OLD AWB's row orphaned — stuck non-final and **double-counting** the order in the dashboard/KPIs. After saving the current shipment's journey, every writer (`updateJourneyForOrder` for RS/DP, `updateKwikshipJourney` + the `kwikship-webhook`) deletes any other **non-final** journey row for the same `order_name` on a different AWB. A final (delivered/RTO) leg is always kept as history.
- **Kwikship (GoKwik) sync** — TWO paths, same as RapidShyp (real-time webhook + a cron backstop):
  - **Webhook (real-time):** GoKwik's dashboard *Merchant Webhooks* (Bearer-token auth; triggers Pickup Completed / OFD / Undelivered / RTO Initiated / Delivered) POST to the Supabase Edge Function **`kwikship-webhook`** (HTTPS; the Node app is HTTP-only, so — like `rapidshyp-webhook` — the receiver is an edge fn, `verify_jwt=false` + its own token check). On each event it fetches the authoritative `status_history` from Kwikship's API and upserts `shipment_journey_ecom` (source=`kwikship`). URL: `…/functions/v1/kwikship-webhook`. **Audit trail:** every hit is recorded in table **`kwikship_webhook_log`** (received_at, awb, order_name, cur_status, outcome, result, **user_agent**, source_ip, raw **payload**) — so it's provable which hits are real GoKwik events (their UA ≠ a manual `curl`) and it captures GoKwik's real payload shape. Query: `select * from kwikship_webhook_log order by received_at desc`.
  - **Cron backstop (`app/api/kwikship_sync.js`):** a **2 AM IST cron** (`syncKwikship`) refreshes journeys for **only Kwikship-allocated orders** — identified by EasyEcom's own field **`raw_data->>'courier_aggregator_name' = 'GoKwik Outbound'`** (RapidShyp reads `'RapidShyp- Outbound'`; both ship from the same warehouse so the generated `location` column can't distinguish them). Skips already-final shipments → one Kwikship API call per non-final shipment, zero wasted calls. Manual re-sync: `POST /api/kwikship/sync` (delivery-perf perm).
  - 🗺️ **KWIKSHIP ZONE NOW COMES FROM THEIR SERVICEABILITY SHEET, NOT OUR STATE GUESS (added 2026-08-13).** Every journey row carries a `zone` that the TAT / RTO% / freight / Last-Mile dashboards slice by, and it was always a **guess**: `zoneFromState()` maps a destination STATE to A–E, so every pincode in Maharashtra got `C` whether it was Mumbai metro or a village 400 km inland — while KwikShip bills on their own **pincode-level** zone table. That sheet now lives in **`zone_mapping_with_pincode`** and KwikShip zones are resolved from it.
    - ⚠️⚠️ **THE COLUMN NAMES LIE — `Pin_code_From`/`Pin_code_To` IS NOT A RANGE.** `Pin_code_From` is the **PICKUP** pincode and is the same value on every row (**122101** = the Gurgaon warehouse — the same `PICKUP_PINCODE` default as `serviceability.js`, and the number in the export's filename `Pincode_Serviceability_Report_122101_….csv`). `Pin_code_To` is the **DESTINATION**. The file is one row per serviceable destination (~30,600). **Lookup is an EXACT match on `Pin_code_To`.** This was built as a From..To range first and caught in preview before any import — worth recording because the failure is silent and severe: as a band each row becomes a 680k-pincode overlapping span that zones shipments essentially at random, **and** a natural "From > To" sanity check drops every destination numerically BELOW the pickup — all of Delhi (110xxx), Faridabad (121xxx) and Gurgaon itself (122001–122100), i.e. exactly the zone A/B core (266 rows in the real file). **If a courier export ever has a constant value in a "from" column, it is an origin, not a range start.**
    - **Each row carries the `Zone` plus a serviceability flag for ~70 courier services** (`Delhivery_Surface_250_Gm`, `EKart_Air`, `Blue_Dart_Select`, …). The table mirrors those 75 headers **exactly — quoted and case-sensitive** — so the CSV imports straight into Supabase with no column-mapping step. ⚠️ **Every query must double-quote them** (`select "Zone" from …`); unquoted `zone` will not resolve. Courier columns are all `text` on purpose, so no unexpected value (`Yes`/`1`/`-`/blank) can fail an import. Only `Pin_code_To`/`Zone` drive the mapping; the rest is stored for reference. A destination listed twice keeps the **first** row (matching `order by id` in the lookup) and the disagreement is reported.
    - **The lookup rule lives in the DB, in ONE place.** `zone_for_pincode(pin)` resolves a single pincode and `remap_kwikship_zones()` does the bulk pass; the Node cron **and** the `kwikship-webhook` edge fn both **call `zone_for_pincode()`** rather than each re-implementing it. That is deliberate — those two have silently drifted apart three times before, and there is now no second copy of the logic to fall out of step.
    - **Precedence: sheet → `zoneFromState()` → stored zone → EasyEcom hint.** A pincode in no range deliberately KEEPS its state-derived zone rather than going blank (a null zone would add an empty bucket to every zone chart). ⚠️ **Scope is KwikShip only — RapidShyp and DocPharma are untouched and still state-derived**, by explicit instruction.
    - ⚠️ **`refresh_journey_dest()` had to be patched too:** its trailing blanket `update … set zone='E' where dest_state ilike 'kerala'` runs on **every server start** and would have quietly undone the mapped Kerala zones hours after each import; it now excludes `source='kwikship'`.
    - **Two ways in:** import the CSV directly into the table from Supabase, then press **Re-apply to shipments** on the admin page; or drag the file onto **Admin → Zone Mapping**, which previews it (destination count, pickup pincode, zone spread, skipped rows, duplicate/conflicting destinations, unmatched columns) before writing and re-maps automatically on commit. The preview surfaces the **pickup pincode(s) found** — more than one means two different reports got merged, since a serviceability export covers exactly one origin.
    - ⚠️⚠️ **THE UNDEPLOYED WEBHOOK ATE THE MAPPING WITHIN SECONDS — the drift bug, caught live (2026-08-13).** The first import ran while `kwikship-webhook` was still on **v10**, which derived zone from state. **7 shipments reverted within 40 seconds** of the import and **16 within four minutes** — every one Karnataka/Maharashtra, re-stamped `C` (a zone KwikShip does not even use) over the sheet's `D`. At ~33 webhook hits per 30 min, and with 749 of 1,146 shipments differing from the state guess, the whole import would have been erased in days. The nightly cron could not have healed it either: it skips `is_final` rows, and one of the first seven was already RTO-final. **Fixed by deploying v11** (calls `zone_for_pincode()`); verified afterwards at **1,146 agree / 0 disagree / 0 unmapped**. **`zone_drift_check(since)` is the standing watchdog — `drift` must always read 0.**
  - 💰 **KWIKSHIP SHIPPING COST, PRICED OFF THE RATE CARD (added 2026-08-13).** With a real zone per shipment, KwikShip freight can finally be costed. The card (surface, ex-GST) lives in **`kwikship_rate_card_ecom`** and the rules the card doesn't state live in **`kwikship_billing_config_ecom`** — both DATA, so a renegotiated contract is an UPDATE, not a deploy.
    - **The ladder:** every zone prices "500 gm" and "Additional 500 g" **identically** (23/25/38/43/58), and the RTO 500 g rate is identical again — so below 2 kg the charge is simply `ceil(weight/500) × zone rate` for each leg. At/above `slab_switch_g` (2 kg) the 2 kg card applies: base + `ceil((w−2000)/1000)` × additional-1kg. ⚠️ The card gives **no additional-RTO rate**, so an RTO over 2 kg reuses the forward additional and raises the `assumed_rto_additional` flag rather than hiding the assumption. No shipment has yet exceeded 1 kg, so that path is unused.
    - **Rules confirmed with the user:** RTO pays **forward + RTO both**; the COD fee (**₹25 or 1.3%, whichever is higher**) applies **only on delivery** (an RTO'd COD order pays freight but no COD fee); **18% GST** is carried separately, with **ex-GST as the headline** because it is input-creditable.
    - **Where the inputs come from:** KwikShip's API exposes neither weight nor order value, so both are read from EasyEcom — **`raw_data->>'Package Weight'`** (present on 100% of GoKwik orders; 15–980 g, median 80 g) and `cod_amount`/`order_total`. ⚠️ That is **DEAD weight** — no parcel dimensions exist anywhere in the data, so volumetric weight cannot be applied and a bulky-light parcel would read low.
    - **Written into the SAME `freight_forward` / `freight_rto` / `cod_charges` / `freight_total` columns the RapidShyp freight lens already reads**, so Delivery Performance, Last-Mile revenue and the claims reports pick KwikShip costs up with no further wiring. ⚠️ **`charges_fetched_at` is deliberately left NULL** (RapidShyp sets it when REAL billed figures are fetched) so a card estimate can never be mistaken for a fetched invoice.
    - ⚡ **REAL-TIME: the DB trigger `trg_kwikship_price` prices every row as it is written (added 2026-08-14).** `kwikship_price_row()` fires BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE on `shipment_journey_ecom` `when (NEW.source = 'kwikship')` and sets zone (sheet first, writer's fallback kept if the pincode isn't listed), `applied_weight`, `shipment_value` and all four freight columns. **This is the durable end of the drift problem** — it no longer matters whether the writer is the webhook, the cron, a backfill or a manual fix, because the DATABASE decides. Proven by forcing the exact old-cron corruption (`zone='C'` + every cost NULLed) on a live row: it came back zone A, weight 70 g, ₹23 + ₹25 COD = ₹48, inside the same statement. RapidShyp/DocPharma pay nothing for it (the `when` clause excludes them).
    - 💊 **DOCPHARMA IS PRICED THE SAME WAY (added 2026-08-14).** Its card is FLAT — **Service ₹65 · RTO ₹40 · COD ₹25** — so there is no rate table, just `docpharma_billing_config_ecom`. Same conventions: RTO pays service + RTO, the COD fee applies on delivery only, GST carried separately. `trg_docpharma_price` prices each row in real time and `apply_docpharma_charges()` is the bulk/reconcile pass. Because nothing depends on weight or value it prices **100%** of the 1,802 rows: **₹1,49,275 ex-GST / ₹1,76,145 incl** (service ₹1,17,130 + RTO ₹18,520 + COD fees ₹13,625). ⚠️ **463 RTOs cost ₹18,520 in return freight on top of ₹30,095 of outbound service** — DocPharma's RTO rate is 26% of shipments vs KwikShip's 12%.
    - 🕒 **Backstop: pg_cron `courier-cost-reconcile-hourly` (`45 * * * *`)** runs `remap_kwikship_zones()` + `apply_kwikship_charges()` + `apply_docpharma_charges()`. The trigger only fires when a row is WRITTEN, so this catches the case where EasyEcom syncs the weight/pincode *after* the journey row was created, and re-costs everything when the sheet or rate card changes. Idempotent — a quiet hour writes nothing.
    - 🐛 **`zone_drift_check` was crying wolf (fixed 2026-08-14).** `dest_pincode` is NOT populated on a fresh journey row — `refresh_journey_dest()` fills it later from the Shopify address. The trigger knows this and falls back to the EasyEcom `shipping_pincode`, but the watchdog and `remap_kwikship_zones()` looked only at `dest_pincode`, so every newly-arrived shipment counted as drift: it read **57** while all 57 zones were in fact correct and priced. Both now resolve the pincode exactly as the trigger does, and `drift` counts only rows whose pincode IS in the sheet but whose zone disagrees (a pincode the sheet doesn't cover legitimately keeps its state-derived zone). A separate `no_sheet_match` count surfaces the latter without raising an alarm. **A watchdog that false-alarms daily gets ignored, which is worse than not having one.**
    - 🐛 **Latent crash in the flag arrays, found 2026-08-14 while adding DocPharma.** `v_flags := v_flags || 'literal'` makes Postgres parse the literal as an ARRAY literal ("malformed array literal"), so **every flag path RAISED instead of flagging**. In `kwikship_charges` that meant a COD shipment delivered with **no order value** would throw — and since the function runs inside the BEFORE-write trigger, the exception would **abort the webhook's upsert entirely and lose the delivery event**. It had never fired only because every COD row happens to carry a value today. Both functions now use `array_append()`. **If you add a flag to either pricing function, use `array_append` — the `||` form compiles fine and fails only at runtime, on the rare row.**
    - ⚠️⚠️ **THE 2 AM NODE CRON REVERTED 82 ZONES THE FIRST NIGHT (2026-08-14).** Only the edge function had been deployed; `kwikship_sync.js` was fixed locally but never shipped, so the live cron still ran `zoneFromState()` first and re-stamped state-derived zones over the mapped ones on every non-final shipment it touched (238 journey writes in that hour). One new shipment also arrived completely unpriced, because the nightly `apply_kwikship_charges()` call lives in the same undeployed `server.js`. **Lesson, now the second instance in this feature: a fix that exists only in the repo is not a fix.** The trigger + pg_cron above make the data correct regardless of what any application deploy is running.
    - `apply_kwikship_charges()` is idempotent and runs automatically after every zone change (upload + `/remap`) **and nightly after the 2 AM sync** — so a shipment that has just become delivered or RTO is re-priced. First run: **1,146 priced, 0 unpriced, 0 missing weight — ₹56,235 ex-GST** (forward ₹45,574 + RTO ₹5,111 + COD fees ₹5,550; ₹66,357 incl GST). Independent sanity check: Zone A 500 g = ₹23 → **₹27.14 incl GST** and 2 slabs → **₹101.48**, the exact figures `rapidshyp_recon.js` records as observed invoice values.
    - **Result of the first real import (2026-08-13):** 30,903 destinations from pickup 122101, **100% coverage** of all 1,146 KwikShip shipments, 749 zones corrected. Live distribution moved from `A 23 · B 139 · C 600 · D 383 · E 1` to **`D 942 · A 164 · B 39 · E 1`**. ⚠️ **KwikShip's model has NO ZONE C** — their sheet only uses A/B/D/E, so Zone C has vanished from KwikShip data while RapidShyp/DocPharma still use it. That cross-courier asymmetry is real and intentional; do not "fix" it.
  - **TWO Kwikship endpoints, used together (added 2026-08-05):**
    - `v1` **auth** `GET /api/v1/shipments/:awb` (headers `gk-app-id` + `gk-app-secret`) → current status, `courier_name`, `shipping_address` (→ zone), `estimated_delivery_date`, payment mode, weight. Scans expose only `{status, description, location, datetime}`.
    - 🔑 ⚠️ **OUR `gk-app-id`/`gk-app-secret` ARE CURRENTLY REJECTED — v1 returns 401 (found 2026-08-12, NOT yet resolved).** Measured: `GET /api/v1/shipments/47607613036994` → **401 Unauthorized**, while `GET /track/v2/public` on the SAME AWB → 200. Not the AWB, not the base URL — the credentials. **It is invisible because every call site is v2-first with v1 as fallback**, so nothing breaks loudly; what is silently lost whenever v2 is thin is v1's unique contribution: current status, `courier_name`, shipping address → **zone**, EDD, payment mode and weight. **ACTION: ask GoKwik whether the keys were rotated or de-scoped, and reissue.** (Established while checking whether the dispute/escalation API could be reached with these keys — it cannot; see below.)
    - 🚫 **DISPUTE / NDR-ESCALATION ENDPOINTS ARE NOT ON THE API-KEY SURFACE (investigated 2026-08-12).** `POST /kwikship/dispute/raise-single-dispute`, `/bulk-manual-dispute`, `/upload-dispute-file`, `GET /dispute/details` are the merchant **dashboard's BFF**, gated on the `dashboard.gokwik.co` **session cookie** — proven by header diff across four captures: the `token` header is byte-identical on all of them including the failing one, and only the request missing the **cookie** 401s; `merchant-mid` alone does not authenticate either. CORS on that surface is pinned to `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://dashboard.gokwik.co` (the API-key surface answers `*`). 14 candidate key-auth paths probed (`/api/v1/disputes`, `/api/v1/ndr`, `/api/v1/shipments/:awb/disputes`, `/api/v1/escalations`, `/dispute/list`, token-exchange) — all rejected. **No public KwikShip API docs exist**, so only GoKwik can say what else the `gk-app-id` surface carries. ⚠️ Also note the session cookie **rotates** between requests, so any "paste a token into Settings" approach cannot work; a bridge would have to capture continuously (the EasyEcom extension pattern). **Separately, a bug on GoKwik's side worth reporting: `raise-single-dispute` 401s for EVERY merchant** — their frontend sends it via a bare `fetch()` without `credentials: 'include'`, so the cookie and `merchant-mid` never go (its `accept` is `*/*`, not axios's `application/json, text/plain, */*`). **The bulk path works; use it even for one shipment.**
    - `v2` **public** `GET /track/v2/public?order_code=<awb|order no>` — **no auth at all** (`merchant_id` is accepted but ignored) → the SAME timeline plus **`shipper_remark`**, `shipper_status` and a separate `creation_datetime`/`status_datetime`. It does NOT have a current status, a shipping address (so no zone) or an unmasked phone.
    - ⚠️ **`shipper_remark` is the ONLY human NDR reason either endpoint gives** — "Consignee Unavailable", "Consignee refused to accept/order cancelled", "Delivery Rescheduled by Customer". v1's `description` is a courier code (`UD_EOD-11_Pending`) that means nothing to an agent. Production had stored exactly those codes.
    - **API budget:** `updateKwikshipJourney()` calls **v2 first**, then reads the stored row, and fetches v1 **only when identity is missing** (new shipment, or no stored zone/courier) or v2 failed. Since zone/courier never change once captured, the nightly run costs the SAME one call per shipment it did before — and that call is now the unauthenticated one. The parser accepts both shapes (`shipper_remark → description → status`; `status_datetime → datetime → creation_datetime`), so either source can feed it.
    - The **scan log** (`GET /delivery-performance/shipment/:awb`) also prefers v2, so the Delivery Performance timeline reads "Consignee Unavailable" instead of `UD_EOD-11_Pending`. ⚠️ It serves `raw.status_history` from CACHE first — rows cached from v1 keep rendering codes until that cache is cleared (11 were cleared on rollout; the next view re-fetches v2).
    - **Backfill:** `backfillKwikshipNdrReasons({ dry })` in `kwikship_sync.js` rewrites `ndr_reasons` on rows still holding courier codes. v2-only (no authenticated calls), targets just the rows that match a code pattern (28 of 271 at rollout), touches nothing but `ndr_reasons`, and is idempotent.
    - ⚠⚠ **The webhook was silently undoing the backfill.** The deployed edge fn re-fetched v1 on EVERY event and overwrote `ndr_reasons` with codes — backfilled rows reverted within minutes, a different one each time (proven on AWB 47607612924014, reverted at 08:00:56 by a `rto_in_transit` event; ~148 webhook hits per 6 h). Fixed in `kwikship-webhook` **v7**, which merges v1+v2 exactly like the Node sync. **If NDR reasons ever regress to codes again, suspect the edge function first — it writes far more often than the cron.**
    - ⚠️ **FULFILLMENT OPS "LIVE TRACKING" WAS BLANK FOR EVERY KWIKSHIP ORDER (fixed 2026-08-08).** `kwikshipTrack()` in `fulfillment_ops.js` — the modal behind the AWB on the Fulfillment Ops table — was the **last v1-only call site**; the sync, the Delivery-Performance scan log and the webhook had all been moved to v2-first earlier and this one was missed. Measured live: **v1 returns a timeline for 0 of 12 recent Kwikship AWBs, v2 for 12 of 12** — so this was not one bad shipment, it was every Kwikship order showing "No tracking events yet" while the parcel was plainly moving (reported on AWB `47607613096671` / TE25-40596, in transit, 16 scans available). Now **v2 public first, v1 as fallback**. The mapper had to read BOTH field namings — v1 `{datetime, description}` vs v2 `{status_datetime, shipper_remark}` — or v2 events would render with **blank timestamps** and a generic status name instead of the real remark. Verified over 10 live AWBs: 10/10 timelines, 0 events missing a timestamp, NDR rows now reading "Consignee Unavailable" / "Consignee refused to accept/order cancelled" instead of nothing.
    - ⚠⚠ **FULFILLMENT OPS SHOWED EVERY NON-RAPIDSHYP ORDER AS "Confirmed" (fixed 2026-08-08).** `POST /fulfillment-ops/orders` read delivery status from **`rapidshyp_tracking_ecom` only** — a RapidShyp-ONLY cache. RapidShyp 400s on a Kwikship/DocPharma AWB, so nothing is ever written for those, `o.rapidshypStatus` stayed undefined, and `fopsGetDS()` fell back to Shopify's `displayFulfillmentStatus` = **"Confirmed" forever**, however far the parcel had actually travelled (reported: 386 active / 0 in transit / 0 out for delivery while Kwikship orders were mid-route and delivered). **Fix:** after reading the RapidShyp cache, any AWB still without a status is filled from **`shipment_journey_ecom`** (`fetchJourneyByAwbs()` + `journeyStatusText()`) — which already holds the truth for all three platforms and costs **one indexed read, no live API calls**. It only ever FILLS GAPS, never overrides a RapidShyp hit (fresher for RapidShyp's own shipments). `journeyStatusText()` emits phrases the substring classifier already understands (Delivered / RTO / Out for Delivery / In Transit / "Undelivered — delivery attempted"); `out_for_pickup` deliberately returns **null** (the courier has not collected it yet, so Shopify's own state is the honest one) and `lost` maps to Attempted so it surfaces as action-needed. **Measured over 1,000 recent AWBs: 312 orders corrected (298 Kwikship + 14 DocPharma) — "Confirmed" 610 → 298, In transit 327 → 552, Delivered 47 → 92, RTO 14 → 27, Out for delivery 0 → 18, Attempted 2 → 13.** The same journey fallback was added to `/track-order/:id` as a last resort after all three live lookups, so a temporary API blip no longer blanks the row.
    - 🚫 **NOTHING NEW IS PUSHED TO SHOPIFY — by explicit instruction (2026-08-08).** The Kwikship / DocPharma / journey lookups above fix what the **DASHBOARD** shows and are **read-only with respect to Shopify**; the customer-facing order is never touched by them. `/track-order/:id` now tracks `statusFrom` (`rapidshyp` | `docpharma` | `kwikship` | `journey`) at each of the four places `rsStatus` can be set, and the push at 3b fires **only when `statusFrom === 'rapidshyp'`** — the pre-existing behaviour, preserved exactly. (The old gate was `!fromDocpharma`, which would NOT have covered the two new sources: once the v2 fix made Kwikship return data, Kwikship statuses would have started pushing on their own.) **The click-AWB path works as before and is the only status writer.** Bulk push stays OFF: `STATUS_PUSH_ENABLED = false`, `syncStatusesToShopify()` is on no cron (the server.js call is commented out) and its CLI entry passes no `manual`, so it is refused at the push. `DELIVERED` also remains blocked outright — Shopify's "shipment delivered" email is shop-level and cannot be disabled.
    - 📧 **ESCALATION EMAIL NOW ROUTES BY THE ORDER'S OWN COURIER PLATFORM (2026-08-08).** `POST /critical-email/compose` routed "DocPharma rows → DocPharma, **everything else (RapidShyp / Kwikship) → RapidShyp**", so **every KwikShip NDR escalation was addressed to RapidShyp** — a courier with no visibility of the parcel. Worse, the rule let the **DOMINANT source win on a mixed batch**: a basket of 6 RapidShyp + 5 KwikShip orders sent all 11 to RapidShyp, and the order-basket feature makes mixed batches easy to build across a shift. **And a second silent leak:** when a platform had no To configured, `recipientsFor()` returned empty → `sendMail` fell through to `cfg.to` (the GENERAL list = the RapidShyp addresses), so DocPharma escalations were quietly going to RapidShyp too. **Fix:** three-way routing off the shipment's own `source`; **mixed batches are REFUSED** with the split named ("3 RapidShyp + 2 KwikShip — send them separately") rather than averaged; an **unmapped platform fails loudly** ("No escalation email is mapped for KwikShip") instead of falling back to another courier — enforced on BOTH compose and send. The draft modal now shows a colour-coded **"Going to <Courier>"** badge plus the resolved recipients, so the agent sees the destination before sending. Verified on live shipments: all-RapidShyp → RapidShyp, all-KwikShip → KwikShip, mixed → BLOCKED.
    - **KwikShip email mapping in Settings → Email & Reports (2026-08-08).** New `kwikship_to_emails` / `kwikship_cc_emails` columns on `app_email_settings` (migration `add_kwikship_email_recipients`), surfaced as **KwikShip To / KwikShip CC** beside the DocPharma pair, and added to `platforms` in `getEmailConfig()`. ⚠️ **Deliberately NO fallback to the RapidShyp list** — a blank mapping must read as "not configured" and block, never become a silent misdelivery to another courier. CC defaults to the shared internal CC. **ACTION NEEDED: KwikShip To is still blank** — KwikShip escalations are blocked until the GoKwik address is entered (DocPharma To is blank too, and was previously leaking to RapidShyp via the fallback described above).
    - 🔕 **DAILY REPORT TO RAPIDSHYP STOPPED (2026-08-08, by request).** The `First-OFD (30 9 * * *)` cron was the ONLY daily email reaching RapidShyp; it now runs with `exclude: ['rapidshyp']` (new `exclude` option on `sendReportPerPlatform`). DocPharma and KwikShip still receive their daily copy, and the report itself is unchanged — RapidShyp's can still be sent on demand from the dashboard. **Untouched:** Silent-RTO claims (weekly, Mon 9:30) and Late-Deliveries (fortnightly, 1st/16th) still go to RapidShyp.
    - 📉 **RTO RATE NOW MEASURED ON SETTLED SHIPMENTS (changed 2026-08-08 by request).** Was `RTO ÷ total tracked`. Now **`RTO ÷ (delivered on 1st attempt + delivered after NDR + RTO)`** = `RTO ÷ resolved` — the "shipped" count already shown in the Delivery Performance header. **Why it is the better number:** a parcel still in transit, or sitting on an open NDR, has not had its chance to come back yet; counting it in the denominator drags the rate down and makes it move whenever shipping VOLUME moves rather than when returns do. On the live 30-day RapidShyp window that is **731/2973 = 24.6%** instead of **731/3798 = 19.2%** — the same returns, honestly measured against the orders that actually finished. Applied to **all three** RTO-rate computations — headline KPI, `coreStats()` (which feeds the card's "vs previous period" delta, so both sides of the comparison move together) and the COD/Prepaid split — so the dashboard never shows two definitions of the same number. The card caption names its base: "731 of 2973 shipped (delivered + RTO)". ⚠️ FASR and delivered-rate deliberately KEEP the `tracked` base (an unshipped order genuinely is a first-attempt miss), so the denominators differ by metric on purpose.
    - **…and the RTO CHIP was left behind (fixed same day).** The composition strip under the KPI cards computes each chip as a **share of tracked**, so RTO still read **735/3808 = 19.3%** directly beneath a card saying **24.6%** — two different bases side by side, which reads as one of them being broken. The chip now takes the percentage from `dpRtoRate(s)`, the SAME formula as the card (`RTO ÷ (1st-attempt + after-NDR + RTO)`), and its delta from the same `coreStats` rate — so the arrows agree too (it showed ▼3.9 against the card's ▲1.3 because each compared on a different base). **Counts are untouched and still sum to tracked** (1923+321+731+2+135+686 = 3798 ✓); only the RTO percentage changed base, so the caption is now "counts sum to tracked" and **every chip carries a tooltip naming its denominator** (RTO: "735 of 2984 shipped (delivered + RTO)"; the rest: "1926 of 3808 tracked"). ⚠️ Known tradeoff, deliberate: the RTO chip shows a RATE while its neighbours show SHARES — chosen so the page has exactly one RTO%.
    - **ESCALATION DRAFT NOW NAMES THE RIGHT COURIER (2026-08-08).** Routing was fixed earlier but the AI system prompt still hardcoded "…writing an escalation email to the courier partner **RapidShyp**", so every draft opened "Hi RapidShyp team" — including KwikShip escalations the routing had correctly addressed to GoKwik. **Right envelope, wrong letter, which is worse than either alone.** The prompt now names the resolved partner and is told never to name another; the no-AI template fallback took the same fix. Verified per platform: RapidShyp/DocPharma/KwikShip each greeted correctly. The batch button also read "Escalate all N marked **to RapidShyp**" — same wrong assumption — and is now just "Escalate all N marked", since compose decides the recipient from the shipments and refuses a mixed batch.
    - 🎯 **COURIER IS NOW A DASHBOARD-LEVEL FILTER (2026-08-12).** The picker existed but drove almost nothing: it sat in the **shipment-explorer toolbar** and called `dpTableRender()`, so choosing a courier narrowed only the table while FASR, NDR Recovery, RTO Rate, the funnel, TAT and the trend all kept showing every courier — the one question the control looked like it answered was the one it didn't. The API had accepted `courier=` all along; the frontend simply never sent it. **Fix:** `dpLoad()` now sends `courier=`, the dropdown moved into the HEADER beside Source / Payment / Order-type, and the RTO-by-courier chart's click-through goes through the same path. The duplicate client-side filter in `dpTableRender()` was **removed** — the server has already excluded other couriers, and a second independent copy of the rule could silently disagree with the KPIs above it. ⚠️ Two traps handled: the API deliberately builds the courier LIST from the **unfiltered** window so the dropdown doesn't collapse to the current selection (verified: 13 couriers before and after filtering); and when the selected courier falls out of range (usually the date window moved) `dpCouriers()` now resets to All **and reloads** — resetting alone left the dropdown reading "All couriers" while the KPIs on screen were still fetched for the vanished one. Verified live: `Delhivery Enterprise` 2,819 tracked · FASR 51.3% · NDR-recovery 31.2%, vs `DelhiveryDirectSurface500G` 972 · FASR 30.6% · NDR-recovery 34.5% — a 20-point FASR gap that was previously invisible.
    - 📊 **NDR RECOVERY CARD NOW SHOWS THE WHOLE COHORT (2026-08-12).** It showed one number — recovery % — which is a third of the story: on the live 30-day window **60.4% of the NDR cohort RTO'd against 28.4% recovered**, with 11% still open, and none of that was on the page. The card now carries a segmented bar + key for all four outcomes (Recovered / RTO / Pending / Lost). **They partition `ndrTotal` exactly** — `ndrRtoCount + ndrPendingCount + ndrLostCount + ndrRecovered === ndrTotal` is asserted in the verification run — which is the point of showing them: the split can be read off the card and checked by hand. `Lost` is usually 2–3 shipments and is still rendered rather than hidden, because dropping it would make the parts not add up. Added to `summarizeAll()` too, so the compare-mode (previous period) figures carry the same fields.
    - 💰 **REVENUE LENS — THE WHOLE DASHBOARD IN ₹ (2026-08-12).** New `# Orders / ₹ Revenue` toggle in the header re-expresses **every** metric by order value instead of shipment count: KPI cards, status partition, RTO composition, NDR funnel, RTO-by-courier (which also **re-ranks by returned value** — 20 cheap returns and 5 expensive ones are not the same problem), FASR trend, COD-vs-Prepaid, and a sortable **Value** column in the explorer. The server sends BOTH numbers for every figure, so the toggle is a pure re-render with **no refetch** and the count numbers are never recomputed or lost. `Avg Delivery Attempts` has no rupee analogue, so in ₹ mode that slot becomes **Revenue at Risk** (open NDRs + in-transit — ₹11.8L live), a number the page could not show before.
      - ⚠️ **IT MUST NOT USE `shipment_journey_ecom.shipment_value`.** That column is written by the RapidShyp freight fetch only — measured over 30 days it covers **79.4% of RapidShyp rows and 0% of Kwikship and 0% of DocPharma** (0 of 1,799). A revenue view built on it would silently report ₹0 for ~46% of shipments. `orders.total_price` covers **99.9% / 99.6% / 100%** across the three sources, so `attachOrderValue()` joins Shopify at read time. Deliberately **NOT denormalised** onto the journey row: that would be a fourth field the webhook and cron must both remember to write — precisely the drift that froze 240 shipments the same day.
      - ⚠️ **ROUNDING IS DONE ONCE, PER ROW, AT THE SOURCE.** Rounding each GROUP instead let the parts disagree with the whole — the status strip summed to ₹46,60,506 against a stated total of ₹46,60,507. A reconciliation strip off by ₹1 reads as broken, and "the parts add up" is that strip's entire promise. Every `order_value` is made a whole rupee on attach, so every sum downstream is exact by construction. **8 reconciliation assertions are checked in the verification run and all pass** on 30d/90d/180d windows.
      - 🔒 **GATED ON A CAPABILITY — `delivery-perf-revenue` (2026-08-12, by request).** Order value is commercially sensitive: a support agent needs the delivery numbers but not what the business bills. New capability under **Actions** on the Users page (beside Send-escalation-emails / Cancel-order / Post-to-Tally), admins always pass. **Enforced SERVER-SIDE, not just in the UI** — hiding the toggle stops the button being clicked, it does not stop anyone reading the JSON off an endpoint they already have `delivery-perf` access to. For a user without it the value lookup is **skipped entirely** (no order price is read from the database at all) and `stripRevenue()` deletes every ₹ field from the payload before it is sent. The strip works **by SHAPE** — any key named `rev` / `value` / `valueCoverage` or ending in `Value` — so a revenue field added to this response in future is withheld automatically instead of leaking until someone remembers to update a list. Client side: the toggle is **hidden rather than disabled** (the page reads as having no revenue view, not a locked one), the explorer's Value column is dropped, and `dpIsVal()` — the single choke point every ₹ decision funnels through — re-checks the permission, so `_dpMode` cannot take effect from stale state or a hand-edited DOM. ⚠️ Two traps handled: the toggle's visibility is decided on **every** `dpInit()`, not once inside the `_dpWired` block, or a different user signing in without a full page reload would inherit the previous user's access; and the detail row's `colspan` is now **passed in** (13 with the Value column, 12 without) rather than hardcoded, which would otherwise leave the expanded panel short of the table edge for one of the two audiences. **Verified against the live endpoint for four identities — admin / legacy bootstrap token / granted / denied:** the denied payload contains **zero** revenue-shaped keys anywhere (recursively checked), while its count metrics, the NDR cohort split and the courier list are all fully intact.
      - ⚠️ **NEEDED A NEW INDEX — and the failure was SILENT without it.** `orders.name` had **no index** (only id/updated_at/order_number/created_at/meta_*), so each lookup batch seq-scanned all 40,891 orders; a 90-day window hit the Postgres **statement timeout**, and because the error is caught the ₹ view would have rendered **0 for whole batches** rather than erroring. Migration **`add_orders_name_index`** applied. Also: every Shopify name is `#`-prefixed (40,891 of 40,891) while the journey stores it bare, so the query asks for `'#'+name` only — sending both spellings halved the usable chunk and tripled the round trips. Chunk is now 900 (≤1000-row cap) with a one-query fallback for anything that misses. **90-day + compare: 32.4s → 3.7s; 30-day: 7.2s → 1.8s; coverage 99.6–99.9%.** A `valueCoverage` object rides on the response and the UI shows an amber banner if a batch failed or coverage drops below 90% — an understated ₹ total must never be presented as the truth.
    - 📦 **PURCHASE ORDER — new Inventory dashboard (2026-08-13).** Read-only view of EasyEcom's PO book via `GET /wms/V2/getPurchaseOrderDetails`. Live baseline: **69 POs back to 18 Feb 2026, ₹63,37,793 ordered**, of which **6 are Waiting for Approval** (matching the portal exactly) and **10 carry genuinely inbound units** (13 have pending quantity, but 3 of those are Rejected/Cancelled); 48 of the 69 are auto POs.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **WITHOUT A DATE PARAM THE ENDPOINT RETURNS ONLY A RECENT SLICE — SILENTLY. This shipped wrong and the USER caught it, not the code.** Called bare it returned **9 POs (06–12 Aug)** while the EasyEcom portal listed **six** "Waiting for Approval" POs going back to 28 Jul — four of which the dashboard never showed. No error, no flag, no truncation notice: just a short list that looks complete. **`created_after=<YYYY-MM-DD>`** (or `updated_after`) widens it — the same account then returns **69 POs back to 18 Feb 2026** over 14 pages in ~5s. ⚠️ **`start_date` / `from_date` / `date_from` / `po_created_after` are ACCEPTED AND SILENTLY IGNORED** (they return the default 9), so the right parameter had to be found by probing and a wrong guess is indistinguishable from success. Now always sent with a **730-day** lookback (`?days=` overrides; the cache is keyed on it).
      - ⚠️ **CURSOR-PAGINATED WITH A 10-ROW HARD CAP.** `limit` above 10 is a 400 ("Limit cannot be greater than 10"); the first page carries 5 rows plus a `nextUrl` **path** (needs the base prefixed). `nextUrl` is followed to exhaustion behind a `PAGE_CAP` guard that sets `truncated:true` and shows a banner rather than silently cutting the list. **Between the tiny page and the hidden date default, this one endpoint had two independent ways to under-report — check BOTH on any third-party list API.**
      - ✅ **OFFICIAL STATUS MAPPING APPLIED (2026-08-13)** — supplied with the updatePoStatus docs, replacing the raw ids the page had been showing. **Not guessing paid off: 4 is `Rejected`, NOT `Cancelled` (that is 7), and 3 is `Approved`, not "partially received".** Labelling 4 "Cancelled" would have told a buyer that a live rejected PO was dead. Full map: 1 Open · 2 Waiting for approval · 3 Approved · 4 Rejected · 5 Completed · 6 Pending on supplier · 7 Cancelled · 8 Payment pending · 9 Payment done · 11–16 fulfilment-centre states.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **REJECTED / CANCELLED POs KEEP THEIR `pending_quantity`** — so "has units outstanding" was reporting stock as inbound that nobody will ever ship. On live data **3 of the 13 pending POs are dead** (po 51 Rejected 600 units, po 45 Cancelled 5,800, po 42 Rejected 2,756 — ₹2.54L between them). `isOpen` now requires `pending > 0` **AND** a non-terminal status; `isDead` rides on the row so the UI can grey them rather than hide them. Genuine inbound POs: **10, not 13**.
      - 🎛️ **DEFAULT VIEW IS `Manual · Open · Last 30 days` (2026-08-13, by request)** — was All-time/All. It opens on the 6 POs actually awaiting action (34,500 units, ₹15.4L) instead of a 69-row ledger. ⚠️ **KNOW WHAT THAT PAIR EXCLUDES:** an OPEN PO raised more than 30 days ago is hidden *by construction*, and that is precisely the one worth chasing — on live data **4** are (PO 52 Approved 600 units, PO 50 and 49 Completed-but-unreceived, PO 38 Pending dispatch on FF). So the subtitle counts hidden **OPEN** POs **separately and first** — "⚠ 4 OPEN POs raised before this window — switch to All time to see them" — rather than folding them into a bland "N outside the window", which is the kind of line a buyer skims past. The earlier All-time default existed for this reason; the warning is what makes the narrower default safe.
      - ✍️ **WRITES: create a PO, change a PO status (2026-08-13).** `POST /api/purchase-orders/create` → EasyEcom `POST /WMS/Cart/CreatePurchaseOrder` (always `createOrUpdate:'I'`, `isCancel:0` — update/cancel are NOT exposed), and `POST /api/purchase-orders/status` → `POST /wms/updatePoStatus`. Both gated on a NEW capability **`purchase-orders-write`** (Actions group) rather than on view access — reading the PO book is reporting, raising one is a buying decision. UI: a **+ New PO** modal (supplier picker built from vendors we have actually ordered from, a searchable SKU picker, repeatable line rows, live goods total) and a status selector inside the expanded row; both confirm with the real numbers before firing.
      - ⚠️ **NEITHER WRITE WAS EVER FIRED AGAINST EASYECOM DURING DEVELOPMENT.** The only way to "test" success is to create a real purchase order or move a real one's status — same rule as the RapidShyp NDR action. Permission and validation paths were exercised in full (17 cases: viewer blocked 403 on both routes, and every malformed cart rejected 400 *before* the outbound call); the happy path was printed for eye-review, not sent. **The first live run should be one small PO you are willing to see in EasyEcom.**
      - 📐 **…AND THEN THE REPLACEMENT PANEL WAS CLIPPED.** The custom picker opened `position: absolute` inside the line-item grid's `.overflow-x-auto` wrapper — **a scroll container is a clipping context**, so the dropdown was cut off at the scroller's edge and the results were unreadable. Now `position: fixed` with JS placement from the button's `getBoundingClientRect()`: opens below by default, **flips above when the space below is under 200px**, clamps so it can never run off either edge, and sizes its height to the room available. ⚠️ Repositioning uses **ONE app-level tracker**, not listeners per picker — the grid re-renders on every keystroke, so per-instance `window` listeners would pile up referencing discarded DOM. Scroll is bound with `capture: true` because the modal body's own scroll does not bubble to `window`, and Esc closes the picker before the event reaches the modal so one press does not shut the whole form. Geometry unit-tested for all four cases (room below / flip up / both edge clamps / short viewport).
      - 🔎 **THE SKU PICKER WAS A NATIVE `<datalist>` FIRST — AND LOOKED AWFUL.** The browser renders one as an OS popup: a full-height black list that escaped the modal and matched nothing else in the app. **Same lesson as the native `<select>`: the browser's own popup cannot be styled, so anything with a list needs a real panel.** Replaced with a searchable `.poc-sku-*` panel that also earns its keep — each row shows the SKU, the **product name** and **what we last paid**, and choosing one **pre-fills unit price and tax from the last PO** (never overwriting something already typed). A price that has moved since last time is then visible *before* the PO is raised rather than when the invoice lands. Enter picks the only remaining match. ⚠️ EasyEcom sends **no `product_description` on PO lines** (0 of 114 live), so names are joined in from `inventory_snapshots` / `inventory_counts_ecom` — **17 of 19 SKUs named**; the lookup is wrapped in try/catch because a missing name must never block the picker.
      - 💰 **GST AND COST ARE AUTO-CAPTURED ON SKU PICK (2026-08-13)** — but from the right sources, and always shown with their provenance. ⚠️⚠️ **GST CANNOT COME FROM PO HISTORY: EasyEcom returns `tax_rate: 0` on ALL 114 live line items**, so inheriting it would have silently stamped **0% GST on every new PO** — worse than blank, because a zero looks deliberate. HSN *is* present (33049990 ×73, 30049011 ×22, 3304 ×2), and in India the HSN chapter sets the rate, so the suggestion is derived from that via `HSN_GST` (cosmetics 18%, ayurvedic medicaments 12%) with a 4-digit-heading fallback. **12 of 19 SKUs get a suggestion; the other 7 have no HSN on record and are left BLANK rather than guessed**, with the hint saying so. The rate is a suggestion the buyer can overwrite, labelled "from HSN 33049990" underneath, and an override is marked as such — rates change and misclassification is a compliance problem, so this must stay visible, never silent.
      - 📈 **COST IS NOT STABLE, so the movement is surfaced rather than just inherited.** 10 of 19 SKUs have changed price (TE-BDR1 alone has been ordered at ₹27.30, ₹27.50, ₹28.875 and ₹30.135; TE-10NBS1 went ₹80 → ₹89 → ₹94.40). The picker shows the last price *and* the previous one with its date; once a line is filled, the hint under the price box turns into a live delta (**"▲ vs ₹94.4 last (+12.3%)"**) as the buyer types. The point is that a price rise is visible **at the moment of ordering**, not when the invoice arrives.
      - 🧾 **Line-grid layout fixes.** The row's own **Line total** was never updated on input — it read ₹0 while the footer read ₹70, two totals disagreeing on screen. Now the edited row's cell and the grand total both update, and deliberately **without a full re-render**, which would rebuild the inputs and throw away focus and caret position mid-typing. Number spinners are hidden (`appearance: textfield`) because they sit *inside* the box and ate ~18px of a 90px field, rendering "10" as a sliver beside an arrow; `inputmode` keeps the numeric keypad on mobile. Modal widened to 1180px with explicit column widths.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **`referenceCode` IS MANDATORY — UNDOCUMENTED, and it fails as HTTP 200.** The first live create attempt came back `HTTP 200 {"code":400,"message":"referenceCode field is mandatory/cannot be left blank"}` — **the exact trap the create path was written to catch**, and the reason `body.code` is checked rather than the HTTP status. EasyEcom's own docs list it as an ordinary field. It is now required on both sides and the form arrives **pre-filled** with `EC-YYYYMMDD-HHMM`, so the round trip is never spent discovering it. ⚠️ Their docs may under-specify other mandatory fields the same way — when a create fails, read `easyecom.message` in the response, which is passed straight through.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **THE VENDOR FIELD IS `vendor_id` (snake_case), NOT `vendorId` AS DOCUMENTED.** EasyEcom's own sample shows `"vendorId": 100176`, but our numeric id sent that way is rejected: *"Vendor cannot be found with vendorId 260128 or vendor_id"*. **That message is the clue** — it looks `vendorId` up as a vendor **CODE** and `vendor_id` as the numeric id. Confirmed by probing: `vendorId: 260128` ✗ · `vendor_id: 260128` ✓ · `vendorId: "VCPL"` ✓. We send the numeric `vendor_id`, falling back to the code only when no numeric id exists — sending both invites them to disagree. 🔒 **How this was found WITHOUT creating a PO:** every probe carried a deliberately invalid SKU, so the call could never succeed; a correct vendor announced itself by the error *moving on* from "Vendor cannot be found" to "Unable to find the sku". **Reuse that trick for any write API where success has real-world consequences.**
      - 🏭 **Vendors now come from the master, `GET /wms/V2/getVendors`** (also undocumented in what we were given; found by probing 15 candidate paths). Previously the picker was derived from PO history, so it could only ever offer suppliers we had already ordered from — a new supplier was unreachable. The master also carries `vendor_code` (the fallback id), payment/delivery terms and the dispatch city. Disabled vendors (`active = 0`) are filtered out, and the picker shows each supplier's PO count so a familiar name is obvious. Live: 3 active — Vive Cosmetics (VCPL, 56 POs), Mefoh Healthcare (MH, 10), Zoween (DS, 2).
      - 💬 **`ecConfirm()` replaces `window.confirm()` for these actions.** The native dialog cannot be styled, shows the browser's own "localhost:5002 says" chrome, and collapses everything into one blob of text — so the figure a user must check hardest was rendered worst. `ecConfirm({title, intro, rows, note, danger, confirmText})` returns a Promise and renders a labelled table with the headline number emphasised; Esc/backdrop cancel, Enter confirms (suppressed while focus is in a field). **Create** lists supplier / reference / lines / units / expected date with the goods total called out, and warns about any line priced away from what we last paid. **Status change** names the current and new status, and when the new one is Rejected or Cancelled it switches to the danger style and spells out the outstanding units being written off. It is generic — any other destructive action can use it (the remaining 12 native `confirm()` calls were left alone rather than retrofitted blind).
      - ⚠️⚠️ **CreatePurchaseOrder LANDS THE PO ON "Approved" (status 3), NOT "Waiting for approval".** Confirmed on the first real create — PO 70 (id 2144985) went straight to status 3, **skipping the approval step entirely**, so an unsigned-off PO could reach a supplier. There is no documented status parameter on create, so the route immediately follows up with `updatePoStatus(poId, 2)`. ⚠️ If that second call fails the PO **still exists, and approved** — reported loudly (a `warning` on the response and a red toast) rather than swallowed, because a half-completed write reported as clean success is the worst possible outcome here. `setPoStatus()` is shared by create and the status route so the two can never drift on how success is judged.
      - ⬇️ **PO DOWNLOAD — EasyEcom's own PDF, via the host that actually works.** `GET /downloadPurchaseOrder?po_id=…` returns JSON with an S3 `file_url`; we fetch that and stream the bytes. ⚠️ **THE SAME PATH EXISTS ON BOTH HOSTS AND ONLY ONE IS USABLE:** the browser calls it on **app.easyecom.io** authenticated by PORTAL SESSION COOKIES (PHPSESSID / laravel_session) — server-side that is `401 Unauthenticated`, and app.easyecom.io is also the host the VPS is WAF-blocked from (why warehouse routing had to become a browser extension). The identical path on **api.easyecom.io** accepts our ordinary api-key + JWT. Measured: 401 on app.\*, **200 + a 34 KB PDF on api.\*** in ~0.5s. **If a portal-only call ever needs porting, try api.easyecom.io before assuming it needs a browser session.** A locally generated pdfkit document (2.3 KB, our letterhead) stays as a fallback for any PO their generator will not render, reachable with `?local=1`; the served source is stamped in an `X-PO-Pdf-Source` header so a fallback is never silent. The route reuses the cached book — refetching all 14 pages to print one PO earned an **HTTP 429** during testing.
      - 💸⚠️⚠️ **UNIT PRICE: CREATE TAKES *NET*, READ RETURNS *GROSS* — AUTOFILL WAS RE-TAXING EVERY RE-ORDER.** Proven on our own POs: we sent `unitPrice` 15 with `taxRate` 18 and EasyEcom stored `item_price` **17.70** (= 15 × 1.18); we sent 70 and it stored **82.60**. Feeding the stored figure back into the create form therefore compounds — **15 → 17.70 → 20.89 → 24.65** over three re-orders of the same SKU, with nothing on screen to show why. Every line now carries `netPrice` and `grossPrice` **separately and never merged**; the picker autofills and the form submits the NET figure, and every price on screen is labelled *excl. tax* with the gross shown alongside. This also explains part of the earlier "price movement" readings — TE-10NBS1 80→94.4, TE-BB1 92→108.56, TE-LB1 22→25.96 are all exactly ×1.18, i.e. the same net price recorded gross, not a supplier increase.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE FIRST FIX SILENTLY DID NOTHING: `tax_rate` COMES BACK AS THE STRING `"18%"`, NOT `18`.** `Number("18%")` is NaN, so the numeric parse read every line as 0%, `netOf()` divided by 1, and net came out equal to gross — the split looked implemented *and verified* while the re-taxing bug was still live. It was caught only because the check asserted a **known** value (TE-Test1 must autofill 15, not 17.7) rather than an internal consistency that a no-op also satisfies. **Assert against a number you independently know, not against the code's own arithmetic.** Parsing is now `pct()`, which strips non-numeric characters.
      - 📄 **CREATE NOW ENDS IN A RESULT PANEL, not a toast.** A PO is a commitment, and the thing the buyer most needs afterwards — the status EasyEcom ACTUALLY gave it, read back from their API rather than assumed — is what a three-second banner conveys worst. `poResultModal()` states PO id, live status, supplier, reference, lines/units and the goods total, carries the approval warning when one applies, and offers **Download PO** right there, which is the moment you most want the document.
      - Write-path details: only statuses **1–9** are settable — 11–16 are what the fulfilment centre reports, and setting them by hand would invent a state nothing else agrees with. ⚠️ **HTTP 200 is not trusted alone on create** — EasyEcom returns 200 with a failure code in the body on some errors (the trap that made the warehouse-router extension report false successes), so `body.code` is checked too. `updatePoStatus` documents **no response body**, so there success is judged on HTTP status and only an explicit non-2xx `code` counts as failure. Both API-key header spellings (`x-api-key` and `x_api_key`) are sent, because the two endpoints document different ones. The PO cache is dropped after any write so the list can never come back stale.
      - ⚠️ **`po_status_id` IS UNDOCUMENTED — so nothing that matters depends on it.** EasyEcom ships no meaning for the id; the labels (2 Open / 3 Partially received / 5 Completed) are **inferred** from live data (status 2 were the only POs with pending quantity). Guessing a label is how a dashboard starts lying confidently, so **open-vs-received is derived from `pending_quantity`**, which is factual, and the status chip is informational only, with the raw id in its tooltip. If EasyEcom re-numbers statuses the labels go stale but every number on the page stays right.
      - ⚠️ **`total_po_value` is cross-checked, not assumed.** EasyEcom's header total agrees with Σ(`item_price` × `original_quantity`) on **69/69** live POs, but a per-PO `valueMismatch` flag is computed anyway and surfaced as a ⚠ on the row plus a page banner — if their header total ever drifts from the line items that is worth SEEING, not silently preferring one side. Money and quantities are converted to numbers server-side (EasyEcom returns stringified decimals like `"798.0000"`) so the client never re-parses them per render.
      - 🙈 **AUTO POs ARE HIDDEN BY DEFAULT (2026-08-13, by request) — AND THE PAGE SAYS SO.** EasyEcom stamps its own reorder documents with `po_ref_num = "Auto PO"`; on live data they are **48 of 69 POs** — so they bury the 21 buyer-raised ones. The **Manual / Auto / All** toggle defaults to Manual. ⚠️ **A view that silently shows a third of the book while printing confident totals is just a wrong page**, so the subtitle always names what is held back ("6 auto POs hidden — switch to Auto or All to see them") and how many rows fall outside the date window. Matching is `/^auto\s*po$/i` on the ref, not an exact string.
      - 📅 **DATE FILTER DEFAULTS TO ALL TIME, DELIBERATELY.** A purchase order is a **live document, not a time-series point** — an open PO raised four months ago is precisely the one you must not lose sight of, and a rolling default window would hide it. Presets are offered (Today / Yesterday / Last 7 / 30 / 90 / Custom) but none is imposed. Filtering is **client-side** over the already-fetched book (small, cached), so switching is instant with no refetch, and the day is compared as a `YYYY-MM-DD` **string slice** — no `Date` parsing, hence no UTC-vs-IST day shift.
      - ⚠️ **The vendor and status dropdown counts are computed over the TYPE+DATE scope (`poBase()`), not the whole book.** Using the server's all-POs tally would advertise "Vive Cosmetics (5)" while the default view shows 2 of them — a small lie that makes every other number look suspect.
      - "Value still to arrive" is the **pending units at their line price**, not the whole PO value — a PO 90% received does not have 100% of its money outstanding. Responses are cached 3 minutes (EasyEcom is rate-limited and this changes a few times a day); Refresh passes `?fresh=1`. An open PO whose expected-delivery date has passed shows that date in red.
    - 🚚 **LAST-MILE FUNNEL — new top-level dashboard (2026-08-12).** Delivery Performance answers *how shipments ended up*; this answers a different question — **of the parcels that actually reached the doorstep, how many landed, how many came back, and how fast**. Three transitions, all derivable from existing columns with no new capture: `OFD → Delivered` (`out_for_delivery_at` set + `outcome='delivered'`), `OFD → RTO`, and `In-Transit → RTO` (**no** `out_for_delivery_at` + `outcome='rto'`). The third matches the existing `rto_no_attempt` flag **exactly** — verified over 90 days on the active couriers, **2,742 / 129 with zero disagreement** — so both signals are safe; the timestamp is used so all three definitions read off one field. Cohorted on **OFD date** (the two OFD funnels) with the never-attempted set anchored on **RTO date**, since by definition it has no OFD to cohort by; it is reported in its own panel so it can never contaminate the OFD arithmetic. Live 30-day baseline: **4,358 out → 3,100 delivered · 1,003 RTO · 255 open**, median **4.2 h** to deliver, 88.4% landing same-day. Endpoint response 138 ms (Today) to 752 ms (30 days).
      - 🚨🚨 **EACH PARCEL IS COUNTED ON THE DATE ITS OUTCOME HAPPENED — `attributed_at` (FINAL rule, 2026-08-13).** A generated column: `coalesce(delivered_at, rto_at, last_ofd_at)`, indexed, so the range query is ONE index scan and the value cannot drift from whatever a writer remembered to set. **Delivered on the 8th means the 8th** — which is what a human, an invoice and EasyEcom all mean. Found because TE25-39612 (₹748) went out 07-08 16:35 and delivered 08-08 19:44: under the previous rule it was counted on the **7th**, so an 8-onward window was one parcel and ₹748 short. Properties held: each parcel in exactly ONE window; adjacent windows still ADD UP (241 + 158 = 399 parcels, 98 + 67 = 165 delivered, ₹2,16,169 + ₹1,44,712 = ₹3,60,881, all asserted); every resolved parcel counted on its own outcome date (0 mismatches); and because `delivered_at`/`rto_at` never change, **a resolved parcel is frozen into its window for ever**, so past reports stay reproducible. Only unresolved parcels move, and only forward. The cohort still requires `out_for_delivery_at is not null` — this page is about parcels that reached a doorstep. ⚠️ **AN RTO IS COUNTED ON `rto_at`, WHICH CAN BE DAYS AFTER THE LAST DOOR TRIP** (a parcel sits before the return is raised — TE25-36926: last trip 28-07, RTO 08-08). Deliberate and symmetric with delivered: the window reports **when we lost the money**, not when the van went out. On the 08–10 window that moved RTO from 103 to 132. The explorer keeps the three dates separate — **Counted on** (outcome), **Last door trip** (departure), **First door trip** — and the row shows "out DD-MM" whenever the departure differs from the counted date, so an overnight delivery never looks misplaced.
      - **Superseded rule 3, kept because it is easy to fall back into: "attribute to the DECISIVE trip".** Two wrong rules were tried first and BOTH are easy to fall back into, so the code carries all three in a block comment. **(1) "First trip in the window"** — the original; see the paragraph below for the 13.9% of delivered parcels it hid. **(2) "Any trip in the window"** — the first fix; it found those parcels but a parcel whose trips straddle a boundary then matched BOTH windows. TE25-41076 went out 10 Aug 19:44 *and* 11 Aug 08:53, so it appeared in 08–10 **and** 11–12 and its single delivery was counted twice; **65 parcels were double-counted across just those two adjacent windows, so two periods did not add up to the combined period** and exports could not be stacked. Caught by the user diffing two CSVs. **(3) The rule now: the DECISIVE trip** — the last trip at or before the outcome, or the latest trip so far while unresolved. Properties, all asserted against live data: a parcel lands in **exactly one** window (0 overlap between 08–10 and 11–12); **adjacent windows ADD UP** (211 + 126 = 337 parcels, 97 + 67 = 164 delivered, 103 + 32 = 135 RTO, verified against the combined window); the daily bars sum to the headline; the outcome is counted on the day it was actually earned; and **once a parcel resolves its attribution is FROZEN** — a delivered parcel can never move between historical windows, so past reports stay reproducible. Only still-open parcels move, and only forward, which is correct because they are not settled yet. The explorer column is **"Counted on trip"** (not "went out"), and the row's detail panel lists every trip with **← counted on this trip** against the decisive one.
      - **The underlying capture bug that made all of this possible (2026-08-13).** Found from a user report: nine AWBs delivered on 11–12 Aug were absent from an 11–12 Aug view. **All nine had genuinely gone to the door again inside the window** (TE25-39964 went out six times: 09-08 ×2, 11-08 ×2, 12-08 ×2). The cause: **`out_for_delivery_at` stores only the FIRST out-for-delivery scan.** `attempts` counted every later trip but their DATES were thrown away, so the parcel was cohorted on day 1 and vanished from every window after it. Scale, measured over 30 days: **47.4% of RapidShyp parcels and 20.7% of Kwikship parcels go to the door more than once** — RapidShyp made **6,867 door trips across 3,716 parcels** and the dashboard could see only the 3,716 firsts; on the user's own window, 243 parcels really went to a door and **57 (23%) were invisible** while the page reported 128.
        - **The fix — a new `ofd_dates timestamptz[]` + `last_ofd_at` on `shipment_journey_ecom`.** Every parser now records every trip; `ofd_dates[1] = out_for_delivery_at` by construction. The range query is a **range-overlap index scan** (`out_for_delivery_at <= to AND last_ofd_at >= from`, both indexed) followed by an exact per-trip test in memory. **Each parcel is still counted ONCE per window**, so every rate stays per-parcel and comparable to before.
        - ⚠️ **FOUR PARSERS CARRY THIS LINE AND MUST CHANGE TOGETHER** — `app/api/delivery_journey.js`, `app/api/kwikship_sync.js`, `supabase/functions/rapidshyp-webhook`, `supabase/functions/kwikship-webhook`. The two edge functions are hand-ports that have drifted three times before. **Belt and braces: a DB trigger (`trg_sje_fill_ofd_dates`) fills `ofd_dates = {out_for_delivery_at}` whenever a writer supplies one without the other**, so a future fifth writer that knows nothing about these columns degrades to today's single-trip behaviour instead of making shipments vanish. Both edge functions redeployed to **v10** (`verify_jwt:false` preserved, health-checked).
        - **Backfill + result.** RapidShyp derived from the `track_scans` already in `order_tracking` (15,667 rows; a dry run first confirmed **trip #1 matched the existing `out_for_delivery_at` on 15,569 of 15,569 rows — zero disagreement**, independently validating the parse and the IST→UTC shift). Kwikship had almost nothing stored locally, so 107 multi-trip parcels were re-fetched from **GoKwik's PUBLIC v2 tracking endpoint — no credentials needed, which matters because the `gk-app-id`/`gk-app-secret` pair is currently 401ing**; 73 parcels / 95 trips recovered, writing ONLY `ofd_dates`+`last_ofd_at` so no shipment's state could move. Net effect, swept over every one of the last 30 days ("was a parcel delivered that day visible in that day's view?"): **435 missing → 28** (RapidShyp 388 → 9, Kwikship 47 → 19).
        - ⚠️ **DURATIONS NOW RUN FROM THE TRIP THAT PRODUCED THE OUTCOME**, not the parcel's first ever trip — otherwise "OFD → delivered" measures the idle days between failed attempts. Identical for single-trip parcels; the median moved 5.5h → 4.5h on the test window. The daily trend groups on the **in-window** trip, and the explorer shows that trip with an "N door trips" hint plus the full list in the row's detail panel, so a parcel dated before the range is never unexplained.
        - **Expect the headline to look worse, because it was flattering.** Same window and filters, before → after: **128 → 179 parcels, 48.4% → 39.7% delivered, 10.9% → 20.1% RTO.** The parcels being added are 2nd/3rd/4th-trip ones, which RTO far more often; the true 11–12 Aug RTO rate was 20.1%.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **HEADLINE RATES DIVIDE BY THE WHOLE COHORT — AND THE "PENDING OUTCOME" CARD IS WHAT MAKES THAT HONEST (changed 2026-08-13, by request: "OFD → Delivered = 755/1243").** The original build divided by **resolved** only. Both bases are now computed server-side and both are on screen: the **headline is `deliveredRateAll = delivered ÷ ofd`**, and each card footnotes the resolved-basis figure (`"812 of 1277 sent out · 79.5% of the 1022 resolved"`). Delivered + RTO + **Pending** therefore sum to **exactly 100%** — verified on live data — which is the whole reason the third card exists. **Do not remove the Pending card or the footers.** The cost of this basis is real and unchanged: a young cohort reads low, because a parcel that went out this morning has not had its chance yet. On 12 Aug, **130 of 241 (54%) were still open**, so cohort-basis reported **40.5%** delivered on a day that was going perfectly well (resolved-basis: 84.5%). The Pending card, the funnel strip's own slice, and the strip's caption ("*the cards above divide by all 1277 — so 20% still in flight holds the rates down until this cohort matures*") are the three things that keep Today and Yesterday from being alarming and wrong. **The per-attempt cards are the deliberate exception** and divide by *resolved within their bucket*: inside one bucket the question is "delivered **or** RTO", and an open parcel has not answered it — each card states its own open count beside the figure. The explorer likewise shows a dash rather than a running clock for an unresolved parcel, which would otherwise read as a slow delivery.
      - 🔢 **ATTEMPT-WISE CARDS: 1st / 2nd / 3rd-or-more (2026-08-13).** `byAttempt()` buckets the cohort on the courier's own `attempts` count (always ≥ 1 on an OFD cohort — 0 rows with `attempts = 0` over 7 days), so the three buckets **partition the cohort exactly** (1277 = 647 + 478 + 152, asserted in test). Each card shows the bucket's delivery rate plus two clickable halves. **Read the two halves differently:** `attempts = 1, delivered` is "landed first time"; `attempts = 1, RTO` is **"the courier gave up after ONE attempt"** — the claimable half. Live signal: first-attempt buckets deliver **84.4%**, the 3rd+ bucket only **47.7%**.
      - 🖱️ **EVERY CARD FILTERS THE EXPLORER, THROUGH ONE REGISTRY.** `LM_FOCUS` is the single source of truth for what the table shows; the dropdown *and* every clickable card write to `_lmFocus`, so the two can never disagree or silently AND themselves down to an empty table. A key the dropdown cannot represent (the attempt filters) surfaces as a dismissible chip, so an active filter is never invisible. **`no_attempt` swaps the SOURCE ARRAY rather than filtering it** — those rows have no out-for-delivery event (that is their entire point), so no predicate over `shipments` can reach them; they are returned in the same row shape but never concatenated, because a single leak would corrupt every cohort denominator on the page. Regression-tested: every clickable figure equals the number printed on the card it sits on, and the cohort/no-attempt sets have zero overlap.
      - 🗺️ **DESTINATION CHART IS ONE DIMENSION AT A TIME (State ⇄ Zone toggle, 2026-08-13).** Previously zones and states were concatenated into a single bar list, which read as one ranking even though **a state sits inside a zone** — so the same shipment appeared twice and the rows could not be compared. The `.seg-toggle` switches dimension **client-side with no refetch** (`seg(…, local = true)`), since it is a pure view change over data already in hand. Bar length now uses the cohort basis to match the cards, but **bar COLOUR still comes from the RTO rate on resolved** — a group whose parcels are merely young must not be coloured green for having nothing decided yet. The tooltip carries every number behind both.
      - 🔍 **CLICK ANY EXPLORER ROW → COURIER SCAN LOG (`GET /api/last-mile/shipment/:awb`, 2026-08-13).** Fetched **lazily, once per AWB, cached for the session** — 3,000 shipments × ~20 scans in the main payload would be a megabyte of JSON on a view that refetches on every filter change. Only the opened cell is patched on arrival, never the whole table, so another row opened meanwhile survives. ⚠️ **WHERE THE LOG ACTUALLY LIVES, measured — do not assume:** RapidShyp → `order_tracking.tracking_details.records[].shipment_details[].track_scans`, **791/797** rows; KwikShip → `shipment_journey_ecom.raw.status_history`, but only **49/642 (7.6%)**, because only the webhook writes `raw` and it overwrites rather than accumulates; `journey.raw` for RapidShyp is **13/797** and is *not* a usable source. So the response **always** includes a milestone timeline synthesised from the journey's own timestamp columns, and `scanSource` (`courier` | `milestones`) is stated on the panel — a KwikShip parcel with no log must not look like a parcel that never moved. ⚠️ **RapidShyp stamps scans `DD-MM-YYYY HH:MM:SS` IN IST**, which `new Date()` reads as **Invalid Date** (the same format trap as `/get-orders` `date`); `istStampToISO()` parses it and shifts −05:30, verified against the journey's own UTC column (scan `11-08-2026 10:22:34` ↔ `out_for_delivery_at 2026-08-11T04:52:34Z`). Both parsers guard `new Date(null)` explicitly — that is the **epoch, not Invalid Date**, so a missing scan time would otherwise render as 01-01-1970. The endpoint is covered by the existing `/^\/last-mile/i` permission rule (confirmed: no new gate needed, and no earlier rule swallows it).
      - 🏷️ **THE DATE FILTER IS LABELLED "COUNTED ON", AND SO IS THE EXPLORER COLUMN (2026-08-13).** Deliberately the SAME two words in both places: a user picks a range labelled *Counted on*, then sees a *Counted on* column with a date in it, and the rule teaches itself without anyone reading a sentence. The label sits ON the control (inside a `.lm-dategrp` so flex-wrap can never orphan it from its select) rather than as a footnote under it — a footnote is read once and becomes wallpaper. Full rule behind an **ⓘ** on hover/focus: *delivered parcels count on their delivery date · RTOs on the date the return was raised · still-moving ones on their last delivery attempt · each parcel appears in one period only, so two periods add up to their total.* That last line matters — it is the property people rely on when stacking exports, and nothing on screen said they could.
      - 🔇 **THE DOCPHARMA-EXCLUSION BANNER BECAME AN ⓘ ON THE SOURCE TOGGLE, AND THE STRIP IS NOW WARNINGS-ONLY (2026-08-13).** The note never changes, so a permanent full-width blue banner above the KPI cards was furniture on every load. It now hangs off the control it explains, answering the only question that control raises ("why is DocPharma not an option?") at the moment someone asks it; the text still comes from `coverage.note`, so a change in courier mix follows automatically. The strip that carried it is kept but **recoloured amber and shown ONLY when the ₹ figures are genuinely incomplete** (`Revenue covers N of M shipments — ₹ totals exclude K with no order value on file`). A strip that is always on gets ignored, and then the one time it matters it gets ignored too.
      - 💴 **IN THE ₹ LENS THE MONEY LEADS AND THE SHARE RIDES SMALL (2026-08-13, by request).** `₹84.2k` in 2rem type with `39%` beside it in 1rem (`.lm-kpi-sub`), on the SAME baseline so the pair reads as one measurement rather than two competing numbers; the exact amount is on the headline's `title` since the compact form rounds. Applied to all five outcome cards and the three attempt cards. **The orders lens is unchanged — the % still leads there.** Someone in revenue mode came for the money; a 28.7% set in 2rem type buries it.
      - 🧨 **BUMP `app.js?v=` ON EVERY FRONTEND CHANGE — THIS WAS MISSED FOR A WHOLE SESSION (2026-08-13).** `/static` is served with **`maxAge: '30d'`**, so the query string in `<script src="/static/app.js?v=…">` is the ONLY thing that makes a browser re-fetch. `index.html` is correctly served `no-store`, which hides the problem: the shell reloads, the JS does not. An entire session of Last-Mile and Purchase Order work shipped against a tag last touched on 3 Aug and was invisible in the browser — and the symptom ("data still shows the same as before") is indistinguishable from the fix not working, so it burns real debugging time. Two things are needed after ANY change here: **bump the `?v=` tag** (frontend) and **restart node** (backend — `last_mile.js` and friends are `require`d once at boot). Current tag: `2026-08-13-lastmile-revenue`.
      - 💰 **ORDERS ⇄ REVENUE LENS (2026-08-13).** The whole dashboard switches unit: KPI cards, the attempt cards, the funnel strip, the daily chart, the by-courier and by-destination bars, the RTO-reason ranking, the explorer's Value column and its footer total. **Both lenses read the SAME partition — only the unit changes**, so a reader toggling is looking at one dataset counted two ways, never two different datasets. Every ₹ figure reconciles: shipment values, attempt buckets, daily bars and the courier cut each sum to the headline exactly (asserted).
        - 🔗 **THE REVENUE HELPERS ARE NOW SHARED, IN `app/api/order_value.js`.** They previously lived inside `delivery_reports.js`; when Last-Mile needed them the choice was copy or share, and hand-copied logic is the single most expensive recurring bug in this repo (four journey parsers, three multi-week data corruptions). **One implementation of `canSeeRevenue` / `attachOrderValue` / `sumV` now serves both pages**, so they can never disagree about who may see ₹ or what a shipment is worth. Delivery Performance was re-verified after the extraction — 2,560/2,562 matched, ₹20,78,828, and its own gate still strips for an un-permissioned user.
        - 🔒 **Same capability as Delivery Performance (`delivery-perf-revenue`) — one grant covers both pages**, and the label on the Users page was widened to say so. Enforced server-side: when the capability is absent the value lookup is **skipped entirely** (no price is read from the database at all) and `stripRevenue()` removes every `rev`/`value`/`valueCoverage` key at any depth before the response is sent. That stripper is **key-name based on purpose**, so a revenue field added anywhere in this payload later is stripped automatically rather than leaking because someone forgot to update a list of paths. Verified: an un-permissioned caller gets `revenueAllowed:false`, zero ₹ keys anywhere in the JSON, and unchanged order counts.
        - ⚠️ **THE THREE SHARES ARE MADE TO RECONCILE, NOT LEFT TO ROUND.** Delivered + RTO + Pending are presented as a partition summing to 100% and the page says so, but rounding the three independently breaks that promise about a third of the time — live data gave **51.1 + 27.0 + 22.0 = 100.1%**, and a strip that adds to 100.1% reads as broken however correct the figures behind it are. `share3()` therefore DERIVES the residual (pending) from the other two. Same class of fix as rounding order value once at source to stop the ₹1 reconciliation error.
