        - The header states ₹ coverage when it is short (`Revenue covers 126 of 128 shipments — ₹ totals exclude 2 with no order value on file`) rather than quietly under-reporting. The RTO-reason column in ₹ ranks **value EXPOSED to each reason** and deliberately does not partition RTO revenue — a parcel with several reasons contributes its full value to each — and the tooltip says so.
      - 📥 **SHIPMENT EXPLORER → DOWNLOAD CSV (2026-08-13).** **The CSV carries `Order value (INR)` in BOTH lenses** — the file is what gets pivoted in Excel, so it should hold the number regardless of which toggle happened to be on. Written as a **raw integer, never "₹1,234"**: a formatted string is text in Excel and will not SUM. The column count is identical in both lenses, and the file's value total reconciles to the API headline exactly (₹1,15,955 on the test window). Exports **exactly what the table is showing** — the active card/dropdown filter, the search text and the sort order. The guarantee is structural, not a convention: `lmFilteredRows()` is the SINGLE definition of "what the explorer is currently showing", and both the table and the export read it, so a downloaded file cannot disagree with the screen it was taken from. Anything that changes what is displayed belongs in that function, never in `lmTable()`. **It exports every matching row, not just the 500 the table draws** — that cap is a rendering limit, not a filter — and the toast names the filter and warns when the server-side 3,000-row payload cap means the window held more. 21 columns incl. the in-window trip, first trip, door-trip count and NDR reasons; dates in the dashboard's DD-MM-YYYY so a row is recognisable as the row on screen, while the FILENAME stays ISO (`last-mile-delivered-2026-08-11_to_2026-08-12.csv`) so downloads sort chronologically and two filters never overwrite each other. UTF-8 BOM so Excel opens it correctly; RFC-4180 quoting verified against commas, quotes, newlines, null and zero. Regression-tested across all 10 reachable filters: CSV row count and AWB ORDER matched the filtered view every time. Also added the missing `.filter-btn:disabled` style — an unstyled disabled button keeps its hover and reads as clickable, so a user presses it repeatedly and concludes the feature is broken.
      - 🧩 **`ecSyncSelect(sel)` — new shared helper.** Setting `sel.value` **in code** fires no `change` event *and* does not touch the value **attribute**, so neither the `.csel` change listener nor its MutationObserver sees it and the custom dropdown keeps showing the old label. `ecSyncSelect()` dispatches a `csel:sync` event that repaints the button face without dispatching a real `change` (which would re-enter the caller's own handler and recurse). Use it anywhere a `.filter-select` is set programmatically.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **DOCPHARMA IS EXCLUDED AND MUST STAY EXCLUDED.** Its API returns status **milestones only, no scan log**, so `out_for_delivery_at` is never set for a DocPharma shipment and every DocPharma return looks like "the courier never tried". Measured over 30 days: **216 of its 216 RTOs** would land in that bucket, turning a true figure of 18 into 234 — a ~13× fabrication in the headline number the panel exists to report. `LM_SOURCES = ['rapidshyp','kwikship']` is enforced in the query and the exclusion is stated on the page. (It is also the courier being wound down.) Same family of trap as `shipment_value` and `freight_total` — **before building any metric on a journey column, check its coverage per source.**
      - 📅 **DATE FORMAT IS `DD-MM-YYYY` EVERYWHERE ON SCREEN, `DD-MM` ON CHART AXES (standardised 2026-08-12).** Last-Mile, Delivery Performance and Rate Watch were all printing raw `YYYY-MM-DD` in their header ranges, and the trend axes read `MM-DD` (`r.date.slice(5)`) — which is not just non-standard but genuinely ambiguous: `08-07` reads as 8 July to an Indian user. All now go through the dashboard-wide **`_dmy()`**, plus a new **`_dm()`** for the compact axis form. ⚠️ **`_dmy()` now REFORMATS A BARE `YYYY-MM-DD` BY SLICING, never by re-parsing** — `new Date('2026-08-07')` is UTC midnight, so converting it to IST afterwards can land on the wrong day, and these strings are already the IST calendar day the server computed. Full ISO timestamps still convert through IST exactly as before, so existing callers (claims/SLA) are unchanged — verified both paths. **The one place ISO stays is export FILENAMES** (`rapidshyp-recon-2026-08-07_to_….csv`), so downloads sort chronologically.
      - 🎨 **FILTER CONTROLS MUST USE THE APP-WIDE CLASSES — this shipped wrong first time.** The dropdowns were built with ad-hoc Tailwind (`text-sm px-3 py-2 border …`), which looks fine CLOSED and completely foreign the moment it is opened: a native `<select>` cannot style its open option list, so it renders the raw OS list. The project already solves this — `ecEnhanceFilterSelects()` upgrades every `select.filter-select` into the custom `.csel` dropdown (native select stays the source of truth, so `value` and `change` handlers are untouched, and a MutationObserver keeps the label right when options are populated later). **A NEW DASHBOARD MUST BE ADDED TO THAT SELECTOR LIST** — it now reads `#delivery-perf-view header select, #ops-control-view select, #last-mile-view select, #fulfillment-ops-view select`. Standard classes: **`.filter-select`** (dropdown), **`.filter-input`** (text), **`.date-input`** (date), **`.filter-btn`** / **`.filter-btn-primary`** (secondary / confirming action) — all 38px so a filter row keeps one baseline. Also added **`.seg-toggle`**, the reusable form of the All/COD/Prepaid pill switch, which until now was an ID list (`#dp-source, #dp-payment, …`) every new dashboard had to be hand-added to; missing it renders the control ~34px beside 38px dropdowns and visibly breaks the row. Rate Watch was corrected to the same classes and its duplicate `#ops-rt-dim` CSS deleted in favour of `.seg-toggle`.
      - Implementation notes: previous window is always the **equal-length period immediately before**, so deltas compare like with like; the trend groups on **IST calendar days** (`istDay()`) because grouping on raw UTC dates splits an Indian evening across two days; date presets use `_ymd()` (local), never `toISOString()`, which between 00:00–05:30 IST returns yesterday and would shift every preset back a day; option lists are built from the **unfiltered** window so choosing a courier never empties the zone dropdown; and negative durations (bad timestamps) are dropped rather than allowed to distort the medians.
    - 🗄️ **OPS CONTROL: FOUR TABS ARCHIVED, RATE WATCH ADDED (2026-08-12).** The original four tabs (NDR Queue, Risk Orders, Claims & Recovery, Insights — six panes) went unused, so they were folded **wholesale into a single "Archived" tab** rather than deleted. Every pane, loader, endpoint, sort and export still works untouched; they are one click away instead of four tabs wide. **To bring one back:** move its entry out of `OPS_ARCHIVED_SUBTABS` in `app.js` into its own top-level tab and add a matching `<button data-t="…">` in `index.html` — nothing else needs restoring. 🔒 **The Archived tab is ADMIN-ONLY** (2026-08-12, by request) — hidden for everyone else so the team sees only the live tab; `opsSwitchTab()` also refuses `'archived'` for a non-admin so stale state can't open it, and anyone sitting on it falls back to Rate Watch. Visibility is decided on **every** `opsInit()`, not once inside the `_opsWired` block, or a different user signing in without a full page reload would inherit the previous user's tab set. ⚠️ **This is a visibility change, not a security boundary** — the archived panes call the same `/ops-control/*` endpoints, which remain open to any holder of the `ops-control` permission. Lock those routes to admin if the data itself ever needs restricting. While there, `OPS_ALL_PANES` is now **derived** from `OPS_TAB_PANES` instead of a second hardcoded pane list that `opsSwitchTab()` kept in sync by hand — adding a pane and forgetting that list would have left two panes visible at once.
    - 💹 **RATE WATCH — "is the courier charging us more than it did?" (new tab, 2026-08-12).** `GET /ops-control/rate-watch?dim=zone|state|city|courier&days=N&min=M` compares average shipping charge per shipment over the last N days against the **immediately preceding N days** (equal length, so seasonality and volume growth cancel), returning per group: was/now, Δ%, **₹ spend impact** (the per-shipment move × this period's volume — a +₹2 move on 1,500 shipments is ₹3,000, which a percentage alone never shows), avg weight, ₹/kg, RTO% and a `thin` flag when either side is under the minimum. UI: dimension toggle, diverging inflation/deflation bar chart, spend-impact chart, sortable table with a plain-English verdict per row.
      - ⚠️ **THE WEIGHT COLUMN IS NOT DECORATION.** Average charge moves for two different reasons — the courier repriced, or **we** shipped heavier parcels — and mistaking the second for the first means arguing with a courier about a rate that never changed. `applied_weight` is in **GRAMS** (median 80 g, max 1,030 g). Live proof on the first run: **Zone A +17.7% charge while its parcels got 28.6% LIGHTER** (a genuine rate rise) versus **Andhra Pradesh +11.1% charge with +13.2% weight** (just a heavier mix). `rwVerdict()` labels each row accordingly — Rate up / Rate down / Heavier parcels / Lighter parcels / Flat — instead of showing a bare percentage. ₹/kg is returned too but is deliberately **not** the headline: courier pricing is slab-based (everything under 500 g pays the same), so ₹/kg swings wildly on light parcels.
      - ⚠️⚠️ **THE CURRENT WINDOW MUST NOT END TODAY — FREIGHT ARRIVES ~9 DAYS LATE.** RapidShyp bills after settlement, so `freight_total` backfills days later. Measured 2026-08-12: coverage was **0–7% for the previous six days**, 24% at day 6, 43% at day 7, 70% at day 8, ≥87% from day 9 back. Ending "current" at today compared **58 charged shipments against 922** — and worse, the handful that settle early are a **biased sample** (the quickest deliveries), so the AVERAGE was wrong too, not just the volume: the 30-day figure read **−2.5%** when the truth was **−6.65%**. The endpoint now scans per-day coverage, finds the newest day at ≥70% (`settledThrough`), and ends the current window **there**, with the previous window the same length immediately before. **Detected from the data, never hardcoded** — a magic "9" would rot silently when the courier's billing lag changes. The response carries a `settlement` block and the UI states the real window and why it stops short of today. Windows are now balanced 92–128% instead of 6%.
      - ⚠️ **RAPIDSHYP ONLY, BY DATA NOT BY CHOICE** — same trap as `shipment_value`. `freight_total` covers 95.1% of RapidShyp rows over 180 days and **0 of 1,796 DocPharma and 0 of 974 KwikShip**. Rows without a charge are **excluded, not counted as ₹0** — averaging in zeros would invent a rate cut every time KwikShip volume grew. The response returns the sources it actually saw and the UI states the scope rather than implying full coverage. ⚠️ **If KwikShip freight ever lands, this dashboard silently becomes multi-courier — check the `coverage.sources` note still reads correctly.**
    - 🎨 **RTO-BY-COURIER CHART HALVED IN HEIGHT (2026-08-08).** ~450px → ~240px for 11 couriers: 24px bars → 14px, and the "of 2351" total moved INLINE beside the rate instead of wrapping to a second line (that wrap was roughly half the row height). ⚠️ While in there: the chart was already relying on `text-[10px]`, `min-w-[56px]`, `px-1.5`, `mt-0.5`, `py-0.5` and `hover:bg-slate-50` — **none of which exist in the prebuilt tailwind.css** — so its "small" text was never small, the rate pill had no padding, and row hover never worked at all. Sizing is now inline styles and hover is a real `.dp-cr:hover` rule.
    - 🔎 **SIDEBAR SEARCH (2026-08-08).** `#nav-search` at the top of the sidebar filters the nav to dashboards whose name matches — **39 links across 8 collapsed groups** meant finding one required remembering which group it lived in. Matching groups are force-opened via a `.search-open` class; **Enter opens the first match** (highlighted) and **`/` focuses the box from anywhere** (suppressed while typing in any input/textarea/contenteditable, and on the login screen); **Esc or ×** clears and restores the accordion exactly as it was. ⚠️ **Permission-safe by construction:** `applyPermissions()` owns `style.display` on nav links, so the search hides with a **`.nav-filtered-out` CLASS** instead — the two never overwrite each other, and a link the user cannot access keeps its inline `display:none` no matter what is typed. Matching also tests `navPermVisible(a)` explicitly. Verified against the real markup: "deliv"→1, "custom"→2 (across two groups), "xyz"→0 (shows "No dashboard matches."), and **"user" returns 2 hits for an admin but 0 for a non-admin** whose Users / User Analytics links are permission-hidden.
    - 🏠 **HOME IS NOW THE LANDING PAGE (2026-08-08).** New `home` view + `nav-home` above the nav groups; `navigate(viewFromHash() || 'home')` so a bookmarked `#delivery-perf` still opens straight there. Shows the dashboards this user actually opened, most recent first — history is per-user `localStorage` (`ec_recent_views_v1:<email>`), recorded inside `navigate()` right after the permission gate, so no table and no endpoint. ⚠️ **The catalog is read from the SIDEBAR DOM, never a second hardcoded list**: `applyPermissions()` has already hidden what this user may not open, so Home can never surface a dashboard they lack, a revoked one disappears from Recent by itself, and a nav item added later appears with no extra work (verified: admin 39 dashboards, non-admin 33; a revoked `users` entry and a removed view both drop out). Home itself is EXEMPT from the permission gate — it only lists what you can already open, so gating it would bounce non-admins off their own landing page. **Design:** icon mosaic — one hue per business area (Operations indigo · Reconciliation cyan · Analytics violet · Marketing pink · Customer Support teal · Influencer orange · Inventory amber · Finance emerald), so a dashboard is recognised by mark + colour before the name is read; marks redrawn at `stroke-width:1.75` because the nav icons are drawn for 20px and look thin at 24–28px. Tints/shadows are computed in JS (`homeVars()`) rather than CSS `color-mix()` — the tint IS the tile background, and a colour function that failed to resolve would leave the mosaic flat white. Greeting uses the FIRST name only (all-caps entries title-cased; mixed case like McDonald left alone). Empty state points at the sidebar and the `/` search, since there is no dashboard list to fall back on.
    - ⚠️ **TWO LAYOUT MISTAKES ON HOME, both mine, both worth remembering.** (1) I pulled the content up 44px to overlap the brand band — the boundary then ran straight through the CARD ROW, leaving every tile half on dark and half on light. **Overlap only works when the crossing element is one clear object; a row of separate tiles is not.** The band now ends cleanly and content starts below it, like every other view. (2) The 'Last opened' badge was `position:absolute; top:12px; right:12px` inside a **centre-aligned** card — the icon centres itself vertically and rode up underneath it, and no amount of spacing makes a floating element collision-proof against centred content that resizes. The marker now lives IN the flow as part of the time line ('**Last opened** · 20 minutes ago', tinted with the card hue). No absolute positioning and no negative margins remain in the Home CSS.
    - **BROWSER TAB TITLE FOLLOWS THE OPEN DASHBOARD (2026-08-08).** `setTabTitle()` in `navigate()` → "Delivery Performance · Ecom Central"; with eight tabs of this app open every one used to read the same. The label comes from the SIDEBAR LINK — the same single source the nav search and Home read — so renaming a nav item retitles its tab with no second list. That shared source exposed a real bug in all three: `span.textContent` returned **"Voice Agent BETA"**, because that label carries a nested badge span. `navLabelOf()` now reads only the label span's DIRECT text nodes. All 41 views resolve a title. **The "Unified Dashboard" tagline was dropped** — the base title is just `Ecom Central`.
    - 🔕 **ALL AUTOMATIC MAIL TO RAPIDSHYP STOPPED (2026-08-08).** The first attempt excluded RapidShyp from the DAILY First-OFD cron only — the **weekly Silent-RTO (Mon 9:30) and fortnightly Late-Deliveries (1st/16th) kept mailing them**, because each cron carries its own recipient logic and Silent-RTO calls `recipientsFor('rapidshyp')` directly without going through `sendReportPerPlatform`. **Now enforced in the SENDERS, not the crons:** `AUTO_MAIL_BLOCKED = new Set(['rapidshyp'])` + `autoBlocked(platform, auto)`, checked in `sendReportPerPlatform` AND in `sendSilentRtoReport`; the three crons pass `auto: true`. A per-cron opt-out is something you must remember three times over and again for every future report — blocking at the send path makes a blocked courier silent by default. **Manual dashboard sends are unaffected** (that is the point: no unattended mail). To resume: remove the entry from that one set. Verified: all three crons report "RapidShyp: automatic mail disabled", manual send still resolves its 4 recipients.
    - ⚠️ **…AND THE FOURTH CRON WAS STILL MAILING THEM DAILY (fixed 2026-08-11).** The blocklist above was written around the three courier crons I knew of — **`Intransit-Late (35 9 * * *)`**, the daily in-transit-overdue report, was never wired to it, so RapidShyp kept receiving it every morning. **The design was the bug, not the omission:** crons had to opt IN with `auto: true`, and an opt-in scheme fails silently the moment someone adds a sender without knowing the rule. **Now inverted — automatic is the DEFAULT.** `autoBlocked(platform, manual)` blocks unless the caller explicitly passes `manual: true`, which only the four dashboard `/send` routes do; the crons pass nothing. A NEW cron is therefore silent to a blocked courier without anyone remembering to wire it up. **Full audit of all 26 crons:** exactly four can mail a courier (Silent-RTO, Late-Del, First-OFD, Intransit-Late); `EscMail` only POLLS replies over IMAP, `influencer_crm` sends only from a user-triggered route to influencers, and the WH/DP/Hold reports go to **Teams**, not email. **Verified with `dryRun` (nothing sent): 4/4 crons reach RapidShyp = NO; all four dashboard buttons still resolve their recipients.**
    - ⚠️ **`dryRun` on the report senders — added after a real mis-send.** Verifying the change above by calling `sendSilentRtoReport()` directly **actually delivered an 18-shipment report to RapidShyp**: there was no way to exercise the send path without sending. Both senders now take `dryRun` (resolve recipients + build the mail, send nothing). **Never test a sender without it.**
    - 🚨🚨 **176 ORDERS SHOWED "DELIVERED" WHILE STILL OUT FOR DELIVERY — deployed-webhook regex drift (fixed 2026-08-08, edge fn v8).** Reported on **TE25-38414** (AWB 36426411864424): Delivery Performance read **"Delivered · after NDR"** while RapidShyp reported OUT_FOR_DELIVERY with 3 NDRs and no delivery scan. **Cause:** `supabase/functions/rapidshyp-webhook/index.ts` — the live function RapidShyp calls on every scan — still carried the OLD loose test `/deliver/.test(status)`, which **matches OUT_FOR_DELIVERY** (and UNDELIVERED, and "not delivered"). The Node parser had been fixed to `delivered` months earlier; **the edge function was never updated**, despite its own header promising it "PORTS the Node parseRapidshypJourney() logic exactly". Same failure mode as the Kwikship webhook overwriting the NDR backfill: **a fix applied in Node but not in the deployed edge function.** **Why it never self-healed — the real damage:** the webhook also set `is_final=true`, and the 6-hourly gap-fill **SKIPS final shipments by design** (a real delivery never changes), so the row was locked out of every future correction. **Signature = `outcome='delivered' AND delivered_at IS NULL`** — impossible from correct parsing, since a genuine delivery always carries a DEL scan. **Scale:** 264 frozen rows; re-checked ALL of them against RapidShyp live (0 unreachable) → **176 were NOT delivered (120 in transit, 54 NDR pending, 2 RTO)**; the other 88 had genuinely delivered since. August was **153 of 355 "delivered" rows fake (43%)**; July 111 of 3,151; June and earlier **clean** — 171 rows were being rewritten per day, so it was actively worsening. RTO was unaffected (0 rows). **THREE LAYERS OF FIX, in this order (order matters — repairing first would have been undone by the webhook within minutes, exactly as the Kwikship backfill was):** (1) **edge fn v8 deployed** with `delivered` + the not-delivered/undelivered exclusions (verified 7/7 on real statuses, where the old test failed 4) and `reached_delivery` widened to `/out[s_]for[s_]delivery/` (RapidShyp sends the UNDERSCORE form, which the old `/out for delivery/` never matched); (2) **evidence-required finality in BOTH parsers** — `is_final` for a delivery now requires a **DEL code or a real delivered scan**, so a text-only "delivered" still reports as delivered but stays re-checkable; **this is the structural guard: a wrong outcome self-corrects on the next sync, a wrong is_final never does**; (3) **`auditJourneyIntegrity()`** wired into the 6-hourly Journey cron — 3 counting queries for states that cannot exist (delivered-without-timestamp, frozen-delivered, rto-without-timestamp), logging a warning naming the repair. The gap-fill skips final rows by design, so nothing was watching the one place the bug lived. **Repair:** `reprocessBadDelivered()` (ignores `is_final` — the flag being corrected) — **175 rows corrected, audit now 0/0/0**. `reprocessDocpharma()` was the pre-existing twin for the identical DocPharma bug; RapidShyp never got one, which is why these sat unrepaired. **Kwikship is structurally immune** — both its parsers use `codeOut === 'delivered' || !!deliveredAt` with **no text matching at all**. DocPharma still text-matches (`delivered`) but currently has 0 bad rows, its own repair function, and is now covered by the audit.
    - ⚠⚠ **KWIKSHIP WAS NEVER IN THE BUCKET ENGINE — every Kwikship order sat in `order_to_dispatch` for life (fixed 2026-08-08).** The `order_buckets` VIEW — which drives the Call Queue **Undelivered** tab, the Delivered/RTO/age buckets, `dispatch_at`, and the repeat/hold rules — is built ONLY from **`order_tracking` + `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom` + `orders.tracking_status`**. RapidShyp and DocPharma both write `order_tracking`; **Kwikship wrote to none of them**, only to `shipment_journey_ecom`. With no tracking row the view finds no `dispatch_at` and falls to the `order_to_dispatch` branch — permanently, whatever the parcel is doing. Measured: **528 of 532 Kwikship orders stuck in `order_to_dispatch`** — 323 in transit, 140 delivered, 39 RTO, and **26 sitting on a live NDR that never appeared on the Undelivered list** (reported on TE25-40300 / AWB 47607613040881, "Consignee Unavailable" since 07 Aug). `order_tracking` held **0** kwikship rows. **Fix — no view change:** `mirrorKwikshipToOrderTracking()` in `kwikship_sync.js` writes the same `order_tracking` row the other two platforms write (called from `updateKwikshipJourney()`, so webhook + 2 AM cron both feed it), plus `backfillKwikshipOrderTracking({dry})` for the existing rows (**pure DB, zero API calls**). Two traps handled: (1) `order_tracking.order_id` is **UNIQUE**, so the mirror refuses to clobber another platform's row — it writes only when there is no row, or the existing row is already ours (same source or same AWB); at rollout that guard mattered for exactly 1 of 539 orders. (2) `status_updated_at` is set to the real **dispatch** moment (`dispatched_at`), NOT the latest scan — the view derives dispatch from `MIN(status_updated_at)` over non-pre-pickup rows and with one row per order that IS this value, so using the newest scan would drag `dispatch_at` forward on every update. Kwikship codes pass through to `tracking_status` unchanged (the view normalises `_`/`-`/spaces, so `undelivered`→undelivered, `delivered`→delivered, `rto_in_transit`→rto all classify correctly) EXCEPT `new` → **`manifested`**: `new` means "manifest uploaded", not dispatched, and it is absent from the view's pre-pickup exclusion list, so left raw it would fake a dispatch. ⚠️ **The status text comes from the journey OUTCOME, not the raw code** — the view matches on text (`rto` via the regex `(^| )rto( |$)`, `undelivered` via an exact list) and several Kwikship codes carry neither word while being that outcome: `reached_at_seller_city` IS an RTO leg, and an NDR shipment often still reads `out_for_delivery`. Trusting the raw code put 3 RTOs and 1 NDR into an age bucket (caught on the first 116 backfilled rows); `ksTrackingStatus()` now maps delivered/rto/ndr_pending/lost from the outcome the parser already resolved from the whole timeline, and only falls back to the code for the non-terminal in_transit case. **Backfill result: 539 journeys → 537 written + 2 re-run (idempotent upsert), and 538/538 now bucket correctly — 26/26 NDR → undelivered (the reported TE25-40300 included), 145/145 delivered, 40/40 RTO, 327/327 in-transit spread across the age buckets.** Side benefit: `order_buckets.partner` now reads **`kwikship`** for these orders instead of the courier service name (`delhiverydirectsurface500g`), which is what the courier-platform tag falls back to when a journey row is missing.
    - ⚠️ **`last_scan_at` — THE ONLY TRUTHFUL "LAST SCAN" (added 2026-08-11).** The Call Queue derived it from `order_tracking`, which the nightly syncs rewrite — so the column reported **when the sync last ran**, not when the courier last moved. TE25-40300's real last scan was 07 Aug 18:23 yet the tab read "10h ago"; **152 of 166 rows** were showing a sync timestamp. Milestones cannot substitute: `out_for_delivery_at` is the FIRST time a state was reached, so a reattempted parcel under-reports (TE25-41004: first OFD 10 Aug, latest 11 Aug 09:17). New column on `shipment_journey_ecom`, set by ALL FOUR parsers (RapidShyp + Kwikship, Node **and** both edge functions) to the newest timestamp in the actual scan log; written conditionally so a partial webhook cannot blank it. **kwikship-webhook v8 deployed**; backfilled from the public v2 endpoint → **543/547** non-final Kwikship rows carry a real scan (4 have no upstream timeline).
    - ⚠️ **…and the overlay must be AUTHORITATIVE, not "whichever is newer" (fixed same day).** My first version took the MAX of the journey scan and the tracking estimate. That is precisely backwards for a STALLED parcel: TE25-40300's tracking row had been rewritten by a sync 10 hours ago while its real last scan was four days old, so max chose the sync and reported activity that never happened. **A shipment sitting still must LOOK like it is sitting still — that is the whole signal.** `last_scan_at` now wins outright whenever present. The fallback was likewise reverted from "newest of the three" to a TRUST order (`status_updated_at` → `last_tracked_at` → `updated_at`): the last two are OUR write times, so ranking by recency systematically prefers the least truthful value.
    - **DOCPHARMA ORDERS OFTEN HAVE NO JOURNEY ROW**, so the terminal-drop above could not see them — TE25-37876 read "partially-delivered" on the panel while the partner portal had it delivered on 27 Jul. `terminalByAwb()` now also consults `docpharma_orders.order_status` (DocPharma's own portal-synced status, authoritative for their shipments). 6 of 168 undelivered rows had no journey row and **every one was already delivered** — pure wasted calls on delivered parcels.
    - 🚨🚨 **240 KWIKSHIP SHIPMENTS FROZEN AT THE WRONG STATUS — the bucket-engine fix was never ported to the webhook (fixed 2026-08-12).** Reported on **TE25-40173** (AWB 47607613036994): delivered 10 Aug 2:53 pm, still reading **"undelivered"** on 12 Aug in the Undelivered panel, while the Live Tracking modal showed the full delivered scan log. **Both were reading correctly — from different tables.** `shipment_journey_ecom` (what the modal reads) had `outcome=delivered, is_final=true`; `order_tracking` (what `order_buckets`, and therefore the Undelivered panel, Call Queue, buckets, delivery performance and RTO% all read) still said `undelivered`, last written 09 Aug. **Cause:** the 2026-08-08 fix above added `mirrorKwikshipToOrderTracking()` to **`kwikship_sync.js` only** — `supabase/functions/kwikship-webhook/index.ts` had **zero references to `order_tracking`**, so every real-time event updated the journey and nothing else. **Why it never self-healed, and this is the part that made it permanent:** the webhook ALSO sets `is_final`, and `syncKwikship()` **skips finals by design** — so the moment the webhook recorded a delivery the nightly cron stopped fetching that AWB forever, and `order_tracking` froze at whatever the last cron run happened to see. **The faster the webhook fired, the more certain the freeze** — which is why 144 of the stuck rows read `in_transit`: delivered so quickly the cron never even saw an out-for-delivery. **Scale: 240 of 426 final Kwikship shipments diverged — 183 delivered and 57 RTO misreported** (144 delivered→`in_transit`, 31 rto→`in_transit`, 22 rto→`undelivered`, 13 delivered→`out_for_delivery`, 11 delivered→`undelivered`, 9 delivered→`reached_at_destination_center`, 5 delivered→`pickup_failed`, 3 rto→`reached_at_destination_center`, 2 →`out for pickup`). Delivery-performance and RTO% were being computed off a table missing 183 deliveries; the most recent bad write was the same day, so it was live, not backlog. **Fix, three layers:** (1) **`mirrorToOrderTracking()` ported into kwikship-webhook, deployed v9** (health-checked; `verify_jwt` stays false) — it logs a warning when a mirror fails rather than failing silently; (2) 🛡️ **`reconcileKwikshipTracking()` — the structural guard.** `syncKwikship()` no longer merely skips finals, it **reconciles** them: for every final AWB it compares `order_tracking.tracking_status` against `ksTrackingStatus(outcome, status_code)` and rewrites only the rows that disagree. **Pure DB work on rows we already store — ZERO courier API calls** — so it is cheap enough to run on every sync, and it heals divergence from ANY cause (edge-function drift, a failed write, a race), not just this one. *"Skip" must mean "make no API call", never "stop looking"* — the same lesson as the RapidShyp `is_final` freeze, and the reason that one needed `auditJourneyIntegrity()`; (3) **repair run: 240 rows reconciled + `backfillKwikshipOrderTracking()` (970 written) — divergence now 0 across all 426 final shipments**, TE25-40173 verified reading `delivered` with the true delivered timestamp. ⚠️ **This is the THIRD edge-function-drift incident** (Kwikship NDR reasons v7, RapidShyp `/deliver/` v8, this one v9). The pattern is always the same: a fix lands in Node, the deployed function keeps the old behaviour, and `is_final` locks the damage in. **Whenever a fix touches a parser or a write path, check the edge function in the same session — and prefer a reconcile that cannot drift over a port that can.**
  - The AWB EasyEcom stores (the courier AWB, e.g. Delhivery) resolves directly on Kwikship's `GET /shipments/:awb` (verified: GoKwik AWBs 200, RapidShyp AWBs 404 — so the RapidShyp/DocPharma cron never writes a conflicting row). If an order is re-allocated (e.g. Kwikship→cancelled→RapidShyp), `courier_aggregator_name` updates and it moves to the correct sync automatically.
- **Server-enforced permissions:** UI hiding is cosmetic; every view-specific API group is gated by middleware in `server.js`.
- **Fail-safe reporting:** report dedup lists abort the run when unreadable (never re-spam), and Slack posting is code-disabled (Teams-only) unless explicitly re-enabled.

## 4. Repository Structure

```
ecom-central-Staging/
├── server.js                  # Express app: mounts, auth/RBAC gates, all cron schedules
├── config.js                  # Central config — every env var is read here
├── package.json
├── tailwind.config.js         # Tailwind content scan (templates + static JS)
├── tw-input.css               # Tailwind input file (@tailwind base/components/utilities)
├── cron_job.js                # Standalone: adset-performance PDF report emailer
├── data_fetcher.js            # Standalone: bulk data sync utility (npm run sync)
├── tests/selftest.js          # npm run selftest — regression net for SILENT failures (see §14)
├── cod_confirmation.js        # Standalone: COD confirmation processing
├── docpharma_import.js        # Standalone: DocPharma order import
├── docpharma_timeline_backfill.js
├── debug_easyecom.js          # Dev helper
├── Caddyfile.example          # Reverse-proxy (HTTPS) reference config
├── supabase/                  # SQL migrations + edge functions
│   ├── *.sql                  # table definitions (serviceability, dp_rejected_handled, …)
│   └── functions/             # rapidshyp-webhook, kwikship-webhook, msg91-cod-webhook (Deno edge functions)
└── app/
    ├── auth.js                # JWT issue/verify, scrypt hashing, requireAdmin/requirePermission
    ├── supabase.js            # Supabase service-role client singleton
    ├── templates/index.html   # The single-page app shell (all views)
    ├── static/
    │   ├── app.js             # Entire frontend logic (~6.5k lines)
    │   ├── tailwind.css       # COMPILED Tailwind output (rebuild after UI changes!)
    │   └── vendor/chart.min.js
    └── api/                   # One module per domain
        ├── orders.js              # Shopify orders + enrichment
        ├── delivery_journey.js    # Journey parser/writer + freight sync + backfills (CLI)
        ├── kwikship_sync.js       # Kwikship (GoKwik) tracking → journeys (source=kwikship); nightly 2 AM cron (CLI)
        ├── delivery_reports.js    # Delivery-perf API + Silent-RTO/Late/Overdue + marks + critical email
        ├── warehouse_slack_report.js  # Warehouse/DocPharma-rejected/On-Hold reports (Teams)
        ├── teams.js               # Outbound Teams Adaptive Cards (Power Automate webhooks)
        ├── teams_listener.js      # Inbound Teams keyword triggers (MS Graph delegated)
        ├── amazon_auto_review.js  # Auto review-request flow (sends unattended; result card to Teams)
        ├── amazon_review.js / amazon.js / amazon_reports.js / amazon_fba.js / amazon_inbound.js
        ├── easyecom.js            # EasyEcom OMS integration
        ├── fulfillment_ops.js     # Fulfillment operations + status sync
        ├── ops_control.js         # Ops Control dashboard
        ├── serviceability.js      # Pincode serviceability / EDD estimates
        ├── docpharma_*.js         # DocPharma recon, invoices, ledger, overview, inventory, portal
        ├── ad_performance.js / adset_performance.js  # Meta ads analytics
        ├── auth_routes.js         # /login /signup /me + 2FA OTP (verify/resend) (+ rate limiting)
        ├── users.js               # Admin user management (/api/admin/users)
        ├── email_settings.js      # Portal SMTP settings + shared sendMail()
        ├── email_replies.js       # Escalation reply tracking: IMAP inbox poll, thread matching, AI scoring
        ├── cod_risk.js            # Shared pre-dispatch COD risk scorer (customer history + pincode RTO)
        ├── support_console.js     # Customer Support console API (queue/orders/calls/notes/contacts/team/blacklist)
        ├── influencer_crm.js      # Influencer Marketing CRM API (/inf/* — discover/influencers/videos/lists/calendar/mentions/send-product)
        ├── tally.js               # Finance → Tally connector (/tally/* — masters mirror, voucher queue, bridge endpoints)
        ├── tally_xml.js           # Pure Tally XML builders/parsers (voucher Import Data, Collection exports) — no I/O
        ├── tally_batch.js         # Nightly 23:50 batch push + Teams approval + result watcher
        ├── tally_bank.js          # Bank statement import: parse xlsx/csv, dedup, create draft vouchers
        ├── tally_bank_match.js    # Narration -> ledger matcher learned from the company's own history
        ├── crypto_util.js         # AES-256-GCM encrypt/decrypt for stored secrets
        ├── ai.js                  # AI client (Gemini native / OpenAI-compatible)
        ├── excel_report.js / pdf_generator.js
        ├── webhook_handler.js     # Inbound webhooks (RapidShyp / EasyEcom / DocPharma / DocPharma-NDR)
        ├── shipping.js / helpers.js / middleware.js
        └── docpharma_import_lib.js
```

## 5. Dashboards & Features

Navigation is permission-gated per user (see §6). Permission keys in parentheses.

### Operations
| Dashboard | Key | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Orders Dashboard (KPI note) | `orders-dashboard` | **KPI cards** (2026-07-24): (1) **count the EXACT selected date range** — client sends `from`/`to` instants, `/get-orders` counts `created_at` within `[from,to]` (was a rolling `now-days` window that made "Yesterday" show ~2 days incl. today); table still capped at 500 (`TABLE_LIMIT`) so KPI = genuine total + "most recent 500 shown" note when truncated. (2) **Cancelled and RTO are SEPARATE cards** (6 total) — server returns disjoint `cancelled` (cancelled/lost, non-RTO) + `rto` (`tracking_status ilike '%rto%'`); in-transit = total − delivered − cancelled − rto − new. (3) **Every card is CLICKABLE** → filters the table to that bucket. Filtering is **server-side** (`bucket` param on `/get-orders`, same tracking_status conditions as the counts) so clicking Delivered/RTO fetches those (older) orders instead of filtering the recent-500 sample; `KPI_CARD_FILTER`/`STATUS_BUCKET` in app.js; re-click clears; active card ring-highlighted; the status filter is excluded from the KPI-recompute so cards stay a stable breakdown. |
| Order Insights | `order-insights` | **Accurate over the FULL range** (2026-07-24) — was computed from the capped 500 client rows (showed Delivered/RTO≈0, undercounted revenue). New endpoint `GET /orders-insights?from&to` calls SQL RPCs `orders_insights_summary` / `_trend` / `orders_top_products` / `orders_top_cities`. KPIs (revenue/AOV/status split), daily **revenue trend**, **Order Status** donut, **Payment Method** donut, plus detail cards: **COD vs Prepaid** (orders/revenue/delivered%/RTO%), **Top Products**, **Top Cities**.<br><br>⚠️ **Status comes from the COURIER, not `orders.tracking_status` (fixed 2026-08-17).** Both RPCs used that column alone, and it is never written for a large share of orders (Kwikship especially) — so `pending` was defined as `tracking_status is null` and anything missing it counted as **Pending Processing forever**. Month to date it read **1,798 pending**, of which **1,707 already had a courier journey: 644 DELIVERED (₹5,22,343 of delivered revenue uncounted), 159 RTO, 817 genuinely moving** — the true pending figure was **91**. `orders_insights_summary` / `_trend` now resolve status from `shipment_journey_ecom` (one row per order via `distinct on`, ties settled delivered → rto → newest) and fall back to `tracking_status`; precedence is **RTO → delivered → cancelled → in transit → pending**, courier facts ahead of Shopify flags. Corrected MTD: pending 1,799 → **91**, delivered 940 → **1,587**, in-transit 603 → **1,494**, RTO 243 → **447**, delivered revenue ₹7.37L → **₹12.61L**. Added `idx_shipment_journey_order_name` for the join (the warehouse report queries by that column too).

**Revenue waterfall (2026-08-17).** Row 1 is Total Revenue → After Cancellation → After RTO → Collected So Far, each card the previous one minus a named leak, so the drop between them *is* the loss (MTD: ₹31.53L → ₹29.69L → ₹25.77L, **81.7% of booked survives**, ₹12.67L collected so far). Needs `cancelled_value` / `in_transit_value` / `pending_value` from the summary RPC. A separate **In Transit Value** card projects what may come back at the current RTO rate (₹12.21L riding, ≈₹2.68L at 21.9%).

**Period comparison + depth (2026-08-17).** `/orders-insights` also returns `prev`, `prevTrend`, `prevProducts`, `prevCities` and a `range` block for the **same number of days immediately before** the selection — the same four RPCs called twice, no new SQL. Every KPI carries a live Δ, and a banner names both windows so "vs prev" is never ambiguous. ⚠️ **Direction is per metric** (`goodWhen`): rising revenue is green, rising RTO is red — a single sign-based rule would paint a worsening RTO as an improvement. **Some metrics have no good direction**: In Transit and Pending are pipeline state, not performance, and colouring them made the page read as self-contradictory — two ▲ cards side by side, one green one red, both only saying "more orders are in flight". They pass `goodWhen:'flat'` and render neutral grey. The rule: **an arrow always means the direction the number moved, colour only ever means good or bad, grey means the question doesn't apply.** Each delta also names its baseline, so a large percentage can be judged rather than just felt. ⚠️ **Rates are computed over SETTLED orders (delivered + RTO), never over every order in the range.** A young range is mostly still in transit, so dividing by all orders shows a collapsing delivery rate that is only parcels not having arrived: on 2026-08-17 the raw delivered count read **−29.3%** while the settled delivery rate was **74.4% vs 75.8%** — essentially flat. When ≥25% of the range is still open the banner says so outright. **The RTO card breaks the loss down (2026-08-18):** headline RTO% plus Cancelled% and a Combined loss figure, with a two-colour bar over closed orders. ⚠️ **The two rates have DIFFERENT denominators and must never be added.** RTO is measured over orders that shipped and settled (`delivered + RTO`); a cancellation almost always happens *before* dispatch, so it is measured over every closed order (`delivered + RTO + cancelled`). The combined figure is recomputed from the counts — on 2026-08-18, RTO 23.8% + cancelled 8.2% gives 32.0% by addition but the true combined loss is **30.0%**. That combined number is exactly what the **Ad Set Breakdown calls "RTO%"**, which is why that page reads higher than Insights; the card now shows both so the two pages reconcile. New second KPI row: delivered (realised) revenue · delivery rate · RTO rate · RTO loss ₹ · COD share. New visuals: the trend takes a **metric picker** (revenue/orders/delivered/RTO) and a **dashed previous-period overlay** aligned *by position, not by date* (day 1 against day 1, or the two lines sit in different places on the axis); a **fulfilment funnel**; **COD vs Prepaid outcome** on settled orders — which is the chart that earns the page, showing **COD RTO 47.4% against prepaid 1.0%** on near-equal volume; and a **weekday** average-orders bar, Monday-first. Product/city lists show movement vs the previous period matched **by name, not by rank** (a rank change says nothing about whether the item sold more), which is why the previous window is fetched 50 deep. |
| Orders Dashboard | `orders-dashboard` | **Shopify orders only** (Amazon excluded 2026-07; EasyEcom-only orders — Flipkart `OD…` etc. — excluded 2026-07-17; EasyEcom data still mapped for hold/unhold/warehouse) enriched with tracking, COD-confirmation & repeat flags. Each order row shows **AWB + courier name** (`orders.courier_name`, fallback `awb_cache_ecom.courier`). Filters (**compact toolbar**): status, date, **payment method (COD / Prepaid)**, **customer type (New / Repeat)**, COD-status, **Hold-status (On Hold / Not Held)**, platform (Shopify), **source RapidShyp/DocPharma**. New/Repeat is computed client-side from the Shopify `repeat` tag OR the same phone/email on >1 order (`computeRepeatFlags` → `_isRepeat`, shared by the row badge AND the Type filter so they always agree). The ⏸ **Hold button hides once the shipment is MANIFESTED-or-later** — EasyEcom rejects a hold after the manifest is generated. Manifested = pickup scheduled (`awb_cache_ecom.pickup_scheduled`), our status is Shipped/In-Transit/OFD/Delivered/RTO, or EasyEcom `order_status` literally says manifested/shipped; pre-manifest (New / Processing / **Ready-To-Ship** — AWB may be printed but pickup not yet scheduled) stays holdable. **Separately**, the local `ee_hold` mark is auto-cleared only once **actually picked up** (real tracking signal: `orders.tracking_status` / RapidShyp cache raw_status: IN TRANSIT / OUT FOR DELIVERY / DELIVERED / RTO / RETURN / REACHED / UNDELIVERED / PICKUP COMPLETED / LOST), NOT the AWB or EasyEcom `order_status` (neither reflects hold — the old parse wrongly wiped active holds each sync) — so the manifest gate controls only the button's visibility, not stale-mark clearing. Server sets `order.holdable`; the ⏸ Hold button shows only when `holdable`. **Hold is detected from a local `ee_hold` mark OR EasyEcom's hold state** (orders held directly in the panel). ⚠️ EasyEcom's text `order_status` is UNRELIABLE for holds — a held order often keeps `order_status='Open'`/`'Shipped'` while only the item is flagged, so an `ilike '%hold%'` catches ~4 of ~65 real holds. The authoritative signal is **`raw_data.order_status_id = 44`** (On Hold) — `/api/ee-hold-marks` filters on `raw_data->>order_status_id=44` (plus the text ilike, minus cancelled) so panel holds actually surface (14→50 detected). **Held orders are ALWAYS shown even if outside the 500-row table cap** (fetched by name and appended — held orders need action). **Held orders surface on every dashboard**: Orders `eeHold` + the ⏸ HOLD chip on Ops Control / Delivery Performance / Claims-SLA / Customer Support, via `/api/ee-hold-marks` (which merges local marks + EasyEcom "On Hold"). (via EasyEcom `location` = `shipPlatform`, tag fallback). **DocPharma-rejected orders** (from `dp_rejected_handled_ecom`) show a red **DP Rejected** tag + rose row tint when un-actioned, flipping to green **DP Rejected → MWH** once auto-routed/handled (`routed_at`) or logged-moved. Date presets include **Last 30 Days**. `/get-orders?days=N` is **date-aware** (default 30, clamp 1–90): the table returns the most-recent ≤500 rows in the window (kept small for a snappy render; date change shows an instant branded loader + guards overlapping fetches), but the **KPI cards use accurate server-side count queries over the full window** (`{ orders, kpis, total, shown, truncated }`) — so 7-day vs 30-day show genuinely different numbers (e.g. 1,125 vs 5,159 total) even though the table is capped (shows a "most recent N shown" hint). Changing the date preset RE-FETCHES (server window); other filters (status/COD/hold/source/search) filter the loaded set client-side. **KPI cards are filter-aware**: with no filter they show the accurate server full-window counts; once any filter is active they recompute from the filtered set (subtext "matching current filters") so the numbers track what's shown. Progressive-render + **lazy expanded details** show item **SKUs**, EasyEcom workflow (Approve, ⏸ Hold/▶ Unhold), and **🏬 Change warehouse** — shown ONLY for DocPharma orders (never Shifupro/RapidShyp); disabled "✓ Moved · from → to" once routed. **Cancelled orders never show a hold chip or hold/unhold button** (the hold is moot once cancelled — a cancelled order can't ship): enforced in `/api/ee-hold-marks` (drops any held order whose `orders.cancelled_at` is set — same source as the dashboard's Cancelled badge; `enriched_orders_ecom.cancelled_at` lags → chip disappears on EVERY dashboard) AND in the Orders dashboard chip + order-detail (`ordHoldChipHtml` / EE- & Shopify-hold blocks skip when `status==='Cancelled'` or `cancelled_at`) |
| Fulfillment Ops | `fulfillment-ops` | Fulfillment pipeline operations, AWB fetch, label download |
| Delivery Performance | `delivery-perf` | **Order basket (2026-08-05):** an expanded row on an **NDR-pending or in-transit** shipment (only those two states) offers **🧺 Add to basket**; a floating bar then shows the count with a single **✉️ Send one email for all**, which feeds the existing compose → AI-polish → review → send flow (that endpoint already writes ONE email for an array of AWBs — it is what "Escalate all N marked" uses). Basket persists in `localStorage` so orders can be gathered across filters and reloads, capped at 60 to match the compose endpoint. 📋 **PUSH TO ESCALATION SHEET (2026-08-19) — the basket's PRIMARY exit; email is now the secondary button.** The team escalates to RapidShyp through a Google Sheet shared with them (“TE <> Rapidshyp Escalations”, `ESCALATION_SHEET_ID` in .env, tab `ESCALATION_SHEET_TAB` default Sheet1), so the basket now appends one row per AWB via **`POST /api/escalation-sheet/push`** (same `send-escalation-emails` gate) instead of mailing. Columns A–P match the sheet's header exactly: Date (IST `d/M`, their format) · Agent (⚠️ the column is a DROPDOWN — `sheetAgentName()` reads its validation list off the sheet (ONE_OF_LIST or ONE_OF_RANGE, 10-min cache) and maps the portal user to the sheet's own option, `sugandhm881` → “Sugandh”; raw username was red-flagged “not on the list” on every row; no match → raw local part, visibly) · AWB · Duplicate · MOP (COD/PPD) · Courier · Escalation type · stage · Reason · EDD (dd/mm/yyyy) · 2×Remarks · “Under Follow up” · comment · 2×video. **Type/reason are derived per shipment from the journey** (NDR>0 → `Reattempt/Fake NDR` with the latest NDR reason; past `first_edd` → `EDD Breached_no attempt`; else `Escalation` / “status is not update” — their phrasing), overridable per batch in a small modal. The COURIER cell is the normalised last-mile name (`DelhiveryDirectSurface500G` → “Delhivery”) because that is what the sheet's filters use. **Repeats are appended and marked “Duplicate”, never silently skipped** — the sheet's own column is the team's dedup. ⚠️ **RAPIDSHYP SHIPMENTS ONLY (user, 2026-08-19):** the sheet is shared WITH RapidShyp, so a KwikShip/DocPharma AWB on it escalates to a partner who never carried the parcel — the same wrong-courier class the email routing was fixed for. The server filters on `shipment_journey_ecom.source === 'rapidshyp'`; non-RS shipments are pushed nowhere, reported back (`skipped`/`skippedAwbs`), and **stay in the basket** for the ✉️ email flow (which routes by platform) — only pushed AWBs leave it. An all-non-RS selection is a 400 naming the split. Verified live: mixed RS+KwikShip batch → 1 pushed + 1 kept; all-KwikShip → refused. ⚠️ **The basket bar's colours live in `smooth.css` as REAL CSS** (`#dp-basket-bar`, `.dpb-ghost`, `.dp-sheet-overlay`) — plain `bg-slate-900`, `bg-white/10`, `z-[60]`/`z-[80]` are NOT in the prebuilt tailwind.css (only `bg-slate-900/40|50` exist), so the bar had shipped as a white pill with white text — invisible except the emerald button (prebuilt-Tailwind trap, again). **THREE REFINEMENTS (user, 2026-08-19 evening):** (1) **a sheet push is NOT an email** — it wore the ✉️ badge (“Critical mail sent”) and the email-thread footer; rows now carry `sheet_pushed` SEPARATELY from `mail_sent`, with their own 📋 badge (“Pushed to escalation sheet”) and the likely-fake insight reverted to email-only marks. (2) **the sheet IS the reply channel** — `GET /api/escalation-sheet/thread/:awb` (whole-sheet read, 60s cache) returns this AWB's sheet rows, and the expanded shipment row renders them like the email thread: our push (emerald, right) with type/reason/status chip, RapidShyp's Remarks/comment cells (white, left) as their replies — “No response on the sheet yet” when the cells are empty. (3) **the escalation REASON is asked at “Add to basket”** — a small popup captures the agent's own wording per AWB (stored in localStorage beside the basket, cleared on remove/push), and on the sheet that beats the batch override, which beats the auto-derived NDR text; blank = auto, shown in the placeholder. **Agent dropdown mapping** (`sheetAgentName`) covered above. **2026-08-19 late refinements (user):** the add-to-basket popup asks for the **Escalation type** (dropdown, default = auto from the journey) AND the reason — per-AWB, both stored beside the basket, both winning over the batch/auto values on the sheet; the popup has **ONE Add button** (empty reason → the sheet cell stays BLANK, typed → verbatim — no Skip button, no auto-fill); the **EDD column is never filled from our side** (the team maintains it by hand); the push modal is now a plain confirm (everything is captured at add time); and the **basket also lives on Call Queue · Undelivered** — a 🧺 button per row (`supBasketBtn`, und tab only, needs an AWB), same localStorage basket and floating bar as Delivery Performance, one `dpBasketRepaintAll()` keeping whichever view is on screen in step. **DP's add-to-basket was REMOVED the same day** (user: “these escalation thing should be removed from delivery performance”) — the Call Queue · Undelivered 🧺 button is the ONLY add door, the floating bar renders on the support-queue view only, `dpBasketToggle` is removal-only and the basket cap moved to the CQ add path. DP keeps the RESPONSE side — ✉️/📋 badges, thread viewers, Send-critical-email — those are about seeing and mailing, not basketing. **Final 2026-08-19 shape:** the popup is roomier (max-w-xl) and its type dropdown has **NO Auto option** — the agent picks a concrete type (Reattempt/Fake NDR · EDD Breached_no attempt · Fake Attempted Urgent Delivery · Escalation); **the popup appears for RapidShyp shipments ONLY** — a KwikShip/DocPharma add goes STRAIGHT into the basket (its exit is the ✉️ email, which asks nothing per order; toast says so). **New “Escalated” column on Call Queue · Undelivered** (sortable): when a sheet push or critical email actually leaves, the mark timestamps feed 📋/✉️ icons + **date AND time** (`supDT`, IST) with a **view** link → `supEscThreadModal(awb, kinds)` — a modal showing the SAME response threads DP shows inline (email replies via `/api/escalation-emails`, sheet Remarks/comment via `/api/escalation-sheet/thread/:awb`), rendered by the same `dpThreadHtml`/`dpSheetThreadHtml` through their caches, because Call Queue rows open the order popup on click and have no inline expansion. Server: `/support/queue` (und/changed panels) attaches `escalated_kind` ('mail'|'sheet'|'mail+sheet') + `escalated_at` (latest mark) from `order_marks_ecom`. **Type dropdown = THE SHEET'S OWN LIST (2026-08-19):** `GET /api/escalation-sheet/options` reads the Escalation-type column's data validation (19 values today) via the shared `readValidationList(sheets, a1)` (also now powering `sheetAgentName`; per-cell 10-min cache); the popup fetches once per session and renders it verbatim — the team editing the sheet's dropdown changes ours with no deploy. Fetch failure falls back to the core four. **Bar exits by platform mix:** every add records the AWB's platform (`_dpBasketPlats`, localStorage); the 📋 sheet button renders only when the basket holds ≥1 RapidShyp order (count line says the split, e.g. “3 orders (2 RapidShyp · 1 email-only)”); ✉️ Email serves every platform. Unknown platform counts as RapidShyp — the server's source filter is the real gate, the bar only avoids offering a dead button. Pushed orders get a `sheet_escalated` mark and the explorer's “escalated” badge now reads `critical_mail_sent` OR `sheet_escalated`. Auth: the `GOOGLE_CREDENTIALS` service account (sheet shared with it as Editor). ⚠️ **googleapis' JWT must be built with the OPTIONS OBJECT** — positional `new JWT(email, null, key, scopes)` silently drops the key and every call 403s “unregistered callers”, which reads like a sharing problem and is not (selftest-pinned). Verified end-to-end 2026-08-19: two live shipments pushed, both auto-type paths correct on the real sheet, test rows deleted after. **Shipment charges (2026-08-05):** the expanded row shows Freight / Forward / RTO / COD fee / Order value / Billed weight. Stored charges are used when present; otherwise `/delivery-performance/shipment/:awb` fetches them **live on expand** and persists them, so it is one call per shipment ever. ⚠️ **"Already fetched" keys on `charges_fetched_at`, NOT on freight being present** — RapidShyp only prices a shipment once it is FINAL (delivered 89% / RTO 88% priced, but **in-transit 0% and NDR-pending 3%**), so a live in-transit fetch legitimately returns order value + billed weight with freight still null; keying on freight would re-call their API on every expand forever. The row says "freight not billed yet" rather than showing a bare dash. Charges are deliberately NOT added to the list query — 7 more columns × ~5.5k rows on every page load, never used. |
| Delivery Performance | `delivery-perf` | ⚠️ **"Silent RTO" means the courier NEVER ATTEMPTED delivery — it is evidence-based (fixed 2026-08-18).** It used to be inferred as *RTO with no NDR record* (`rto.length - ndrRto.length`), which is true only for **RapidShyp**, whose parser logs an NDR for every failed attempt. **Kwikship does not**: a parcel can go out for delivery and be returned with no NDR event at all (`Code verified cancellation`), so **139 of the 143 Kwikship RTOs labelled "silent · no attempt" had an out-for-delivery scan in their own timeline** (TE25-41826 among them) — only **4** were genuine. `isSilentRto()` now requires **no evidence of an attempt anywhere**: no counted attempt, no `out_for_delivery_at`, no NDR. Corrected 30-day split: Kwikship **143 → 4**, RapidShyp **33** (unchanged — it was always right), DocPharma **145 → 0**. ℹ️ DocPharma publishes **no scan log**, so it can neither prove nor disprove an attempt; the evidence test therefore never calls a DP return silent, which is the conservative side for a claim. ✅ **The claims report was never affected** — `fetchSilentRto()` filters on the parser-set `rto_no_attempt` flag AND `source='rapidshyp'`, so no wrong claim was ever emailed to a courier. ⚠️ **The verdict is computed ONCE, on the server, and shipped per row (`silent: isSilentRto(r)`).** The first pass fixed only the aggregate, and the Shipment Explorer's row filter was a second copy of the rule still testing `ndr_count===0` — so **the chip read 4 while the list returned 143**, visibly including rows with `ATT 1`. The explorer now filters on `r.silent`; never re-derive it client-side. This is the same hazard the courier-filter comment in `delivery_reports.js` already warns about: a second independent copy of one rule drifts silently from the KPI above it. Covered by `npm run selftest`. **First-Attempt Strike Rate breaks down (2026-08-18)**, like NDR Recovery: **Delivered 1st · RTO 1st · NDR · Still in transit** — four buckets that **sum to `tracked` EXACTLY** (KwikShip, 25 Jul–08 Aug: 390 + 92 + 164 + 12 = 658), so the card accounts for every shipment instead of reporting one percentage and leaving 41% unexplained. ⚠️ **`NDR` is `ndr_count > 0` — deliberately the same cohort the NDR Recovery card uses as its denominator**, so the two cards can never state different NDR totals for the same shipments (164 = 164). **`RTO 1st` is RTO with NO NDR ever raised** (silent RTOs included); the returns that went through NDR first are counted in the NDR bucket, or they would be double-counted and the total would exceed tracked — it reconciles as 92 + 83 NDR-originated = 175 = total RTO. ⚠️ **The fourth bucket is called "Still in transit", not "Not attempted"**: only 7 of those 12 were never attempted, 5 had been tried and were moving again. Naming a bucket after what most of it looks like is precisely how "silent RTO" came to report 143 when the truth was 4; the in-transit breakdown row splits the two properly. ₹ mode shows no split — an attempt has no rupee value. **The RTO row carries three chips, but only two are terms of the sum (2026-08-18):** `RTO 175 = [RTO 1st · no NDR 92] + [after NDR 83] │ of which [silent · no attempt 2]`. ⚠️ **Silent is a SUBSET of RTO 1st**, not a third term — a parcel with no delivery attempt cannot have raised an NDR — so it renders after "of which". Rendered as three `+` terms, RapidShyp would read 35 + 763 + 35 = **833** against a true **798**, because there every RTO-1st IS a silent RTO (that parser raises an NDR on every failed attempt, so skipping NDR means nothing was attempted). `RTO 1st` matches the FASR card's bucket and `after NDR` matches the NDR Recovery card's RTO outcome, so all three cards state the same numbers. All chips filter the Shipment Explorer via per-row server verdicts (`rto_first`, `silent`) — never re-derived client-side. **In-transit gets its own breakdown row beside the RTO one** — `In-transit 572 = reattempting · tried ≥1 54 + not attempted yet 518` — both chips filtering the Shipment Explorer, with `reattempting` shipped as a per-row server verdict like `silent`. On a 30-day window 518 of 572 had never been attempted at all: a pickup/linehaul problem, not a delivery one. KPIs (FASR/RTO/NDR-recovery/TAT), status partition, FASR trend, NDR funnel, RTO-by-courier, **FASR vs NDR Prepaid-vs-COD**, **🚩 Likely-Fake tracking insight** (marked → delivered conversion %), shipment explorer (sortable, filterable, expandable rows with date/scan logs, **Promise date column**, per-order actions: *mark likely-fake*, *send critical email* → becomes *View email & replies* with the **escalation mail thread auto-loaded** (sender/receiver format, AI reply scores + next-action), *🔁 NDR action* on NDR-pending rows) |
| Silent-RTO & SLA | `claims-sla` | 4 tabs: **Silent-RTO Claims** (RTO with zero attempts + claimable freight/invoice value), **Late Deliveries** (delivered after promise date), **In-transit · Overdue** (promise passed, still moving), **First-OFD Late** (**RTO-only**: RTO shipments whose first out-for-delivery scan was AFTER the promise EDD — courier didn't even attempt until the promise passed; a claimable SLA breach; **range filters on RTO date** not order date). **Date range is PER-TAB** — each tab keeps its own from/to (seeded to last 30 days), Apply reloads only the active tab, switching tabs restores that tab's range (lazy-loaded on first view). All tabs: KPIs, DD-MM-YYYY dates, sortable columns, shared client filters (search/platform/payment/courier/zone), expandable rows, email-send buttons (admin **or** `send-escalation-emails` permission). Report emails send **one email per platform** — RapidShyp rows → RapidShyp recipients, DocPharma rows → DocPharma recipients (DocPharma has no OFD scans so First-OFD is RapidShyp-only in practice) |
| Purchase Order | `purchase-orders` | **EasyEcom's PO book, read-only** (new 2026-08-13, Inventory group). `GET /api/purchase-orders` → EasyEcom `GET /wms/V2/getPurchaseOrderDetails` (`x-api-key` + `Bearer <JWT>`, JWT auto-refreshed through the existing `getEasyecomToken()`). KPI cards (POs, open POs, value still to arrive, total ordered), by-vendor value bars, a "still to arrive" panel by SKU, and a sortable PO table with **click-to-expand line items** (SKU, EAN, HSN, ordered / received / pending, unit price, line value). Filters: **PO type (Manual / Auto / All — defaults to Manual)**, Open/Received toggle, vendor, status, **date (All time / Today / Yesterday / Last 7 / 30 / 90 days / Custom)**, and free-text over PO no / ref / vendor / SKU / EAN. **Writes** (capability `purchase-orders-write`): create a PO, and change a PO's status. ⚠️ **THE SKU PICKER UNIONS PO HISTORY WITH THE LIVE PRODUCT MASTER (2026-08-19).** Built from history alone, a SKU that had never been on a PO could never be put on one — the first order of every new product was blocked (TE-ABD1, created in EasyEcom at 12:52, was in NO local table and none would learn it before the nightly snapshot). `fetchProductMaster()` pulls `GET /Products/GetProductMaster` (~126 products, cursor-paginated, 10-min in-process cache) — ⚠️ needs **BOTH** `x-api-key` AND the Bearer; Bearer-only is 403 Forbidden. Never-ordered actives are appended after the history rows flagged `neverOrdered: true`, carrying name, `masterCost` (EasyEcom's cost field — prefills the price but the hint names its source so it is never mistaken for a price we paid) and suggested GST from the master's `tax_rate` (⚠️ a FRACTION — 0.05 = 5%) with the HSN chapter as fallback. **`combo_product` is excluded** (53 of 124 actives — a bundle EasyEcom assembles, not a thing a supplier sells); children, normals and oddly-named marketplace imports stay. Master failure degrades to the old history-only picker. Module `app/api/purchase_orders.js` |

it had also LEAKED: **the shared `sendMail` helper AUTO-FILLS THE CONFIGURED REPORT CC LIST when no `cc` is passed** — an "approvers-only" mail went to the whole report audience. **Every future sendMail call must pass `cc: []` explicitly unless the report CC list is intended.**) The in-app channel, three shapes off one poll (`GET /po-approvals/pending-count` → `{count, value}`, 60s, approvers only): **a red count badge on the PO Approvals nav item**, **a sign-in POPUP** (dark-premium floating card, once per session, delayed past the welcome splash; click → the dashboard, ✕ dismisses; never shown while already there) and **a toast when the count rises mid-session**; deciding requests updates the badge instantly. **Approve fires the REAL EasyEcom create through `performPoCreate()`** — the create logic extracted from the direct route so the two can never drift; the direct `POST /purchase-orders/create` shrank to an **admin-only escape hatch** (a broken approval flow must never mean nobody can buy stock). **Maker-checker enforced:** a non-admin cannot approve their own submission. **status: pending → created (EE po id + landed status recorded) / rejected (with note, shown to the buyer) / failed** — an approved-but-refused EE create parks on `failed` with `create_error` and **Approve acts as Retry**; a half-done approval never vanishes. **UI:** gradient hero (inline styles — prebuilt tailwind has no gradient classes) with queue weight (pending count · ₹ on hold · released this month), one rich card per pending draft (full cart, requester, expected delivery, Approve/Reject/⬇ Draft PDF; Approve disabled on own drafts), rejection modal that demands a reason, decision history table. **⬇ Draft PO PDF (`GET /api/po-approvals/:id/pdf`) reproduces EASYECOM'S OWN PO SHEET** (from a real EE document, PO-2177045.pdf): GST header, vendor block with **TIN/address/contact from the raw vendor master**, Billing/Shipping boxes, item grid **with PRODUCT IMAGES** (master `product_image_url`, fetched + cached, degrade-to-blank), IGST vs CGST/SGST split by vendor state, Item/Tax/Grand totals in EE's raw number style, **EE's quirky value-in-words reproduced exactly** ("twenty thousands … point three eight Paise"), signatory block — plus one deliberate difference: an amber **"DRAFT — pending approval"** tag so it can never pass as a released PO. ⚠️ **Helvetica has NO ₹ glyph** (first render printed "¹101.1") — a rupee-capable system font is registered when present (Arial Bold on Windows, DejaVu Bold on the Ubuntu VPS), else amounts degrade to "Rs ". ⚠️ Grid widths must sum to the sheet width exactly — the first render pushed Unit Total off the page. Table `po_approvals_ecom` (migration `20260827_po_approvals.sql`, APPLIED; RLS, service-role only). Server gates: submit→`purchase-orders-write`, pdf→either perm, rest→`po-approvals`. Flow proven 7/7 without touching EasyEcom (submit, list, self-approve refusal, draft PDF, reject, double-decide refusal, cleanup). Module `app/api/po_approvals.js` |
| GRN — Goods Receiving | `grn` | **The receiving half of the purchase cycle** (new 2026-08-26, Inventory group, below Purchase Order). `GET /api/grn` → EasyEcom `GET /Grn/V2/getGrnDetails` + the PO book, fetched together so both halves describe the same moment. **Page:** 4 KPI cards (receipts · units/value received · **Awaiting receipt — live, ignores filters** · short receipts), a **Receiving pipeline strip** (Awaiting receipt → GRN in progress → Completed → Short received — LIVE whole-book state, on purpose: a date-filtered pipeline shows a process with stages missing), an **Awaiting receipt** panel (open POs oldest-first with % received progress bars, age, overdue-vs-expected flag, linked GRN count, pending-item tooltip), a **Short receipts** panel (per-SKU received-vs-ordered gaps), and a sortable GRN table whose rows **expand to line items** — ordered/received/still-owed, derived unit rate, line value, **batch, expiry, QC pass/fail/pending, damaged/lost**. Filters: All/Manual/Auto (**All is the default, unlike the PO page** — an AutoGrn is still real stock arriving, and autos are 49 of 67 live), vendor, status, date preset, free text over GRN/invoice/PO/SKU/batch. ⚠️ **`getGrnDetails` has the SAME silent-slice trap as the PO endpoint** (verified live: bare call = 6 GRNs / one week; `created_after=2024-01-01` = 67 back to Feb over 14 cursor pages; `limit` hard-capped at 10) — always send `created_after` (730-day lookback), always walk `nextUrl`. ⚠️ **GRN status ids DO NOT match EasyEcom's own doc** (doc: 1=CREATED, 3=QC Complete; live: 2=In Progress, 5=Completed) — the API's own `grn_status` STRING is used verbatim, no id map exists. ⚠️⚠️ **`grn_detail_price` is the LINE TOTAL, not the unit rate, though their doc sample reads as per-unit** — proven on 62/62 non-zero live GRNs (TE-BDR1: 391 received, price 10752.50 = 391 × ₹27.50 = header total); multiplying by quantity would inflate a 391-unit line 391×. The unit rate is derived by division. ⚠️ The endpoint **429s under bursts** — fixed properly 2026-08-26 night after "coming so frequently" (user): **(1) a 429 mid-walk retries THAT PAGE with growing backoff, never the whole walk** — the whole-walk retry re-fetched every earlier page, doubling the very burst that tripped the limiter, which is why the error kept recurring; **(2) pages are paced 150 ms apart** in both walkers; **(3) the GRN page reads the PO book through the shared `poBookCached()` (3-min TTL, purchase_orders.js)** instead of independently walking the same 14 pages the PO page just walked. The outer one-retry ladders stay as last resort. Verified live: a no-cool-off run right after heavy API traffic returned 200 in 13 s with zero 429s. ⚠️ **When the PO book still fails, the Awaiting-receipt KPI and pipeline stage show a DASH, never 0** — "0 units awaited" reads as *nothing is owed to us*, the exact wrong reading of *we could not check* (seen live: the banner said unavailable while the card said 0). **"Awaiting receipt" reuses `fetchAllPurchaseOrders` + `shapePo` (new exports from `purchase_orders.js`)** so open/dead/received semantics can never drift from the PO page; a PO-book failure degrades that panel (`poBookAvailable:false` warning), never the GRN list. 3-min cache, `?fresh=1` recomputes. **WRITES — Receive stock (capability `grn-write`, added same day from the user-pasted doc):** `POST /api/grn/create` → EasyEcom `POST /wms/QueueGrnApi` — **one endpoint serves both flows**: with `purchase_order_id` it is a GRN against that PO (partial receipts allowed), without it an Auto GRN. The job is **ASYNC** (`{code:200, queueId}`); `GET /wms/CheckGrnStatus?queue_id=` is polled ~12 s and the caller hears EasyEcom's verbatim message (finished / still processing / failed) — never an assumed success. UI: **"+ Receive stock"** header button + a **Receive** button per awaiting-PO row → modal with PO picker (pending lines prefilled: qty = still owed, cost = the PO's net unit price) or Auto-GRN mode (supplier picker from the vendor master, free-typed lines); fields qty · cost/unit · batch · MRP · expiry · shelf. **Guards:** PO-based lines are validated against the PO's PENDING quantities (a typo'd 6000 against a 600-unit line would create phantom sellable stock — hard 400 "only owed N"), unknown-SKU-on-PO refused, dead POs refuse receipts, and for a PO-based GRN **the vendor is taken from the PO, never the client**. ⚠️ **The PO-based param table documents `vendorId` ("Vendor Code") but BOTH of EasyEcom's own body samples send numeric snake_case `vendor_id`** — the CreatePurchaseOrder trap again; the samples win. ⚠️ Blank optionals are OMITTED, never sent as `''` (the PO-create blank-batch lesson). ⚠️ `cost` is **per-unit — PROVEN LIVE 2026-08-26 on the first real GRN** (2355800 against PO 79: sent 588 x cost 33.70, EasyEcom stored Rs19,815.60 = 588 x 33.70; PO line consumed to pending 0) — note this is the OPPOSITE of what getGrnDetails returns (line totals) — the two sides of the module deliberately use different readings because EasyEcom does. ⚠️ **QueueGrnApi reports validation failures as an ARRAY of `{SKU, Error}` objects inside an HTTP-200 body** (seen on the first real attempt) — fed raw into an error string it renders "[object Object]"; `eeMsgText()` flattens them per SKU. ⚠️ **Our products are LOT-configured, so EasyEcom demands the MANUFACTURING date on every GRN line** ("Manufacturing Date is mandatory as the product category is configured with LOT") — the form carries a Mfg-date column beside Expiry, and LOT products need BOTH.**End-to-end verified live by the user the same day**: PO 79 → 588 x TE-AAD1 @ Rs33.70, batch MDR-150, mfg+expiry, job 191182997 "Job Finished", GRN 2355800 on the book minutes later — and EasyEcom stamps API-created GRNs `grn_invoice_number="AutoGrn"` even PO-based, so dashboard receipts surface under the **Auto** toggle. **📄 GRN DOCUMENT + rich confirmation + MRP auto-fill (same evening, user request):** `GET /api/grn/:grnId/pdf` — ⚠️ **EasyEcom has NO GRN-document endpoint** (probed live: downloadGrn/downloadGRN/downloadGrnDocument/Grn/downloadGrn/downloadGrnReport all 404, unlike POs' /downloadPurchaseOrder), so the document is ALWAYS generated by us (pdfkit, `GRN-<id>.pdf`, footer says so): header/supplier/warehouse/PO blocks, line table with batch+expiry+receiving arithmetic+rate+amount, QC/damage note per line only when non-zero, totals. ⚠️ Columns laid out from ONE edge table (first render overprinted RATE on AMOUNT) and each row advances by the WRAPPED height of the product name (a fixed 15px let long names collide with the next line) — verified on the real GRN 2355800 PDF. **After a create finishes, the new GRN is READ BACK** (matched by PO in an **IST-naive string window** — `new Date()` on EasyEcom's zoneless stamp shifts by server timezone; Auto GRNs match newest-in-window since EasyEcom builds an auto-PO behind every one) and the confirmation shows the COMPLETE recorded GRN — header chips, full line table, part-received banner — with **⬇ Download GRN document** right there; plain summary is the fallback when the job is still queued or the read-back fails. Every GRN row's expansion also carries the ⬇ button. **MRP auto-fills** from the live product master (probe: master carries `mrp`; `skuInfo` map in the payload): PO lines get it via `pendingItems.mrp`, Auto-mode lines on SKU blur match — a typed value is never overwritten. **Mfg/expiry are MONTH pickers** (`type="month"` — product dating is month-level, per the user): the form sends `YYYY-MM`, expanded to the **first of the month on both sides** (client + server), matching EasyEcom's own convention (a July-2029 expiry is stored `2029-07-01`). ⚠️ **The portal's `app.easyecom.io/wms/printGRN?grn_id=` is PORTAL-SESSION-ONLY — the PO's downloadPurchaseOrder method has NO GRN counterpart** (exhaustively probed on api.easyecom.io: 11 route variants 404, printGRN itself GET-only and "Not authorised" with api-key+JWT under every param spelling; app.* is the cookie-auth, VPS-WAF-blocked host). **So the generated PDF now REPRODUCES EASYECOM'S OWN GRN LAYOUT** (user supplied a real portal print of GRN 2355558; the interim "EasyEcom print ↗" tab link was removed on request): bordered company header (warehouse name + `WAREHOUSE_ADDR` map — Shifupro's address hardcoded, an unmapped warehouse prints its name alone), centred "GRN" band, vendor/PO info block with EE's `:-` labels — **vendor address + TIN come from the RAW vendor master via `rawVendors()` (10-min cache), because `fetchVendors()` strips `tax_identification_number` and `address`, and the GRN payload has neither** — then their exact 14-column bordered grid (S.No · SKU Code · Product Name · SKU Desc · Vendor SKU · Colour · Size · GRN MRP · Exp Qty · Recv Qty · Cost Price · Batch Code · Additional Cost · Total; colour/size/MRP joined from the product master; PO No prints the po_id, as EE's own doc does) and the **Grand Total / Total Quantity** band. Verified pixel-for-field against both real documents (EE's grn_2026082664211.pdf vs our GRN-2355800.pdf). **✓ COMPLETE GRN from the dashboard (user request — an API-created GRN lands "In Progress" until the portal's Complete GRN button is pressed):** `POST /api/grn/complete {grnId}` → **`POST /wms/completeGrn` — UNDOCUMENTED, found by probing** (the one candidate of 12 answering 405-on-GET); payload `{ grn_id, c_id }` where **`c_id` is the GRN's own `inwarded_warehouse_c_id`** (never hand-typed; `company_id`/`companyId` answer "Company Id is missing", and the `c_id` shape was proven by the API answering "Cannot complete GRN as it is already in completed status" on a completed one). Failures arrive as **HTTP 200 + `status:400`** (the updatePoStatus trap). Gated on `grn-write`; the ✓ button renders on not-yet-completed GRNs (detail row + create confirmation) behind an ecConfirm; already-completed is refused server-side before EasyEcom is called. **After a complete: an ecResult panel states what happened (not a vanishing toast), the row flips to Completed optimistically, and the fresh fetch runs SILENTLY (`grnLoad(fresh, silent)`)** — a full-page loader after an action that already succeeded reads as a reload (user, 2026-08-26 night). ⚠️ **`qc_pending` LIES on API-created GRNs** (2355800: qc_pending 588 with line status "QC Complete" and all 588 units AVAILABLE — the API path skips the portal's manual QC step and the counter never moves): the QC cell and the PDF show the pending counter **only beside an actual pass/fail count**, otherwise the LINE STATUS is displayed (raw counter in the tooltip). **AutoGrn-stamped rows lead with the GRN number** ("GRN 2355800", sub "invoice: AutoGrn") since the stamped invoice name is meaningless as an identity. Server gate `[/^\/grn/i, 'grn']` + `canWriteGrn` inside; module `app/api/grn.js`; selftest-pinned |
