| RapidShyp Recon | `rapidshyp-recon` | **NEW 2026-08-03.** Reconciles what RapidShyp BILLED against RapidShyp's own rate behaviour. ⚠️ **No rate card and nothing hardcoded** (the opposite of DocPharma Recon, which multiplies a hand-entered `docpharma_rate_card`): charges are RapidShyp's own `freight_forward` / `freight_rto` / `cod_charges` / `applied_weight` on `shipment_journey_ecom`, synced from their `shipment_details` API by the nightly `syncChargesBatch`. **The benchmark rate is DERIVED from their invoices** — the MEDIAN billed forward freight per **delivery zone × 500 g slab** — so it self-updates when RapidShyp revises pricing and needs no per-shipment API call (their serviceability endpoint quotes TODAY's rate, not what was billed months ago). Measured live and it reproduces their card exactly: zone A ₹27.14 · B ₹27.14 · C ₹50.74 · D ₹50.74 · E ₹68.44 (≤500 g), and the 1000 g slab is precisely 2× (C ₹101.48, E ₹136.88). A cell is only trusted at **≥5 samples** (`MIN_SAMPLES`); tolerance for "over rate" is **+10%** (`OVER_TOLERANCE`). **Flags:** `over_rate` (the disputable overcharge) · `unpriced` (final but no charge synced — a billing gap) · `rto_not_rto` · `cod_on_prepaid` · `no_weight`. **UI — a 5-tab workspace mirroring DocPharma Recon** (`rsreInit`/`rsreTab` in app.js, view `#rapidshyp-recon-view`, shared date range in the sticky header): **Overview** (6 KPI cards, charge-breakdown bar, "needs attention" chips that jump into a filtered Reconciliation, spend-by-zone and spend-by-outcome bars) · **Reconciliation** (sortable 12-column table, flag/zone/outcome/payment filters + search, rows expand exactly like DocPharma's into Charge / Timeline / Scan log — the detail lazy-loads from `/rapidshyp-recon/shipment/:awb` and shows the billed-vs-zone-rate verdict) · **Rate Card** (the derived zone × slab grid with sample counts; cells under 5 samples are hidden as too thin to trust) · **Ledger** (month-by-month charges + GST vs payments with a running balance; **+ = payable to RapidShyp**) · **Payments** (record/delete payments to RapidShyp). CSV export covers the filtered set. ⚠️ **The date window is `awb_assigned_at` — RapidShyp's OWN AWB-assigned date, NEVER the order date and (since 2026-08-03) no longer the close date.** Their billing pivot filters on "assigned AWB date", so that is the only basis that can tie out to their invoice; measured against their report for July we get 4,326 shipments vs their 4,344 (99.6%). An earlier version windowed on the CLOSE date (`delivered_at`/`rto_at`), which both mismatched their invoice and silently **excluded every in-transit shipment**; `closeOf(r)` still exists but only to render the close date in the table. `awb_assigned_at` is a column on `shipment_journey_ecom` (indexed) captured from the same `shipment_details` call the charges sync already makes — no extra API cost. Ledger months group on the same basis. **COD/prepaid money view:** per month and in total — COD vs prepaid shipment counts, COD delivered (+ delivery rate), **expected COD remittance** (order value of COD shipments that DELIVERED — an RTO collects no cash so it owes no remittance; RTO value is shown separately), **prepaid total value**, `valuePending` (shipments whose value hasn't synced yet, surfaced rather than silently understating), and a **net position** card = expected COD remittance − freight outstanding. ⚠️ **`unpriced` means FINAL-but-unbilled only** — the Overview KPI once counted every shipment without a charge, which made 763 in-transit + 195 NDR parcels look like missing invoices (1,131 vs the true 181); it now matches the row flag and the ledger, and still-moving shipments are counted separately as `inFlight`. **Endpoints:** `GET /api/rapidshyp-recon` (from/to/outcome/payment/zone/flag/search) · `/rapidshyp-recon/benchmark` · `/rapidshyp-recon/shipment/:awb` · `/rapidshyp-recon/ledger` (FIFO settlement — payments clear the oldest month first — plus aging buckets 0-30/31-60/61-90/90+ measured from month end) · `GET|POST|DELETE /api/rapidshyp-payments`. ⚠️ **Payments are entered by hand — RapidShyp exposes NO wallet/billing API** (verified 2026-08-03: 15 billing paths probed live on GET+POST, all 404; their published docs cover only serviceability, orders, returns, B2B, wrapper, tracking, pickup; the charge response carries no balance). Ask RapidShyp for a billing/wallet API to automate it. **Table `rapidshyp_payments`** (new, mirrors `docpharma_payments`) — deliberately SEPARATE per partner, because each ledger is its own money flow; RLS on, service-role only. Server gate `[/^\/rapidshyp-(recon|payments)/i, 'rapidshyp-recon']` covers the payments routes too. First live run over 90 days: 12,024 shipments · ₹9.67 L billed incl. GST · **1,144 unpriced** · **85 over-rate = ₹1,823 disputable** (e.g. a 70 g zone-D parcel billed ₹93.53 against the ₹50.74 zone rate). ⚠️ **Known, DEFERRED (2026-08-04): billed freight reads low against RapidShyp's own total** (₹3,34,789 for the period) because `syncChargesBatch` only prices `is_final` shipments — non-final ones are ~1.3% priced (8 of 637). Closing it means pricing non-final shipments too, ~600–1,000 extra API calls/day. Deliberately postponed, not a bug: do not "fix" the totals by any other route. |
| KwikShip Freight Recon | `kwikship-recon` | **NEW 2026-08-20.** Freight reconciliation for KwikShip (GoKwik shipping). ⚠️⚠️ **THIS IS OUR COMPUTED EXPECTATION, NOT A KWIKSHIP INVOICE — the opposite of RapidShyp Recon**, where the charges are RapidShyp's OWN `freight_forward`/`freight_rto`/`cod_charges` read from their API (i.e. what they actually billed). KwikShip publishes no billing endpoint, so every rupee is computed BY US from the rate card the team entered (`kwikship_rate_card_ecom`) under the rules in `kwikship_billing_config_ecom`, applied by the SQL function `apply_kwikship_charges()` which writes the freight columns onto `shipment_journey_ecom` after each sync. The page says so in a **permanent amber banner on every tab** — a modelled number presented as a settled one is how a wrong rate card survives for months. Hold a real invoice against this page, not the reverse. **Pricing model** (all editable, no constant in the JS): forward = zone × weight slab (base to `slab_switch_g`, then +500g/+1kg adders) · RTO its own zone rate, with `rto_bills_forward` deciding whether a return ALSO pays the forward leg · COD fee = `cod_pct` of shipment value floored at `cod_min`, limited to delivered shipments by `cod_on_delivered_only` (an RTO collects no cash) · GST = `gst_pct`, **always computed and shown SEPARATELY**, never merged into freight. **UI — 5 tabs** (`ksrInit`/`ksrTab` in app.js, view `#kwikship-recon-view`, per-tab date ranges + presets, MTD default, reusing the `pgr*` formatters rather than copying them): **Overview** (KPI cards, charge-breakdown and outcome bars, COD-remittance cards, clickable “needs attention” chips) · **Reconciliation** (per-shipment table with zone/outcome/payment/flag filters + search) · **Rate Card** (the zone × slab grid and the billing rules, effective-dated) · **Ledger** — ⚠⚠ **REBUILT 2026-08-21 ON THE DOCPHARMA MODEL, because the first cut was a freight bill list and that reads the account BACKWARDS.** KwikShip COLLECTS the COD and remits it net of their freight, exactly the way DocPharma settles, so a payable-only ledger announced “₹358.72 payable to KwikShip” for a month in which KwikShip was holding **₹3.87 lakh of ours**. Now two-sided, column for column with the DocPharma ledger: **Receivable** (COD collected on DELIVERED COD shipments — an RTO collects no cash, so `codRtoValue` is a memo, as is `prepaidValue`) − **Payable** = **Net KwikShip should remit**, − payments = **Outstanding**. The payable side is deliberately TWO figures kept apart — `payableExpected` (rate card + GST) and `payableInvoiced` (the real bills from the Invoices tab) — and **only the INVOICED figure is netted**: a month KwikShip has not billed is not a liability yet, so the estimate rides along as an un-deducted memo (`unInvoicedMemo`). Clicking either payable card **re-bases Net and Outstanding on that basis** (a what-if), and every KPI card opens a full breakdown; each month row expands into the same three columns DocPharma uses (Fulfilment · Payable — freight · Receivable & settlement) with a variance line and a settled / partial / pending chip. Settlement is **FIFO with a stated frontier** (`settledThrough`, `unsettledMonths`, `overpaid`) because partners remit irregular lump sums — an averaged shortfall would hide which months are actually clear. CSV export included. ⚠ Two bugs fixed in the rebuild: a payment landing in a month with **no closed shipment** was silently dropped by the old `if (months[m])` (it now creates the month), and invoices/payments are clamped to the requested window so an August bill no longer appears as a phantom −₹386 month on a July ledger. ⚠ The payments form now records a **direction** (Received from KwikShip — the default — vs Paid to KwikShip) and the tab shows the **net** movement; summing two opposite flows as one total overstated what had moved. ⚠ The new UI uses NO `md:` / `lg:` grid classes: the prebuilt `tailwind.css` contains **no responsive grid variants at all**, so `lg:grid-cols-3` is a silent no-op — an `auto-fit` inline grid does the same job and cannot rot, and a selftest asserts none creep back in. **Live at rebuild (Aug MTD):** receivable ₹3,87,908.13 across 439 delivered COD shipments, payable ₹89,467.86 expected vs ₹386 invoiced, net **₹3,87,522.13 owed to us by KwikShip** — the opposite sign to what the old ledger showed. · **Invoices** · **Payments**. 🧾 **INVOICES — the half that makes the rest trustworthy (2026-08-21).** Every other tab agrees with itself by construction (it is all one rate card), so only a real bill can prove the rate card right. Upload a KwikShip invoice (PDF / Excel / CSV) → `POST /api/kwikship-invoices/parse` reads it and FILLS THE FORM — **a parse never saves**; you check the fields and press Save. The table then shows, per invoice, what they billed against **what we computed for the same period**, with the variance (red = billed more than the rate card says, amber = less). ⚠️ The variance only appears when the invoice STATES a period — a guessed window would invent a difference that is really a date mismatch. Upsert is on `invoice_no`, so re-uploading a bill corrects it instead of duplicating. Parsers are BORROWED from `docpharma_invoices.js` (now exporting `pdfText` / `parseInvDate` / `rowsFromExcel`) — one definition of “read a PDF”, not two that drift. ⚠️ **THE RATE IS NOT THE AMOUNT:** a naive “first number after the label” reads `CGST9 (9%) 4,265.79` as **9** and `IGST 18%` as **18** — small, believable figures that would be saved unchecked. Percentages are stripped, the LAST number on the line is the amount, and a rate-only line yields **nothing** rather than a wrong total. ⚠️ **A BILL STACKS SEVERAL TOTALS (fixed 2026-08-21).** The second real layout (Goexcelsior `HR/26-27/0014132`) prints `Sub Total 327.00`, then `Total ₹386.00`, then `Balance Due ₹386.00` — and the July bill prints `Total Taxable Amount 47,397.66` above its `Total 55,929.00`. Matching the first line containing “Total” therefore returned the **PRE-TAX** figure as the invoice value: short by exactly the GST, which is the one number this page exists to check. Money is now read by `amountOnLine(text, label, skip)`, one helper shared by freight, GST and total: it walks the lines, skips the qualified labels (`skip` rejects sub-total / taxable / in-words), strips percentages and takes the last number. Freight falls back through `Taxable Amount` → `Sub Total` → the `Freight Charge` line item; the total tries `Grand Total` → `Invoice Amount` → bare `Total` → **`Balance Due` LAST** (on a part-paid bill it is smaller than the invoice, and the invoice is what a variance measures). A final cross-check re-adds `Rounding` and rejects any total short of freight + GST. ⚠️ A lump-sum freight line carries `Qty 1.00` — a **line-item** count, not a shipment count; inventing `shipments = 1` would show a variance of −1,966 against the month, so it stays null. Likewise a bill that states **no** service period leaves the period blank rather than guessing the previous month — `HR/26-27/0014132` is a ₹386 one-off, and dating it “July” would score it against the whole month's ₹1,04,687. Verified against BOTH real invoices: the July monthly (2,765 shipments, ₹47,397.66 freight, ₹8,531.58 GST, `Jul'26` → full calendar month) and the stacked-totals one (₹327 freight, ₹58.86 GST, **₹386 total**). ⚠️ **Needs one migration** — `supabase/migrations/20260821_kwikship_invoices.sql`; until it runs the tab shows a setup card instead of an error (PostgREST reports a missing table as **PGRST205**, not Postgres' 42P01, because the request never reaches Postgres). 💸 **Payments** (record/delete — hand-entered, they expose no wallet API). **Method was dropped** on request (2026-08-21) and replaced by **📄 From PDF / Excel**: upload a payment advice or bank receipt and `POST /api/kwikship-payments/parse` fills date, amount and UTR/reference for review — again, it never writes a payment by itself. ⚠⚠ **Window basis is the TERMINAL STATUS DATE — `delivered_at` / `rto_at`, not the order date** (set on request the same day it was built). Freight is earned when a shipment CLOSES: a parcel ordered 28 Jul and delivered 3 Aug is August freight, and billing it to July misstates both months. DocPharma Recon reaches the same place with TWO controls (an order-date range plus its separate “Delivered / RTO” range); this is the single-control version. Implemented as two paged queries (delivered-in-window, returned-in-window) de-duplicated by AWB — PostgREST cannot range-filter two columns in one `.or()`, and a row carrying both timestamps must count once. **Live proof at the switch: July held 85 shipments by order date but 8 by close date**, because most July orders closed in August. ⚠️ Consequences, deliberately surfaced rather than hidden: a shipment still moving has no terminal date and so belongs to NO window (correct — no freight is due yet), which `openShipments()` counts and the Overview states outright (“640 shipments still moving, ~₹27,475 will land in the month each closes”); `lost` shipments have neither timestamp and never appear (~3 — they need their own date if KwikShip ever bills for them). The table's date column is the CLOSE date with the order date beneath it, the CSV header says “Closed (billing date)”, and the ledger groups months the same way. Not `awb_assigned_at` (KwikShip has 0 of 1,878 — that is RapidShyp's pivot) and not `dispatched_at` (1,824 of 1,878, so 54 priced shipments would silently vanish). ⚠⚠ **`apply_kwikship_charges()` MUST ONLY BE CALLED THROUGH `applyKwikshipCharges()` (2026-08-21).** The RPC RECOMPUTES `applied_weight` from the courier payload on every run, so it WIPES any weight we supplied from our own order and un-prices that shipment again. The backfill therefore has to follow every single call — and the RPC was called from THREE places (nightly cron, zone upload, manual remap) with the backfill after only one, so TE25-42790 reverted to unpriced the moment anyone re-zoned. The wrapper in `kwikship_sync.js` does costing + backfill as one operation and is now the only caller; a selftest asserts `rpc('apply_kwikship_charges')` appears **exactly once** in the codebase. The pairing is self-healing rather than order-dependent: the RPC nulls the weight, the backfill immediately refills it, and the row settles priced — verified by running it twice back to back (TE25-42790 → 80 g → ₹86 both times, **0 unpriced across all 1,967 shipments**). ⚠⚠ **BUT A NODE-SIDE PAIRING IS NOT ENOUGH, AND TE25-42790 CAME BACK A THIRD TIME (2026-08-21).** Not a new bug: **the wrapper had never left the staging folder.** Live's `kwikship_sync.js` was last deployed at `f2a2dff` and contains **no backfill at all**, so the VPS cron ran the raw RPC and blanked the weight again that night. Two lessons, both now acted on: a fix only counts once it is DEPLOYED, and a rule enforced in Node protects nothing against a statement run in the SQL editor, a Supabase cron, or a stale server. The durable guard is therefore in the DATABASE — `supabase/migrations/20260821_kwikship_keep_weight.sql` adds trigger **`a_kwikship_keep_weight`** (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE on `shipment_journey_ecom`, named to sort first because BEFORE triggers fire alphabetically and the weight must be in place before anything prices off it): for `source='kwikship'` only, a weight the row already had is never overwritten with NULL, and a row with none is seeded from `orders.total_weight`. The courier still wins whenever it actually reports a weight — we only fill the silence. Scoped to KwikShip deliberately: RapidShyp reads real applied weights from its own API and must keep the right to correct one downwards. The migration also repairs the rows already emptied. **KwikShip has no weight for a parcel cancelled before pickup** (TE25-42790: “seller initiated cancellation”, RTO with 0 attempts), which is why this one shipment, and not the other 1,966, kept surfacing. **Flags:** `unpriced` (FINAL-but-unbilled only — counting in-flight shipments as gaps is what made RapidShyp Recon report 1,131 missing invoices against a true 181) · `no_weight` · `no_zone` · `cod_on_prepaid` · `rto_no_attempt`. **Endpoints:** `GET /api/kwikship-recon` (summary + **buckets** + capped rows) · `/kwikship-recon/export.csv` (**server-side, filtered but NEVER capped** — the PG recon's export was built from the rendered page and silently produced 5,000 of 5,592 rows) · `/kwikship-recon/rates` · `/kwikship-recon/ledger` · `GET|POST|DELETE /api/kwikship-payments`. Server gate `[/^\/kwikship-(recon|payments)/i, 'kwikship-recon']`. New table **`kwikship_payments`** (mirrors `rapidshyp_payments`; separate per partner because each ledger is its own money flow), RLS on, service-role only. **Live at build:** 1,878 shipments (27 Jul–20 Aug), 1,877 priced, 4 zones, 2 couriers · forward ₹75,925 + RTO ₹13,002 + COD ₹10,726.61 = **₹99,653.61 ex-GST**, GST ₹17,937.65 → **₹1,17,591.26**, avg ₹53.06/shipment · 6 flagged. Selftests pin the arithmetic, the bucket partition, the flag rules, all six registration points, both real invoice layouts, the two-sided ledger, new-product registration, the ShopifyHold classifier, the snapshot determinism guards, a tailwind audit, the claims remark rules and the claims permission gate, the RTO re-pricing rule, the silent-RTO COD claim, the three admission rules, the PG tag basis and the voice-agent model + STT format + tool-call leak, the Rejected-COD tab and the manual WhatsApp sequences with the no-answer gate (248 checks in total). |
| GoKwik PG Recon | `gokwik-pg-recon` | **NEW 2026-08-18.** What GoKwik SHOULD charge us in payment-gateway fees, per order, from the first GoKwik order to today. **Rates are DB config, never code** — `pg_charge_config_ecom` resolved per order by **`pg_charge_for(payment_type, gateway, date)`**, and **effective-dated**, so renegotiating a rate never re-prices months already settled: **prepaid 2.15%** of order value (gateway `gokwik%` only) · **COD 2.00%** · **partial 2.50% of order value EXCLUDING shipping** (a COD order whose COD/shipping fee was paid online, `base_excludes_shipping=true`). **GST 18% is computed and shown SEPARATELY from the fee throughout** — fee, GST and total are three distinct columns everywhere, per the user's instruction; nothing anywhere shows a GST-inclusive figure alone. ⚠️ **THE GATEWAY MUST COME FROM SHOPIFY, NOT EASYECOM.** EasyEcom leaves `payment_gateway_name` blank on a large share of rows (124 of August's prepaid orders, ₹93,351). Sampling those against Shopify: 7 of 9 were GoKwik — **but one was Cashfree and one was manual**, so treating blank as GoKwik would bill GoKwik for payments it never processed. `syncOrderGateways()` fills `orders.gateway` from Shopify's **`paymentGatewayNames`** (an ARRAY — the legacy scalar `gateway` field is absent on modern orders), batched 50 per GraphQL query and written back **grouped by gateway value**, one `UPDATE … WHERE id IN (…)` per 200 rows: the per-order version was ~40 minutes of round-trips at 28k orders. Anything still unresolved is reported as **`unclassified`**, never folded into the bill. **UI — a 5-tab workspace mirroring DocPharma/RapidShyp Recon** (`pgrInit`/`pgrTab` in app.js, view `#gokwik-pg-recon-view`): **Overview** (KPI cards + charge split by payment type and by gateway) · **Reconciliation** (per-order table: type, gateway, order value, rate, fee, GST, total; filters + search) · **Rate Card** (every effective-dated rate, with its note) · **Ledger** (month by month) · **Refunds**. **Refunds are their own tab and ARE charged** (user, 2026-08-18): GoKwik does not reverse the fee, so a refund costs twice — the goods come back AND the fee stands. Voided orders are charged too (confirmed with the user). **Date filtering is PER TAB** — Overview / Reconciliation / Ledger / Refunds each keep their own from/to + preset and fetch independently, so the Ledger can sit on "Last year" while Reconciliation shows this month; the Rate Card hides the date controls entirely (it lists effective-dated rates, so a range would do nothing). Presets: **This month (default = MTD) / Last month / This year / Last year**. ⚠️ The controls MUST carry `.filter-select` / `.date-input` / `.filter-btn` — `ecEnhanceFilterSelects()` upgrades `.filter-select` app-wide into the custom `.csel` widget, and hand-rolled classes leave raw OS dropdowns; programmatic value changes must call **`ecSyncSelect()`** or the painted face keeps the old label. ⚠️ **MTD once defaulted to the wrong day** — `toISOString()` on a local date shifts IST back 5:30 and produced 2026-07-31 for 1 August; dates are built from local parts by **`pgrYmd()`**. ⚠️ **`pg_recon_rows()` is a set-returning RPC and PostgREST caps it at 1000 like any select** — it is paged with `.range()`; the unpaged version silently under-reported. ⚠️ **AND THE 5,000-ROW TABLE CAP LEAKED INTO THE NUMBERS (2026-08-20, user-reported).** Paging fixed the fetch, but the Reconciliation count line and the CSV were still built from `_pgr.rows` — the capped page. For July the header read “4,911 charged” + “89 not charged”, a pair that sums to the **cap**, not to the window's 5,592 orders, and understated the charge by **₹11,421** (₹97,763 shown vs ₹1,09,184 real) — while the banner claimed “every total is computed over all of them”. Same flaw hit the **CSV export**, which is worse: 5,000 of 5,592 rows in a file an accountant reconciles from and cannot tell is short. Fixes: the server ships **`buckets`** (per payment_type × charged, aggregated over EVERY row) and the count line reads those — a text search is the one case answerable only from what is loaded, and it now says so; the reconciliation CSV is built by **`GET /api/pg-recon/export.csv`** server-side over the full window, through `pgrFilter()` — the filter defined ONCE so the export and the screen cannot drift. Banner reworded to say only the ROWS are trimmed. Verified live: 5,489 charged + 103 not charged = 5,592 = `rowsTotal`, export 5,592 rows, charged-only export 5,489. Six selftests pin it, including a behavioural partition test on `pgrBuckets`. ⚠️ **Delivered COD orders were being priced as prepaid** (₹90,323 missing) because financial status was tested before the gateway; the gateway is classified first now. **Endpoints:** `GET /api/pg-recon` (from/to — returns `summary`, `rows` (cap 5000), `refunds`, `refundTotals`) · `GET /api/pg-recon/rates` · `POST /api/pg-recon/sync-gateways`. Server gate `[/^\/pg-recon/i, 'gokwik-pg-recon']`. **Nav is STATIC HTML** — adding the permission to `PERM_GROUPS` is not enough; the sidebar needs its own `<a id="nav-gokwik-pg-recon">` (this cost a debugging round when the dashboard was invisible despite being permissioned). **Live July 2026:** 5,397 orders · ₹43.62 L gross · fee **₹89,024.36** + GST **₹16,018.59** = **₹1,05,049.01**, split COD ₹60,828 / prepaid ₹41,143 / partial ₹3,077; **101 orders excluded** (19 Cashfree + 76 manual + voided) and **0 unclassified**. ⚠️ **Still unverified against a real GoKwik statement** — these are OUR computed expectation, not their invoice. |

The same `getAdsetPerformanceData()` serves the dashboard, the daily PDF (`cron_job.js`) and the per-order Excel export, so the three cannot disagree |

### Customer Support (port of the standalone Support Console)
| Dashboard | Key | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Support Dashboard | `support-dashboard` | KPIs (orders/calls/delivered/pending/MSG91-confirmed) + 8 clickable bucket tiles |
| Call Queue | `support-queue` | 3 tabs — Repeat customers (COD repeat pre-dispatch), Undelivered, Status-changed; note/age/hold/**payment** filters, **clickable column-header sorting** (Order / Bucket / **Payment** / Age / Courier / **Last scan** — asc/desc toggle with a caret; default = server order confirmed→oldest). The **Last scan** column (Undelivered tab **only** — irrelevant for pre-dispatch Repeat) shows the latest AWB scan by the courier (`order_tracking.status_updated_at` → `last_tracked_at` → `updated_at`, attached as `last_scan_at` by `scanTimesByOrder()`); empty scans always sort last. **Repeat tab hides already-dispatched orders — EXCEPT ones the team is working.** `findRepeatCandidates` classifies a row as dispatched via `fulfillment_status ∈ {fulfilled, partial}` (a fulfilled order has an AWB + courier assigned, out-for-pickup/manifested → un-holdable) plus a `tracking_status` in-transit safety-net regex (DocPharma `in-progress` / in-transit / OFD / delivered / RTO). The `order_buckets` `order_to_dispatch` bucket is keyed off a lagging Shopify-fulfillment state, so it wrongly kept shipped orders. Two tiers: **MOVED** (`_moved`) = the courier physically has it — `tracking_status` matches picked-up / in-transit / OFD / RTO / sorting / delivered (courier status, fresher than the fulfillment bucket); **DISPATCHED** (`_dispatched`) = moved OR merely fulfilled (AWB assigned but maybe not yet picked up — e.g. pickup-scheduled / out-for-pickup, still holdable). The Call Queue calls with `skipDispatchFilter=true, anyReason=true` (returns the whole COD/pre-pickup base, tagged); the /support/queue repeat block then **hides MOVED orders outright** and shows a row only if it **matches ≥1 call-reason OR is being worked** (held EasyEcom/Shopify incl. failed, or has notes). **Each shown order is TAGGED with which of 4 call-reasons put it on the list** (chip in the Customer column, via `c.reasons`): **`in_flight`** (🚚 — the customer has ANOTHER order not yet terminal = a live/pending delivery), **`recent_undelivered`** (⚠️ — ≥1 of the customer's last 3 prior orders not delivered — **RTO counts as a failure**; cancelled doesn't count), **`high_value`** (💰 — order **≥ ₹1500**; **TRUST EXCEPTION 2026-08-01: skipped when the customer has EVER taken delivery of a ≥₹1500 order** — their **COMPLETE history**, no last-3 window and no time limit, since a proven high-value acceptor makes the value alone a non-signal), **`short_address`** (📍 — the shipping address (address1+address2+city+province+zip joined) is **under 60 chars** — often incomplete → RTO-prone; **trust exception: skipped if a PAST order for this customer was DELIVERED to the same address**, normalised case/space/punctuation-insensitive). Reasons are OR (an order can carry several tags). **Reliability exception on the two history reasons** (`reliable`): DON'T hold if the customer has **1–2 delivered orders in the last 3 AND their latest or 2nd-latest order is an RTO** — a one-off RTO for an otherwise-delivering customer. (All-3-delivered has no "not delivered" order, so never holds; all-RTO / all-undelivered → holds.) **Payment gate:** FULLY-prepaid (`paid`/`refunded`/`partially_refunded`) never held; **COD** → all 4 reasons; **`partially_paid`** → held on **high-value (≥₹1500) only** (it still carries a COD balance; history/short-address reasons stay COD-specific — gated by `isPartialPaid` in holdReasons + findRepeatCandidates, added 2026-07-28). The **auto-hold qualifies by ANY of the 4 reasons** (high-value / in-flight / recent-non-delivery / short-address orders auto-hold, matching the panel) — `qualifiesForHold` (webhook, instant) short-circuits `totalPrice ≥ 1500` then applies the same reliability-gated recent-non-delivery / in-flight check by phone history; `findRepeatCandidates` default (cron backstop) returns `reasons.length > 0`. **UI:** on the Repeat tab the **Bucket column is hidden** (always order_to_dispatch) and the EasyEcom/Shopify hold badge shows **once** (in the Actions cell, not also under the order id); the note button is pinned to the right of the Actions cell. **EasyEcom-cancelled orders are dropped entirely** (`eeCancelledSet` — `b2c_order_easycom.order_status="Cancelled"`): EasyEcom can cancel an order while Shopify's `cancelled_at` + the bucket still read active (sync lag), so a cancelled order was wrongly appearing as a live candidate and as "active" in the customer-history table. Now such orders are excluded from candidates, don't count toward the prior-order repeat criterion, and the order-detail **customer-history table shows them as Cancelled**. Notes dialog, RapidShyp sync button. **Cancel held order (2026-07-29):** a held Shopify order gets a **Cancel order** button → reason picker (Customer refused / Unreachable / Duplicate / Fake / Other + optional note) → cancels on Shopify (**restock, NO customer email**) via `shopify_hold.cancelOrder`: (1) `refundCapturedIfAny` first **refunds any amount captured online** (a COD fee / advance on a partially-paid order; pure-COD held orders have nothing captured → skip) via `POST /orders/{id}/refunds.json` — a refund failure ABORTS before cancelling so a paid amount is never left stuck; (2) `POST /orders/{id}/cancel.json` (our label → Shopify enum customer/fraud/other); then clears the hold marks, records a **`shopify_cancelled`** mark (reason + who) + a `shopify_cancel` entry in the hold log. Endpoint **`POST /api/support/cancel-order` {orderId,orderName,reason}** gated by the **dedicated `support-cancel-order` capability** (Users page → Actions group; admins pass via `*`; frontend button hidden without it, server enforces via `requirePermission`). Needs the Shopify token to carry **`write_orders`** (for cancel + refund) in addition to the fulfillment-order scopes. **HOLD/RELEASE/CANCEL ANSWER IN PLACE, NOT WITH A RELOAD (user, 2026-08-20):** all five actions (Shopify hold/release, EasyEcom hold/unhold, cancel) used to end in `supLoadQueue()` — a full re-fetch of the heavy queue endpoint that flashed the grid and lost the agent's scroll position per click. The outcome is known at API success, so `supRowPatch(oid, fn)` now updates that ROW's data (`shopify_hold` → held/released with who+when so the 🔓 history chip renders correctly, `ee_hold` flag + the shared `_eeHold` chip cache) and `supRowDrop(oid)` removes a cancelled order outright — client-side repaint only, same optimistic pattern as the Raised control; failure paths still restore the button and say why. The 30s poll below reconciles any drift within half a minute. **CONFIRM → LOADER → DONE, all in one popup (same day):** `supConfirm` gained `tone`/`icon` (releases are 🔓 emerald “go”, holds ⏸, cancels stay rose) and a `work` callback — on confirm the popup does not vanish, it BECOMES the loader (brand spinner + “Releasing #TE… · Talking to Shopify”) until the API answers; Escape/backdrop are dead while in flight (closing would hide the action, not undo it). Shopify release and EasyEcom release are deliberately the SAME experience — selftest-pinned so they cannot drift. Hold-Shopify (no confirm step) and Cancel (confirms via its reason picker) show the same loader through `supBusyModal`; no action signals progress through button text any more. **And the RESULT shows in the popup too (same day):** success is a ✓ emerald beat in the same modal (“#TE… released · The order can now ship”) that closes itself after ~1.3s; a FAILURE swaps to a ✕ state with the real error message and a Close button — errors stay until read, an auto-closing failure is a missed failure. Callers no longer toast errors (the popup owns them); the success toast remains as the after-the-fact record. Behaviourally pinned in selftest (patch/drop exercised with fake rows; zero `supLoadQueue()` calls allowed in the handlers). **Real-time:** the queue **auto-refreshes every 30s** (quiet poll while the Support view is visible; skipped when a hold/cancel dialog is open) and updates instantly after any hold / unhold / cancel. **Auto-drop resolved rows (2026-07-29):** a hold that's been **RELEASED on Shopify** and has since **moved forward** (dispatched — fulfilled / AWB assigned) and isn't still held in EasyEcom is dropped from the queue (the call is done and it's shipping). Picked-up / delivered / RTO were already dropped via `!_moved`; this closes the AWB-assigned-but-not-yet-scanned gap — combined with the 30s poll, such orders leave the list in ~real-time. **Phone backfill (2026-07-29):** `findRepeatCandidates` backfills a missing `phone` from `order_shipping_addresses` — the `order_buckets` view's `phone` is null for some orders (it uses the order/customer phone, but COD orders often carry the number only in the shipping address), which made them show **"no phone"** in the queue AND blocked the phone-based reason recompute (so a correctly-held order looked reasonless). Filled before the history/in-flight reason checks. **Payment column — COD / Prepaid (2026-08-01):** every queue row now carries `payment` (+ raw `financial_status`), read from `orders.financial_status` because the `order_buckets` view has **no payment column**. Rule matches the Orders dashboard: `paid`/`refunded`/`partially_refunded` → **Prepaid** (emerald chip), everything else → **COD** (amber chip — money still to collect on delivery); `partially_paid` shows as `COD •` with a tooltip ("part-paid online, balance due on delivery"). Rendered by `supPayChip()` as a sortable **Payment** column on the **Undelivered + Status-changed** tabs (hidden on Repeat, which is COD-only by definition so the column would be noise), plus an **All payments / COD / Prepaid** filter (`sup-f-pay`, included in the Clear-filters reset). Verified on live data: 158/158 undelivered rows resolve (147 COD · 11 Prepaid) |
 **Courier platform tag (2026-08-08):** every row on the **Undelivered + Status-changed** tabs shows a colour-coded aggregator chip next to the courier name — **RapidShyp** (indigo) / **DocPharma** (teal) / **KwikShip** (amber) — so an agent sees which partner portal / escalation route owns the parcel without opening it. Resolved server-side by `platformByOrder()` and returned as `row.platform`; rendered by `supPlatformTag()`. **Do NOT use `order_buckets.partner` for this** — it is the courier SERVICE name for KwikShip shipments (`delhiverydirectsurface500g`, 532 rows) and sometimes for RapidShyp (`delhivery enterprise`, `ekart brands`), and is null on 128 recent rows that plainly shipped. The authoritative signal is **`shipment_journey_ecom.source`** (written by whichever sync owns the shipment); `partner` (only when it literally reads rapidshyp/docpharma/kwikship), then courier name / `EL…` AWB prefix → DocPharma, are fallbacks for orders with no journey row yet. Note the join must strip **`#`**: `order_buckets.order_name` carries it (223/223), `shipment_journey_ecom.order_name` never does (0/18,397). Skipped on the Repeat tab (pre-dispatch — no shipment, so the lookup would return only nulls). Verified on live data: **167/167** undelivered rows resolve (166 RapidShyp · 1 DocPharma), zero untagged. **Courier-platform FILTER (2026-08-08):** `sup-f-platform` — All platforms / RapidShyp / DocPharma / KwikShip / **No platform** — filters the same `row.platform` the chip renders. Shown only on the shipped tabs and **forced back to `all` when the Repeat tab is selected**: those orders have no shipment, so a filter left set on Undelivered would silently empty the tab. `No platform` isolates rows the server could not resolve — the ones whose tracking nobody is syncing. Included in the Clear-filters reset. Live check after the Kwikship bucket fix: the Undelivered tab holds **193 rows over 14 days — 167 RapidShyp + 26 KwikShip, zero unresolved** (the 26 are the NDR orders that were invisible before that fix). **UNDELIVERED PANEL DROPS TERMINAL SHIPMENTS (2026-08-11).** `order_buckets` decides "undelivered" from `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom.raw_status` + `order_tracking.tracking_status` — both **periodic snapshots**, so when a parcel moves on to RTO or is finally delivered the bucket keeps reading undelivered until the next sync rewrites that text. `shipment_journey_ecom.outcome` is the courier-derived truth and updates in real time from the webhooks, so `terminalByAwb()` now drops `delivered`/`rto`/`lost` from the tab. Measured over 14 days: **149 → 119 rows, removing 21 RTO and 9 DELIVERED** (calling a customer about a parcel they already received is worse than calling about an RTO). ⚠️ `in_transit` is deliberately NOT terminal — a parcel reattempting after a failed delivery reads in_transit while its NDR is still open and genuinely needs the call. The drop runs BEFORE the `undelivered_tracking` upsert, so a terminal order is still remembered and simply moves to Status-changed. ⚠️ **THAT LAST SENTENCE WAS A LIE IN CODE UNTIL 2026-08-18** — see "Status-changed lost the orders it exists to show" below. **STATUS-CHANGED IS NOW DERIVED FROM THE COURIER, NOT FROM WHO HAD A TAB OPEN (2026-08-18).** Two defects, both fixed: (1) the terminal filter reassigned `rows` and the upsert ran on the FILTERED list, so the settled orders — the entire point of the tab — were the only ones never recorded, and they vanished from Undelivered and Status-changed at the same moment. Recording now happens first, via `rememberUndelivered()`. (2) More fundamentally, `undelivered_tracking` was only ever written as a **side effect of a human loading the Undelivered tab**, so any parcel that failed a delivery and then settled between two visits was lost for good. `syncUndeliveredFromJourney(from,to)` now derives the same fact from `shipment_journey_ecom.ndr_count > 0` — a courier fact, true whether or not anyone was watching — and runs on every Status-changed load (~1,500 journeys / 30 days, pure DB work, **zero courier calls**, idempotent). `first_seen_at` is set to the **first out-for-delivery scan** rather than "when a human refreshed", which is also the correct boundary for the note-scoping rule below. Backfilled over all history 2026-08-18: **`undelivered_tracking` 3,982 → 6,949 rows (+2,967)**; of orders with a real NDR since 16 Jun, **447 were missing (218 RTO + 229 delivered) and are now 0**. Status-changed for Aug MTD now lists **595 orders (270 RTO · 242 delivered · 83 other)**. DocPharma shipments have no journey row and stay covered by `rememberUndelivered()`. Also removed a third silent cap: the candidate fetch was `maxRows: 3000` ordered by `last_seen_at DESC`, so past 3,000 rows the oldest-seen orders fell off the tab permanently. **THE RULE, set by the user 2026-08-18: "if an order is undelivered → delivered or RTO, it should go on Status changed."** The tab therefore lists **`SETTLED_BUCKETS = ['delivered','rto']`**, not "any bucket other than undelivered" as before. Aug MTD **595 → 513 rows (270 RTO · 243 delivered)**, dropping 72 orders cancelled after going undelivered and 10 that are moving again (8 `five_days_plus`, 2 `order_to_dispatch`) — still in flight, so on no settled list. **WHAT COUNTS AS "UNDELIVERED" — `undeliveredMoment(j)`, the single definition both tabs share.** The parcel was not in the customer's hands when it should have been, which happens **two** ways and BOTH count: (a) the courier logged a **failed delivery attempt** (`ndr_count > 0`); (b) the parcel went **past its promised date** (`first_edd`) still undelivered — no failed attempt required. An RTO or lost parcel is undelivered by definition whatever its dates say; an **on-time delivery with no failed attempt never enters the list**. ⚠️ Rule (b) exists because rule (a) alone is blind to the commonest support case: **TE25-39935** was promised 09 Aug, sat in a Moradabad facility for a week and delivered on 18 Aug on its **first and only** attempt — `ndr_count` is 0 and always will be. Its live GoKwik feed was re-fetched from the API to confirm (33 events, matching our stored journey exactly, so no parser drift). `first_seen_at` is the **earlier** of the first delivery attempt and the missed promise date — for that order 09 Aug, not the 18 Aug attempt — which is also the note-scoping boundary. Effect on the user's own window (19 Jul – 18 Aug): Status changed **825 → 1,386** rows (799 delivered · 587 RTO). It stays an exception list, not a settlement log: of 4,048 settled orders in that window, the 2,663 that delivered on time and first-time never appear. Rule tested behaviourally in `tests/selftest.js` (5 cases incl. the TE25-39935 shape), not just asserted to exist in the source. **HANDOVER IS REAL-TIME, AND BOTH TABS NOW READ THE SAME FACT (2026-08-19).** Undelivered drops a parcel the moment `shipment_journey_ecom.outcome` turns terminal (webhook, seconds), but Status changed was reading `order_buckets.bucket`, which only follows once the periodic `order_tracking` / `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom` snapshot is rewritten — leaving a window in which an order was on **neither** tab (3 such orders on 19 Aug, one a RapidShyp RTO invisible since 11 Aug). Status changed now takes its settled verdict from `terminalByAwb()`, the same call Undelivered uses (returns a **Map** awb→outcome now, not a Set; `.has()`/`.size` unchanged), and rewrites the displayed bucket to the courier's verdict. ⚠️ It never overrides `cancelled` — that is our decision about the order, not a lagging snapshot; overriding it too dragged 607 cancelled orders onto the tab. Verified: **0 orders appear on both tabs** across 7d/MTD/30d. **Three further defects found while verifying this.** (1) **`chunkedIn()` swallowed errors** — it ended `parts.flatMap(p => p.data || [])`, so a failed chunk silently contributed zero rows and the caller reported a confident WRONG total. Symptom: Status changed returned **0 rows for August while returning 207 for a range containing August**, and repeat runs disagreed. It now **throws**, and its fan-out is capped at 6 concurrent chunks — the fan-out, not the query, was what broke. Two consecutive runs are now byte-identical. (2) **The response was silently capped at 1,500 rows** (`rows.slice(0, 1500)`, all tabs) — the tab crossed it the day it started catching late parcels. The server now returns `total` + `capped` and the count line reads “1,500 of 1,993 — narrow the dates to see the rest” instead of implying completeness. (3) **Perf:** candidates are pre-filtered on `first_seen_at >= from` (never earlier than the order date, so it cannot drop an in-window order), the journey derivation is cached 60s (the queue polls every 30s and this is catch-up work; orders already tracked stay real-time regardless), and the Last-scan lookups are skipped on the tabs that hide that column. 30-day load **17.6s → ~8s**; 7-day 2.5s. ⚠️ The remaining cost is the **`order_buckets` view itself — ~1.1s per 300 rows** (5 CTEs with DISTINCT ONs); shaving API-side work will not move it, an index or a materialised view would. **STATUS-CHANGED NOW HONOURS THE DATE FILTER (2026-08-11):** that tab ignored the range picker entirely and listed every order ever seen undelivered (back to 2026-05-26 — 2,820 rows regardless of the control above it). Now windowed: 7d→10, 14d→284, 30d→1,034, 90d→2,820. Filtered after the fetch rather than in the query because the candidate ids come from `undelivered_tracking`, which has no order date of its own. **UNDELIVERED PANEL REWORK (2026-08-11).** (a) **Bucket → latest STATUS.** That column said "Undelivered" on all 133 rows — the tab already says so. It now shows the courier's current status via `supStatusText()`, colour-coded by urgency. The feed mixes casing and separators for the same state, so it is normalised first: **14 raw values collapse to 6 labels** instead of reading like 14 statuses. (b) **STATUS FILTER** (`sup-f-status`) with live counts — Undelivered (71) · Out For Delivery (34) · In Transit (16) · Delivery Delayed (7) · Partially Delivered (6) · Misrouted (2). ⚠️ Options are built from the rows ACTUALLY LOADED, never hardcoded: a fixed list goes stale the moment a courier adds a state and offers choices matching nothing. Rebuilt only when the option set changes (signature check) so the 30s auto-refresh cannot reset a selection mid-use; a vanished selection falls back to All. Shares `supStatusText()` with the column so a status can never be listed under a name the column does not use. (c) **RAISED TO COURIER** — the team recorded this in free-text notes ("raised", "raised with VOC"), so it could not be filtered, sorted or counted. Now a `courier_raised` mark in `order_marks_ecom` (unique on order_name+mark_type → one per order, updated in place), `note` = kind, `created_at` = the raised date. Per-row dropdown (Raised / Raised with VOC / Clear) via `POST /support/raise`, a sortable **Raised** column, and an All/Not-raised/Any/Raised/VOC filter. ⚠️ The date deliberately MOVES when escalating to VOC — that is a fresh escalation, and the column must show when it happened. ⚠️ **IT IS NOT A NATIVE `<select>` AT RUNTIME, WHATEVER THE MARKUP SAYS** (corrected 2026-08-13). It carries `.filter-select`, and `ecEnhanceFilterSelects()` upgrades every one of those app-wide into the custom `.csel` widget — the real `<select>` is moved into a wrapper and the **visible button and option panel are siblings beside it**. That broke two things at once, both reported live: (1) the `stopPropagation` guard was bound to the `<select>`, so it never saw the click that actually happened on the `.csel-btn`, and choosing a value saved the mark **and opened the order popup on top of the confirmation**. The row handler now guards on the ANCESTOR (`.csel, select, button, a, input, label`), which covers the button, the panel and every option row at once and survives changes to the widget's internals. (2) Any patch that swaps the `<select>` element leaves the new one orphaned inside the old wrapper with a dead button — so `supPatchRaise()` rewrites the **options in place** and repaints the face with `ecSyncSelect()` (setting `.value` in code fires no `change` event and does not touch the value *attribute*, so neither the widget's listener nor its MutationObserver would otherwise notice). **Any clickable row containing a `.filter-select` anywhere in the app has this trap.** ✨ **The mark is also OPTIMISTIC (2026-08-13):** the chip changes the moment the agent chooses rather than after the round-trip — an agent works this queue row by row and a control that does nothing for ~300ms gets clicked twice — and only that row's chip + dropdown are repainted. The old code called a **full table re-render** on success, which flashed the grid and lost the agent's place, reading as a page reload. While the write is in flight the chip sits at half opacity and the control is locked (on screen instantly, but honest that it is not saved yet); on success the **server's** timestamp and user are adopted over the optimistic guess; on failure the row is restored exactly as it was and the error names the order, so the screen never claims something that was not stored. (d) **UNIVERSAL SEARCH** — the box searched only the latest note. Now order number · order id · AWB · phone · email · **customer name** · courier · note, on all three tabs. Name had to be ADDED (`namesByOrder()` from `order_shipping_addresses`; `order_buckets` has phone/email but no name, so the queue could only be searched by number) — 166/166 rows resolved one, with EasyEcom's literal "DUMMY" placeholders filtered out. ⚠️ Digits are matched SEPARATELY from text (4+ digits) so a pasted `+91 77740 77208` finds the stored `7774077208` and an AWB tail like `766166` works — formats differ per order, which is why a plain substring test kept missing. |
| Support Orders | `support-orders` | Full order search (bucket/partner/courier/status/free-text), 50/page, order-detail modal (MSG91 column/filter removed 2026-07-16; detail modal shows MSG91 thread with template-variable JSON rendered as plain text) |
| Call Logs | `support-calls` | My calls (admin: all), outcome stats, follow-ups |
| Escalation Contacts | `support-contacts` | Courier/region/pincode escalation directory (admin CRUD) — powers "Whom to call" in order detail |
| Customer Profile | `customer-profile` (+ `support-store-credit` to issue credit) | **Renamed + rebuilt 2026-08-04 from "Blacklist Numbers".** URL hash, nav id, view id and the permission key all moved `support-blacklist` → **`customer-profile`** (the frontend's `canView()` uses the view name AS the permission key, so the URL cannot be renamed without renaming the permission). ⚠️ Existing users were granted the new key **additively, keeping the old one** — the Supabase DB is shared with live, so a straight swap would have removed the page from live until it deployed; the server gate accepts either key until the old one is retired. Nav icon changed from the circle-slash "ban" glyph to a person icon. Search a customer by phone / email / name / order number, then one page for everything: **identity** (name, phone, email, first & last order, Influencer flag) · **7 KPI tiles** (orders, lifetime value, delivered + delivered value, RTO with rate, cancelled/in-flight, COD vs prepaid, store-credit balance) · **Order history** (per order: value, payment, outcome, courier, AWB, SKUs, destination, NDR count, silent-RTO flag) · **Top products** (units, spend, order count, last bought, bar) · **Store credit** (balance + our audit log + Shopify's own ledger incl. checkout redemptions) · **Blacklist** (banner if blocked, block/unblock inline). The blacklist register moved to a panel behind the header **Blacklist** button. ⚠️ View ids stay `support-blacklist*` — the permission key and server gate are keyed on them, so only the LABEL changed. |

**Customer Profile — how identity is resolved (get this wrong and the page lies):**
- Keyed on the **last 10 digits of the phone**. Numbers are stored as +91 / 0-prefixed / bare, so any other key silently splits one person into several.
- Sourced from the Shopify **`orders`** mirror, **NOT `b2c_order_easycom`**. EasyEcom writes the placeholder **9999999999** when it has no phone — **4,364 orders across 880 different emails** share that one number, so keying on EasyEcom's phone would fuse 880 strangers into a single profile. Shopify's copy has a phone on 98.4% of orders and zero placeholders. `PLACEHOLDER_PHONES` guards the rest.
- ⚠️ **`orders.name` has a leading `#`, `shipment_journey_ecom.order_name` does not** — joining without stripping it returns **0 rows** (verified: 17,575 with the strip vs 0 without).
- Line items come from **`enriched_orders_ecom.line_items`** (full Shopify payload: sku, title, price, qty). EasyEcom's `line_items` has the sku but an empty name and price 0, so it cannot drive Top Products.
- **Identity CLOSURE, not a single lookup.** One person legitimately has several phones and emails, linked through shared orders. Expanding one hop was not enough — starting from different identifiers converged on different answers for the SAME customer (6 vs 18 vs 19 orders), which makes the page untrustworthy. `ordersFor()` therefore keeps pulling on every newly discovered identifier until nothing new appears, so the result is identical whichever identifier you arrive by (verified: 4 entry points → 19 orders / ₹7,835.98 / 2 phones / 3 emails). Bounded by `MAX_ROUNDS` (6) and `MAX_MERGE` (400 orders): if the expansion balloons past the cap it is abandoned and the last trustworthy set kept — that is the signature of a shared/dummy address, and one profile wrongly showing 880 strangers is far worse than a slightly short history.
- **Names.** The Shopify `orders` mirror has **no customer-name column at all**, and EasyEcom's `customer_name` is frequently a placeholder — real orders carry literally `"DUMMY"`. A `JUNK_NAME` filter drops dummy/test/n-a/none/guest/dots so junk never renders as a person, and the display name comes from the **Shopify customer record** (`displayName`), which the store-credit lookup already returns. The header lists EVERY merged phone/email with the searched one in bold — showing only the newest made the header contradict what had just been searched.
- `rto_rate` is computed over **settled** orders (delivered + RTO) only — including in-flight parcels would understate a good customer.

**Store credit (Shopify).** Real money, written live via the GraphQL Admin API 2025-01 — `storeCreditAccountCredit` / `storeCreditAccountDebit`. Scopes were already granted (`read_store_credit_accounts`, `read/write_store_credit_account_transactions`); no re-install was needed. ⚠️ **The token env var is `SHOPIFY_TOKEN`**, not `SHOPIFY_ACCESS_TOKEN`. ⚠️ **A store-credit account does not exist until the first credit** — "no account" is the normal empty state, and a *debit* needs the ACCOUNT id (not the customer id), so deducting from a customer who has never been credited is correctly refused. **No amount cap, by design** — the controls are the separate **`support-store-credit`** permission and a **mandatory reason**. Every movement is mirrored into **`store_credit_log_ecom`** because Shopify records the money but not which of our staff moved it or why; Shopify stays the source of truth for the balance. The tab also shows Shopify's own transactions, which is how checkout **redemptions** appear (they never pass through us).

**Blacklist — now by ANY identifier.** `blocked_numbers_ecom` gained `email`, `shopify_customer_id`, `customer_name`, and **`phone` is now nullable**, so an email-only block is legitimate; a CHECK constraint still requires at least one identifier. Partial unique indexes keep one ACTIVE block per phone / per email / per customer id while released rows stay as history. Matching is normalised (last-10 phone, lower-cased email).
| Voice Agent (BETA) | `support-voice` | **Sarvam-AI voice-calling tool** (STT saaras:v3 + LLM sarvam-30b + TTS bulbul:v3) for COD-confirm / RTO-recovery / feedback / upsell / win-back calls in 10 Indian languages. Self-contained **`app/static/voice-agent.html`** embedded in an **iframe** (`allow="microphone"`; keeps its own green theme). Sarvam API key entered at runtime (NOT committed). Live order/AWB lookup from the operator's Supabase (`orders`+`shipment_journey_ecom`); logs + training examples saved to Supabase tables `agent_call_logs` / `agent_training_examples`. That one static file gets a **scoped relaxed CSP** (`CSP_VOICE` in server.js — connect to api.sarvam.ai + *.supabase.co, media data:/blob:); rest of app keeps strict CSP. Testing phase |

Backend: `app/api/support_console.js` reads the `order_buckets` **view** + `order_notes`, `call_logs`, `escalation_contacts`, `undelivered_tracking`, `msg91_messages`, `tracking_run_lock`, `profiles`. Portal agents are bridged to Supabase auth via shadow users (`auth.admin.createUser`) because `call_logs.agent_id`/`profiles.user_id` FK to `auth.users`.

### Influencer Marketing (port of the standalone Influencer CRM — added 2026-07-17)

- **Video metrics refresh themselves weekly — Friday 11:00 PM IST cron, orchestrated from NODE (2026-08-25).** New cron `InfVideos (0 23 * * 5)` in server.js calls `refreshVideoMetrics()` (influencer_crm.js), which queries last-30-days videos (created_at OR live_date; pages past the 1000-row cap) and dispatches `fetch-reel-metrics` (Apify → views/likes/comments/`metrics_fetched_at` into influencer_videos) per video ~1.5s apart with 429/503 retries. ⚠⚠ **WHY NODE AND NOT THE EDGE FN — the first run exposed that the old `refresh-recent-video-metrics` orchestrator was dispatching ZERO:** its edge→edge calls used the runtime-injected `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` and every dispatch 401’d at the gateway (“Refresh: dispatched 0/32” in its own logs), while this server’s legacy service JWT gets 202 — meaning the panel’s “Refresh Last N Days” button had been silently broken too. Both the cron AND `POST /inf/refresh-videos` now share the Node dispatcher (same scope options: recent-N-days / influencerId / all); the edge orchestrator is retired from the call path (left deployed, unused). Same fragility class as the recurring Lovable-republish auth wipes — edge→edge chaining is the link that breaks. First verified run: 32 videos dispatched from Node. ⚠ Standing Apify caveat: over the monthly limit the Instagram actor silently AI-estimates — numbers keep arriving but stop being real.
| Dashboard | Key | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Influencer Dashboard | `inf-dashboard` | Pipeline KPI tiles (total/partnered/in-discussion/reached-out/lists, clickable) + recent-activity feed + quick actions |
| Discover | `inf-discover` | AI-vet any @handle (`analyze-influencer` edge fn: Apify profile+reels scrape → Gemini via Lovable AI Gateway; AI-only fallback) — result card with recommendation/pros/cons/brand-fit/conversion-potential, **Add to CRM** + add-to-list; analysis history (from `analysis_queue`); "Process pending queue" drains brand-scan discoveries |
| Influencers | `inf-influencers` | Full CRM table (1,200+ creators): search, status/niche/follower-bucket (nano<10K/micro/macro/mega>1M)/contact filters, sortable headers, chunked render; multi-select **bulk actions** (status change, add to list); CSV export; **Refresh last 10 days** bulk reel-metric re-scrape with live progress bar (polls `metrics_fetched_at`); add-influencer modal; row → full **detail modal**: profile sidebar (contact/address/deal terms/bio/notes, edit modal), status select, **🗂 Lists** dropdown (map to / unmap from campaign lists via checkboxes + create-and-map a new list inline; updates the count + "Lists:" line live), always-visible **📝 Note** button (quick-note popup → activity timeline, no need to open the Activity tab), a **📌 Latest note** card shown above the tabs (most recent `note` activity, always visible without opening the Activity tab), 💬 DM (ig.me + auto reached_out), Videos tab (cards with views/likes/comments/shares, payment-status select, GST, fetch-metrics with poll, edit/delete, **Send product** → creates a **real prepaid Shopify order** directly (no Shopify redirect) via `create-influencer-order` edge fn (GraphQL **draftOrderCreate → draftOrderComplete(paymentPending:false)** — the draft path reliably persists the shipping address + recipient name; a direct REST order-create silently dropped the address): order marked `paid` (prepaid, no COD), **99% discount** so value stays nominal-but-non-zero (avoids EasyEcom/RapidShyp ₹0 issues), tagged `Influencer, influencer-shipment`, recipient **name suffixed `(Influencer)`** so it prints on the courier label + EasyEcom invoice (tags and the address `company`/`INFLUENCER @handle` line do NOT print on those — verified), custom attribute Order Type=Influencer Shipment; a create-confirmation prompt guards the click; address prefill+saveback, **🚚 Track order** button → in-app courier-status modal (status badge + AWB/courier/ETA/destination + milestone timeline, read from `orders`+`shipment_journey_ecom` via `/inf/order-tracking`, no Shopify admin redirect). **Orders survive video deletion (2026-07-29):** deleting a video that has an order preserves the order ref on **`influencers.orphan_orders`** (jsonb: order_id/name/url/at), shown as an **"Orders from removed videos"** list under the Videos tab — each still trackable via `/inf/order-tracking?orderId=` (was orphaned in Shopify when the video was hard-deleted). **Edge fn `create-influencer-order` v73 also attaches a CUSTOMER** to each order (`customerCreate` by name+phone; reuse existing by phone/email; link via `draftInput.customerId`; E.164 `+91` phone) — was creating orders with no customer; needs the Shopify token to carry `write_customers`+`read_customers`. The tracking modal's timeline marks each milestone **done by STATUS, not just a timestamp** — a Delivered/RTO order shows the final step completed even when the journey is missing `delivered_at`/`rto_at` (fixed the badge-says-Delivered-but-timeline-shows-OFD discrepancy). **professional printable invoice** — **FROM the influencer → BILL TO Shifupro Technologies Pvt Ltd** (Shop No. 19, AIPL Boulevard, Sector 70A, Gurgaon, Haryana - 122101, GSTIN 06ABOCS1954R1ZG); invoice no `INV-YYYYMMDD-{influencerId}-{videoId}`; accent header, itemized Description/Ad-Code/Qty/Rate/Amount table, subtotal/GST/total card, amount-in-words, influencer signatory, "Thank you for the partnership" footer; optional 18% GST. **Products for this collab** multi-select (searchable dropdown + chips, `infMountProductPicker`) on both the video add/edit modal and the influencer edit form → persists `influencer_videos.product_ids` / `influencers.product_ids`; the Send-product picker preselects those products. **Per-product QTY on the collab pickers too (2026-08-01):** each row in the dropdown has a qty box (1–100) and the chip shows **`×N`** when N>1. Stored in a NEW `product_qty` **jsonb** column (`{productId: qty}`) on **both** `influencers` and `influencer_videos` — deliberately SEPARATE from `product_ids` (text[]) so every existing reader/writer of `product_ids` is untouched, and a product absent from the map simply means qty 1. Server-side `sanitizeQtyMap()` clamps 1–100 and drops non-object/array/≤0/unparseable entries (the columns are `NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'`, and the sanitizer never returns null). `getQty()` returns quantities only for STILL-SELECTED products (unticking a row drops its qty) and omits qty-1 entries to keep the map small. **The Send-product modal prefills these quantities** from the same level it takes the products from — video-level overrides influencer-level — so a saved default collab of "2× Brightening Drops" arrives pre-filled at the send step. **Both the collab picker AND the Send-product list show SOLO + COMBO products** (`infProductGroups` — drops only junk: ₹0 "freebie" dupes [🎁 / "freebie" tag] and no-SKU test items). Representative variant: combo → its bundle "Combo Pack" variant; solo → base single unit (explicit "Pack of 1"/"Default Title" else cheapest). **This EXACT representative variant is now shipped (2026-07-29 fix):** `infProductGroups` exposes `variantId` and Send-product sends `productVariantIds` → `/inf/send-product` forwards them to the edge fn (only when they 1:1-cover the picked products, else falls back to `productIds`). Previously the edge fn re-resolved each product to its **first in-stock variant**, shipping the wrong pack size (chose BDR1 → shipped BDR4; same for combos). No edge-fn redeploy — it already accepts `productVariantIds`. **Per-product QUANTITY (2026-08-01):** each row in the Send-product picker has a **qty box** (1–100, defaults 1, enabled only while the row is ticked; turns amber if qty exceeds the stock the row shows, but never blocks — stock can be stale). The qty input sits **outside** the row's `<label>` on purpose — inside it, a click is forwarded to the checkbox and would untick the row. Quantities flow frontend → `/inf/send-product` `{quantities:[…]}` (index-aligned with `productIds`, clamped 1–100 and padded server-side so a short array can't mis-align line items) → edge fn **v74** `qtyAt(i)` → `lineItems[].quantity`. **Alignment gotcha handled:** the edge fn's `productIds` path SKIPS products it can't resolve, so the qty travels WITH each resolved variant (`resolvedItems:[{id,qty}]`) rather than being re-indexed afterwards — otherwise a skipped product would shift every later item's quantity. Fully backward compatible: no `quantities` → every line qty 1 (the old behaviour). The activity log and the create-confirmation both show total units when qty > 1. Each row shows **SKU + actual MRP** (`compare_at_price`) and a **COMBO** tag for bundles; `/inf/products` returns compare_at_price/product_type/tags + a `names` map (all statuses) so archived/saved product_ids still resolve to a title. Payment-status dropdowns use the project-standard `.csel` (ecEnhanceSelect), not the native OS select — Shifupro Technologies, optional 18% GST), Activity tab (timeline, notes highlighted, add note) |
| Lists & Campaigns | `inf-lists` | Campaign lists: create/delete, member counts; auto-detected date range from list name (month or festival + year, e.g. "Diwali 2026" → Oct-Nov); detail: financial rollups (views-in-range, quoted, final, GST, spend, blended CPM) per member + totals, add/remove members, per-member **payment/due date** column — ⚠️ **that badge is the LATEST deliverable's OWN status, not an all-videos roll-up (fixed 2026-08-04)**: an influencer paid for January and unpaid for July is UNPAID, whereas the old roll-up called that *partial*, which both hid the outstanding payment and collided with `partial`, a real per-video status. Recency = live_date → expected_date → created_at, id as tiebreak; the Paid/Due date underneath is read from that same video so the two can never disagree. (The old code also read `all[0]` after a `.find()` had matched a *different* video, and the query has no `.order()`, so the fallback status was effectively arbitrary.) Product/Ad Run stay "any deliverable" — they are one-off milestones, not per-video money. **Email Sent became a real action on 2026-08-04** (it was previously inherited-but-dead UI: the flag existed, 188 rows carried it, and NOTHING in Ecom Central could write it — every tick came from the old standalone Lovable CRM, which stopped marking on 2026-07-24). The cell is now three-state: **✓** once emailed (clickable → the email thread and any replies) · **✉ Send Email** when the influencer is `partnered` and has never been emailed · **—** otherwise. The same control sits in the **influencer detail panel** header. Both surfaces re-derive the rule locally via `inflCanEmail()` on every status change and stay in sync through `inflSyncMember()`, so the button appears/disappears in the same beat as the dropdown — the list used to re-render only when a status *filter* was active, leaving a stale cell until the next page load. ⚠️ Render the chip as a `<button>`, not a `<span>`: `.filter-btn` sets `height:38px` with no vertical centring, so a span's label sits at the top of the box while every real button beside it is centred. Both rules are enforced SERVER-side in `POST /inf/email/send` as well as in the UI, so a stale tab cannot double-send or email a non-partnered profile. "Already emailed" = `emailedIds()` = the new send log **∪** the legacy `influencer_videos.email_sent` flag, so historical ticks survive; once true the button never returns, whatever the status later becomes. **⬇ Excel** (CSV) export of the filtered member table (`inflListExportCsv`) |
| Video Calendar | `inf-calendar` | Month grid of deliverables — green live (`influencer_videos.live_date`) / amber expected / red overdue badges per day, click → influencer detail. Expected & overdue markers come from `influencers.next_video_expected_date` (merged in by `/inf/calendar`; `influencer_videos` rarely carries a forward `expected_date`). A next-expected marker is **suppressed once the influencer has a live video on/after that date** (delivered → not overdue, and no green+red duplicate on the same day) — 2026-07-23 |
| Brand Mentions | `inf-mentions` | Scan a brand @handle for tagging creators (`scan-brand-mentions` → Apify tagged-post scraper; auto-polls `check-brand-scan` every 8s); new handles auto-queued into `analysis_queue` for AI vetting; scan history with found/queued/skipped + queued handles |

Backend: `app/api/influencer_crm.js` (all routes `/api/inf/*`) reads/writes `influencers`, `influencer_videos`, `influencer_lists`, `influencer_list_members`, `influencer_activities`, `analysis_queue`, `brand_mention_scans`, `shopify_products` — the SAME tables the standalone app used (1,209 influencers, 224 videos live at port time) — and invokes the already-deployed edge functions (`analyze-influencer`, `fetch-reel-metrics`, `refresh-recent-video-metrics`, `scan-brand-mentions`, `check-brand-scan`, `create-influencer-order`, `process-analysis-queue`) with the service-role key (passes their `verify_jwt`). Activity logging (status_change/video_added/payment/product_sent/note) attributes the portal user by **real name** (`actorName(req)` = JWT `name` claim, not email; legacy email-authored rows were backfilled to names). Influencer deletes remove children first (no FK cascade). The standalone app's own Users page was NOT ported — portal RBAC (`inf-*` keys) governs access instead. NOTE: actual schema differs from the old app's doc — `follower_count` (not `followers`), `profile_image_url` (not `profile_pic_url`), flat variant-level `shopify_products` rows.

**Two companies open at once (two financial years).** `TALLY_COMPANY` is only a **fallback** for requests that don't name a company — every screen names one explicitly, so both years stay usable even when it points at one. Manual entry has a **Company selector** (shown only when more than one exists) which scopes the ledger picker: loading ledgers unscoped returns both charts of accounts mixed together, letting you pick a ledger from the *other* year that the server then rejects after you've typed the whole voucher. Changing the company **clears the entry rows** — they hold ledger names that may not exist in the newly chosen books. A voucher's XML carries its own `<SVCURRENTCOMPANY>`, and the bridge agent posts that XML **unchanged**, so an FY 2025-26 voucher posted from live lands in FY 2025-26 regardless of what `TALLY_COMPANY` says. The agent mirrors masters for **every open company**, not just the first — otherwise the other year's chart of accounts freezes at whatever was last synced. Setting `TALLY_COMPANY` on the *agent* pins it to one company deliberately; leaving it blank follows whatever Tally has open.

#### Collaboration outreach email (added 2026-08-04)

Draft → optional AI polish → send → ✓, from the **Lists & Campaigns** member table.

- **Subject is fixed brand copy** — `Collaboration opportunity with theelement.skin`. Set server-side in `OUTREACH_SUBJECT` and NOT taken from the client; the field is read-only in the modal and the AI step is explicitly told not to reword it.
- **Body** is the house pitch with variables filled from the saved profile: greeting name (first name only — a trailing `(handle)` parenthetical and emoji are stripped, an honorific keeps the name after it), the sender's own display name, and the fee. **Fee preference: the latest deliverable's `final_price` → `quoted_price`, then the influencer's.** ⚠️ **An unknown fee renders a visible `₹______` blank and is NEVER invented** — the modal warns, and sending with the blank still present asks for a second confirmation.
- **AI polish** (`POST /inf/email/polish`) uses the shared `ai.js` client. ⚠️ **`maxTokens` must comfortably exceed the whole email** — at 1,400 Gemini returned a reply cut off MID-STRING, so the JSON never closed and every polish silently failed; it is 3,000. A **sanity gate** then rejects the rewrite (keeping the user's draft, with the reason shown inline) if it lost the sign-off, lost the `Commercials:` line, or came back under 60% of the original length — a truncated or gutted rewrite must never reach a real influencer. AI is an assist, never a gate: unconfigured or failing simply keeps the draft.
- **Endpoints:** `GET /inf/email/draft` · `POST /inf/email/polish` · `POST /inf/email/send` · `GET /inf/email/log`. All under the existing `/inf/*` permission gate.
- **Thread view + reply tracking (added 2026-08-04).** The **✓ Email sent** control (campaign-list cell and detail-panel chip) is clickable and opens the thread: what we sent plus every reply. `GET /inf/email/thread?influencerId=&refresh=1` — `refresh=1` runs an IMAP poll first, so opening the panel shows a reply that landed seconds ago; `POST /inf/email/poll` is the manual/cron trigger. Replies are stored in the same table with `kind='reply'` + `parent_id`.
  - ⚠️ **Polls the SENDER'S OWN mailbox, not the shared reports one** — outreach leaves from the portal user's address, so the reply lands in their inbox. `pollInfluencerReplies()` groups the sent rows by `from_email` and connects per mailbox with the credentials in `app_user_smtp_ecom` (IMAP host = the SMTP host with `smtp.` → `imap.`, same app password).
  - ⚠️⚠️ **NEVER match a reply by subject.** The outreach subject is fixed brand copy, so every influencer's reply normalises to the *same* thread subject — the first live run of a subject fallback attached **17 unrelated people's replies to one influencer's thread**. Matching is exact keys only: the `In-Reply-To` chain (including reply-to-a-reply, whose Message-IDs are mapped back to their parent) **or** the sender's address equalling the `to_email` we actually wrote to. This is the opposite of the escalation poller, where subject matching IS safe because each escalation has its own subject.
  - Same imapflow trap as the escalation poller: envelopes are collected in one pass and bodies downloaded in a second, because calling `client.download()` inside an active `client.fetch()` iterator deadlocks. Quoted history is trimmed off each reply.
- **Table `influencer_emails_ecom`** — deliberately a LOG, not a boolean: it records to/from/subject/body/`ai_polished`/`message_id`/`sent_by`/`sent_at`, and it works for an influencer with no video row, which a flag on `influencer_videos` cannot. The legacy per-video flag is still written in step so anything reading it agrees.

#### Pincode → City/State autofill (added 2026-08-04)

Type a 6-digit pincode in an address form and City/State fill themselves, **both still editable**. Backend `app/api/pincode.js` → `GET /api/pincode/:pin` (any logged-in user; deliberately not behind a dashboard permission, since every address form needs it). Source is **India Post’s free public API** (api.postalpincode.in — no key, no quota); answers are cached in `pincode_geo_ecom` so a pincode costs one upstream call ever (~400ms first time, ~50ms after). The call is made SERVER-side, which also keeps it clear of the browser CSP.

⚠️ **Choosing the city is the fiddly part — no single API field is right on its own:** District gives "Central Delhi" for 110001 and "Kamrup" for 781001; Block is "NA" (122018), "Gmc" (781001) or "Bangalore North" (560001). The rule is **Division with the postal suffixes stripped** (North/South/East/West/Central/City/GPO/HQ/H.O) → Block → District, which returns the expected city for all 18 pincodes tested across 18 states. Rejected api.zippopotam.us: it 404s on common pincodes (122018) and returns a locality ("Janpath" for 110001) rather than a city, so a wrong-looking autofill would be worse than none.

Frontend helper `ecPincodeAutofill(pinEl, cityEl, stateEl)` (app.js), wired into the two forms that take a real address: **Edit profile** (`infe-*`) and **Send product** (`infsp-*`). It debounces 300ms, fires on input/blur/paste, shows an inline result hint, and **never silently overwrites a value the user typed** — though changing the PINCODE clears that protection, since a new pincode invalidates the old city/state. Failure is quiet and non-blocking: the hint says so and the fields are left alone.

#### Per-user sending mailboxes (added 2026-08-04)

Outreach leaves from the **logged-in user's own address**, not the shared brand mailbox — an influencer's reply has to reach the person who wrote to them. Table **`app_user_smtp_ecom`** (one row per portal user, keyed on the lowercased login email; `smtp_password_enc` is AES-256-GCM, same scheme as `app_email_settings`, and the plaintext is never returned to a client). `sendMailAs(userEmail, opts)` in `email_settings.js` resolves it; `sendMail()` is unchanged and still sends the automated reports from the brand mailbox.

**WHO MAY SEND (`outreachSender()`) — two ways in, nothing else:** (1) a user with their **own** mapped mailbox sends **as themselves**, so the reply reaches them; (2) a **master/admin** with no personal mailbox sends from the **shared brand mailbox**. Everyone else is refused. The check runs **first in the handler, before payload validation and before the already-sent/partnered state checks**, so a permission failure is never masked by a 400 or a 409. ⚠️ **READING is not gated this way** — any user with influencer access can open a thread and read the replies; only SENDING is restricted.

⚠️ **No fallback to the brand mailbox for a NON-admin.** Sending "from" someone with no credentials either fails SPF/DMARC at the provider or quietly misattributes the mail, so an unmapped sender is refused with the exact fix named. The compose modal resolves the mailbox up front and disables Send with that message, rather than letting someone write an email and fail at the last step. A mapping that exists but has no password, or is marked inactive, counts as unmapped.

Admin UI: **Settings → Email & Reports → Sending mailboxes** — every portal user is listed mapped or not, with state chips (Ready / No password / Disabled / Not mapped), map/edit, per-user **Test** (sends that user a mail from their own address), and remove. Blank password on an edit keeps the stored one. Gmail/Workspace need an **app password**. Endpoints `GET|POST /api/admin/user-mailboxes`, `DELETE /api/admin/user-mailboxes/:email`, `POST /api/admin/user-mailboxes/test` — all admin-only.

### Finance (Tally Prime data entry — added 2026-07-30)
| Dashboard | Key | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Data Entry | `finance-entry` | Compose an accounting voucher and post it to Tally. Voucher-type tabs (**Payment / Receipt / Contra / Journal** — Sales/Purchase/Credit Note/Debit Note pending the GST-split helper), ledger `<select>` grouped by Tally group and populated **only** from the mirrored chart of accounts, Dr/Cr grid with a live **debit / credit / difference** panel that keeps Save disabled until the two sides agree **to the paisa** (compared in integer paise — float rupees fail on 0.1+0.2), bill-reference field that appears only on bill-wise ledgers (without it a Payment/Receipt lands "on account" instead of settling the invoice), two-row amount mirroring, auto-inferred `PARTYLEDGERNAME` (first row under Sundry Creditors/Debtors — not typed twice), **Preview XML**, and a bridge/Tally connection chip |
| Tally Books | `finance-books` | **Read-only view of what is already in Tally** (live pull, 60s server-side cache; the endpoints only ever issue Export/Collection so they cannot alter anything). Two tabs: **Trial Balance** — every ledger's opening + closing balance split into Dr/Cr, group rollup (click a group to filter), search, sort, and a Dr=Cr balance indicator; **Day Book** — vouchers already entered over a date range (defaults to the current Indian FY, 1 Apr → today IST), type chips with per-type counts and totals, free-text search, row → drill-down modal showing the voucher's own ledger lines with Dr/Cr totals. `OPT`/`CANC` badges mark optional and cancelled vouchers. Needs `TALLY_MODE=direct`; in bridge mode it returns an explicit 501 explaining that the agent would have to upload the books (not built) rather than showing an empty statement |
**Bank statement import** (Data Entry -> *Bank statement* tab). Upload an `.xlsx`/`.csv`; the header row is found by looking for the columns rather than a fixed offset.
