Every line gets a suggested ledger **with its reasoning and a confidence score**, from `tally_bank_match.js` — measured **98.8% correct with 0 wrong ledgers** leave-one-out against this company's own 161 bank vouchers. Ranked: a learned rule > the same narration seen before > exact party name > fuzzy name > distinctive wording. It returns **nothing** rather than guess, because a wrong ledger costs more than a blank one.

Safeguards: the bank's own **running balance is re-derived for every row** (a free proof the parse missed nothing); each line is hashed on date+amount+narration+ref into `tally_bank_lines_ecom` with a UNIQUE index, so **re-uploading an overlapping statement cannot double-book**; rows dated before the company's `BooksFrom` are refused with an actionable message instead of creating drafts Tally would reject; and the import stays **disabled while any chosen ledger does not exist**. Confirmed rows become ordinary DRAFT vouchers. Every correction is stored in `tally_bank_rules_ecom`, so the same narration is never asked about twice.

**An under-80% match never becomes a draft on its own.** The matcher's confidence is a *lead*, not a decision: below `CONFIDENT = 0.8` the row is shown with its percentage and reasoning but the ledger field is left **blank**, so it counts as unmapped until a person picks one. Accepting it is still one click — the picker carries it at the top as an amber **"Suggested · 72% — VIVE COSMETICS / too unsure to fill in for you"** entry — but it has to *be* a click. Neither that entry nor a "create ledger" entry is ever pre-selected, so Enter can't commit something nobody looked at. Enforced **again server-side** in `/tally/bank/import`, which re-derives the suggestion rather than trusting the browser and refuses any row whose submitted ledger equals a sub-80% suggestion without `confirmed: true` (*"only 72% sure of X — confirm it on screen before it can become a draft"*); a rule enforced only in the page is not enforced at all, since a stale tab or a replayed request walks straight past it. Verified end-to-end against the live route with a deliberately misspelled payee: refused unconfirmed, **claimed no bank line on the way out** (so the statement row stays mappable), and accepted once confirmed. ⚠️ On the FY 2025-26 statement this rule currently changes nothing — **all 994 narrations score either ≥80% or produce no suggestion at all**, so there is no sub-80% row to catch; the guard is for future statements and companies where fuzzy name matches do appear.

**Narration labels — telling apart two kinds of transaction on one running account.** A service partner's ledger carries transactions that mean opposite things but whose bank narrations look identical: with RapidShyp (`Open Source Business Solutions Pvt. Ltd.`), money **out** is a wallet **Recharge Topup** and money **in** is a **COD Remittance**. Direction is the only thing that distinguishes them, so `tally_narration_labels_ecom` keys a label on `(company, ledger, direction)` and the bank import prefixes it: `COD Remittance | NEFT CR-ICIC0SF0002-OPEN SOURCE…`. The label goes at the **front** on purpose — Tally shows a limited amount of narration, and the label is the part worth keeping when the reference number is cut off. Two details that matter: the learned mapping rule is still built from the **original** bank wording (matching on our own label would teach the matcher a pattern that never appears in a real statement), and re-labelling is idempotent, so a re-import or an edit can't stack `COD Remittance | COD Remittance | …`. Managed via `GET/POST /tally/narration-labels` and `DELETE /tally/narration-labels/:id`; POST refuses a ledger that doesn't exist in the company, since the label would otherwise silently never fire. No UI yet — labels are set through the API.

**The pending pile — mapping a statement over several sittings.** A big statement is not mapped in one go, and originally an unmapped line existed only in the browser tab that parsed the file: closing it meant re-uploading the whole statement to get back to "what is still unmapped". Every uploaded line that has not become a voucher is now kept in `tally_bank_pending_ecom` (UNIQUE `line_hash`, same identity as the imported-lines table, so **a line is in exactly one of the two at any moment** — pending or booked). The *Bank statement* tab then opens with **"N row(s) from an earlier upload are still waiting to be mapped → Continue mapping"**, which serves the saved rows through `GET /tally/bank/pending` in the same shape `/parse` returns, so the review screen renders them unchanged. Importing removes the line from the pile; deleting or releasing a voucher **puts it back** (with the deletion reason), so pulling a doubtful entry out of the register returns it to the mapping list instead of it vanishing until the next upload.

Suggestions are **recomputed on every read, never stored**: as ledgers get created and corrections learned, a row that was unmatchable last week can become obvious, and a stored guess would go stale. Rows dated outside the books are kept in the pile too — they become importable the moment `Books beginning from` is widened in Tally, and dropping them would hide the problem. The saved list shows an indigo banner instead of the running-balance verdict, because a partial statement has gaps by design and asserting either way would mislead. `GET /tally/bank/pending/count` backs the badge.

**The ledger picker is hand-drawn, not a `<datalist>`.** The browser renders a datalist popup itself, so it ignores the page completely: on a dark-mode machine it painted a **black panel over the light UI**, sized itself to the longest ledger name, and listed every ledger at once — on a 28-ledger company that covered the whole viewport. None of it is styleable. It is now one shared panel (120 inputs can be on screen; 120 panels would fight for z-index), positioned under the focused field and flipped above it when the row sits low, with arrow-key/Enter/Escape navigation and the group shown under each name. Focusing a row that already has a ledger lists **everything** with that one highlighted, rather than filtering down to the single value you are trying to change. ⚠️ `app/static/tailwind.css` is **pre-compiled and there is no build script**, so only classes already in that file work — `hover:bg-indigo-50`, `hover:bg-slate-50` and `last:border-0` are not in it (this is why the older SKU combobox at `app.js` ~9798 has a dead hover). The picker therefore drives its highlight from JS, which also means the mouse and the arrow keys move the *same* selection instead of showing two.

**Creating a ledger from a NO MATCH row.** On a fresh company most lines have no ledger yet (185 of the 199 pending rows), so the picker offers **`+ Create ledger "NAME"`** rather than dead-ending — and pre-fills the name with the payee read out of the narration, so a party ledger is one click instead of retyping. Choosing it opens the group picker for that single name, because a ledger created without a group is precisely the failure this feature exists to prevent (Tally files it under Suspense). A create entry is **never pre-selected unless it is the only option**, so Enter can't invent a ledger nobody looked at. `newLedgerCandidates()` in `tally_bank_match.js` builds the names — kept **separate from `extractParty()`** on purpose: that one feeds the matcher, where a spare candidate is harmless because it simply fails to match, whereas these are shown to a person and a bad one costs attention. It reads `<gateway ref>/<merchant>` (`K4RHQ54EAR62LNOXD4/PAYUOPENSOURCEBUSINE` → `PAYUOPENSOURCEBUSINE`), offers **both halves** of `TPT-<purpose>-<person>` since the purpose is often not a ledger, drops a candidate that is another with junk bolted on the front, and holds `extractParty`'s catch-all "longest alphabetic run" back as a **last resort** (it reads a NEFT line as `CNRB RAKESH SHARMA NETBANK HDFCN UPTODATE` — good to match against, terrible as a ledger name). Measured on the live pending set: **184 of 185** unmatched rows get a usable name, only 21 need a choice between two. Leave-one-out re-run after the change: **still 98.8%, 0 wrong**.

**⚠️ The group is as important as the name — and `propose` used to lie about it.** A ledger's GROUP decides which statement it lands in. Paying a **Sundry Creditor** that was never invoiced records *no expense at all*: it leaves a debit balance sitting on the balance sheet, so profit is overstated and a phantom receivable appears. The same payment under **SALARY** or **INFLUENCER MARKETING EXPENSES** correctly hits the P&L — and is what the CA's TDS (192 / 194C / 194J) and GST workings are built from. `/tally/ledgers/propose` detected the right precedent group but, when that group did not exist in the target company, **silently substituted `Sundry Creditors` while still printing the reason "sits under INFLUENCER MARKETING EXPENSES in your other company"** — the screen said one thing and did another. Worse, the modal's group `<select>` only listed *existing* groups, so a proposed new group matched no option and the browser selected the first one (**Bank Accounts**). Fixed: the proposal now returns the real group plus `needsGroupCreating` + `groupParent`, the dropdown carries it as *"— will be created"*, and `POST /tally/ledgers` accepts a `groups: [{name, parent}]` list which it creates (via the new `buildGroupCreateXml`, a `<GROUP>` master import) **before** the ledgers, re-syncing the group mirror in between. Found by auditing the first mapped month: **9 ledgers across 28 vouchers** were filed as Sundry Creditors that FY 2026-27 files under SALARY or INFLUENCER MARKETING EXPENSES — the two groups FY 2025-26 was missing.

**Sales & GST from the filed GSTR-1 (FY 2025-26).** A bank statement gives receipts and payments; it cannot give sales, purchases or GST, none of which pass through the bank as separate entries. Without them the marketplace ledgers carry the money received as a credit with no sale behind it (₹72,97,063 at one point) and the P&L shows expenses against **zero revenue**. FY 2025-26 sales were therefore built from the **filed GSTR-1 returns** — the authoritative source, and the one that guarantees books = returns at assessment. The **JSON** download (Returns Dashboard → GSTR-1 → DOWNLOAD → GENERATE FILE) is essential: the PDF gives only table totals, and deriving the rate split from those silently fails once more than one rate is in play. Sep–Mar carried **5% sales alongside 18%** (ayurvedic/medicament HSN 3003/3004 next to cosmetics), growing from ₹6,279 to ₹4,45,899 a month — invisible in the summary, and the reason a pure-18% derivation came out ₹3.2 lakh short in March. `b2cs[].sply_ty` gives INTER/INTRA outright; B2B direction is read from whether the line carries `camt` (intra) or `iamt` (inter). Every month is reconciled against its own filed PDF **before** a voucher is produced; a month that doesn't tie is skipped, not booked. Result: 10 monthly Sales vouchers, Jun–Mar (Apr/May were NIL returns), totalling **₹99,45,790.37 sales + ₹16,00,970.60 GST**, matching the filed returns exactly. ⚠️ **Table 14 (supplies through an ECO) is NOT booked** — it is a *disclosure* of how much of Table 7 went through Amazon/Flipkart so the department can cross-check their TCS, not additional supply. In July it equals 100% of Table 7, so booking both would double that month's sales.

**Where this format differs from the CA's.** FY 2026-27 raises one Sales voucher **per marketplace** (Amazon / Flipkart / Shopify), debits that marketplace's own ledger, and adds a `ROUND OFF` line. That is better in one respect — the sale clears against the receipts already sitting on those ledgers. It cannot be reproduced from GSTR-1, which summarises B2C by state and rate only and never names the marketplace; splitting it would mean inventing an allocation. So FY 2025-26 books one monthly voucher per month to a single `ECOMMERCE RECEIVABLE` (Sundry Debtors), and the marketplace accounts are cleared against it with one year-end journal.

**Opening a fresh set of books** — a new company has only `Cash` and `P&L A/c`. `POST /tally/ledgers/propose` suggests a group for each missing counterparty **by looking at how the same name is filed in your other company**, which carries the knowledge across without copying data between two deliberately separate companies. `POST /tally/ledgers` then creates the confirmed ones via a real Tally master import. This is the safe inverse of the hazard the project guards against: the operator names the ledger and picks its group, rather than Tally inventing it under Suspense.

| Voucher Register | `finance-register` | Audit trail: every voucher with status pills (draft/queued/posting/posted/failed/cancelled), Tally voucher no., filters (status, date range, free text) → detail drawer showing the ledger entries, Tally's own error text, and the **exact request + response XML**; Retry / Cancel. Cancel is a soft status change. **A draft can be re-booked against a different ledger from the drawer** ("Book against" + an editable narration), so correcting one mis-mapped entry doesn't mean going back to the bank-statement screen and re-importing. It reuses the bank tab's ledger picker via `data-pickmode="plain"`, loads the *voucher's own* company's ledgers (tracked in `_fb2.ledgerCompany`, deliberately **not** `_fb2.company` — that one drives the bank tab's dropdown and decides which books an import posts into), and **refuses a ledger Tally doesn't have**, since that is exactly what gets auto-created under Suspense at posting time. It swaps only the line naming the current party: identifying the bank side by a `/BANK|CASH/` guess would rename *every* line the moment the guess failed, putting both sides of the voucher on one ledger. **Sorted oldest transaction first by default** (`sort=date_asc`), with date ↑↓ / amount ↑↓ / recently-entered in the filter bar — the previous `created_at DESC` ordered by *when the row was keyed in*, which is meaningless after a bank import where hundreds of rows share one timestamp and their order is really just the order the spreadsheet was walked. `created_at` remains the tie-breaker so paging is stable when many rows share a date; an unknown `sort` value falls back to the default rather than erroring. Changing the sort **clears the selection on purpose** — the list is capped, so a different order can return a different set of rows, and a tick surviving into a batch the operator can no longer see is how the wrong voucher gets posted. When the cap is hit the register says so (*"Showing the first 1000 only"*) instead of quietly truncating, because the status counts and "select all" then describe only what is listed. A **checkbox column + "Post N to Tally"** bar handles the bulk case (importing a year's statement leaves hundreds of drafts); it shows the selected count and value, warns in red if the selection spans **more than one company**, and posts in chunks of 25, halting on the first failure. Only an admin sees it, and the server enforces that regardless. **Admins additionally get "Delete entry"** — a real delete, so clearing a junk entry never means opening the Supabase SQL editor. It removes only our row and **never touches Tally**: a voucher already in Tally must be removed there (Alt+D), and the dialog says so rather than leaving the two silently out of step. Refused while a voucher is `queued`/`posting`/`awaiting_approval` (the agent could be mid-post, or it belongs to an open batch — reject the batch first). A reason is **required** when the voucher had reached Tally, optional otherwise. Every delete snapshots the whole voucher — ledger lines, who entered it, who removed it, why — into `tally_voucher_deletions_ecom`, shown as a **"Deleted entries"** panel any finance user can read |

**Optional-voucher dry run:** the Data Entry panel has a **Post as Optional** toggle → `<ISOPTIONAL>Yes</ISOPTIONAL>`. Tally records the voucher in the **Day Book** but excludes it from the P&L, balance sheet, ledger balances and GST returns until a human marks it regular inside Tally. This is the correct way to validate the write path **against real books** — the round-trip is genuine, the accounts are untouched. It is not a stored column: the flag lives in `request_xml` (the audit record), and the Register badges it by reading the flag back from there, so a retry stays faithful to the original intent. The confirm dialog changes wording and drops its danger styling when the toggle is on.

**Nightly batch push (23:50 IST) with Teams approval — the normal route to Tally.** Non-admins cannot push at all; their entries reach Tally like this:

```
day        user enters vouchers  →  saved as `draft`   (Tally never contacted)
23:45      cron warms the Tally masters
23:50      cron re-validates every draft, groups the VALID ones into a batch (ref TB-YYYYMMDD-n),
           sets them `awaiting_approval`, posts an approval card to the Teams finance channel.
           Invalid ones stay `draft` with `validation_error` and are listed as **Blocked** — one
           typo never blocks the rest, and fixing it puts the voucher in the next run.
admin      replies **yes** in Teams (or Approve in Finance → Voucher Register) → vouchers → `queued`
+seconds   the bridge agent posts each one and acks Tally's raw reply
watcher    once all are terminal → result card ("Posted 12 of 12" / per-voucher Tally errors)
```

Exception cards cover the paths that would otherwise fail silently: **bridge offline** at 23:50 (batch held, nothing lost), a batch **stalled >1 h**, **every draft blocked**, **expired unapproved** (vouchers return to `draft`), and *approved but `TALLY_POST_ENABLED` is false*. The batch lives in `tally_push_batches_ecom`, **not** a memory variable — unlike the Amazon review approval, it survives a restart. Teams approval is recognised as bare `yes`/`no` (unambiguous in a dedicated channel) or `approve TB-20260730-1`, so a stale card cannot be approved by accident.

⚠️ **Teams gives the listener no role information**, so *membership of the finance channel IS the approval boundary* — only admins should be in it. Dashboard approval is separately gated on `role=admin`.

**Sharing one channel with the Amazon review approval is supported** (the live setup does exactly this — both point at `cron_confirmation`). Two things make it safe: the poll loop maps channel → flows and fetches each channel **once** (fetching twice would be a bug, not just waste — `lastSeen` is keyed by channel id, so the first read advances the watermark and the second sees nothing), and `parseApproval()` requires the command to be the **whole message**. That second rule matters more than it looks: a loose `/^no/` would treat *"no idea what happened there"* as a rejection, which in a channel where people actually talk **will** happen. Routing: an explicit target wins (`tally yes`, `amazon no`, `approve TB-20260730-1`); a bare `yes`/`no` goes to whichever flow is genuinely pending (`hasPendingBatch()` / `hasPendingReview()`); if **both** are pending it actions nothing and replies asking which, quoting the batch ref so the answer is copy-pasteable. Nothing pending → silence.

Optional `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_FINANCE_RESULT` sends result/exception cards to a different channel from the approval request, so routine confirmations need not sit in the admin channel.

**Capability:** posting is a separate right from drafting — `finance-post-tally` (see §6). A user with only `finance-entry` can compose, validate and preview a voucher but the Post button is disabled, and the server refuses `/tally/vouchers/:id/post` regardless of the UI.

Backend: `app/api/tally.js` + `app/api/tally_xml.js`; tables `tally_vouchers_ecom`, `tally_masters_ecom`, `tally_bridge_status_ecom` (§8); integration notes and the mode/kill-switch behaviour in §10.

### System
| Dashboard | Key | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Reports | `reports-view` | Excel/PDF report downloads |
| Amazon Review | `amazon-review` | Review-request eligibility, bulk send, history. Never re-solicits an order that's already `sent` / permanently-failed (4xx) / `ineligible` — cached in `amazon_review_requests` (unique `order_id`) and excluded at report time; `runBulkSend` also re-checks status **at send time** so a stale approval / double-"yes" / cron-overlap can't fire a duplicate Amazon call |
| Serviceability | `serviceability` | Pincode serviceability + EDD lookup |
| Settings | `settings` | Connected accounts + **Email & Reports** card (admin-only: SMTP host/port/password, From, To/CC, RapidShyp claims recipient, **DocPharma To/CC**; test-send). To/CC = RapidShyp report recipients; DocPharma To/CC = DocPharma report recipients (DocPharma CC blank → falls back to CC) |
| Users | *(admin only)* | Approve signups, grant per-dashboard permissions, reset passwords, **edit display name** (shown on the welcome splash), edit mobile |
| User Analytics | *(admin only, under **System** nav)* | Who uses which dashboards, how often & how long: KPIs (active users / page views / logins / est. active time), Feature-usage bars, Activity-by-day chart, per-user table (last active, active days, views, est. time, top dashboard) + per-user drilldown modal (dashboards used + recent activity). Fed by `user_activity_ecom` (logged client-side on every `navigate()` + login) |

### Notable feature mechanics

- **Freight & invoice value in DB:** every final RapidShyp shipment is priced via the `shipment_details` API (`final_freights` → `freight_total/forward/rto`, `shipment_value`, `applied_weight`) — backfilled for ~12k shipments, kept current by a nightly cron.
- **Likely-Fake workflow:** any Delivery-Performance user can flag an order (🚩) → stored in `order_marks_ecom` → insight card computes **marked → delivered conversion %** (delivered after being flagged = proven fake attempt) by joining live journey outcomes. Admins **or users granted `send-escalation-emails`** escalate single orders or all marked orders via **AI-polished critical email** to RapidShyp; sends are logged (`critical_mail_sent`) and badged (✉️).
- **DocPharma limitations (by API design):** DocPharma's `fetch-details` returns **no scan log** and no granular NDR history — only status milestones, `eta`, and a public `tracking_url`. The UI synthesizes a milestone timeline and links out ("Track on DocPharma ↗"). DocPharma journeys derive attempts from `reattempt_count` only.
- **Performance & UI polish (2026-07-29):**
  - **`/get-orders` no longer scans the whole tracking table.** It used to fetch **all** of `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom` (~27.5k rows) on every load just to map `raw_status` onto ~500 visible orders — the dominant ~2s load cost. Now it collects the page's AWBs and fetches only those rows via a chunked, primary-key `.in('awb', …)` lookup (parallel with the Shopify-hold-marks query). Same data, ~2s → a few hundred ms.
  - **Delivery Performance + Ad Set Breakdown parallelized.** `fetchJourneys` (delivery_reports.js) pages via `Promise.all` (page 0 with an exact count, then all remaining pages at once) instead of sequential `.range()` round-trips, and the current/previous compare windows fetch concurrently. Ad Set Breakdown (adset_performance.js) fires the live Meta/Facebook insights call concurrently with the paginated `enriched_orders_ecom` fetch instead of back-to-back.
  - **server.js:** `compression` (gzip) enabled app-wide; `/static` (30d) + `/templates` (7d) served with `maxAge`/etag caching. Requires `npm install` on deploy (new `compression` dep in package.json).
  - **UI:** `accent.css` recolours the old indigo accent → the login/welcome **violet gradient** globally (nothing else changed); `smooth.css` adds a motion/polish layer (popups/loaders/transitions) on the original palette. Orders-dashboard order **details open in a popup modal** (`openOrderModal`) instead of heavy inline expand rows.

---

## 6. Authentication & Authorization

- **Login:** `POST /api/login` — checks the `.env` bootstrap admin first (never lockable), then `app_users_ecom` (scrypt-hashed passwords). Returns a **6-hour** JWT with `{ sub, role, permissions[], name }`.
- **6-hour session + auto-logout:** JWTs expire after 6h (`SESSION_TTL` in `auth.js`). The client reads the token's `exp` and proactively logs the user out the instant it passes (`scheduleAutoLogout()` in app.js), and drops an already-expired token on page load; any API 401 also forces logout. Re-armed on every fresh login and silent token-restore.
- **Sign-out flow:** the manual **Sign Out** button runs `signOut()` — a ~2.5s branded loader (logo), then a **"Signed out successfully" confirmation page** (`#signout-view`, thank-you + "Click here to log in") rather than snapping straight to login. `_clearSession()` also **drops the `#view` hash** so the next sign-in lands on the **home** dashboard (not the page they signed out from). Silent logouts (expiry / 401 / auto-logout) still go straight to login via `logout()`.
- **Activity logging:** `logActivity(event, view)` (app.js, fire-and-forget, `keepalive`, 2s per-view debounce) POSTs `/api/activity` on every `navigate()` and on login → `user_activity_ecom` (email from the token, never the body). Powers the **User Analytics** dashboard.
- **Welcome popup:** after a successful sign-in (password-only or OTP), a ~3.4s **dark-premium greeting card** floats over the dashboard, which stays visible but **blurred + dimmed behind** (a modal, not a full-screen takeover). Layout = time-aware greeting ("Good morning/afternoon/evening", no emoji) + the user's **display name** (gradient-shimmer hero) + "Welcome back to Ecom Central"; glowing logo ring + sparkles (`showWelcomeSplash` / `_wsGreeting` in app.js). When no name is set it shows "Welcome back" as the hero and the email as subtext. Not shown on silent refresh-restore. The name is admin-editable on the Users page (`app_users_ecom.name`); the `.env` bootstrap admin's name comes from `APP_USER_NAME` **or, if that's unset, falls back to the `name` on its own Users-page row** (`adminName()` in auth_routes.js) — so setting the name in the portal is enough, no `.env` change needed.
- **2FA (added 2026-07-16 for admins; switched from MSG91 SMS to EMAIL OTP and extended to ALL accounts 2026-07-17):** when the portal can send mail (Settings → Email / `app_email_settings`, sender `digital@theelement.skin`, falling back to `.env` `EMAIL_*`), `/login` for any user does not return a token — `app/otp_mail.js` generates a 6-digit OTP, stores only its HMAC-SHA256 hash in a 5-minute in-memory record (max 5 wrong attempts, single-use, 25s server-side resend throttle — a duplicate `/login` inside the gap **reuses** the in-flight OTP instead of erroring; a pm2 restart just voids pending OTPs), emails it via the shared `sendMail`, and `/login` returns `{ otp_required, otp_token (5-min pre-auth JWT, stage:'otp2fa', no permissions), email_hint }`. The client then calls `POST /api/login/verify-otp { otp_token, otp }` → real JWT (role/permissions re-fetched fresh from DB, never trusted from the pre-auth token), or `POST /api/login/resend-otp`. Both are public + rate-limited. OTP mails carry **no CC** (`cc: []` — the default report CC from Settings is explicitly excluded), and after expiry each OTP email is **auto-purged from the sender's Sent folder** over IMAP (moved to `[Gmail]/Trash` + expunged; a plain expunge in Sent would only archive on Gmail) — scheduled at send-time +5m15s, plus startup sweeps at +60s/+6.5m to catch purges lost to a restart. If email/SMTP isn't configured, logins fall back to password-only with a server-side warning — never a lockout. **Emergency 2FA off-switch:** blank the SMTP settings (or fix SMTP) — there is no separate 2FA env var. The OTP email shows the Ecom Central logo as a hosted image (`DASHBOARD_URL/static/assets/ecom-logo.png`, not an attachment — no Gmail attachment chip).
- **Signup:** `POST /api/signup` — open signup creates a **pending** account; an admin must approve and grant dashboard permissions. **Mobile (Indian 10-digit) is mandatory** on signup, admin add-user, and editable per-user (`POST /admin/users/:id { mobile }`).
- **Rate limiting:** per-IP (`req.ip`, trust-proxy aware) + per-account (10 attempts / 15 min); bootstrap admin exempt from the account limit.
- **RBAC:**
  - Admin (`role=admin` or permission `*`) → everything.
  - Users hold an array of permission keys — **dashboard-view keys** (see §5) plus **capability keys** that grant an action without full admin. Capability keys (Users page → "Actions" group): **`send-escalation-emails`** — lets a non-admin **send escalation/critical/claims emails** (✉️ Send critical email, ✉️ Escalate all to RapidShyp, Silent-RTO/Late/In-transit claim sends); **`support-cancel-order`** (2026-07-29) — lets a support agent **Cancel a held order** from the Call Queue (cancels on Shopify + refunds any online-paid COD fee); **`delivery-perf-revenue`** (2026-08-12) — lets a non-admin see the **₹ Revenue lens on Delivery Performance** (the `# Orders / ₹ Revenue` toggle, order values on every metric, and the explorer's Value column). Without it the server **skips the order-value lookup entirely and strips every ₹ field from the response** (`canSeeRevenue()` + `stripRevenue()` in `delivery_reports.js`) — so it is withheld, not hidden, and the count view is completely unaffected; **`finance-post-tally`** (2026-07-30) — *superseded:* pushing to Tally now requires **`role=admin`**, not a capability key. The key is retained so existing grants don't error, but `requireAdminPush` in `tally.js` and `requireAdmin` in `tally_batch.js` are the real gates — a non-admin holding the key still gets a 403. A non-admin drafts; the nightly batch is how their entries reach Tally, after an admin approves. Only the admin can grant them. Email **Settings** (SMTP creds) remain admin-only.
  - **Server-side enforcement** (`server.js`): a path-prefix middleware maps API groups → required permission, e.g. `/delivery-performance` → `delivery-perf`, `/silent-rto-claims|late-deliveries|intransit-late` → `claims-sla`, `/order-marks|likely-fake-insight` → `delivery-perf`, `/docpharma` → `docpharma-recon`, `/fba/` → `amazon-fba`, `/ops-control` → `ops-control`. The shared shipment-detail endpoint accepts *either* `delivery-perf` or `claims-sla`. Mount-level `requirePermission` guards `/api/amazon` and `/api/fulfillment-ops`. `/tally/vouchers/:id/post` → `finance-post-tally`, the rest of `/tally/` → `finance-entry`/`finance-register` — **but that rule is written `/^\/tally\/(?!bridge\/)/i`**, because listing `/tally/bridge/` in `PUBLIC_API` only skips the JWT gate: this permission middleware still runs, and a key-authenticated request carries no permissions, so a broad rule would 403 the bridge agent. Any future non-JWT route group needs the same exclusion. **The 6 email-send routes** (`/critical-email/{compose,polish,send}`, `/silent-rto-claims/send`, `/late-deliveries/send`, `/intransit-late/send`) are gated by `requirePermission('send-escalation-emails')` (admins pass via `*`); the claims sends additionally sit behind the `claims-sla` view gate. ⚠️ **`delivery-perf-revenue` is enforced INSIDE the handler, not by a path rule** — `/delivery-performance` is one endpoint serving both audiences, so the route stays gated on `delivery-perf` and the ₹ fields are removed from the payload per-request. Any capability that hides *part* of a response rather than a whole route needs this shape. `/api/admin/*` requires `role=admin`.
- **JWT secret hygiene:** the app refuses to boot with a missing/weak `JWT_SECRET` (<32 chars or known-default).
- ⚠️⚠️ **A ROUTER-LEVEL `use()` WITH NO PATH GATES EVERY ROUTE MOUNTED AFTER IT (shipped and fixed 2026-08-14).** `zone_mapping.js` self-gated with `router.use(tokenRequired, requireAdmin)`. In Express a router's `use()` **without a path runs for every request that reaches the router**, not just that router's own routes — and because it is mounted at bare `/api`, it silently made **admin-only** every route registered after it in server.js: **DocPharma Recon, RapidShyp Recon, DocPharma Invoices / Ledger / Overview / Inventory**, and the Teams bot endpoint. Non-admins holding the correct permission got 403; it shipped in `f2a2dff` and was live for several hours. **It hid because all testing used an admin token**, which sails through `requireAdmin`. The tell was `/api/webhook/zzz-nonexistent` answering **401 instead of 404** — a route that does not exist should never be *authenticating*. Fix: scope the gate, `router.use('/zone-mapping', tokenRequired, requireAdmin)`. **Rule: a self-gating router mounted at bare `/api` MUST pass its path prefix to `use()`.** Verified after the fix: a non-admin with `docpharma-recon` gets 200/404 (auth passed) while `/zone-mapping/*` still 403s.

---

## 7. API Reference

All endpoints require `Authorization: Bearer <JWT>` unless noted. Base path `/api`.

### Auth & Admin
| Method | Path | Auth | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/login` | public (rate-limited) | Issue JWT, or start 2FA (`otp_required` + `otp_token`) — all accounts |
| POST | `/login/verify-otp` | public (rate-limited) | 2FA step 2: pre-auth token + OTP → real JWT |
| POST | `/login/resend-otp` | public (rate-limited) | Re-email the 2FA OTP (new code, 25s throttle) |
| POST | `/forgot-password` `{email}` | public (rate-limited) | Self-service reset step 1 — **verifies the account exists first** (internal tool → clear feedback over enumeration-hardening): `404 No account found` for unknown email, `403` for pending/disabled; only an **active** account gets a 6-digit code emailed (reuses the 2FA OTP infra). Returns `email_unconfigured` if SMTP isn't set up (client shows "contact admin"); the `.env` bootstrap admin is refused (its password lives in config) |
| POST | `/reset-password` `{email,otp,password}` | public (rate-limited) | Self-service reset step 2 — verifies the code, sets the new `password_hash` (min 6). Admin can also reset any user via `/admin/users/:id/password`. Login errors ("Invalid credentials!", pending/disabled, rate-limit) now surface as a persistent inline alert on the login card (not just a toast). **Input caps + password policy on every auth route + input** (login/signup/forgot/reset/admin-reset): email ≤254, password **8**–128 (min bumped from 6; rejected BEFORE scrypt so a huge value can't block the event loop), OTP must match `^\d{6}$`, name ≤80, mobile = Indian 10-digit. A **live password-strength meter** (`pwStrength()` → Too short / Fair / Good / Strong) shows under every new-password field (signup, forgot-reset, admin reset). All six auth POSTs are rate-limited (10/15min per IP+account); OTP = 5 tries / 5-min TTL / 25s resend |
| POST | `/signup` | public (rate-limited) | Create pending account (mobile mandatory) |
| GET | `/me` | user | Current user + permissions + `name` |
| POST | `/activity` `{event,view}` | user | Log a login/dashboard-view (email from token) for User Analytics |
| GET | `/admin/user-analytics?from&to` | admin | Aggregated usage: totals, feature-usage, by-day, per-user table |
| GET | `/admin/user-analytics/user?email&from&to` | admin | One user's dashboards-used + recent activity + totals |
| GET/POST | `/admin/users`, `/admin/users/:id`, `/admin/users/:id/password` | admin | User management (incl. `name`) |
| GET/POST | `/admin/email-settings` | admin | Read/write portal SMTP settings (password write-only, stored encrypted) |
| POST | `/admin/email-settings/test` | admin | Send a test email |

### Delivery Performance & Claims
| Method | Path | Perm | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/delivery-performance?from&to&source&payment&zone&state&order_type&compare` | delivery-perf | Full dashboard payload: kpis, statusBreakdown, tat, zones, fasrTrend, ndrFunnel, rtoByCourier, `byPayment` (Prepaid-vs-COD), shipments[] (with `edd`, `pastPromise`, `marked_fake`, `mail_sent`) |
| GET | `/delivery-performance/shipment/:awb` | delivery-perf / claims-sla / **any support-\*** | One shipment's journey + scan log (DB-cached, live-fallback; DocPharma → synthesized milestones + `dp` info). Support perms added so the **Customer Support live-tracking modal** can use it |

**Customer Support live-tracking modal (`supTrackOpen`/`supAwbLink` in app.js — builds its own `#sup-track-modal` DOM dynamically, appended to `<body>` with an **inline `z-index:95`** — a Tailwind `z-[90]` class isn't in the compiled CSS so it silently did nothing and the popup fell behind the `z-[80]` order-detail modal — no static HTML needed):** in the Customer Support console, clicking an **AWB** opens a modal (like Fulfillment Ops) that fetches `/delivery-performance/shipment/:awb` and renders the Journey (date-log) + Scan log (RapidShyp scans / DocPharma milestones + "Track on DocPharma ↗"). `supAwbLink()` renders a `.sup-awb-track` link; a single **capturing** delegated listener handles them (fires before a parent row's click so `stopPropagation` prevents the row also navigating). The modal is `z-[90]` so it sits ABOVE the `z-[80]` order-detail modal (else it opens hidden behind it). The link renders the **AWB in monospace slate** (subtle — reads as clean data, `no-underline`, only a faint indigo tint on hover; no icon, not a blue hyperlink), with the carrier shown alongside. (`index.html` cache-busts `app.js` via `?v=…` — bump it on frontend changes so browsers don't serve a stale file.) Wired in Customer Support spots: **Call Queue** rows — repeat / undelivered / status-changed — (courier name + the AWB link beneath it in the Courier cell), order-detail "Delivery timeline" AWB, order-**search** results AWB, and the **"ALL ORDERS FROM THIS CUSTOMER"** table's Tracking cell shows the AWB link (backed by `awb_number` added to `CUST_SEL` in `support_console.js`). Scoped to Customer Support only — no other dashboard changed.

| POST | `/late-deliveries/send` | admin | Email the late report to default recipients |

| GET | `/first-ofd-late?from&to&source` | claims-sla | First-OFD-late **RTO** rows (RTO whose first OFD was after promise EDD) + summary; **range filters on rto_at**, not order date |
| POST | `/first-ofd-late/send` `{from,to,source?}` | admin | Email the First-OFD-late RTO report to RapidShyp (one email per platform) |
| POST | `/order-marks` `{order_name, awb, mark_type}` | delivery-perf | **Toggle** a manual mark (default `likely_fake`) |
| GET | `/order-marks?type=` | delivery-perf | List marks |
| GET | `/likely-fake-insight` | delivery-perf | Marked-orders outcome split + delivered-conversion % |
| POST | `/critical-email/compose` `{awbs[], kind?}` | admin | Build an AI-polished escalation draft (template fallback). **Context-aware (2026-07-29):** `classifyEscalation()` picks the narrative from the actual shipment data — **fake** (delivered-after-NDR), **stuck** (no scan/movement), **ndr** (repeated failed attempts), or **general** — instead of always "fake delivery"; a `fake_attempts` hint is honored only if the data shows a fake signal. Returns `kind` + `aiError` (the real reason AI fell back, e.g. "rate-limited", "empty response", "key rejected") so the UI shows WHY. Same surfaced reason on `/critical-email/polish` failures. **Robust AI-reply parsing** (`parseAiEmail`) tolerates ```` ```json ```` fences + literal newlines in the body (was dumping raw JSON into the message). **Order + AWB** are listed **inline in the body for ≤6 shipments** (else "see attached table"); the full order/AWB/status table is always attached on send AND now **previewed in the compose modal** |
| POST | `/critical-email/send` `{subject, body, to?, platform, tableHtml, orders[]}` | admin | Send + log `critical_mail_sent` marks + record the thread for reply tracking. **Courier-aware recipients (2026-07-29):** compose derives `platform` from the shipment **source** (DocPharma rows → DocPharma team, else RapidShyp), and send routes **To = that courier partner, CC = our team** via `recipientsFor(platform)` (was always hardcoded to the single RapidShyp email). A hand-typed recipient overrides To; the platform CC (internal team) always applies |
| POST | `/critical-email/polish` `{subject, body, tone}` | admin | AI-rewrite the (hand-edited) draft in a chosen tone: polite / direct / formal |
| GET | `/escalation-emails?awb=\|order=` | delivery-perf or claims-sla | Mail thread(s): sent escalation + replies with `direction` (outbound/inbound) + AI score/status/suggestion |
| GET | `/escalation-emails/recent` · POST `/escalation-emails/poll` | delivery-perf | Latest replies (order-labeled) · manual inbox poll |
| POST | `/ndr-action` `{awb, action: RE_ATTEMPT\|RETURN, phone?, address1?, address2?, order?}` | ops-control or delivery-perf | Fire RapidShyp's NDR action (auto-retries the alternate action spelling on rejection); logs to `ops_actions_ecom` |
| POST | `/easyecom/hold-order` `{orderName, reason}` · `/easyecom/unhold-order` | user | Hold/release in EasyEcom (keyed by invoice_id); writes/clears the `ee_hold` live-state mark; "already held/unheld" counts as success |
| GET/POST | `/easyecom/warehouses` · `/easyecom/change-warehouse` `{orderName, targetCid}` · `/easyecom/session` (admin GET/POST) · `/easyecom/session/check` (live health) | user (session admin) | **Warehouse routing via panel-session cookie replay** (added 2026-07-17). `/Orders/UpdateVendor` refuses the API JWT (needs the interactive panel session) but accepts the browser `laravel_session`+`PHPSESSID` cookie replayed from our server (verified live: TE25-36487 moved DP Bangalore→Shifupro; success body = literal `0`). Admin pastes the EasyEcom Cookie (DevTools → Cookie header) in the "🏬 Change warehouse" modal → stored **AES-GCM encrypted** (`crypto_util`) in `easyecom_panel_session` → replayed against `app.easyecom.io/Orders/UpdateVendor` with `invoice_id&vendor_c_id=<target>&c_id=<company>`. `session/check` reports a **healthy / stale / not-set** badge in the modal from the **freshness of the synced cookie** (`easyecom_panel_session.updated_at` ≤ `SESSION_FRESH_MIN` = 45 min), NOT a live ping — the VPS can't reach EasyEcom (WAF blocks its IP), so any server-side `UpdateVendor` probe returns a WAF/redirect HTML page that the old code misread as "expired" even seconds after a fresh re-paste (fixed 2026-07-19). The browser extension re-pushes a logged-in cookie every ~20 min, so a recently-synced cookie IS a live session; stale = extension offline. **Constraint:** only before a shipment/AWB is assigned. Warehouses `EE_WAREHOUSES`: Shifupro 257282, DP Bangalore 271096 (sub), Amazon FBA 288922/288927. **Auto-route:** detection (cron :47) reports + records rejections; the actual DP→Shifupro move runs through the **browser-extension executor** (`/ee-routes` serves `dp_rejected_handled_ecom` rows where `routed_at IS NULL`; genuinely-already-on-Shifupro rows are closed out in place). ⚠️ **Current warehouse = `raw_data.assigned_warehouse_id`, NOT `warehouse_id`** (fixed 2026-07-19). `warehouse_id` holds the parent SELLER company (Shifupro **257282**) for every order, so `resolveInvoiceAndWarehouse()` reading it made DP-Bangalore orders (`assigned_warehouse_id`=**271096**) look "already on Shifupro" → the route was silently marked done WITHOUT moving the order (it stayed on DP Bangalore; e.g. TE25-36787/36810). Now reads `assigned_warehouse_id`; null when the sync snapshot omits it → currentCid left null so the real UpdateVendor call decides, never a false skip. The old server-side pass `autoRouteHandledRejections()` (cron :56) still runs but is WAF-dead from the VPS (redundant with the extension). **Freshness-watch cron** (`*/20`) warns in the server log only if the synced cookie goes stale (extension offline) — the VPS cannot itself keep the session warm. All logged to `api_logs_ecom` (`easyecom_change_warehouse` / `easyecom_autoroute_warehouse`). (Old JWT/location-token path — `EASYECOM_WH2_KEY`, `mintTokenForLocation` — unused; kept for reference.) |
| GET/POST | `/support/*` (summary, queue, orders, order/:id, notes, calls, contacts, team, refresh-tracking) | any `support-*` perm (writes: notes/calls any agent; contacts & team admin) | Customer Support console API (see §5) |
| GET/POST | `/support/blacklist` · `/support/blacklist` (block `{phone,reason}`) · `/support/blacklist/:id/unblock` | any `support-*` perm | Blacklisted phone numbers — list (active + `history`) / block / soft-unblock. Table `blocked_numbers_ecom` |
| GET/POST/DELETE | `/inf/*` (summary, influencers CRUD+bulk, influencer/:id, videos CRUD + :id/metrics, refresh-videos + refresh-progress, activities, lists CRUD + members, calendar, discover + history + process-queue, mentions + scan + check, products, send-product, order-tracking) | any `inf-*` perm | Influencer Marketing CRM API (see §5) |
| POST/GET | `/zone-mapping/upload` `{filename, contentBase64, dryRun, replace}` · `/zone-mapping/summary` · `/zone-mapping/lookup?pincode=` · `/zone-mapping/remap` | **admin only** (router self-gates with `tokenRequired`+`requireAdmin`) | Admin → Zone Mapping. Upload KwikShip's serviceability sheet (.xlsx/.csv, base64 in JSON like the Tally bank import — no multer; `/upload` gets its own **32mb** body cap in server.js). Needs `Pin_code_From`/`Pin_code_To`/`Zone` headers (`Pin_code_To` = the destination; `Pin_code_From` = the pickup pincode, reported back so a merged/wrong-origin export is obvious); every other header that matches a table column is stored as-is and unmatched ones are REPORTED, not silently dropped. `dryRun:true` parses and reports only; committing also re-derives `zone` on all KwikShip shipments via `remap_kwikship_zones()`. `/remap` re-runs that pass on its own — the path to use after importing the CSV straight into Supabase. `/lookup` returns the zone **and the band that decided it**. Table `zone_mapping_with_pincode` |
| GET | `/ee-hold-marks` | any authenticated user | Live **hold marks — EasyEcom AND Shopify** (`order_marks_ecom` types `ee_hold` + `shopify_hold`, plus EasyEcom synced "On Hold"), each tagged `hold_type` (`ee`/`shopify`/`both`) — powers the **HOLD chip on EVERY dashboard** so a held order is never shown as actionable anywhere (2026-07-20 extended to Shopify holds): Ops Control (NDR queue/exceptions/prepaid-risk), Delivery Performance, Claims/SLA (all 3 tabs), Customer Support (queue/orders/order modal + **customer-history table**) fetch it at load (60s client cache, busted on hold/unhold) and decorate rows via `eeHoldChip()` (⏸ EE HOLD / 🔒 SHOPIFY HOLD / 🔒 HOLD (SHOP+EE)); the Orders dashboard bakes both `eeHold` + `shopifyHold` into `/get-orders` (chip via `ordHoldChipHtml`, and the On-Hold filter counts either). |

### Other domains (mounted routers)
- `/api/orders…` — Shopify orders + enrichment (shared across dashboards, not view-gated)
- `/api/easyecom/*`, `/api/fulfillment-ops/*`, `/api/serviceability/*`
- `/api/amazon/*` — review requests (perm `amazon-review`)
- `/api/fba/*` — FBA dashboards (perm `amazon-fba`)
- `/api/docpharma*` — recon/invoices/ledger/overview/inventory (perm `docpharma-recon`)
- `/api/ops-control/*`, `/api/adset*`, `/api/ad*`, Excel/PDF report endpoints
- `/api/webhook/*` — inbound webhooks (public): `/rapidshyp`, `/easyecom`, `/docpharma` (order journey), **`/docpharma-ndr`** (NDR logs → `docpharma_ndr_logs`; optional `DOCPHARMA_NDR_TOKEN` via `?token=`/`x-webhook-token`; accepts single/array/`{records|data|orders}` payloads; extracts order/awb/status/ndr_reason/attempt/courier/ndr_at + full `raw`), **`/ee-session`** (EasyEcom panel-cookie auto-sync from the `ee-session-extension` Chrome extension → `easyecom_panel_session` via `savePanelCookie()`; auth `EE_SESSION_PUSH_TOKEN` via `?token=`/`x-ee-token`; 503 until set. Server-side auto-login is impossible — EasyEcom uses Google SSO + 2FA — so a browser extension with the `cookies` permission reads the HttpOnly session cookie and pushes it every ~20 min; the `*/20` keep-alive cron holds it between syncs), **`/ee-routes`** + **`/ee-route-result`** (browser-executor warehouse routing — see below), **`/shopify-order`** (Shopify `orders/create` webhook → (a) **real-time order feed**: upserts the order into `orders`+`order_line_items`+`order_shipping_addresses` via `orders_ingest.upsertShopifyOrder()` so Support Orders / Call Queue show it instantly instead of waiting ~30 min for the external sync; (b) auto-hold repeat COD orders. HMAC-verified with `SHOPIFY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` over the raw body; 503 until set — see Shopify auto-hold below)

  - **⚠️ EasyEcom AWS-WAF constraint (2026-07-18):** the panel (`app.easyecom.io/Orders/UpdateVendor`) sits behind AWS WAF that **blocks the VPS's datacenter IP** with a "Human Verification" challenge — so the server CANNOT call UpdateVendor directly (works only from a residential IP). EasyEcom declined both an API and an IP-whitelist. Workaround: a **Firefox extension** (`ee-session-extension-firefox/`) runs the calls from the admin's browser (residential IP the WAF trusts). Flow: VPS `GET /api/webhook/ee-routes` returns pending DocPharma-rejected orders (`dp_rejected_handled_ecom` where `routed_at IS NULL` → `{orderName, invoiceId, targetCid=SHIFUPRO_CID, cId}`, all DB reads); the extension POSTs `UpdateVendor` from Firefox (`credentials:'include'` → sends the browser's `aws-waf-token`+session, passes WAF) then reports to `POST /api/webhook/ee-route-result` which calls `markWarehouseRouted()` + sets `routed_at`. Runs auto every ~20 min while Firefox is open + a manual "Run pending routes" button. **The dashboard's per-order "Move order" button** also feeds this: `POST /api/easyecom/change-warehouse` now QUEUES (upserts a `route_pending` mark in `order_marks_ecom` with `note`=target cid) instead of calling UpdateVendor from the WAF-blocked VPS; `/ee-routes` serves those manual marks (any target) alongside the DP list; `/ee-route-result` clears both queues on success and marks the correct destination via the `targetCid` the extension echoes back. So all routing (auto DP + manual moves) flows through the one extension executor. The old server-side `change-warehouse`/`autoRouteRejectedToShifupro`/keep-alive still exist but are WAF-dead from the VPS. Both endpoints auth via `EE_SESSION_PUSH_TOKEN`. The server-side `autoRouteRejectedToShifupro`/keep-alive still exist but are WAF-blocked from the VPS.
  - ⚠️ **THE EXTENSION'S 20-MINUTE TIMER HAD SILENTLY STOPPED (fixed 2026-08-10).** The alarm was created in **one place only** — inside `chrome.runtime.onInstalled`, which fires exactly once per install/update. Once the alarm was lost for any reason (a temporary add-on load, a profile that dropped it, an early `create()` failure) **nothing ever recreated it**, and the cycle never ran again with no error anywhere. The only tell was the status line still reading `(manual)` from the last button press. **Fix:** `ensureAlarm()` is asserted from FOUR entry points — top level (every background start, including an alarm-triggered wake), `onInstalled`, `onStartup`, and on the popup's `alarm-info` message. `alarms.create()` replaces an alarm of the same name, so re-asserting is free and the schedule **self-heals** the next time anything wakes the background. Added `delayInMinutes:1` so a fresh schedule runs a minute later rather than after a full period. **And made it observable:** the popup now shows 'Auto-runs every 20 min · next at HH:MM:SS' (or 'Timer not scheduled'), because the real defect was that a stopped timer was undetectable. Status tags now distinguish `(installed)` = ran on load · `(manual)` = button · `(auto)` = the timer fired. Same single-point-of-creation flaw fixed in the Chrome `ee-session-extension`. Versions bumped (Firefox 1.1.2→**1.2.0**, Chrome 1.0.0→**1.1.0**) so the browser treats it as an update.
  - ⚠️ **`UpdateVendor` SUCCESS IS A BARE `0` **OR** `200` (corrected 2026-08-10).** The code accepted only the literal `0` and carried a comment warning that `200` was an odd echo, NOT success. **The data says otherwise.** On TE25-41231 the extension was the ONLY caller that reached UpdateVendor — the VPS cron hit the WAF ("Human Verification", HTTP 405) on every hourly attempt for a full day — it received `



200` twice, and the order's EasyEcom `location` duly became "Shifupro Technologies Pvt. Ltd.". Same body on TE25-41471. A rejected success is **not** harmless: the order is never marked routed, so it stays queued and gets re-POSTed on every run, and the popup reports "Routed 0" on work that actually landed. Both paths now accept `0` / `200` / a JSON `{code:200|status:true|success:true}`, and the extension's test is a **mirror of `callUpdateVendor()`** so the same shipment can never be judged routed by one path and failed by the other (verified 7/7 on the real bodies in `api_logs_ecom`, incl. the WAF page, a `code:400` business error and "Server Error" — all still correctly fail).
  - ⚠️ **TWO COPIES OF THE EXTENSION FOLDER EXIST** — `ecom-central-Staging/ee-session-extension-firefox/` and `ecom-central-live/ee-session-extension-firefox/`. Firefox loads whichever PATH was picked in `about:debugging`, so editing one and loading the other silently runs stale code (exactly what happened on 2026-08-10: the 200-fix was in staging, Firefox had the live copy, and TE25-41471 still reported failed). **Keep both in step on every extension change**, and check the Location shown under the add-on in `about:debugging` when behaviour does not match the source. ⚠️ Firefox Release **refuses unsigned XPIs** (`xpinstall.signatures.required` only works on Developer Edition / Nightly / ESR), so a permanent install needs an AMO re-sign (unlisted / self-distribution, same extension id `ee-warehouse-router@theelement.skin`); a `about:debugging` temporary load runs unsigned but is **dropped on Firefox restart, taking the timer with it**.
  - **HTTPS variant (for external partners):** Supabase Edge Function **`docpharma-ndr-webhook`** = same logic as `/api/webhook/docpharma-ndr` but always-on HTTPS. Public URL to hand to DocPharma: `https://urtwdqmiypjhnduspmwk.supabase.co/functions/v1/docpharma-ndr-webhook` (health probe: `?health=1`; optional `DOCPHARMA_NDR_SECRET` function-secret enforced via `?token=`/`x-webhook-token`; `verify_jwt=false`). Mirrors the `easyecom-b2c-webhook` edge-function pattern.
- `/api/tally/*` — Finance → Tally (perms `finance-entry`/`finance-register`; the post route additionally needs `finance-post-tally`):
  - `GET /tally/status` — mode, company, connection health (direct = live probe; bridge = heartbeat age, stale >60s), `postEnabled`, per-kind master counts
  - `GET /tally/books/trial-balance[?to=]` · `GET /tally/books/vouchers[?from=&to=&type=&q=]` — **read-only** (perm `finance-books`, or either data-entry perm). Live Export/Collection pull with a 60s in-memory cache — the Day Book is ~5MB of XML for 4 months, so a refresh must not re-fetch and re-parse it. `direct` mode only; bridge mode returns 501 with an explanation. Two non-obvious accounting rules are applied server-side so the UI never has to reason about them:
    - **`Profit & Loss A/c` is excluded from the totals** (`derived: true`). Tally derives its balance from the period's income and expenses — no voucher touches it — so adding it to those same ledgers double-counts. Verified on the live company: it reports Dr ₹5,44,108.55 while zero voucher lines reference it.
    - **Unbalanced opening balances surface as "Difference in Opening Balances"**, the same balancing line Tally shows (here HDFC BANK carries a ₹4,73,597.23 opening debit with no contra). With both handled, Dr === Cr to the paisa — a check the UI displays, since a trial balance that doesn't balance must not be presented as fact.
  - `GET /tally/companies` — every set of books the dashboard can act on. Direct mode asks Tally; **bridge mode cannot** (the VPS never reaches Tally), so it falls back to the DISTINCT companies already in `tally_masters_ecom`. That fallback is what keeps **both financial years selectable on live**, where `TALLY_COMPANY` can only name one.
  - `GET /tally/masters?kind=ledger&q=` — picker data, served from the `tally_masters_ecom` mirror (never live)
  - `POST /tally/masters/sync` — direct: pull from Tally now; bridge: set `sync_requested` for the agent
  - `GET /tally/vouchers?status=&from=&to=&type=&source=&q=` · `GET /tally/vouchers/:id` (adds request/response XML) · `POST /tally/vouchers` (draft, server-validated) · `PUT /tally/vouchers/:id` (only while draft/failed; an edited failure resets to draft + `attempts=0`) · `DELETE /tally/vouchers/:id` (soft → `cancelled`)
  - `DELETE /tally/vouchers/:id/hard` — **`role=admin` only.** Hard-deletes a dashboard entry after snapshotting it to `tally_voucher_deletions_ecom`. Aborts if the audit insert fails (no audit ⇒ no delete). 409 while `queued`/`posting`/`awaiting_approval`; 400 for a `posted` voucher with no reason. Never issues anything to Tally.
  - `GET /tally/deletions` — the deletion log (any finance perm; response carries `canDelete`)
  - `POST /tally/vouchers/post-bulk` — **perm `finance-post-tally`**, max 50 ids per call. Posts them **one at a time through the same `postVoucherById()` the single-voucher route uses** (no second code path that could drift), and **stops at the first failure** rather than ploughing on: if voucher 3 of 40 is wrong, the remaining 37 are usually wrong the same way, and 40 bad vouchers in real books cost far more than a second click. The Voucher Register's checkbox column drives it in chunks of 25 so progress is honest.
  - `POST /tally/ledgers/propose` · `POST /tally/ledgers` — suggest a group for missing ledgers from how the same name is filed in the other company, then create the confirmed ones via a real master import. Guards the project's core hazard: an unknown `LEDGERNAME` in a voucher makes Tally invent it under Suspense. `POST /tally/ledgers` also takes `groups: [{name, parent}]` and creates those **first** (re-syncing the group mirror in between). **A groups-only request is valid** — a company can be missing a group that no *new* ledger needs, because an existing ledger is about to be re-grouped into it; rejecting it would force a throwaway ledger to be invented purely to carry the group, leaving junk in real books.
  - `DELETE /tally/ledgers` — **`role=admin` + `finance-post-tally`, direct mode only.** Removes ledger masters (`ACTION="Delete"`). Guarded **twice**: refused if our own register still holds a voucher on the ledger (it knows about drafts Tally has never seen), and Tally refuses independently if its books do. Note the response check differs from create/alter — a delete reports `DELETED`, never `CREATED`/`ALTERED`, so the shared `ok` test would read a successful delete as a failure.
  - `POST /tally/ledgers/regroup` — **`role=admin` + `finance-post-tally`, direct mode only.** Moves existing ledgers to another group via `ACTION="Alter"`. Also **renames**, via an optional `newName`: Tally matches on the `NAME` attribute and stores `<NAME>`, so a differing `<NAME>` renames in place and **every voucher follows, including posted ones**. That makes rename the right way to merge two ledgers into one — moving vouchers one at a time cannot touch a posted voucher, and the old ledger then can't be deleted because Tally won't drop a ledger a voucher still uses. Refuses if the target name already exists, which would silently merge two accounts. Sends only NAME, PARENT and ISBILLWISEON: **`OPENINGBALANCE` is deliberately omitted**, because Tally treats a supplied value as the new one and including it would silently zero any opening balance the ledger carries. The existing bill-wise flag is read from the mirror and carried through, so the alter can't quietly turn it off. Verifies by re-reading masters **from Tally** and reporting what actually moved, not what was asked for.
  - `GET /tally/bank/pending[?company=]` · `GET /tally/bank/pending/count` — statement lines still waiting to be mapped, served in the same shape as `/parse` (suggestions recomputed live). Backs "Continue mapping".
  - `POST /tally/bank/lines/release` — **`role=admin` only.** Frees a statement line whose voucher had already been POSTED (the claim is kept in that case, because the voucher still exists in Tally and re-importing would create a second copy). Refuses while a voucher that **actually exists** is still attached — a dangling id left by an older delete must not block it. Released lines return to `tally_bank_pending_ecom`.
  - `GET /tally/bank/lines/orphaned` — imported lines whose voucher is gone, including ones whose `voucher_id` still points at a deleted row (invisible to a plain `IS NULL` filter). The voucher-existence check is **chunked at 100 ids**: a single `.in()` with hundreds of UUIDs overflows the request URL and comes back empty, which would make every line look orphaned.
  - `POST /tally/vouchers/preview-xml` — the exact XML that would be sent; accepts a saved `id` **or** an unsaved form payload
  - `POST /tally/vouchers/:id/post` — **perm `finance-post-tally`**. Re-validates against current masters, claims the row with a status-filtered update (two concurrent clicks can't both proceed), then direct-posts or queues. A transport failure is recorded as *unknown outcome* and a retry then requires `force=true`, because the voucher may already be in Tally — blind re-sends duplicate it
  - `GET /tally/batches` · `GET /tally/batches/:id` — batch history / one batch + its vouchers (any finance perm; the response carries `canApprove`)
  - `POST /tally/batches/:id/{approve,reject}` · `POST /tally/batches/build` — **`role=admin` only**. Approve is what queues the vouchers; a status-filtered update means two admins approving at once cannot both win. `build` runs the nightly job on demand (for testing without waiting for 23:50) and refuses while another batch is still open.
  - `/tally/bridge/{pull,ack,masters,heartbeat}` — **the agent only; no JWT.** In `PUBLIC_API`, authenticated by a constant-time `X-Bridge-Key` compare. ⚠️ Being in `PUBLIC_API` skips only the JWT gate — the `_VIEW_PERMS` gate still runs, so its rule is `/^\/tally\/(?!bridge\/)/i`; without that lookahead the (permission-less) agent gets a flat 403. `ack` sends Tally's **raw reply** and the server decides the verdict with the same parser the direct path uses, so a buggy agent cannot report a failure as a success. Two more, added with the agent: **`/bridge/masters-xml`** and **`/bridge/books-xml`** — the agent uploads RAW Tally XML and the server parses it, keeping `tally_xml.js` the single interpretation of Tally's output. `books-xml` is what makes **Tally Books work on the live dashboard**: it fills `tally_books_cache_ecom`, and `booksData()` transparently serves from there in bridge mode with a `source: 'cache'` + `syncedAt` stamp instead of the old 501
- `/api/teams/test?target=` — Teams webhook smoke test

  - **🔒 Shopify auto-hold for repeat COD orders (2026-07-20):** repeat COD orders are held on **Shopify** (a fulfillment-order hold) so support can phone-confirm before they ship. Chosen because the EasyEcom hold fails once the order is imported/manifested — a Shopify hold placed BEFORE EasyEcom imports keeps the order OUT of EasyEcom entirely (verified live). **Same "repeat" criteria as the Call Queue Repeat tab** (`findRepeatCandidates()` in `support_console.js`, shared): (1) COD, (2) still awaiting dispatch (bucket `order_to_dispatch`) **AND not yet fulfilled/dispatched** — the bucket is keyed off a lagging Shopify-fulfillment state, so a shipped order can wrongly stay in `order_to_dispatch`; `findRepeatCandidates` classifies dispatched via `fulfillment_status ∈ {fulfilled, partial}` (AWB + courier assigned, out-for-pickup/manifested → un-holdable) plus a `tracking_status` in-transit safety-net regex (DocPharma `in-progress` / in-transit / OFD / delivered / RTO). The **auto-hold cron drops all dispatched** (default); the **Call Queue keeps dispatched rows that are held or noted** (`skipDispatchFilter=true` → flag not drop, then filter after enrichment) so the team doesn't lose its worklist, (3) it matches **any of 3 call-reasons** — each order is TAGGED with which fired (`c.reasons`): **`high_value`** = order ≥ ₹1500, **UNLESS the customer has EVER taken delivery of a ≥₹1500 order** (trust exception, 2026-08-01). ⚠️ **COMPLETE order history — no last-3 window, no time limit**: once someone has accepted a high-value COD parcel, the value alone stops being a risk signal. Implemented in BOTH paths as its OWN targeted existence query (`bucket='delivered' AND total_price>=HIGH_VALUE_MIN AND created_at < this order`), deliberately NOT filtered out of the shared recent-history batch — `holdReasons()` caps that batch at 50 rows and `findRepeatCandidates()`'s `chunkedIn` caps each chunk response at 1000, either of which could silently hide an older proving delivery. Threshold shared as `HIGH_VALUE_MIN` (keep the two files in sync). `holdReasons`'s phone-history lookup now also runs for **partial-paid** orders (it sat inside the COD-only branch, so high-value partial-paid orders never had history to check) and is ordered newest-first. Only `high_value` uses full history — `recent_undelivered` stays a last-3 rule by definition; **`recent_undelivered`** = recent non-delivery — holds **only when NONE of the last 3 prior orders was delivered** (**updated 2026-07-22: if ANY one of the last 3 was delivered the customer is trusted → skip this reason**; RTO/undelivered/in-transit count as not-delivered, cancelled doesn't); **`in_flight`** = the customer has an **OLDER** still-live (non-terminal) order open, so THIS order is a repeat stacked on one already in flight (**independent of delivery history**; older-order-only, so a concurrent batch holds the repeat(s) but **never the first** — fixed 2026-07-22, the `*/5` backstop used to reciprocally hold both). The auto-holder **records which reason(s) triggered the hold** (`holdReasons`/`reasonNoteFrom` → stored on the `shopify_hold` mark note, carried into `shopify_hold_released` on release) so the Repeat panel shows the category as a chip even after the live reason changes. The order-detail modal has a **Hold & unhold log** timeline (order placed → each auto/manual hold + reason → released by whom, built from `api_logs_ecom`). The Repeat panel also flags **`🔁 N× same customer`** (+ a fuchsia row stripe) on any row whose phone appears more than once in the current queue (concurrent/duplicate orders). In-transit/not-delivered orders are NOT listed as rows — they're the customer's history, seen via the order-detail click. **Service** `app/api/shopify_hold.js`: 2-step Shopify REST (`GET /orders/{id}/fulfillment_orders.json` → `POST /fulfillment_orders/{fo}/hold.json` `{fulfillment_hold:{reason:'other',reason_notes}}`; `release_hold.json` to undo). **State** = marks in `order_marks_ecom`: `shopify_hold` (held) / `shopify_hold_released` (human released → never auto-re-hold) / `shopify_hold_failed` (attempt failed → manual Hold button). **Triggers:** (1) instant — Shopify `orders/create` webhook `POST /api/webhook/shopify-order` (HMAC via `SHOPIFY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, holds before EE can import); (2) backstop — `*/5` cron `SHOPIFY_AUTOHOLD_ENABLED=true`. **UI:** the Shopify hold is a control **separate** from the EasyEcom hold, clearly labelled, so each system is managed independently: the Orders-dashboard order-detail panel shows **Hold/Unhold on EasyEcom** (`/api/easyecom/hold-order|unhold-order`) AND **Hold/Unhold on Shopify** side by side; the Call Queue → Repeat tab shows 🔒 Shopify-hold badge + **Hold/Release Shopify**. Endpoints: `POST /api/shopify-hold|shopify-unhold` (SHARED — any authenticated user, resolves the Shopify order id from the order name; used by the Orders dashboard) and `POST /api/support/shopify-hold|shopify-unhold` (support-gated; used by the Repeat panel) — both call `shopify_hold.holdOrderManual/releaseOrder`. `SHOPIFY_TOKEN` already carries `write_merchant_managed_fulfillment_orders` + `write_assigned_fulfillment_orders`. **Setup left:** register the `orders/create` webhook → `https://dashboard.theelement.skin:8443/api/webhook/shopify-order` and set `SHOPIFY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`; flip `SHOPIFY_AUTOHOLD_ENABLED=true`. **Auto-hold guards (2026-07-29):** `autoHoldOrder` now refuses to hold unless the order is genuinely NEW and pre-import, because a Shopify hold only works UPSTREAM of the EasyEcom import (holding after does nothing): (a) **age** — skips if the order's `created_at` is > 6 h old; (b) **already-imported** — skips if a `b2c_order_easycom` row exists for it (`reference_code` = order name). This stops retroactive/bulk holds of already-imported/shipped orders — a one-off `<100`-char short-address sweep on 2026-07-28 held 53 already-"Shipped" orders (each pointless, some 422 "uninterruptible"); these guards prevent a recurrence. Both are no-ops for the instant `orders/create` webhook (the order is seconds old and not yet imported), so legitimate holds are unaffected. The webhook now passes `o.created_at` into `autoHoldOrder`.

  - **🧺 Batch hold — hold the WHOLE repeat burst, and hold in EasyEcom once the window shuts (2026-08-05):** the per-order rules only ever hold the order that TRIPS them. On a 2–3 order burst `in_flight` fires on the newest ("an OLDER live order is open → THIS one is the repeat") and the EARLIER orders sailed through, because when they were placed there was no prior live order to trip on — so the team had one held order and one or two shipping. Two changes fix that:
    - **`holdOrderSmart()`** replaces the direct `autoHoldOrder` call in the webhook, the `*/2` cron and the sibling pass. It tries **Shopify first** (the preferred stop — the order never reaches EasyEcom) and, only if `autoHoldOrder` returns `skipped:'in-easyecom'`, holds the order **in EasyEcom** instead and then **mirrors the hold back onto Shopify** (via `autoHoldOrder(..., { allowImported:true })`) so the two systems agree.
    - **`holdSiblingOrders()`** — after a successful hold, the customer's OTHER open orders within `SHOPIFY_SIBLING_HOLD_HOURS` (default 24) are held the same way. Bounded deliberately: without a window a customer's old open order would be retro-held, which is exactly the 2026-07-28 failure that held 53 already-shipped orders.
    - ⚠️ **Why the EasyEcom leg is not optional.** The EasyEcom import lag is ~2 minutes; repeat orders arrive **4 minutes to 15 hours** apart. Simulated over 24 h of live data: **5 repeat bursts, 5 siblings, and in every single one the earlier order had already imported** — including a pair placed 4 minutes apart. A Shopify-only batch hold would therefore have held **zero** extra orders. **EasyEcom hold behaviour itself is unchanged** — the `/hold-order` route's logic was only extracted into `holdOrderInEasyecom()` so the auto-holder can reuse the identical call.
    - ⚠️ **Dispatched orders are never held** (`isDispatchedOrder()`): Shopify refuses a hold on a fulfilled order by itself, but **EasyEcom does not**, so without this guard an in-transit parcel could be pointlessly held there. Blocks on `cancelled_at`, `fulfillment_status='fulfilled'`, a courier `tracking_status` movement, or our own journey (`dispatched_at` / `out_for_delivery_at` / terminal outcome). **Note:** EasyEcom marks an order `fulfilled` at AWB assignment, so in practice the auto-holder stops touching an order the moment a label exists — a manual hold is still possible until pickup.
    - ⚠⚠ **RE-HOLD GUARD (fixed 2026-08-06, shipped broken on 2026-08-05).** `autoHoldOrder` checks **already-in-EasyEcom BEFORE it checks the hold marks**, so for an imported order it returns `skipped:'in-easyecom'` without ever looking at whether a human had released it. `holdOrderSmart()` read that as permission and fell straight through to the EasyEcom hold — so the team would unhold an order and the next auto-hold pass put it **straight back on hold**. Underneath sat a second gap: the EasyEcom unhold **deleted** the `ee_hold` mark and left nothing behind, so even a correct check had nothing to read (Shopify has `shopify_hold_released` for exactly this; EasyEcom had no equivalent). **Fix:** `holdOrderSmart()` now checks the release state BEFORE the EasyEcom fallback and treats **both** tombstones as final — `shopify_hold_released` and the new **`ee_hold_released`**, written by `POST /api/easyecom/unhold-order`. A human release always wins; only a human may hold it again, and a deliberate hold (manual route or an eligible auto-hold) clears the tombstone so the state can never read as held AND released. Verified: released-in-EasyEcom → `skipped:'ee-released'`, already-held → `skipped:'ee-already-held'`, released-on-Shopify → `skipped:'released'`.
    - 🧑‍🤝‍🧑 **A CUSTOMER IS PHONE ∪ EMAIL, NOT A PHONE NUMBER (2026-08-27, from TE25-45095).** The order was not auto-held: ₹798, a 116-character address, and — as far as the rules could see — **no history**. The customer had an RTO (`#TE25-21864`, April) and a cancellation (May) under phone **9607878181** and placed the new COD order under **7030520199**, same email; Shopify tagged it `Repeat`, both copies of our rule (`holdReasons()` in the webhook, `findRepeatCandidates()` in the cron and Call Queue) keyed history on **phone only** and saw a first-time buyer. User: *"don't depend on phone number only, make a robust system to check."* **Fix — `app/api/repeat_rules.js`, ONE module for both callers:** identity is **phone ∪ email, closed transitively** (`closeIdentity`: the seed finds orders, the phones/emails on those find more, up to 4 hops), the way Customer Profile already resolves a person; `fetchHistory()` does the same expansion against `order_buckets` in one chunked round trip per hop; `evaluateReasons()` is the single rule evaluation (in_flight / recent_undelivered / high_value / short_address, with the prepaid, partial-paid and trust-exception branches) that both paths now call — the doc used to say "keep the two files in sync", which is exactly what failed. ⚠️ **Placeholders are never keys** (`9999999999` and friends, dummy/test emails), and a closure that passes **`MAX_MERGE` = 400 orders is abandoned for the seed keys** — a shared contact must never make one customer's RTO hold a stranger. The webhook now passes `o.email`; candidates on the Call Queue carry `identity` (phones/emails/overflow) for display. Replayed on TE25-45095's exact shape in `tests/selftest.js`: phone-only → nothing; phone ∪ email → 3 orders, **`recent_undelivered`** fires. Live re-evaluation of the order after the change: same verdict.
    - 🛡️ **THE COVERAGE GUARANTEE — "no order should be skipped from rule" (user, 2026-08-27).** Identity was one gap; the audit of the whole pipeline found three more, all closed the same day. **(1) Nothing recorded a decision**, so a miss was invisible: new table **`hold_evaluations_ecom`** (migration `20260827_hold_evaluations.sql`, applied) gets one row per evaluation from every path — `path` (webhook / cron / reconcile), `verdict` (hold / no_reason / prepaid), the `reasons`, the resolved `identity` (phones, emails, overflow), `history_count`, `shopify_repeat_tag`, and the `holdOrderSmart()` result as `action`. `recordEvaluation()` in `repeat_rules.js` is fire-and-forget — a ledger write can never break a hold. ⚠️ When Shopify tags an order `Repeat` and we resolved **zero** history, the webhook log says *"identity gap?"* out loud and the ledger row carries `shopify_repeat_tag=true, history_count=0` — the query that finds the next TE25-45095 before a customer does. **(2) The `*/2` cron could starve a new order:** it walked `cand.slice(0, 50)` oldest-first, and already-held orders (`holdOrderSmart` → `skipped`) still consumed the fifty slots, so on a busy day a fresh qualifying order could wait behind settled ones indefinitely. Held/released orders are now dropped via `getHoldStates()` **before** the cap, which bounds real attempts only (100/tick). **(3) A `stale` verdict ended the attempt:** an order the webhook AND cron had missed for six hours returned `skipped:'stale'` from `autoHoldOrder` and `holdOrderSmart` stopped there — even though the parcel was still un-manifested in EasyEcom and holdable. `stale` now falls through to the EasyEcom path exactly like `in-easyecom` (the dispatched guard still protects a parcel that has left), and the Shopify mirror skips the age guard when `allowImported` (the parcel is verifiably held in EasyEcom by then; refusing the mirror as stale left the two systems disagreeing). **(4) The reconciler — `reconcileHoldCoverage()`, cron `HoldCoverage (*/10)`:** work list = COD orders (pending / partially_paid), not cancelled, not test, **5 min–48 h old, with NO ledger row** (`unevaluatedCodOrders()`); each is evaluated exactly as the webhook would (phone from the shipping address first, email from the order), held on a reason, recorded as `path='reconcile'`. A non-empty work list is **itself the alarm** — it means both primary paths missed — so it logs at WARN with the order names, which the cron reporter turns into a Teams card. Same `SHOPIFY_AUTOHOLD_ENABLED` gate as the cron. **Bootstrap:** the first run on live will find every COD order of the last 48 h unevaluated (the ledger is new) and evaluate them all — orders already held are recorded as `skipped:held`, genuine misses get held, and everything else is recorded `no_reason`; after that a quiet run means full coverage.
    - 🛑 **A HUMAN RELEASE IS FINAL FOR AUTOMATION (2026-08-08) — the hard rule.** Once ANY Ecom user unholds an order, **no automatic path may ever hold it again**; only a human can (the manual Hold buttons, which clear the tombstone deliberately). Enforced by `releasedByHuman()` in `shopify_hold.js`, called as **check (0) at the very top of `autoHoldOrder()`** — before the age guard, before the in-EasyEcom guard, before everything. **Position is the whole point:** every auto path bottoms out in `autoHoldOrder` (orders/create webhook → `holdOrderSmart`, `*/2` cron → `holdOrderSmart`, `holdSiblingOrders` → `holdOrderSmart`, and the EasyEcom mirror call), and the original bug was purely an ordering fault — the `in-easyecom` check returned first, so the release check below it never ran. Anything placed after an early-returning guard can be skipped over, so this one precedes them all. It reads **both** tombstones (`shopify_hold_released` + `ee_hold_released`), which `getHoldStates()` deliberately does **not** — that function reads only the three `shopify_*` `HOLD_TYPES` and drives DISPLAY (which button to show); this is the automation gate and must also see the EasyEcom release. Returns `skipped:'released-by-human'` with the releaser's email. Neither tombstone is ever written by automation (there is no auto-unhold), so a tombstone always means a person. **Verified** with a throwaway order name against all four entry points × both tombstones (9/9 assertions, trip-wires on the Shopify/EasyEcom hold APIs confirming **zero** calls were reached). Note an order unheld **directly in the EasyEcom panel** writes no tombstone — it is protected instead by the second layer's `ee-already-held` check (our `ee_hold` mark persists), so it is also never re-held.
    - ⚠️ **STALE `EasyEcom hold` CHIP AFTER AN UNHOLD (fixed 2026-08-08).** `/api/ee-hold-marks` (the chip every dashboard reads) and the Call Queue Repeat tab both derive 'held in EasyEcom' from **`b2c_order_easycom.raw_data->>order_status_id = 44`** — a **SYNCED COPY** of EasyEcom's state, refreshed only when the EasyEcom sync next touches the order. Unhold an order and the id **stays 44** until then, so the dashboard kept showing an EasyEcom hold on an order EasyEcom itself reported as unheld.
