Live case **TE25-40985** (2026-08-08): unheld 13:56 IST, row last synced 13:45, sync caught up 14:04 — for those 8 minutes the agent clicked Unhold **five more times**, EasyEcom answering 'Order is already in Unhold status' each time. **Fix:** the **`ee_hold_released` tombstone timestamp is compared against the synced row's `updated_at`** — the synced hold is suppressed **only while the human release is NEWER than the sync**. If the row was synced AFTER the release its 44 is fresh evidence (someone re-held it in the EasyEcom panel) and the chip correctly stands. Applied in BOTH places (`/ee-hold-marks` `releasedAfter()` and the Repeat-tab `staleHold()`). Verified on 5 replayed states incl. both counter-cases; the pre-existing MOVED filter still separately drops holds on shipped/cancelled orders.
    - **HOLD & UNHOLD LOG NOW COVERS EASYECOM (2026-08-08).** The order-detail timeline queried only the three `shopify_*` actions, so an order **auto-held inside EasyEcom** — what `holdOrderSmart()` does once the order has already imported, now the common path — showed an **empty log** while wearing an 'EasyEcom hold' chip: held, with nothing on record saying who or why. Added `easyecom_hold_order` / `easyecom_unhold_order`, reconciling two differences: the EasyEcom rows key on **`payload.orderName`** (carries `#`) not `payload.order`, and they **always return HTTP 200** — the real result is in the body (`response.code` / `response.message`), so `status_code < 400` would have scored every failure as a success. **No-ops are dropped** ('Order already in Hold Status' / 'already in Unhold status') — they changed nothing, and before the re-hold fix the auto-holder produced hundreds per order (TE25-40985: 18 raw rows → 6 real events). Both EasyEcom routes now log **`by`** so a human hold/unhold is attributed instead of reading as 'auto' (historical rows have none → rendered unattributed). Rendered with system-specific lines: ⏸ Auto-held/Held **in EasyEcom** (orange) · 🔓 Unheld **in EasyEcom** · 🔒 Auto-held/Held **on Shopify** (rose) · 🔓 Released **on Shopify**.
    - **One Unhold releases both.** When an order is held in both systems the dashboard hides the separate "Unhold Shopify" button and shows only the EasyEcom one; `POST /api/easyecom/unhold-order` then releases the Shopify hold too (resolving the Shopify order id from the `orders` mirror). Without this, releasing Shopify alone would leave the order still stopped in EasyEcom with no button left to free it.


  - **📦 Inventory Analytics (2026-07-23):** new **Inventory** nav group → **Inventory Analytics** view (perm key `inventory`). Reads the daily `inventory_snapshots` table — built by the Supabase edge function `snapshot-inventory` @ 00:00 IST (pg_cron `snapshot-inventory-daily-ist`), which merges EasyEcom stock (via `easyecom-proxy`) + `b2c_order_easycom` sales, pack→base expanded via `sku_pack_mapping`. **⚠️ Only SKUs registered in `sku_pack_mapping` as a `base_sku` are captured** — an unregistered product is silently dropped even if EasyEcom has stock (fix: insert a self-map `(pack_sku,base_sku,unit_multiplier)=(SKU,SKU,1)`; 2026-07-23 registered TE-AFW1/TE-ABW1 that were hidden, ~1.5k units). Combos/multi-packs are intentionally excluded so their stock isn't double-counted with the base component. Stock shown is **EasyEcom's** number (NOT DocPharma's own system — a mismatch like TE-BFW1 is an EasyEcom-side sync gap, not a dashboard bug). Backend **`app/api/inventory.js`**: `GET /inventory/snapshot` (latest rows + facets, paginated past 1000, `stale` flag; **filters phantom `warehouse='N/A'` rows** = unmapped / name-as-location-code keys), `POST /inventory/refresh-snapshot` (force the edge fn — a **live EasyEcom re-pull**), `POST /inventory/teams-report`. **DRR** = `units_sold_{7,14,30}d`/period; **DOI** = stock/DRR (999 if DRR=0 & stock>0, 0 if OOS). Status bands (**dashboard + report kept in sync**): OOS / No Sales / Critical ≤7 / Warning ≤15 / Watch <30 / Healthy ≤60 / Overstock >60. Frontend (`initInventory`/`invRefresh`) = 4 KPI cards (Total SKUs, Avg DRR, Avg DOI, **Low-stock = OOS + Critical DOI≤7**) + search/warehouse/category/DRR-period filters + 2 Chart.js bars (DOI distribution, Top-10 DRR) + sortable table with **exact numbers** (no 5.2K abbreviation) and **row-click → detail popup** (DRR/DOI across all 3 windows 7/14/30d, via `infModal`). The **Refresh button** now does a **live EasyEcom re-pull** (`invSyncFromEasyEcom` → `/inventory/refresh-snapshot`), not just a snapshot reload. **Daily report to Microsoft Teams as an IMAGE (2026-07-23):** the same dark "Low Inventory — DOI < 30 days" PNG the Slack `inventory-doi-image` fn renders. New edge function **`inventory-doi-image-teams`** (version-controlled in `supabase/functions/`, kept SEPARATE from the Lovable-owned Slack one so a republish can't clobber it) renders it via Satori+resvg from RPC `inventory_doi_low(30,7)`, uploads to the **public `reports` Storage bucket**, and returns the URL + stats; `sendInventoryTeamsReport()` embeds that image in a Teams card — `teams.js` now maps a Slack `image` block → Adaptive-Card `Image` element + HTML `<img>` twin — via `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_INVENTORY` (falls back to `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_WAREHOUSE`). ecom-central cron **`30 6 * * *` IST (06:30 AM)** (server.js) first **re-syncs live from EasyEcom** (`refreshSnapshot()` → `snapshot-inventory` edge fn) so the morning report reflects current stock, then posts the image. (The Supabase pg_cron `snapshot-inventory-daily-ist` @ 00:00 IST still refreshes the dashboard overnight.)

  - **📐 The order table keeps ALL TEN columns** (SKU · DRR · DOI · Stock · Recommend Qty · Raised PO · Final Qty · Case Size · Case Qty · Order Units). When it first looked squashed I tried fixing it by dropping columns, then by grouping them — both were the wrong fix. The cause was the card's default **narrow width**, not the column count; with `msteams.width: Full` and the native 1.5 `Table`, all ten fit with borders. **Reach for the width before reaching for the data.**
  - **DRR GAVE A DIFFERENT ANSWER EVERY RUN — FIXED 2026-08-22 (edge fn v41).** Reported as “TE-BDR1 DRR/7d is 381 in the 06:30 report and 420 at 10:17”. **It was not timezone and it was not new sales.** The window never moved: `snapshot-inventory` measures whole IST calendar days ending **yesterday** (today’s partial day excluded), so 06:30 and 10:17 on 22 Aug both cover 15–21 Aug. Three runs of that one fixed window produced **2,667 · 2,940 · true 2,830**. ⚠⚠ **ROOT CAUSE: `.range()` PAGING WITH NO `.order()`.** The fn pages ~10,000 orders in 10 requests while the EasyEcom order sync is concurrently UPDATING those rows; Postgres guarantees no row order without ORDER BY and an updated row physically moves under MVCC, so between pages an order can be returned twice or skipped. Whole PAGES were double-counted or dropped — which is exactly why **every SKU moved by the same ~10–15%** (UARO +10.1, NBS1 +12.8, 2SAS1 +10.2, 10NBS1 +13.5, AFW1 +13.3, LB1 +14.6, FM1 +21.2) instead of moving independently. Real demand does not do that. Fixed by ordering on the primary key; **three consecutive runs now return identical rows.** ⚠ **SECOND CAUSE: one warehouse under two names.** An order carries `location = "Shifupro Technologies Pvt. Ltd."` while Confirmed and flips to `"rapidshyp"` on dispatch. `locationMap` held only the second, so the un-dispatched half landed in an orphan row (warehouse `N/A`, stock 0) that nothing reads — and DRR climbed through the day as the warehouse worked its dispatch queue, retroactively, for days already inside the window. Both labels now credit `wo66194027524`. ⚠⚠ **THIRD, AND THE MOST DANGEROUS: a placeholder row could OVERWRITE real stock.** Sales-only rows carry stock 0 / warehouse `N/A` and share the upsert key `(snapshot_date, location_id, sku)` with the real inventory row. EasyEcom’s `getInventory` feed is **not stable run to run**, so a SKU missing from one call had its good stock overwritten with a zero — caught live on **TE-AFW1 (502 units) and TE-ABW1 (472)**, both reading 0 / Out of Stock. A placeholder is now written only for a location the feed did not answer for **at all**, and a run where fewer than **60%** of base SKUs have stock **skips the upsert entirely** — a stale snapshot beats a wrong one. ✅ **The fn is now IN THE REPO** at `supabase/functions/snapshot-inventory/index.ts`; it had lived ONLY in Supabase, which is why a number that changed every run took a day to explain. Deploy via the Supabase MCP/CLI after any edit. **On timezone — checked and NOT the cause:** the fn’s IST handling is correct (IST-midnight cutoffs, IST calendar days, today excluded), and `b2c_order_easycom.order_date` is genuinely UTC — proved by the order-volume histogram, which peaks 22:00 IST and troughs 02:00–06:00 only when read as UTC→IST. ⚠ One real remnant: **58 of 3,977 orders (1.5%) are future-dated by up to 5.32 h** — IST wall-clock written as UTC by some path; all `Confirmed` + Shifupro, so they land in the wrong IST day. Not chased yet.
  - **Silent-RTO & SLA: payment mode, a Lost tab, and an Excel export (2026-08-22).** Asked for as “some more detail in this dashboard like payment mode and download excel report”, then “make new tab of lost and show only lost order”, then “add column of remarks”. 💳 **Payment** is now a COLUMN, not just a filter — COD and prepaid are not the same loss and should not read the same, so each row carries a chip (amber COD / emerald Prepaid). 📉 **New Lost tab** (`GET /api/lost-shipments`, panel `claims-panel-lost`): shipments the courier wrote off — no delivery, no return. KPIs split the loss the way it actually lands: **goods value**, **prepaid value (a refund we still owe)**, and **freight paid anyway**; COD loses the goods only, since nothing was collected. A **Days silent** column counts from the last scan, or from dispatch when tracking never started. ⚠ **Windowed on `order_date`, not a terminal date** — a lost parcel has neither `delivered_at` nor `rto_at`, which is precisely what makes it lost, and most have no `last_scan_at` either; order date is the only timestamp every one of them has, and it is the basis the Silent-RTO tab already uses. **Live: 16 lost — 11 COD, 5 prepaid, ₹12,067 of goods (₹3,889 of it prepaid and refundable), ₹1,180 of freight paid on parcels that never arrived, worst 119 days silent.** 📗 **Excel export** (`GET /api/claims/export.xlsx?tab=…`, exceljs) on every tab, honouring the current filters. ⚠⚠ **The rows are RE-FETCHED ON THE SERVER, never taken from the rendered table** — the PG-recon export was built from the page and silently produced 5,000 of 5,592 rows, which is worse than no export because it looks complete. Dates go in as **real dates** and money as **numbers** (a date-shaped string cannot be sorted or filtered, which is most of the reason to want a spreadsheet), with a title band naming the window and the filters applied, frozen headers and an autofilter. 📝 **Remarks column — why it came back.** “RTO” is an outcome, not a reason, and a claim list that says only that is unarguable with a courier. The reason IS in the scan log, and the vocabulary was counted across **4,175 real RTOs** rather than guessed: `Consignee refused to accept/order cancelled` (65) · `Returned as per Client Instructions` (37) · `Undelivered` (21) · `Returned as per Security Instructions` (17) · `Code verified cancellation` (5) · `Consignee verified cancellation` (2) · ageing-limit and poor-packaging returns. ⚠⚠ **THE FOUR MOST COMMON SCANS ON AN RTO ARE MOVEMENT, NOT REASON** — `return_received` (198), `return_expected` (177), `Dispatched for RTO` (112), `RETURN Accepted` (93). “Take the last RTO-ish scan” would therefore label almost every claim `return_received`, which is worse than blank because it reads as an answer; `MOVEMENT_SCAN` rejects them before any cause pattern is tried, and the reasons are ranked most-specific-first. ⚠ **A blank says WHICH kind of blank it is:** “No scan log stored” vs “No reason in the scan log” — the first is our data gap, the second is the courier’s silence, and they are argued differently. Live on 205 silent-RTOs: 77 no log, 68 log-but-no-reason, 60 with a real reason. Scan logs (~70 entries each) are fetched **only for an export**, chunked 200 AWBs at a time, so no page load carries them. ⚠⚠ **THE DOWNLOAD MUST BE A fetch(), NOT AN <a href> (fixed same day).** This app authenticates with a **Bearer token in a header**, and a link navigation carries no headers — so the first version requested the endpoint unauthenticated, got a 401, and the browser offered to save the ERROR BODY as a file named `export` with no extension (Windows called it a “CUSTOMIZATION File”). A failed download that still produces a file is the worst kind, because it looks like it worked. It now fetches with `getAuthHeaders()`, checks `r.ok`, surfaces the server’s JSON error in a toast, and saves a Blob under the filename from `Content-Disposition` — set explicitly, because an empty `download=""` lets the browser guess and it guesses badly here. ⚠ **AND THE ROUTES WERE UNGATED.** An unlisted path falls through to `next()` in `_VIEW_PERMS`, i.e. **any signed-in user whatever their role**. `/first-ofd-late` had been unlisted since the day it shipped; `/lost-shipments` and `/claims/export.xlsx` were new. The export is the one that mattered — it hands over the whole claims book (customer city and state, order values, freight) in a single file. All three are now behind `claims-sla`, and a selftest **replays the REAL gate table** against every claims path rather than trusting the list by eye. ⚠ **And the header band was painted across the whole sheet.** ExcelJS treats row.fill as a ROW-level style, which Excel applies to all 16,384 columns — the indigo band ran off past column AH into empty cells. The fill, font and alignment are now set per CELL, bounded by the column count; verified by reading the generated file back (fill present at columns 1–16, none at 17 or 34, and no row-level fill left on the row). ⚠ **Lost rows would not expand.** The tab was registered in every other registry and MISSED in the row-click list, so the handler never bound — while all the detail-row code existed and looked correct. The list is now driven off `_CLAIMS_TBODY`, so a new tab cannot be half-wired again. Two more surfaced with it: the detail panel’s `colspan` was pinned at **7** while Silent-RTO had grown to 8 columns and Lost arrived with 9 (it is now COUNTED from the table’s own `thead`), and the timeline offered a greyed-out “Delivered” step for a parcel that will never arrive — a lost shipment now ends at **Last scan** plus an explicit “Written off — lost, never delivered or returned · Nd silent” line.
  - **⚠⚠ RAPIDSHYP CHARGES WERE READ ONCE PER SHIPMENT AND NEVER RE-READ — RTO FREIGHT UNDERSTATED BY ~₹25,000 (2026-08-22).** Reported as “after RTO initiate, COD charges should not apply” → “the RTO fee is missing, that is incorrect” → **“don’t wait for RapidShyp, their portal already shows the RTO charge”**. That last correction was the right one, and it killed two of my wrong theories. **Wrong #1: “RapidShyp is over-charging, ₹27,130 claimable.”** **Wrong #2: “they bill the return leg up to 45 days later.”** ✅ **The truth, from their live API:** one day after an RTO, TE25-42151 read `rto ₹50.74 / cod ₹0 / total ₹101.48` on RapidShyp while our copy said `rto ₹0 / cod ₹29.50 / total ₹80.24`. They re-price within HOURS: the return leg is added and the COD fee REMOVED (nothing was collected). ⚠⚠ **THE LAG WAS OURS.** `syncChargesBatch` selected on `charges_fetched_at IS NULL` — each shipment was priced **once, ever**, and after that stamp never looked at again, so any parcel that turned around AFTER being priced kept its pre-RTO snapshot for good. That is why 982 RTOs showed no return leg and a phantom COD fee. ✅ **Fix:** the batch now also re-prices by SHAPE — `outcome='rto' AND freight_rto = 0`, oldest RTO first, past a 6-hour grace window (the only case where a missing leg is genuinely not posted yet). A failure on that second query is warned and swallowed so brand-new shipments still price. **Backfill run: 1,161 shipments re-priced.** RTO charge shapes went from **70% with a return leg → 99.3%**; COD fees sitting on RTOs fell from **₹28,422.53 → ₹551.77**; return legs now total ₹1,76,367.91. The 24 still legless are ones where RapidShyp itself reports none (verified against their API at 71 and 89 days). 🧾 **UI, corrected twice:** the flag is now `rto_leg_stale` (“Return leg not synced”, amber) meaning **our copy is behind**, not that money is in dispute — with the forward leg used to estimate the understatement — and `cod_on_rto` now reads “Stale COD fee · pre-RTO figure”. The claims detail panel had also been lying by omission: forward ₹50.74 + RTO ₹0 against a total of ₹80.24, never mentioning the ₹29.50. It now names the COD fee, shows “RTO freight: **not synced yet**” rather than a bare ₹0, and marks the total “(understated — return leg not synced)”. ℹ️ Billed figures are still never rewritten locally — the correction comes from **re-reading their API**, which is the only source entitled to change them. ⚠⚠ **AND THE WARNING ITSELF THEN FIRED ON CORRECT DATA — fixed the same day.** After the backfill the claims KPI still read “8 return legs not synced yet”. It was flagging on SHAPE alone (an RTO with no return leg), and **all 24 remaining legless RTOs had already been re-read after their RTO and matched RapidShyp exactly** — checked one by one against their API, 0.0d to 89.0d old. Some returns simply carry no leg. **A warning that fires on correct data is worse than no warning, because it teaches people to ignore it.** The honest test is whether our charge snapshot PREDATES the return: `charges_fetched_at > rto_at` means we have looked since, so a missing leg is THEIR figure, not our lag. Applied identically in three places — the recon flag, the claims detail panel and the claims KPI line — so they cannot disagree. Result: **0 false warnings**, and `cod_on_rto` now means something real (re-read since the return and RapidShyp STILL bills a COD fee with no return leg: **18 shipments, ₹525.81**, worth asking them about). ⚠ **The re-price queue is also self-closing.** Selecting on shape alone would re-read those settled shipments from RapidShyp’s API every night for ever; it now filters to rows whose charge snapshot predates the RTO (done in JS — PostgREST cannot compare two columns) and over-fetches 4× to absorb the settled ones. Proven: run 1 priced 5, run 2 priced **0**. ⚠ **THAT SELF-CLOSING RULE WAS THEN TOO EAGER, AND IT WOULD HAVE LEFT MONEY UNBILLED.** It stopped re-reading after the first post-RTO read — but RapidShyp charges the return leg when the parcel actually gets BACK, days later: TE25-40292 and TE25-41443 were still `IN_TRANSIT` four and five days after their RTO with no leg yet, and a queue that stopped would never have seen it land. Now it re-checks **at most once a day** and gives up after `RTO_RECHECK_MAX_DAYS = 60` — bounded either way at roughly 20 calls a night, and by 60 days the answer is final (verified against their API on returns at 54, 71 and 89 days, all legless and all matching our copy).
  - **🧾 A COD FEE ON A *SILENT* RTO IS THE EASIEST LINE IN THE CLAIM, AND IT WAS BURIED (2026-08-22).** Reported with seven order numbers: TE25-42225 · 41443 · 40292 · 40008 · 39825 · 38695 · 29893 — “COD charges applied but RTO charge is not”. All seven were re-checked against RapidShyp’s live API and **our figures match theirs exactly**, so this is not a data fault: RapidShyp really has billed a collection fee and no return leg. On a **silent** RTO that is indefensible — nobody attempted delivery, so nobody collected cash, so a collection fee cannot be due. ✅ The fee is now **itemised instead of hidden inside the freight total**: `silentRtoSummary` returns `codCount` / `totalCod` (live: **7 of 205, ₹207.68**), the claims table gained separate **RTO freight** and **COD fee** columns so “charged for collection, not charged for the return” is visible at a glance (COD in amber, a missing leg as a grey dash), and the claim mail to RapidShyp carries a COD column, a total row, and a sentence saying **why** it is not due rather than sending them a lump sum to argue with. ⚠ Adding two columns moved the empty-state colspan from 8 to 10 in both the template and the renderer — a selftest pins both, since a short colspan is invisible until the table happens to be empty.
  - **Manual WhatsApp sends from the Call Queue popup — template sequences as DATA (2026-08-24, supersedes the automatic sender built earlier the same day).** The plan changed on explicit instruction: **messages never go out automatically** — the orders/create trigger and the 15-min backstop cron were REMOVED (a selftest asserts they stay dead). Instead: the team calls the customer; on no-answer they open the order popup (Hold Orders / Undelivered) and press **one button** that sends the **next version of a template sequence** — V1 first, then V2, then V3, then the button retires to “all sent” chips. ⚠ **“Next” is decided by the SERVER from the send log** (`wa_sends_msg91`, UNIQUE(order, sequence, version)), so a stale popup or two agents on the same order cannot double-send — the unique row is the turnstile, and a FAILED send frees its slot so the same version can be retried. 🧩 **Templates are ROWS, not code** (`wa_template_sequences_msg91`): sequence_key, version, MSG91 template name, its **exact language code** (the en/en_GB lesson), namespace, a `variables` array naming which order fields fill body_1..N, and `body_text` ({{1}}..{{N}}) for the preview. **Adding V2/V3 or an entirely new sequence is an INSERT** — never a deploy. The field vocabulary (customer_name, product, order_name, amount, phone, awb, courier, tracking_status) is resolved server-side in `msg91_wa.js`; phone prefers the **shipping-address** number — `orders.phone` was null on the very first order tried. 🔍 **A rendered preview gates every send** (asked for explicitly): the button opens a popup showing the exact message with this order’s real values, the destination number and the template name; only “Send Vn now” fires it. Preview and send share one variable mapping, so they cannot disagree. Endpoints `/support/wa/state|preview|send` under the existing support permission gate. ⚠ The MSG91_COD_ALLOWLIST guard applies to manual sends too — seeded row: the proven `cod_confirmation_v1` (en_GB, tested live). **Rejected COD tab unchanged.** ⚠⚠ **THE ESCALATION GATE (same day, on feedback):** V1 is free; **V(n+1) unlocks only after a no-answer call is logged since V(n) was sent** (`NO_CONTACT_OUTCOMES` — currently `['no_answer']`) — the operator’s stated flow “call → no pick → V1; call again → still nothing → V2” encoded server-side, so the sequence cannot be rushed: a send while locked answers **HTTP 423** with the reason, proven live. UI states are honest: green “send Vn”, amber “Vn locked — log a no-answer call”, and a “n/N sent” chip — the earlier “all sent” with only V1 configured read as if V2/V3 had happened (user-reported). Buttons live in the popup **header** beside “Log a call” (bottom placement rejected). **Logging a call no longer rebuilds the modal**: the entry is prepended to the history card in place — like Hold/Release — and the WhatsApp buttons re-evaluate, since a no-answer log is exactly what unlocks the next version. ⚠ A build mishap recurred: TWO more app.js truncations from patch scripts whose `open('wb')` preceded a failing `.encode()` (lone-surrogate emoji escapes); restored from git both times, every script now encodes-then-opens and asserts growth.
  - **Login form: no password-strength meter (2026-08-26).** The meter was bound to the SIGN-IN password field — browser autofill made it pop up “Strong” on a fresh logout screen (user-reported). You don’t rate an existing password at login; the meter stays on signup / forgot-reset / admin-reset only. Also: OTP template upgraded to dashboard_otp_v2 (Copy code + security line + 5-min expiry — the only design WhatsApp permits for Authentication category; Utility-category OTPs are a Meta-pausing time bomb and were advised against).
  - **Dashboard OTPs go WhatsApp-first (2026-08-26).** Login 2FA, resend and forgot-password all flow through the one choke point (`otp_mail.sendOtp`), which now tries the user’s stored mobile (`app_users_ecom.mobile` — all 8 users have one) via the new `dashboard_otp_v1` AUTHENTICATION template (WhatsApp’s fixed format: code in body_1 AND the copy-code button; language `en`, no namespace) and falls back to the existing email automatically on any failure — a template hiccup can never lock the team out. 2FA counts as configured if EITHER channel works. The client OTP screen and forgot-password message say where the code went (“WhatsApp +91 ••••4108”). ⚠ Staff numbers are deliberately NOT gated by MSG91_COD_ALLOWLIST — that list protects customers; logins must work for the whole team. Proven live: real OTP delivered to the admin’s WhatsApp with the copy button.
  - **📋 THE WHATSAPP LADDER, RESTATED BY THE USER (2026-08-27) — and the one change it needed.** Spec, verbatim in shape: **COD** — `cod_confirmation_v1` **3 minutes after order create** → `cod_confirm_reminder_v1` 30 min later if no reply → on a HELD order the manual `cod_confirm_missed_call_v1` button (one use, then disabled) → which enables `cod_order_cancelled_unconfirmed_v1` (one use, then disabled). **NDR** — `ndr_msg` after the 1st failed attempt → `ndr_final_attempt_v1` after the 2nd → `order_rto_v1` once RTO is initiated. Every send logged. All of it already matched except V1, which was the INSTANT `cod_confirmation_v2`: **(1) the registry row for `cod_auto` V1 is now `cod_confirmation_v1`** (same four placeholders in the same order, so `variables` is untouched; body copied from the catalog; migration `20260827_wa_cod_v1_template.sql`, applied) and **(2) it fires THREE MINUTES after `orders/create`, not instantly** — `autoCodOnCreate()` arms an in-process timer (`COD_V1_DELAY_MS`), and `sendCodV1()` **re-reads the order first** so a cancellation or a payment inside those minutes wins. ⚠️ A timer dies with a pm2 restart, so **`codInitialTick()` runs on the same `*/5` cron as the reminder** and picks up COD orders created 3–60 min ago with no V1 row (60 min = the age seal; the UNIQUE turnstile makes timer + backstop unable to double-send). The manual hold buttons already behave as specified — the button IS the progression: after a send the emerald button becomes a "V1 sent" chip and the next version's button appears; after the last, an "n/N sent" chip. ⚠️ **Cut-over note sharpened:** our V1 is now the SAME template n8n sends on order create, so with the allowlist removed and n8n still on, a real customer would get it twice — switch n8n off in the same moment.
  - **⚡ AUTOMATIC WhatsApp IS BACK, BY PLAN (2026-08-26, whiteboard session → explicit spec).** Three new sequences (migration `20260826 wa_auto_sequences`, registry rows carry `mode`/`gate`/`requires_hold`): **(1) `cod_auto`** — V1 `cod_confirmation_v2` INSTANT on Shopify orders/create (COD only: financial_status=pending, not test/cancelled; hooked back into webhook_handler); V2 `cod_confirm_reminder_v1` 30 min later if NO reply of any kind — and the reply must be NEWER than the V1 send (an old reply from the same phone about a previous order must not silence reminders forever); 6h ceiling = age seal. **(2) `cod_hold`** (manual buttons): `cod_confirm_missed_call_v1` → `cod_order_cancelled_unconfirmed_v1`, sequential unlock (gate=seq, no call requirement), buttons EXIST only while the order is held on Shopify (server-checked via getHoldStates; send route refuses otherwise). The cancelled button ONLY SENDS THE MESSAGE — it does not cancel on Shopify (deliberate default; wire it later if asked). **(3) `ndr_auto`** — from `shipment_journey_ecom`: ndr_count≥1 → `ndr_msg`, ≥2 → `ndr_final_attempt_v1`, RTO (outcome/rto_at/status RTO%) → `order_rto_v1`; ndr_reason = latest sensible entry from ndr_reasons (reattempt/instruction noise → generic). **Engines:** `performAutoSend` is the single choke point — allowlist INSIDE it (MSG91_COD_ALLOWLIST gates everything “till test is done”), UNIQUE-row turnstile, every send logged to wa_sends_msg91 (sent_by=auto). Crons: reminder */5, NDR */15. ⚠ **NDR bootstrap seeded 1,008 pre-existing trigger states as ‘seeded’ on first run — history is never messaged** (the 41-customer lesson, structural). **Proven live:** instant V1 delivered to the allowlisted phone; backdated V1 → reminder V2 delivered (recency rule exercised — older replies did not block); prepaid skipped; both re-ticks silent. UI: auto sequences show receipts only (never a send button); hold-gated buttons vanish on un-held orders. ⚠ **CUT-OVER NOTE: n8n still sends cod_confirmation_v1 to every real customer on order create — at cut-over, n8n OFF and allowlist removal must happen in the same moment, or customers get both.**
  - **The order popup gets a real WhatsApp chat, and every sent chip becomes a receipt (2026-08-24, same-day follow-up).** Two asks: “V1 sent — make clickable to view what actually sent” and fix the permanently-empty “Recent MSG91 messages” card. ⚠ **Why the card was ALWAYS “No messages”:** it queried `msg91_messages` by `orders.phone` — null on exactly these orders (the same shipping-address-phone trap already fixed once in `msg91_wa.js`). And one store can never show the conversation anyway: `msg91_messages` (243k rows) is OUTBOUND-ONLY — `direction` is always `1`, replies never land there — while the customer’s CONFIRM/REJECT taps live only in `cod_confirmations_msg91` (one row per order, latest reply wins — a REJECT followed by a CONFIRM keeps only the CONFIRM). **New `GET /support/wa/chat?order=` merges the only three stores that hold pieces of the thread:** the MSG91 outbound mirror (n8n-era sends included), `wa_sends_msg91` (our manual sends, rendered from the send-time field snapshot), and the confirmations (inbound, with a CONFIRM/REJECT chip — matching the stored vocabulary `CONFIRMED`/`CANCEL`, not the button labels). Merged **by PHONE, not by order** — a conversation belongs to the customer, and the reply about order A arriving while order B’s popup is open must still be visible. Our own send and its sync mirror are the same message, so a ±5-min same-template window dedupes them. **UI — both halves of the user’s either/or, on their request for a suggestion:** the card is now a compact WhatsApp-shaped chat (outbound right/green, replies left/white, scrolled to newest) AND a “Full chat ↗” button opens the complete customer history in a popup. **Clicking a “Vn sent” chip shows the exact message that went out** — rendered from the fields snapshotted in `wa_sends_msg91.payload` at send time, so it stays true even after the order data changes; sends predating the snapshot say so instead of guessing. ⚠ Four wanted utility classes (`max-w-[85%]`, `rounded-bl-sm`/`rounded-br-sm`, `justify-start`) are NOT in the prebuilt tailwind.css — caught by grep before shipping, done as inline styles. **Same-day follow-ups:** “I want to see complete chat, not (text not stored)” — the msg91-sync stopped copying variables into the `content` column on newer rows, but they always ride in `raw_data.content`; the chat now reads both, and any template whose `body_text` is in the registry renders the COMPLETE sentence no matter who sent it (our button, n8n, a campaign) — templates without a registry body (e.g. `order_placed`) show their variable values, and pasting their body into a registry row upgrades them to full text with zero code. When a send and its sync mirror merge, the send-time render outranks bare variables. ⚠ MSG91 exposes NO template-list API on the authkey (`client-panel-template` is the CREATE endpoint — probed live, 404/405/400 chain) — template bodies must be pasted into the registry by hand. **And placement, corrected twice on instruction:** not at the modal bottom, and not in the top header — the WhatsApp send button + sent chips now live INSIDE the WhatsApp chat card, with the conversation they belong to. ⚠ **And the in-place call-log row was born BLANK** (empty outcome chip, “just now”, no notes): the save handler read the form fields AFTER `close()` had removed the modal from the DOM. Values are now captured before anything closes, and the fresh row renders exactly as it will after a reload — outcome, agent, real timestamp, notes, follow-up. **V3 exists only when it exists:** the send button offers versions from the registry; after “2/2 sent” there is no V3 because no `cod_confirmation_v3` template was ever created on MSG91 (checked: zero sends in 243k messages) — create it in the MSG91 panel, then one registry INSERT makes the button appear, already unlocked by the logged no-answer call. 🔓 **THE TEMPLATE-LIST API EXISTS AFTER ALL — and the chat now shows the REAL registered message for every template (2026-08-24, “i want real template”):** the endpoint the docs hide is `GET https://control.msg91.com/api/v5/whatsapp/get-template-client/:integrated_number` with the authkey header — recovered by grepping the docs page SOURCE (docs.msg91.com/whatsapp/get-templates is an SPA; every api.msg91.com guess 404s, and `client-panel-template` is the CREATE endpoint). It returns all 182 templates with their registered components. `refreshTemplateCatalog()` in msg91_wa.js syncs body+footer+status into **`msg91_template_catalog`** (migration `20260824_msg91_template_catalog.sql`, applied) — lazy, at most 6-hourly, failure only logs (the chat renders from the last sync when MSG91 is down); MSG91 lists a name+language twice when a rejected draft sits beside the approved revision, so the batch is deduped (approved wins) or Postgres refuses the whole upsert. The catalog OVERRIDES registry body_text when rendering the chat, so `order_placed`, the n8n-era `cod_confirmation` and every other known template now show the exact customer-received message; cod_confirmation_v2’s registry placeholder was replaced with its real body (“We are trying to reach you regarding your order…” — note its MSG91 status is **pending**, though sends deliver). Sends predating field snapshots render with the order’s CURRENT fields instead of “(text not stored)”. Bubbles render WhatsApp `*bold*` (escape first, then substitute). **And the in-place call-log row was born BLANK, fixed:** see above.
  - **Rejected COD — a new Call Queue tab for the customer’s written “no” (2026-08-24).** The MSG91 WhatsApp COD-confirmation template now carries CONFIRM / REJECT quick-reply buttons; the existing `msg91-cod-webhook` edge fn already classifies both words, so **CONFIRM needed no work** (records `msg91_confirmed`, order flows normally). **REJECT → new “Rejected COD” tab in the Call Queue** (`GET /support/queue?tab=rejected`): CANCEL rows from `cod_confirmations_msg91` windowed on the reply time, joined to `order_buckets` for order context. **By explicit instruction the tab takes NO automatic action** — no hold, no Shopify cancel — it exists so the team sees every rejection (shipping a told-you-so parcel is a guaranteed RTO); the row offers the usual Hold control and notes for whatever the team decides. Each row shows the rejection time and the customer’s **verbatim reply** (“Cancel my order”, “No thanks”, “Stop”) — the reply text is the evidence. ⚠ A rejection the webhook could not pin to an order (id_key `PHONE:…`) still gets a row marked **unmatched** with the phone — an invisible rejection is how the parcel ships anyway. Live at build: **84 rejections in 60 days, 32 of them phone-only.** ⚠⚠ **A build mishap worth recording:** a patch script with a lone-surrogate emoji escape truncated `app.js` to 0 bytes — `open(… ’wb’)` had already truncated before `.encode()` raised. Restored from git and re-applied; every doc/patch script now encodes to bytes BEFORE opening the file and asserts the size.
  - **The switch that sticks, the goodbye that listens, and the language OFFER (2026-08-26, from a real Punjabi-refusal call).** **(1) Language switch hardened at three levels:** “पंजाबी में बताओ” now detected first-try (बता/बोलो joined the cues); the “Respond ONLY in {lang}” line no longer licenses refusal (“switching is REQUIRED, never refused”); and after a server-side switch a LANGUAGE OVERRIDE block breaks history momentum — a real call had switched to Punjabi while the model kept answering in Hindi; live test now replies in Gurmukhi. **(2) Screener handover greets first:** after the screening assistant, sentence one is ONLY greeting + introduction; order context comes next — never all in one breath. **(3) Goodbye grace** (user spec): closing audio plays out, then a ~5s listening window (`scheduleGoodbyeCut`: 8s from chunks-stop); a customer mid-sentence (VAD active) is NEVER cut — their finished sentence routes through post-close logic (thanks → polite hangup, question → reopen, timer cancelled); pure silence → cut. **(4) LANGUAGE OFFER:** a customer merely REPLYING in another script (`scriptLangOf`: Bengali/Gurmukhi/Gujarati/Tamil/Telugu/Kannada/Malayalam ranges, ≥3 letters; Devanagari never offered — Hindi vs Marathi is not decidable by script) gets ONE polite question in BOTH languages (live: “क्या आप Bengali में बात करना चाहेंगे? নাকি Hindi में जारी রखें?”); a short multi-script yes (`AFFIRM_RX`) triggers the full switch; an explicit ask still switches instantly; a substantive continuation lapses the offer. **(5)** Stage directions are scrubbed from TRANSCRIPTS too (sanitizeReply drops parentheticals).
  - **The countdown ladder: the deadline is about the REAL customer talking (2026-08-26, after a screened call died at 15s BEFORE Apple’s prompt even started).** Dead air → 15s hangup (unchanged); voice activity but nothing transcribed (`sawVoice` via vad.speech_start) → 30s; **screening assistant detected** (`SCREENER_RX`: name-and-reason / stay-on-the-line phrases, Devanagari transliterations included — unit-tested on the real call’s exact lines) → 60s cap waiting for the human; **only a genuine human utterance cancels the countdown** — the screener’s speech is progress, never presence, and the hello-check is suppressed while a screener is mid-flow. **Opening hardening from the same failed call** (its transcript had NO opening): pre-synth missing AND fallback chat turn failing now drops to a last-resort direct line — the agent can never open with silence.
  - **iPhone call screening handled (2026-08-26, from a real call to 9167770975).** Apple’s screening assistant answered (“your name and reason for calling… please stay on the line”) and the agent pitched the confirm question at the ROBOT. New CALL SCREENING protocol in the prompt: reply in the assistant’s language with ONE sentence — name, brand, reason (“This is Kavya from The Element, calling {firstName} about their order confirmation”) — then stop speaking and wait silently; when the real person says hello, greet fresh and run the call normally. Vendor-agnostic (keys on the ask-for-name-and-reason pattern, not Apple specifically). **Replayed the exact screener dialogue against the live model: name+brand stated, no confirm pitch at the robot, patient hold, fresh greeting to the human.** ⚠ The test also caught the model emitting “(wait patiently)” as literal text — `toSpokenText` now drops parenthetical stage directions whole; they are never speech.
  - **Silent line → hello-check → 15s auto-hangup (2026-08-26).** If the customer makes NO sound after the opening, the agent does not push the script: at ~9s it asks “हेलो? क्या आपको मेरी आवाज़ आ रही है?” in the CALL’S language (`HELLO_CHECK` — vetted lines for all ten), and at 15s from call start it hangs up with “[no response from customer — call auto-ended]” in the transcript. Any transcript — hums included — marks PRESENCE and cancels the timers (presence is marked BEFORE the filler filter ignores the hum as a turn, so a hum proves a person without counting as a yes). `sayLine()` speaks direct lines outside the chat loop (REST TTS → playToCaller). **Proven with a silent-customer sim:** opening t+1.7s → hello-check t+10s → auto-ended t+15.7s.
  - **A hum is not a yes (2026-08-26, live-testing feedback).** The agent was accepting humming (हम्म / hmm / हूँ) as order confirmation. Two layers now: **(1) `FILLER_RX` in the bridge’s onCustomer** — pure backchannel sounds (हम्म incl. chained हम्मम्म, hmm, umm, हूँ) are logged as “filler ignored” and never become a turn; a bare हाँ / जी हाँ / yes still passes (unit-tested 7/7). **(2) CONFIRMATION DISCIPLINE in every prompt** (bridge + browser tool): listening sounds while explaining are NOT confirmation — only a clear affirmative given AFTER the confirm question counts; unclear → one polite re-ask for a clear हाँ या ना, never assume agreement. Also same day: allowlist extended for live testing (9167770975 + TE25-44758, messages AND calls); noted that `vobizConfigured()` also requires SARVAM_API_KEY though the error text lists only VOBIZ_* — the live “Vobiz not configured” was a missing env var on the VPS.
  - **AI CALL BUTTON ON THE PANELS + region language + auto-summary + in-dashboard recording playback + mid-call language switch (2026-08-25, late).** **(1) 🤖 AI Call button** in the order popup — the PANEL decides the purpose (`SUP_AI_CALL_BY_TAB`): Hold Orders → `cod_confirm`, Undelivered → `undelivered` (failed-delivery follow-up), Rejected COD → `cod_rejected` (verify the written rejection, never pressure); server enforces the same purpose in the prompt (`PURPOSES` in vobiz_bridge.js) and the perm rule widened to `['support-voice','support-queue']`. supConfirm flow with busy/success states; logs land as `{type}_vobiz`. **(2) REGION → LANGUAGE:** when no lang is passed, the order’s shipping `province` picks the call language (`REGION_LANG`: TN→Tamil, KA→Kannada, KL→Malayalam, AP/TS→Telugu, WB→Bengali, MH→Marathi, GJ→Gujarati, PB→Punjabi, else Hindi); openings stay pre-synthesized only for vetted hi/en templates — other languages open via the live turn (slower but never mistranslated). **(3) AUTO-CAPTURED OUTCOME:** on hangup a small model pass writes the summary (“OUTCOME: confirmed/wants cancel/will reattempt… + follow-up”), mechanical line kept as fallback — a summarizer outage never loses a log. **(4) RECORDING ON THE DASHBOARD:** the record-API url is captured onto the session → saved to `agent_call_logs.recording_url` (migration applied) → ▶ Play on the Call Logs cards, streamed through `GET /api/vobiz/recording` (Vobiz auth stays server-side; only *.vobiz.ai proxied; fetch→blob because <audio src> cannot carry a bearer token). **(5) MID-CALL LANGUAGE SWITCH** (“if customer ask for another language you should change immediately”): server-side detector (language NAME + speak/understand CUE in one utterance — unit-tested incl. false-positive traps) flips every layer together: STT live `config.update`, prompt via s.lang next turn, next TTS socket in the new language; plus a prompt safety net for phrasings the regex misses. ⚠ Panel-button E2E locally still needs the tunnel pointed at the MAIN server; on the live VPS it just works. **Same night:** the order popup’s Call history now shows the REAL AI calls too — `/support/order/:id` returns `ai_calls` (agent_call_logs by order name) rendered as 🤖 cards with the auto-summary, an expandable transcript, and ▶ recording playback through the /api/vobiz/recording proxy (main CSP gained `media-src blob:` — an <audio src> cannot carry the bearer token, so fetch→blob). Also fixed from live clicks: the button sends only order_name — the route now resolves the PHONE from the order (address-first) instead of demanding it; and the Hold-panel purpose asks “did you place this order?”, never delivery availability (a held order is pre-dispatch — the user caught the agent asking availability).
  - **🎉 FIRST SUCCESSFUL REAL AI PHONE CALL (2026-08-25 evening) — and the four wire-format lessons it took.** Tested LOCALLY (user: “we want to do local test first”): minimal host (`tests/vobiz_inbound_host.js`, :4545) + a public tunnel; outbound call placed via the trial account (“call queued” worked on trial; inbound is blocked — “Trial numbers cannot be linked to a trunk or application”, needs first recharge). Seven calls to get there, each failure isolating one link: **(1) trycloudflare.com is ISP-blocked in India** — Vobiz answered then hung up with hangup_source=Error, our webhook never fetched; switched to pinggy (`ssh -p 443 -R0:localhost:4545 a.pinggy.io`, Indian service) and the webhook arrived. **(2) Raw & in XML** — the wss URL’s `?token=..&sid=..` made the whole document invalid XML; Vobiz’s parser rejected it and cut the call 3s after answer; `&amp;` fixed it and the media WS connected. **(3) The docs’ concepts page is STALE — the real wire format is Plivo-style event-keyed:** receive `{event:"start",start:{streamId..}}` / `{event:"media",media:{payload..}}` (not type-keyed), and SEND must be `{event:"playAudio",streamId,media:{contentType,sampleRate,payload}}` with the streamId from the start event, in ~60ms slices — our type-keyed, streamId-less playAudio was ignored SILENTLY (caller heard nothing while the whole pipeline ran and even transcribed “मुझे आपका आवाज नहीं आ रहा है”). L16 @24k IS accepted. **(4) The goodbye ends the CALL** (user: “customer thank you mean call should be end” — the first success kept trading pleasantries): once the brand closing is spoken, a short thanks/bye hangs up (800ms), silence hangs up after 9s, a real question still gets answered. **Winning call (56s, real order TE25-44697, saved to agent_call_logs):** greeting → “क्या आपने 348 रुपये का Salicylic Acid फेस वॉश ऑर्डर किया है?” → availability → brand closing verbatim; barge-in fired correctly mid-call. **Opening latency** (user: “kavya starts after 6–7s — make it max 2nd second”): the greeting is TEMPLATED (no LLM) and its audio is PRE-SYNTHESIZED while the phone is still ringing (`armOpening()` at call placement AND on the inbound webhook); on the start event it plays from memory — fallback to a live turn if pre-synth is not ready in 3s. **Recording:** `<Record recordSession playBeep=false/>` now precedes the Stream on both paths (shows in the Vobiz console; `VOBIZ_RECORD=false` disables). The simulator was updated to speak the REAL wire format. **Round 2 (same evening):** recording moved from the XML verb to the LIVE-CALL API (`POST /Call/{uuid}/Record/`, fired on the start event) — the verb recorded ONLY the agent leg (user listened: customer silent) and sat in the XML critical path; API answered 202 “call recording started” live. **Measured latency split** (timeline stamps): answer-webhook → stream connect ≈ 3.0s (Vobiz internal setup + tunnel wss dial — unchanged with/without the Record verb), stream → first audio 0.3s (ours). The ~6s the user feels is dominated by Vobiz setup + BOTH tunnel legs; on the live VPS expect ≈ 2s total. **Objection handling proven live:** “मैं बाहर हूँ” → agent asked cancel? → “मुझे मेरा ऑर्डर चाहिए” → confirmed delivery + brand close + auto-hangup. ⚠ Production path unchanged: deploy to the live VPS (no tunnel), recharge Vobiz to link the number for INCOMING calls.
  - **REAL PHONE CALLS — Vobiz ⇄ Sarvam bridge (2026-08-25, “i just create account on vobiz… test a real call”).** New `app/api/vobiz_bridge.js`: the browser agent’s whole Sarvam pipeline, server-side, on a live PSTN call. Flow: `POST /api/vobiz/call` (support-voice perm; body phone/order_name/lang/voice) → Vobiz outbound Call API (X-Auth-ID/Token) → on answer Vobiz fetches `/api/vobiz/answer` → our <Stream bidirectional> XML → Vobiz opens `wss …/api/vobiz/media` on OUR server (ws upgrade on the app’s http server). **The formats align on both legs so the bridge forwards base64, never transcodes:** Vobiz media L16@16k = Sarvam realtime STT’s linear16; bulbul linear16@24k = Vobiz playAudio L16@24k. **Turn-taking is VAD** (endpointing=vad, silence 700ms — a phone call has no push-to-talk); a `vad.speech_start` while the agent talks = barge-in → `{"type":"clearAudio"}` + abort the chat/TTS turn. Transcript saved to agent_call_logs on hangup (call_type `cod_confirm_vobiz`). ⚠ **Safety:** the SAME `MSG91_COD_ALLOWLIST` gates who can be rung while testing; webhooks/WS are public-path but refuse a wrong `VOBIZ_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` (<Hangup/>). ⚠ `VOBIZ_L16_SWAP` (in|out|both) exists because “L16” classically means big-endian while Sarvam speaks little-endian — flip it if first-call transcripts are garbage, no code change. ⚠ Prompts are a compact server-side copy of voice-agent.html’s rules (spoken style, persona, brand closing) — drift risk noted in the file header. **Env (staging .env has commented placeholders + generated webhook token):** VOBIZ_AUTH_ID, VOBIZ_AUTH_TOKEN, VOBIZ_FROM_NUMBER, VOBIZ_PUBLIC_BASE (https://dashboard.theelement.skin), VOBIZ_WEBHOOK_TOKEN, VOBIZ_L16_SWAP. **LOCAL SIMULATOR (`tests/vobiz_call_sim.js`, run: node tests/vobiz_call_sim.js):** stands in for Vobiz byte-for-byte — minimal server with only the bridge, same WS URL/token/start message, a SYNTHESIZED customer streamed as 20ms L16 frames at real-time pace (VAD needs real pacing), every playAudio frame recorded to `tests/out/vobiz_sim_agent.wav` to LISTEN to. First full run: agent opened 1.3s after answer, both customer turns transcribed exactly, correct time-question / subject-ful confirm / brand close, 26.4s agent audio, call logged to agent_call_logs (cod_confirm_vobiz). What only a real call can test: Vobiz’s telephony leg + L16 endianness. **Deploy note:** Vobiz must reach the webhooks + wss — that means the LIVE VPS, and the proxy in front must forward Upgrade headers (Traefik does by default). New dep: `ws`.
  - **Voice Agent grammar round 2 (2026-08-25, “बस दो मिनट का time है — this line is incorrect”).** The opening asked for time as a malformed STATEMENT; the rule now demands a proper QUESTION about THEIR time (“क्या आपके पास दो मिनट हैं?”) with the broken phrasing named verbatim as forbidden, plus a general GRAMMAR rule (well-formed natural sentences; time/availability asks are always polite questions). The same transcript showed the customer-gender guess AGAIN (रहेंगी) — the forbidden forms are now listed explicitly (होंगी/रहेंगी/करेंगी). Verified 3× live: every opening came back “क्या आपके पास दो मिनट हैं?”, zero gendered forms. **And the CLOSE is now the brand line, verbatim from the user:** “The Element को चुनने के लिए आपका धन्यवाद। आपका दिन शुभ हो।” (English: “Thank you for choosing The Element. Have a great day!”) — set as BOTH the CLOSING_LINE fallback and a persona CLOSING rule (“never a bare अच्छा दिन हो”); verified live, the model closes with the exact line.
  - **Voice Agent speaks each language in its OWN texture, and every sentence carries its subject (2026-08-25, “Aapka Order 3 से 5 दिन में… tone as per that language texture”).** From a real call that said a bare “पहुँच जाएगा”: **COMPLETE SENTENCES rule** — spoken words vanish, so every sentence must carry its subject (“आपका order 3 से 5 दिन में पहुँच जाएगा”). **And language texture became PER LANGUAGE** after a live pre-ship test caught the static “everyday Hinglish” rule dragging an ENGLISH call into Hindi: `agentPersona()` now branches — Hindi gets Hinglish + gendered agent forms + respectful-plural customer forms (रहेंगे/करेंगे/होंगे — a call had guessed होंगी); every other language gets its own native phone-call register, a hard “Speak {lang} ONLY” guard, and a language-matched honorific (“Sugandh ji” in Roman for English — the hardcoded जी was leaking Devanagari into English and Tamil turns). **Proven live:** English → pure Indian English; Tamil → colloquial phone-Tamil (“உஙக order பத்தி பேசத்தான் call பண்ணிருக்கேன்”); Hindi → neutral respectful forms; zero Devanagari outside Hindi.
  - **Voice Agent knows WHO is talking (2026-08-25, same day: “When I choose male to speak then why he tell रही हूँ”).** The picked voice is now the agent’s IDENTITY: `SPEAKER_INFO` maps all 10 bulbul:v3 speakers to name+gender and `agentPersona()` joins every system prompt — a male voice speaks male first-person forms (कर रहा हूँ), a female voice female forms, and the agent introduces itself by ITS first name. Also from the same transcript: the customer is addressed by FIRST name + जी only — the call had read out “Sugandh Kumar Mishra जी”, which no human caller does — and the model is told to acknowledge replies naturally (जी बिल्कुल, अच्छा) and mirror the customer’s tone. **Proven live against the real model:** rahul → “मैं Rahul बोल रहा हूँ”, priya → “मैं Priya बात कर रही हूँ”, full name never read out.
  - **Voice Agent stage 3 — it speaks like a person, not a chat window (2026-08-25, after a real test call: “text and speak mismatch… sound robotic… grammar”).** Four fixes from one transcript. **(1) The mismatch was a missing flag:** the streaming TTS config launched without `enable_preprocessing` (REST always had it), so digits, amounts and mixed English came out text-ish; both paths now carry it, plus the picked speaker and pace. **(2) `SPOKEN_STYLE` rules appended to every prompt** — the model’s words go straight to a synthesizer: no symbols/emoji/dashes/lists, NEVER read a full order ID (say “आपका order”), digits stay digits, everyday Hinglish, respectful gender-neutral verb forms with आप (the call guessed “रहेंगी”), one thought per turn, never repeat a sentence. **(3) `toSpokenText()` normalizes everything spoken** (em-dash → a breath; quotes/brackets/symbols vanish — verified on the exact utterances from the real call; the transcript bubble keeps the original text). **(4) Voice + Pace pickers** in setup (10 bulbul:v3 speakers, 0.9/1.0/1.1) — humanness is partly taste, so the user picks; smoke-tested live (simran + preprocessing + pace accepted, first audio 494ms). **And the goodbye no longer repeats:** the machinery-only fallback said the identical closing line twice in a row on the real call; if the previous agent turn already said that exact line, it varies instead.
  - **Voice Agent stage 2 — speech streams BOTH ways over WebSockets; first audio ~1.2s after release (2026-08-25, “delay still happen, i want more real conversation”).** The two remaining serial hops were post-release STT (~1–1.5s) and the first TTS (~1.65s). Both are now streams: **(1) LIVE TRANSCRIPTION** — while the mic button is held, raw 16 kHz PCM (ScriptProcessor capture + JS resample; `AudioContext({sampleRate})` is not honored everywhere) streams over `wss://api.sarvam.ai/speech-to-text-realtime/ws` (`saaras:v3-realtime`, `endpointing=manual` — push-to-talk IS manual endpointing, `stream_type=fast`); release sends `speech_end` and the final transcript lands in ~130–330ms; partials show live in the status bar (“You: …”). Browser auth rides the documented `api-subscription-key.<key>` WebSocket subprotocol. One socket per call, warmed at call start alongside the mic permission, pinged alive, reopened per turn if dead. ⚠ The WAV lesson survives in the FALLBACK: a dead socket mid-utterance wraps the SAME captured PCM in a WAV header (`pcmToWav`) and posts to REST STT — words are never lost to transport. **(2) STREAMING TTS** — one `wss://…/text-to-speech/ws?model=bulbul:v3` socket per agent turn, opened IN PARALLEL with the chat request; each chat sentence is sent + flushed as it exists, and linear16 chunks (first ~0.5s after flush, measured) feed straight onto the player timeline — no decodeAudioData, bytes become samples (`feedPcm16`, tolerant of a WAV-headered first chunk). End-of-turn = chunks stop for 1.2s (the socket has no reliable completion event); socket failure degrades per-sentence to REST TTS, never to silence. **(3)** the very FIRST spoken chunk may leave at a comma-clause (≥18 chars), so the voice starts on “Namaste Sugandh-ji,” while the sentence is still being written. `stopSpeech()` is the single teardown for barge-in / call end / new turn (player + TTS socket). **Full-turn proof, page’s own extracted classes + REAL synthesized Hindi speech streamed as the customer:** release → `transcript.final` 327ms (text exact) → first chat chunk 922ms → **FIRST AUDIO 1,166ms** — from ~6.7s originally and ~3.5s after stage 1. MediaRecorder/`blobToWav` removed (capture is PCM-native now).
  - **Voice Agent answers like a human — the serial turn becomes a streamed pipeline (2026-08-25, “make it fast like real human conversation”).** The old flow after mic release was fully serial: STT → wait for the WHOLE chat completion → TTS of the WHOLE reply → only then audio — measured live: chat first token 445ms but full completion 1.6s, TTS 1.65s for one sentence vs 4.1s for a full reply, so a long turn meant ~6–7s of dead air. Now: **(1) the chat STREAMS** (`stream:true` SSE on /v1/chat/completions — confirmed supported live) and `callChatStream` cuts the reply at sentence boundaries as tokens arrive — the FIRST sentence leaves ASAP (measured emitted at 1148ms), later ones batch to ~60+ chars for prosody; **(2) each sentence goes to TTS the moment it exists** (concurrent) and plays gaplessly in order on one AudioContext timeline (`class SpeechPlayer`; no more per-reply <audio> element); **(3) BARGE-IN** — the mic re-enables on the first audio chunk and pressing it (or ending the call) stops the agent mid-word, like interrupting a real person; the live transcript bubble fills in as the agent speaks. Time-to-first-audio drops roughly in half (~6.7s → ~3.5s on a 2-sentence turn). ⚠ Two safety rules INSIDE the splitter, both verified: a “.” only ends a sentence before whitespace (“Rs.50” is never cut into a TTS chunk), and nothing is emitted past an unclosed “<” — tool-call machinery split across stream deltas is held back whole for `sanitizeReply` (simulated a stream with machinery split over 4 deltas: zero leakage into spoken chunks). `max_tokens` 300→200 (prompts already demand ≤2 sentences); preconnect to api.sarvam.ai shaves the first TLS handshake; non-SSE responses fall back to the same pipeline with one big drain. `callChat`/`playAudio` removed — the model-constant call sites remain exactly two (stream + summary).
  - **Voice Agent: `sarvam-30b` deprecated → `sarvam-105b-conversations` (2026-08-24).** The agent died mid-call with “Model ‘sarvam-30b’ has been deprecated” — the model name was hardcoded at TWO call sites in `voice-agent.html` (the live chat loop and the call summariser). Both replacements were tested against the live API before choosing: `sarvam-105b` is a REASONING model — at a 40-token budget it spent everything thinking and returned `content: null`, which on a voice call is dead air — while `sarvam-105b-conversations` answered directly at every budget tried, including the exact multi-turn Hindi payload `callChat()` sends. Now one constant (`SARVAM_CHAT_MODEL`) used by both sites, so the next deprecation is a one-line change; a selftest pins that no hardcoded model can creep back into a call site. ⚠ **And the STT broke separately the same day:** `/speech-to-text` answered 400 “Failed to read the file, please check the audio format”. Isolated against the live API: **WAV bytes pass under every model param — even named `.webm` — so the server judges the bytes**, and what it cannot read is MediaRecorder’s native webm/opus. The recording is now converted client-side before upload (`blobToWav`: decode → mono → 16 kHz via `OfflineAudioContext` → PCM16 WAV), which is the one shape every STT accepts. ⚠⚠ **And the model then SPOKE ITS OWN MACHINERY to a customer:** at a call’s natural close it output `<tool_call>close_positive …</tool_call>` — a protocol nothing in this codebase defines (`close_positive` appears nowhere); `sarvam-105b-conversations` is agentic-tuned and invented it. Every reply now passes `sanitizeReply()` before the transcript and the TTS: `<tool_call>`/`<think>` blocks (closed OR unclosed), stray tag fragments and markdown emphasis are stripped — angle-bracket content is machinery, never speech, and TTS reads “asterisk asterisk”. A reply that was ONLY machinery falls back to a per-language goodbye (`CLOSING_LINE`, vetted languages only) instead of dead air, and every system prompt now carries “SPOKEN TEXT ONLY — you have NO tools” (the sanitizer is the guarantee; the prompt just makes it fire less). Verified against the exact leaked block from the real call.
  - **PG RECON NOW IDENTIFIES GOKWIK BY SHOPIFY TAGS — AND THE JULY COD INVOICE FINALLY NEARLY MATCHES (2026-08-24).** Asked for directly: “use Tags of Shopify to identify, don’t use EasyEcom as source of gateway”. GoKwik’s checkout stamps every order it processes — the `GoKwik` tag covers ~98% of orders in every month of 2026; `COD` / `UPI` / `Cards` / `Gokwik_ppcod` / `PPCOD-UPI` carry the payment method; every manual and Cashfree order carries none of them. Tags arrive WITH the order at ingest, so unlike `orders.gateway` (per-order GraphQL sync; 103 July nulls, 466 August) they need no sync and have no holes — **July unclassified went 103 → 0**. Rewritten in SQL (migration `20260824_pg_recon_tags.sql`, applied; `pg_recon_rows` gains an `abc` column): tags first, the old gateway/status reading kept only as the fallback for the ~2% without tags, and a tag-only order with no gateway label resolves the gokwik% rate via `'GoKwik (by tag)'`. **Three rules the tags made enforceable:** (1) **membership decides billability** — the old rule charged EVERY ‘cod’ row, so a manual or Cashfree COD was billed into GoKwik’s expectation; now only GoKwik-processed orders are charged and the rest are visibly excluded. (2) **Abandoned-cart recovery (KC_ABC / ABC_tellephant, tags start Jun-2026) is excluded from the charge** — this is the long-hunted July gap: the tag count (~371) matches the ~370-order hole from the first attempt, and the invoice only converges without them. Because GoKwik has not confirmed the rule, the exclusion is a VISIBLE number everywhere — `abc_orders`/`abc_value` in the summary (July: 297 / ₹2.44L), an ABC column in the CSV, a note on the Charged-orders card — never a silent subtraction. (3) **The COD fee base now EXCLUDES shipping** (config flip, `base_excludes_shipping`) — on these orders `total_shipping` is dominated by the COD fee GoKwik itself collects from the customer (`Gokwik_cod_fees` tag), and billing us 2% on their own collection never made sense. **Result against GoKwik’s July COD invoice (2,765 tx / ₹47,397.66): computed ₹47,807.69 — 0.87% apart, vs 12.7% over on the old basis.** The residual ~190-order count gap remains GoKwik’s to explain (tested and rejected against the data: quick-cancels, voided, minimum order value, UTC window).
  - **⚠⚠ WHAT IS ALLOWED ONTO THE SILENT-RTO LIST — three admission rules (2026-08-22).** Stated by the user (“please note this very carefully”) and enforced in the QUERY, not the UI: **(1) the shipment’s CURRENT status must itself be an RTO state.** `outcome` remembers that an RTO event happened once; it does not notice the courier reverting it — TE25-41443 and TE25-40292 sat on the claim list while RapidShyp’s live status said `IN_TRANSIT`, and claiming their freight would have been claiming for a delivery still in progress. Guard: `status_code IS NULL OR ILIKE 'RTO%'` (legacy rows predate status_code and stay), applied to BOTH the claim list and the `/reports/rto-no-attempt` evidence workbook — which had neither this nor even an outcome check, and the two disagreeing is how a wrong row reaches a courier. **(2) Not a single OFD** — already held (`rto_no_attempt`; audited: 0 of 205 carried an OFD timestamp). **(3) A LOST shipment never appears here** — it has its own tab and its own larger claim, and one parcel must never be claimed twice. ⚠⚠ **ROOT CAUSE FIXED IN THE CLASSIFIER: LOST now outranks RTO** (both classifier sites; delivered still outranks both — a delivery cannot be un-happened). A return that goes lost on the way back NEVER CAME BACK; calling it ‘rto’ filed it on the claim list while the Lost tab, where its money is actually recovered, never saw it. **One-time repair: 13 rows** with a lost status code (LST) but outcome ‘rto’ moved to ‘lost’ — including TE25-29893 from the user’s own seven. ⚠ **And two lost shipments had `order_date NULL`**, which the window’s `.gte()` silently drops — invisible in EVERY window on the one tab whose purpose is that nothing lost goes unseen; null-date rows now ride along in every window. **Net effect:** Silent-RTO 205 → 198 rows / ₹22,685.35 (all genuinely claimable), Lost 16 → 29, overlap between the two lists: **0**, verified end to end.
  - **Zone / state filters show the SHARE, not just a count (2026-08-22).** Asked for: “along with zone order counts show % also to get zone ratio”. Each option in the Delivery Performance multi-selects now reads `2,412  38.4%`. The mix is what gets acted on — `2,412` alone does not say whether that is most of the book or a corner of it (live: A 4.1% · B 2.6% · C 28.2% · D 38.4% · E 26.7%). The share is of the options **shown**, so it always totals 100% and can never be read against a different denominator than the one on screen; a non-zero value under 0.1% renders `<0.1%` rather than `0.0%`, which would read as none at all. Counts are thousands-separated. ⚠ Styled with **real CSS** (`.dp-multi-share` in `index.html`, fixed width + tabular figures so the column reads straight down) — `tailwind.css` here is PREBUILT and a utility class would have silently done nothing. 📈 **And a Zone mix CARD on the page itself (2026-08-22)** — asked for right after: “can we add and adjust Zone Ratio % chart in this place, make other required chart size a bit small and fit new zone ratio”. The chart row went from **3 columns to 4**: the FASR trend keeps half the row (`lg:col-span-2`) and the NDR funnel gives up a third of its width, so the zone mix lands on the same line instead of making the page longer. Bars are **ordered biggest-first** — the question is “where is the weight”, not “what is the alphabet” — and each bar’s WIDTH is its share of the largest zone (so the shape stays readable when one zone dominates) while the NUMBER beside it is always the share of the total, which is the figure being compared. Clicking a bar drives the **same `_dpZone` filter the dropdown uses**, so the card and the menu can never disagree about what is selected, and a selected bar is ringed. ⚠⚠ **A CORRECTION TO AN EARLIER ENTRY:** the note claiming `tailwind.css` carries **no** `md:`/`lg:` grid variants was **WRONG** — `.lg\:grid-cols-2/3/4` and `.lg\:col-span-2` ARE compiled, which is why every existing responsive row on this dashboard works. The grep that “proved” otherwise had lost its backslash to shell escaping. The KwikShip ledger’s auto-fit grid stays (it reflows on content rather than a breakpoint, which suits a KPI strip whose card count varies) but its stated reason was false. A **`tailwind-audit` selftest now checks the stylesheet itself** rather than trusting memory: `lg:` grids, `text-[10px]`, `text-[11px]`, `z-[90]` and `hover:bg-*` ARE present; opacity modifiers (`bg-white/10`, `bg-emerald-50/30`), `gap-y-2`, `pl-8` and the `lift` class are NOT.
  - **🆕 A NEW PRODUCT NOW JOINS THE DASHBOARD BY ITSELF (2026-08-22).** ⚠⚠ **The snapshot’s SKU list was never “everything we stock” — it is the `base_sku` set in `sku_pack_mapping`, and nothing else.** Proven on live data: 17 registered base SKUs, 17 SKUs in the snapshot, **identical sets**. So a product launched last week was invisible in the Inventory Analytics table, the DRR/DOI charts, the Recommended Order sheet AND the 06:30 Teams report until somebody remembered to hand-add a mapping row. **TE-ABD1** (Advanced Brightening Drops — 524 units on the shelf, 10 sold in 7 days) had been missing exactly that way. `registerNewProducts()` in `inventory.js` now runs **inside `refreshSnapshot()`, BEFORE the edge fn**, so the dashboard Refresh button and the 06:30 cron both pick a new product up on the same run. ⚠ **The list cannot simply be “every EasyEcom SKU”:** of ~60 unmapped SKUs at our own warehouse, all but four are drafts, FBA variants, channel codes, retired SKUs and combos — and EasyEcom’s own `is_combo` flag reads **false for every one of them**, so it cannot be the filter. The test is instead *physical stock at OUR warehouse* (`wo66194027524`, qty > 0) **plus** a real product name (a name that merely repeats the SKU is a channel listing code) and no draft / FBA / test marker. ⚠⚠ **PACK VARIANTS ARE REGISTERED FROM EVIDENCE, NOT FROM THE NAMING CONVENTION.** EasyEcom states one physical pool in each pack’s own unit — TE-ABD1 **524**, TE-ABD2 **262**, TE-ABD4 **131** — so the ratio itself proves the multiplier. Registering TE-ABD2 as its own base would **triple-count the same bottles**; taking ×2 from the trailing digit alone would corrupt DRR silently. A pack is auto-mapped only when **three independent signals agree** (trailing digit, exact stock ratio, shared product name); otherwise it is left alone and logged as “needing a human”. ⚠ **A brand-new SKU’s FIRST snapshot can come back empty** — observed on TE-ABD1 the run it was registered: stock 0, warehouse “N/A”, the SKU echoed back as its own product name, while EasyEcom reported 524 on the shelf (the edge fn does not see a mapping row committed moments earlier). It self-heals next run, but next run is AFTER the 06:30 Teams report — which would have announced a freshly-stocked product as **Out of Stock**. So the result is now checked against EasyEcom and the snapshot **re-run once** rather than trusted. Registration failure never costs the snapshot: an un-registered SKU is one missing row, a skipped snapshot is a stale dashboard for everything. **Live at build:** registered `TE-ABD1` + `TE-ABD2×2` + `TE-ABD4×4`; the dashboard went 17 → 18 SKUs with TE-ABD1 showing 524 units, its real name and 10 units sold in 7 days.
  - **📥 Reorder now subtracts what is ALREADY ON ORDER (2026-08-14):** the sheet was recommending stock that live POs were already covering. The table gained **Stock**, **Raised PO** and **Final Qty**, where `Final Qty = Recommend Qty − Raised PO` (floored at 0), and **case rounding now happens on the NET need**, not the gross — you only buy what the open POs don't cover. **Measured the day it shipped: the order dropped from 23,811 units to 451 — 23,360 units of double-ordering avoided, with 7 of the 9 recommended SKUs already fully covered** (TE-BDR1 alone had 27,441 units on order against a 16,837 recommendation). Covered SKUs stay in the table reading **"✓ on PO"** rather than vanishing, so the buyer can see WHY a critical-looking SKU is not being ordered. Source is `openPoQtyBySku()` in `purchase_orders.js` — see the table registry for why a Rejected/Cancelled PO must not count. ⚠️ **Degrades safely — in a LADDER (hardened 2026-08-20** after the 06:30 report warned “Open POs could not be read”; reproduced fine seconds later, i.e. a transient EasyEcom flake in the busy morning window, and one bad page among ~14 threw the whole lookup): `openPoQtyBySku` now **retries once after 3s**, then serves its **last good copy flagged `stale`** (`_lastGoodOpenPo`, kept past the 5-min TTL exactly for this) — the report note then reads “subtracted from the last good copy (<IST time>)”; only with **no history at all** (first run after a pm2 restart) does it throw and the old “not subtracted” warning appear (`poSubtracted:false` on the API), rather than being suppressed or silently over-stating. `buildReorder(rows, caseSizes, openPo = {})` takes the map as an optional third argument, so the no-PO path is byte-identical to the previous behaviour. `GET /inventory/reorder` applies the same subtraction so the API and the report can never disagree. **The Inventory Analytics dashboard now carries the SAME sheet** (`invReorder()`, rendered above the DOI/DRR charts) off that same endpoint — page and 6:30 AM Teams card cannot diverge. ⚠️ Two things in that card deliberately ignore the filters above it, and its caption says so: it is **Shifupro only**, and it uses the **server's fixed 7-day DRR / 45-day cover**, so the "DRR: 7/14/30 days" selector does NOT move order quantities — letting a dropdown silently change what you buy is worse than the inconsistency. Covered rows are tinted and chipped **"on PO"**. ⚠️ The tint is `bg-emerald-50`, NOT `bg-emerald-50/30`: tailwind.css here is PREBUILT and carries no arbitrary opacity modifiers, so the `/30` form compiles to nothing and the row silently loses its highlight. ⚠⚠ **RAISED PO NOW EXPLAINS ITSELF (2026-08-21).** The column showed a bare total, and a total is unreadable when it spans POs: TE-BB1 read **549** against a PO the buyer had raised for **500**. It was correct — 500 from PO 69 plus a **49-unit remnant of PO 38**, raised 5 June, 1 of 50 units ever received and still Open 77 days later — but nothing on the page could say so. `openPoQtyBySku()` now returns `detailBySku` (every contributing PO with its number, status, vendor, ordered / received / pending and age) and `staleBySku`; `buildReorder()` carries them onto each row as `open_po_detail` / `open_po_stale`. The cell hovers to name every PO behind the figure, shows “(n POs)” when more than one contributes, and marks a ⚠ when any of them is stale. **A line still pending more than `PO_STALE_DAYS` (45) after it was raised is flagged but STILL SUBTRACTED** — EasyEcom calls it open, and silently dropping it would be a different guess — so the stale POs are also **named in the footnotes**, with the SKUs and units they are holding back and the remedy (close the PO in EasyEcom, re-run). A tooltip nobody hovers is not a warning. This is the failure mode behind the earlier “GRN without PO → recommendations mismatch”: a forgotten PO quietly suppresses a re-order for a SKU nobody is actually shipping.
  - **🧾 Reorder rework — cover bands, case rounding, Shifupro-only (2026-08-04):** the report is now a purchase-order sheet, not just a low-stock alert. **Cover bands changed to 20 / 30 / 45** (`PLACE_ORDER_DOI` / `WARNING_DOI` / `TARGET_COVER_DAYS` in `inventory.js`). **DRR window is 7 days** (`DRR_WINDOW_DAYS` in inventory.js, `LOOKBACK` in the edge fn — keep the two in step). ⚠️ **DRR is not just a display column — DOI and Recommend Qty are both derived from it**, so changing the window moves the order quantities: going 30d → 7d on 2026-08-04 took the sheet from 146 cases / 16,988 units to 191 cases / 22,192 units (+31%), because the last week ran hotter than the monthly average. The dashboard's reorder view reads the same constant, deliberately — a report and a dashboard disagreeing on the same number is worse than either window. `buildReorder()` derives per SKU: **Recommend Qty = ceil(DRR×45 − stock)** (units still needed), **Case Qty = ceil(Recommend Qty ÷ case size)** = **how many CASES to order**, and **Order Units = Case Qty × case size** (what lands in the warehouse). ⚠️ **Case Qty is a count of CASES, not units rounded to a case boundary** — an earlier build had it the other way round. Case sizes live in the new table **`sku_case_size`** and are **not** guessed: a SKU with no row shows its raw quantity flagged `*` rather than being silently dropped. **⚠️ Shifupro only** — `loadLatestSnapshot()` filters `location_id = wo66194027524`, and so does the image edge fn. DP Bangalore is DocPharma's stock held on our behalf and reconciled in DocPharma Recon; including it double-counted the same 17 SKUs. **The Teams report is two halves that no longer duplicate each other:** the PNG is the STOCK picture (every Shifupro SKU — not just the ones under threshold, because you can't plan a PO from a list that hides the healthy items — sorted by DOI ascending, columns SKU/Product/Stock/DRR-30d/DOI/Status), and the card below it is the ORDER sheet (a `table` block: SKU · DRR/30d · DOI · Recommend Qty · Case Size · Case Qty · Order Units + a TOTAL row), placed directly above the `Ecom Central · Inventory Analytics · <date>` footer. The edge fn **`inventory-doi-image-teams` reads `inventory_snapshots` + `sku_case_size` directly instead of the `inventory_doi_low` RPC** — that RPC filters by threshold, uses a 7d lookback and knows nothing about case sizes. **Satori glyph trap:** the Roboto latin subset the fn loads has no `≤` or `⚠` glyph and renders a tofu box — spell it out ("under 20d", "no case") in anything drawn into the PNG.

  - **🧮 Stock Count — physical inventory reconciliation (2026-07-23):** new **Stock Count** view under Inventory (perm **`inventory-count`** — a WH-only perm so floor staff get *just* this page, not the analytics/valuations; server enforces it via `[/^\/inventory\/count/i, ['inventory-count','inventory']]` in server.js, ordered before the general `/inventory` rule). Purpose: find where EasyEcom drifts from real stock. WH member does a **blind, one-SKU-at-a-time count** at **Shifupro** (`wo66194027524`, our own WH — fixed, no selector): pick a single-product SKU → type **physical qty** (the only input; system qty is hidden while counting) → submit. On submit the backend reads **LIVE EasyEcom `availableInventory`** for that SKU *at that instant* (`easyEcomInventory(fresh)` via `easyecom-proxy getInventory`, no cache) and appends a row to **`inventory_counts_ecom`** `{counted_at, counted_by, location_id, warehouse, sku, product_name, system_qty, physical_qty, difference (generated = physical−system), cost, notes}` — append-only audit trail. The SKU picker = **registered `sku_pack_mapping` base_skus intersected with EasyEcom Shifupro** (17 real singles); EasyEcom's `is_combo` flag is unreliable (combos/drafts/variant-IDs leak) so it's NOT used — register a product in `sku_pack_mapping` to make it countable. Backend `app/api/inventory.js`: `GET /inventory/count/skus` (blind picker list, no qty), `POST /inventory/count` (capture live system + log), `GET /inventory/count/log` (history + summary: counts, mismatches, net unit variance, ₹ variance at cost; filters sku/from/to/mismatch). Frontend (`initInventoryCount`/`icSubmit`/`icLoadLog` in app.js; view `inventory-count-view` + nav `nav-inventory-count`) = a custom searchable SKU combobox + physical-qty box + **Submit** + 4 KPI cards + filterable mismatch-highlighted log. ⚠️ Caveat: `availableInventory` is EasyEcom *Available* (net of reserved) — if diffs look like reserved/allocated units, add an on-hand source in v2.

  - **🔬 Count Analysis — deep reconciliation analytics (2026-07-23):** sibling manager-only view under Inventory (perm **`inventory-count-analysis`**; server rule `[/^\/inventory\/count\/analysis/i, ['inventory-count-analysis','inventory']]` ordered BEFORE the count rule). Endpoint `GET /inventory/count/analysis?from=&to=` rolls the `inventory_counts_ecom` log into: per-SKU **current state** (latest count) + full history, **inventory accuracy %**, net/gross unit + ₹ variance (at cost), **shrinkage (short) vs excess (over)** split, **coverage** vs the 17 tracked SKUs (lists uncounted), day-by-day variance **trend**, and per-counter stats. Frontend (`initCountAnalysis`/`icaRenderTable`/`icaHistory`/`icaRenderReserved` in app.js; view `inventory-count-analysis-view` + nav `nav-inventory-count-analysis`) = 6 KPI cards + 2 Chart.js charts (top true-₹-variance SKUs bar, variance trend line) + uncounted-SKU chips + sortable per-SKU reconciliation table (click row → count-history modal) + reserved-stock table + per-counter cards + date-range filter.

  - **📥 Reserved-stock adjustment (2026-07-23):** the root cause of most physical↔EasyEcom mismatches — EasyEcom's `availableInventory` is on-hand MINUS reserved (units allocated to orders assigned to the warehouse but not yet shipped), yet those units are still physically on the shelf. So **true on-hand ≈ Available + Reserved**, and the real variance = **Physical − (Available + Reserved)**.
