Reserved is reconstructed from `b2c_order_easycom` via Postgres RPC **`inventory_reserved_lines_shifupro()`** — orders at Shifupro (`location` ∈ `rapidshyp` / `Shifupro Technologies Pvt. Ltd.`) in statuses **Confirmed / Assigned / On Hold / Printed** (allocated, before packed; **'Open' excluded** as often unpaid/unallocated; no fixed recency cap — but the RPC takes `p_from`/`p_to` so Count Analysis's from/to date filter scopes reserved by `order_date` (IST), e.g. BDR1 673 all-time → 327 for Jul 1–23), `line_items` expanded pack→base via `sku_pack_mapping` (combos credit their base components, matching EasyEcom's deduction). `POST /inventory/count` now captures `reserved_qty` **live at count time** (new column on `inventory_counts_ecom`). Count Analysis (`GET /inventory/count/analysis`) computes per-SKU **Available · Reserved · Adj-system · Physical · True-diff · True-₹**, headline **True accuracy** & **True ₹ variance** (net of reserved), shrinkage/excess based on true-diff, plus a **Reserved-stock table** (per-SKU reserved units + open-order count + date range → click a SKU for the underlying orders: date · order · status · units). Helper `reservedData()` in inventory.js.

  - **📦 Inventory-movement ledger + 4-bucket reconciliation (2026-07-23):** EasyEcom's `availableInventory` excludes not just reserved but ALSO stock physically on the shelf in a non-sellable state. Full equation: **Physical = Available + Reserved + Blocked + Awaiting-putaway**; **True variance = Physical − all four** = real shrinkage. Fed by a webhook **movement ledger**: table **`inventory_movements_ecom`** (`event_type` grn/adjustment/return, sku, qty, adjustment_type, old_status→new_status, bins, ref, raw) + edge fn **`easyecom-inventory-webhook`** (verify_jwt OFF, version-controlled in `supabase/functions/`). EasyVMS triggers → this URL: **GRN Details/Complete GRN** `?type=grn`; **Inventory Adjustment V2 / Cycle Count / PutAway / RTV / QC Fail / Lot Locking / Move-to-Expired** `?type=adjustment`; **Mark/Pending Return** base URL (auto-detected — GRN/adjustment self-identify by payload fields, everything else = return). Token field can be anything (endpoint doesn't enforce it unless `EE_INVENTORY_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` is set). **`bucketBalances()`** derives current **Blocked** (net units moved INTO a non-Available status: Damaged/QC-Fail/Expired/Lot-Locked) + **Awaiting-putaway** (returns received − putaway adjustments — the limbo where returned/cancelled stock is physically in but not yet binned into system Available) per SKU. `POST /inventory/count` freezes reserved+blocked+awaiting at count time (cols `blocked_qty`, `awaiting_putaway_qty`); Count Analysis shows Avail · Reserved · Blocked · Await · Adj-sys · Physical · True-diff. **⚠️ Blocked & Awaiting are running ledger balances that firm up as webhook events accumulate** (and once the status/adjustmentType classification is verified against real payloads). For a perfect reco: blind, by-bin, idle-time counts + freeze all buckets at the count instant + post confirmed variances back as EasyEcom adjustments (which re-flow through the ledger).

---

## 8. Database Schema

> The Supabase project also hosts tables owned by a sibling data platform (Meta/Google/GA4/CRM/influencer syncs). Tables **owned by this app** end in `_ecom` (plus a few DocPharma tables). All tables have RLS enabled; the server uses the service-role key.

### Core tables (this app)

| Table | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `shipment_journey_ecom` (~14k) | **Heart of delivery analytics.** One row per AWB: source (`rapidshyp`/`docpharma`), outcome (`delivered/rto/lost/ndr_pending/in_transit`), attempts, `ndr_count`, `first_attempt_success`, `rto_no_attempt` (silent RTO), timestamps (order/dispatched/OFD/delivered/RTO), `first_edd` (promise date — DB trigger keeps earliest), zone/dest, payment mode, order type, raw scan cache, **freight columns** (`freight_total/forward/rto`, `cod_charges`, `shipment_value`, `applied_weight`, `charges_fetched_at`), **`awb_assigned_at`** (indexed — RapidShyp's own AWB-assigned date; the billing basis for RapidShyp Recon, captured by the charges sync from `shipment_details.awb_assigned_date`) |
| `enriched_orders_ecom` (~33k) | Shopify orders enriched for dashboards |
| `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom` (~26k) | RapidShyp status cache for EasyEcom-shipped orders |
| `rapidshyp_unknown_awbs_ecom` | AWBs RapidShyp answered **HTTP 400** for — parcels booked with another carrier. Added 2026-08-17: the RS Sync cron learned this in an in-process `Set` that every restart wiped, and since a foreign AWB never gets a `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom` row it stayed on the "missing, go fetch" list forever. Measured that day: **1,438 of 1,449 AWBs in the 7-day window were foreign** (708 GoKwik + 730 with no aggregator) against **11 real ones** — ~14 minutes of pointless calls every 2 hours. ⚠️ Keyed on RapidShyp's **own verdict**, never on courier name: some GoKwik-labelled AWBs *are* tracked by RapidShyp, so filtering by aggregator would silently drop real shipments |
| `b2c_order_easycom` (~27k) | Synced EasyEcom B2C orders. `customer_type` col (default `Regular`) — set to `Influencer` by `rawToDbRow` when the `Influencer` tag / `INFLUENCER` company line is present (flows from Shopify through EasyEcom). Surfaced as an **Influencer** badge on Orders Dashboard rows |
| `order_marks_ecom` | Manual per-order marks: `likely_fake` (toggle) + `critical_mail_sent` (audit log) — unique per (order_name, mark_type) |
| `app_users_ecom` | Portal users: email, **name** (display name, admin-editable), mobile (mandatory, kept on profile), scrypt hash, role, status (pending/active/disabled), permissions[] |
| `app_email_settings` | Single-row portal SMTP config; `smtp_password_enc` is AES-256-GCM encrypted, never returned to clients. Recipients: `to_emails`/`cc_emails` (RapidShyp reports), `docpharma_to_emails`/`docpharma_cc_emails` (DocPharma reports; CC blank → falls back to `cc_emails`), `rapidshyp_email` (Silent-RTO claim). All report-email dates render **DD-MM-YYYY** |
| `escalation_emails_ecom` | Escalation mail threads: every SENT critical email (RFC Message-ID) + every REPLY pulled via IMAP, with AI resolution score/status/suggestion |
| `easyecom_token_cache` | Shared EasyEcom JWT (single row id=1) — one login per ~90 days instead of per process |
| `order_buckets` (**view**) + `order_notes`, `call_logs`, `escalation_contacts`, `undelivered_tracking`, `msg91_messages`, `tracking_run_lock`, `profiles`, `user_roles` | Customer Support console data layer (shared with the original standalone console; portal agents bridged via shadow auth users) |
| `blocked_numbers_ecom` | Customer Support blacklisted phone numbers (phone, reason, added_by, active, unblocked_by/at). Soft-delete keeps history; partial unique index = one active block per phone. RLS-locked (service-role only) |
| `user_activity_ecom` | Per-user activity feed (event login\|view, view key, created_at) → the admin User Analytics dashboard. Written by `POST /api/activity` (email from token). RLS-locked (service-role only) |
| `store_credit_log_ecom` | Local audit of every Shopify store-credit movement made from Ecom Central (customer, direction, amount, **reason**, balance_after, Shopify account + transaction id, actor). Shopify holds the money and the balance; this holds the accountability. RLS-locked |
| `pincode_geo_ecom` | Cache of pincode → city/state answers from the **free India Post API** (pincode PK, city, state, district, source=`indiapost`). ⚠️ **A pure API cache — never seeded from our own orders/shipments or from a courier**: the requirement is explicitly that this comes from a free public API, since our shipping history only covers pincodes we happen to have delivered to and can be stale. RLS-locked |
| `teams_bot_channels_ecom` | Bot Framework `serviceUrl` per Teams channel (channel_id PK, service_url, tenant_id, team_id), learned from any inbound activity — it is NOT derivable from the channel id and there is no API to look it up, so the bot must be spoken to (or installed) once before it can post proactively. Upserted on every activity because Microsoft rotates/regionalises the value; a stale one fails 401/403 and self-heals on the next message. ⚠️ The value is **tenant-scoped**, so one row serves every channel in the tenant. RLS-locked |
| `zone_mapping_with_pincode` | KwikShip's serviceability sheet — **one row per DESTINATION pincode**. 75 columns mirroring the source CSV headers **exactly (quoted, case-sensitive)** so the file imports with no mapping step. ⚠️ **`Pin_code_From` is the PICKUP pincode (constant 122101), `Pin_code_To` is the DESTINATION — despite the names this is NOT a range**; lookup is an exact match on `Pin_code_To` (indexed). `Pin_code_From` is nullable reference metadata; `Pin_code_To` is NOT NULL. `Zone` plus ~70 courier-service flags (`Delhivery_Surface_250_Gm`, `EKart_Air`, `Blue_Dart_Select`, …) are all typed `text` so no unexpected value can fail an import. Surrogate `id` PK. ⚠️ **Queries must double-quote the column names.** Consumed **only** by `source='kwikship'` shipments — RapidShyp/DocPharma keep the state-derived zone. Helper fns: **`zone_for_pincode(pin)`** (single lookup — called by BOTH the Node cron and the webhook edge fn so the rule has one definition), `remap_kwikship_zones()` (bulk re-derive; destinations absent from the sheet are left alone), `zone_mapping_stats()` (screen KPIs in one round trip), `zone_mapping_columns()` (live column list for the uploader), **`zone_drift_check(since)`** (watchdog — returns webhook hits, rows, agree and **`drift`, which must always be 0**; a non-zero value means some writer is re-stamping the state-derived zone again). RLS-locked (service-role only) |
| `docpharma_billing_config_ecom` | DocPharma's FLAT rate card — no zones, no weight slabs, no percentage, so it is a single config row rather than a rate table: `service_rate` 65, `rto_rate` 40, `cod_fee` 25 (flat, not a %), `gst_pct` 18, `rto_bills_forward` true, `cod_on_delivered_only` true. Same conventions as KwikShip. Fns: **`docpharma_charges(outcome, payment_mode)`** and **`apply_docpharma_charges()`** (bulk, idempotent), enforced in real time by trigger **`trg_docpharma_price`** on `shipment_journey_ecom`. Because every component is flat it prices **100%** of rows — no weight or order value needed. 50 rows carry no `payment_mode`, so COD can't be determined; they are charged no COD fee and raise the `no_payment_mode` flag rather than being silently assumed prepaid. RLS-locked |
| `kwikship_rate_card_ecom` · `kwikship_billing_config_ecom` | KwikShip surface rate card by zone (`rate_kind` = fwd_base_500 / fwd_add_500 / rto_base_500 / fwd_base_2000 / fwd_add_1000 / rto_base_2000, `zone`, `rate`, `effective_from`) + the billing rules the card doesn't state (`cod_min` 25, `cod_pct` 1.3, `gst_pct` 18, `rto_bills_forward` true, `cod_on_delivered_only` true, `slab_switch_g` 2000). Deliberately DATA not code so a renegotiated contract is an UPDATE. Fns: **`kwikship_charges(zone, weight_g, outcome, payment_mode, value)`** (costs one shipment → jsonb with forward/rto/cod/ex-GST/GST/gross + flags) and **`apply_kwikship_charges()`** (bulk; writes into `shipment_journey_ecom.freight_*`, idempotent). **Real-time enforcement is the trigger `trg_kwikship_price` / `kwikship_price_row()` on `shipment_journey_ecom`** — every kwikship row is zoned and priced as it is written, whoever writes it; pg_cron **`kwikship-zone-cost-reconcile-hourly`** is the backstop for late-arriving EasyEcom data and sheet/rate changes. ⚠️ Card prices a Zone C that KwikShip's serviceability sheet never uses. RLS-locked |
| `influencer_emails_ecom` | Collaboration outreach emails actually sent (influencer_id, video_id, to/from, subject, body, ai_polished, message_id, sent_by, sent_at). Source of truth for the Email Sent ✓ — a LOG not a flag, so it also covers influencers with no video row. RLS-locked (service-role only) |
| `app_user_smtp_ecom` | Per-portal-user sending mailbox (user_email PK, from_name/from_email, smtp_host/port/user, `smtp_password_enc` AES-256-GCM, active). Influencer outreach sends from the logged-in user's own address; no mapping = cannot send, by design. RLS-locked |
| `influencers`, `influencer_videos`, `influencer_lists`, `influencer_list_members`, `influencer_activities`, `analysis_queue`, `brand_mention_scans`, `shopify_products` | Influencer Marketing CRM data layer (shared with the original standalone CRM app) |
| _(inventory reorder, 2026-08-27)_ **DOI + PO column** | **"DOI Based On Raised PO"** on the Recommended Order table (dashboard), the Teams reorder PNG (edge fn `inventory-doi-image-teams` v13, column after Raised PO, traffic-light: red ≤20d / amber ≤30d / green above) and the fallback card ("stock · DOI ▸ DOI+PO"). Formula, computed ONCE in `buildReorder`: **`doi_with_po = (stock + raised-PO pending units) ÷ DRR`** — same 7-day DRR and rounding as today's DOI, null when DRR=0. Answers "how many days of cover once everything already on open POs arrives" — today's DOI says whether the shelf is short, DOI+PO says whether MORE ordering is needed beyond what's in flight. Verified live: TE-UARO 4.7d → 99.2d (1,500 on PO), TE-BDR1 19.6d → 45.3d, TE-10NBS1 19.8d → **22.8d (still amber — the in-flight PO is not enough)** |
| _(no table — EasyEcom live)_ `purchase_orders.openPoQtyBySku()` | Units already on a LIVE purchase order, per SKU, read from EasyEcom's PO book (5-min cache). Feeds the reorder subtraction below. ⚠️ "Open" = `pending > 0` **AND the PO is not dead** — a Rejected (4), Cancelled (7) **or Completed (5, added 2026-08-20)** PO keeps its `pending_quantity`, so counting it would report units as inbound that nobody will ship. Completed joined the dead set after a real GRN-without-PO case: goods received by an unlinked GRN, the PO short-closed as Completed, its pending frozen forever — POs 49+50 sat with **4,717 phantom inbound units** (2,441 TE-BDR1 · 2,022 TE-2SAS1 · 254 TE-LB1) silently suppressing every reorder of those SKUs. Marking a PO Completed IS EasyEcom’s way of closing it short, and the fix means doing exactly that in the dashboard re-aligns the reorder sheet within 5 minutes Defined once here so it cannot drift from what the Purchase Order page shows |
| `sku_case_size` | Per-SKU carton/case size for the reorder sheet (`sku` PK, `case_size`, `alt_case_size`, `note`). Populated by hand from the physical INVENTORY COUNT sheet — **never inferred**; a SKU absent here reports its raw quantity flagged `*` instead of being rounded on a guess. `TE-AP1` is deliberately absent (not being restocked) |
| `rapidshyp_payments` | Payments made to RapidShyp — mirrors `docpharma_payments`, deliberately a separate table per partner since each ledger is its own money flow. Hand-entered: RapidShyp exposes no wallet/billing API. RLS on, service-role only |
| `kwikship_payments` + `kwikship_rate_card_ecom` + `kwikship_billing_config_ecom` | KwikShip freight recon. The rate card (zone × slab, effective-dated) and the billing rules (COD %/min, GST %, RTO-bills-forward, COD-on-delivered-only, slab switch) that `apply_kwikship_charges()` uses to price every KwikShip shipment — ⚠️ these produce OUR EXPECTATION, not a KwikShip bill; they publish no billing API. `kwikship_payments` mirrors `rapidshyp_payments`: hand-entered, deliberately separate per partner, RLS on, service-role only |
| `pg_charge_config_ecom` + `pg_charge_for()` / `pg_recon_rows()` / `pg_recon_summary()` | GoKwik PG reconciliation. Effective-dated rate config (one row per payment type, matched by gateway pattern, most specific first) and the functions that price every order from it. `orders.gateway` is the input, filled from Shopify by `syncOrderGateways()`. ⚠️ `pg_recon_rows()` is set-returning — PostgREST caps it at 1000 rows, so it must be paged |
| `inventory_snapshots`, `inventory_counts_ecom` | Inventory Analytics daily snapshot (unique snapshot_date+location_id+sku) + **Stock Count** reconciliation log (physical vs live-EasyEcom system qty at count time; `difference` is a generated column = physical−system; append-only) |
| `tally_vouchers_ecom` | Finance → Tally voucher pipeline & audit trail. `status` draft→queued→posting→posted\|failed\|cancelled, `entries` jsonb (`[{ledger,dr_cr,amount,bill_ref?}]`), `total_amount`, `attempts` (hard stop at 3), `reference` (carries the `ECOM-xxxxxxxx` dedup marker), `request_xml`/`response_xml` (exact bytes exchanged with Tally), **unique `idempotency_key`** (blocks a double-post at the DB, not in app logic), `source`/`source_ref` (`manual` today; reserved for derived vouchers), plus `batch_id` and `validation_error` (why a draft was kept out of a batch — it stays `draft` so a fix puts it in the next run). Status gained `awaiting_approval`. RLS-locked (service-role only) |
| `tally_masters_ecom` | Mirror of Tally's chart of accounts — `kind` (ledger/group/voucher_type/stock_item/cost_centre), `name`, `parent`, `is_billwise`, unique (kind, name). Feeds the entry form's pickers **and acts as the whitelist every ledger name is validated against** — an unknown `LEDGERNAME` does not error in Tally, it auto-creates the ledger under Suspense. Refreshed by the bridge agent (~30 min) or `POST /tally/masters/sync`. Stale rows (renamed/deleted in Tally) are pruned each sync |
| `tally_push_batches_ecom` | One row per nightly push. `ref` (TB-YYYYMMDD-n, quoted in Teams), `status` awaiting_approval→approved→pushing→done\|failed (+rejected/expired/empty), counts and `total_amount`, `blocked` jsonb (what was excluded and why), `approved_by`/`approval_source` (teams\|dashboard\|auto), `result_notified` (so the watcher reports exactly once), `expires_at`. RLS-locked |
| `tally_books_cache_ecom` | Trial balance / day book / meta uploaded by the bridge agent every ~15 min, so the LIVE dashboard can show Tally reports it cannot read directly. Unique (company, kind, period_from, period_to); `synced_at` is surfaced as the UI's "as of" stamp |
| `tally_voucher_deletions_ecom` | Audit trail for dashboard entries deleted by an admin. Full snapshot before removal — `entries` jsonb, `prior_status`, `was_posted` + `tally_masterid` (so a record that reached Tally is identifiable there), `batch_ref`, `entered_by`/`entered_at`, `deleted_by`/`deleted_at`, `reason`, plus the whole row in `snapshot`. Append-only; RLS-locked |
| `tally_narration_labels_ecom` | Per-`(company, ledger, direction)` prefix for the narration, e.g. RapidShyp money-out = `Recharge Topup`, money-in = `COD Remittance`. See the narration-labels note above. RLS-locked |
| `tally_bank_pending_ecom` | Statement lines uploaded but **not yet booked**, so "what is still unmapped" survives closing the tab. UNIQUE `line_hash` — the same identity `tally_bank_lines_ecom` uses, so a line is in exactly one of the two: pending or booked. `returned_reason` records why a line came back (a voucher was deleted or released). Deliberately a separate table: that other UNIQUE index *means* "already imported", so pending rows living in it would make every uploaded line look already-done. RLS-locked |
| `tally_bridge_status_ecom` (id=1) | Bridge-agent liveness: `last_seen_at`, `agent_version`, `tally_reachable`, `company`, `masters_synced_at`, `sync_requested`. Drives the connection chip (stale after 60s) |
| `easyecom_panel_session` (id=1) | EasyEcom panel session cookie (AES-GCM encrypted) for warehouse-change routing via `/Orders/UpdateVendor` |
| `dp_rejected_handled_ecom` | Dedup ledger: DocPharma-rejected orders already reported → never re-reported |
| `docpharma_check_ecom` | "Not-in-DocPharma" check cache (TTL) to avoid re-hitting their rate-limited API |
| `serviceability_edd_ecom` | Pincode serviceability / EDD estimates |
| `amazon_review_requests` | Review-request send history |
| `fba_fc_snapshot_ecom`, `fba_inbound_ecom` | FBA stock snapshots / inbound |
| `docpharma_orders`, `docpharma_invoices`, `docpharma_goods_*`, `docpharma_charge_*`, `docpharma_payments`, `docpharma_rate_card`, `docpharma_recon_log`, `docpharma_*_stock` | DocPharma reconciliation domain |
| `docpharma_ndr_logs` | DocPharma NDR (Non-Delivery Report) events received via the `/api/webhook/docpharma-ndr` webhook (order/awb/status/ndr_reason/attempt/courier/ndr_at + raw jsonb) |
| `ops_actions_ecom`, `order_awb_ecom`, `awb_cache_ecom`, `shipment_cache_ecom`, `api_logs_ecom`, `cod_confirmations_*` | Support/ops tables |

### Key invariants
- `shipment_journey_ecom.outcome='delivered'` **must** have `delivered_at`. A `/deliver/`-regex bug once mis-marked out-for-delivery orders as delivered in **both** parsers (DocPharma fixed 2026-07-14, RapidShyp fixed 2026-07-16 — 344 rows repaired); both classifiers now require `\bdelivered\b` and exclude "not delivered"/"undelivered" phrasings. Never reintroduce a bare `/deliver/` status test.
- Silent RTO ≡ `outcome='rto' AND rto_no_attempt=true`.
- `first_edd` is preserved to the **earliest** promised date by DB trigger (RapidShyp EDD or DocPharma `eta`).

---

## 9. Scheduled Jobs (Crons)

All schedules run in **Asia/Kolkata** inside the server process (`server.js` unless noted).

| Schedule | Job |
|---|---|
| `45 */6 * * *` | Journey gap-fill — refresh non-final shipments (webhooks are primary) |
| `15 3 * * *` | **RapidShyp charges sync** — price newly-final shipments (freight + value + EDD backfill), batches of 2500 |
| `40 */3 * * *` | DocPharma portal ingest (they have no webhooks) + once ~40s after boot |
| `0 2 * * *` | **Kwikship (GoKwik) tracking sync** — backstop for the `kwikship-webhook`; pull `status_history` for non-final Kwikship-allocated orders (`courier_aggregator_name='GoKwik Outbound'`) → `shipment_journey_ecom` (source=kwikship) |
| `30 2 * * *` | New/Repeat order-type + destination refresh (SQL RPCs) + once after boot |
| `20 */3 * * *` | **PG gateway labels** — re-ask Shopify for `paymentGatewayNames` on orders still `gateway IS NULL` in the last 5 days. The orders/create webhook records the gateway, but on a COD or a slow UPI capture Shopify has none yet at that moment; without this sweep those orders sit permanently in the PG recon "excluded" bucket and the bill reads low |
| `*/2 * * * *` | **ShopifyHold auto-hold backstop** — catches repeat-COD orders the webhook missed. ⚠️ **HARDENED 2026-08-20** after **six red cards in one hour** (22:20–23:20), every one a NETWORK error: `TypeError: fetch failed` (Node/undici's wrapper for DNS failures, resets, refused connections) and one axios `timeout of 20000ms exceeded`. Nothing was wrong with the job. THREE faults turned a blip into an outage, all now fixed: **(1) no overlap guard** — two runs lasted **148s and 243s against a 120s schedule**, so runs overlapped, concurrent batches hit the same Shopify endpoint, timeouts got likelier and runs got longer: a self-feeding spiral. A tick now SKIPS if the previous run is still going (`_shRunning`), logged but never carded. **(2) one bad order aborted the batch** — `holdOrderSmart` makes live Shopify + Supabase calls, so a throw on order 7 abandoned the other 43 and surfaced as a whole-cron failure; each order is isolated now, its reason counted, one summary line per run. **(3) any error raised a card** — `cron_report.js` turns `console.error` into an immediate ❌, and this cron **self-heals in TWO MINUTES**, so the bar has to be higher than one blip: a transient failure WARNS, and only **`SH_ALERT_AFTER` consecutive failures (default 5 ≈ 10 min of continuous failure)** escalate, then hourly while it stays down. ⚠️ **A non-transient error is still loud immediately** — muffling a real bug is the failure mode of this kind of fix and is worse than the noise it removes. The candidate lookup also retries once after 2s (“orders lookup failed” was one of the six). Measured on the real messages: the 20 Aug pattern (6 failures, longest streak 1) now raises **0 cards**; a 60-minute total outage raises **2** instead of 30. Seven selftests pin it, including that genuine bugs stay non-transient  ⚠⚠ **AND IT STILL CARDED, BECAUSE THE LIST HAD THE WRONG VOCABULARY (fixed 2026-08-22).** Four more cards on 21 Aug 20:42–20:44 — and **three of them read `TypeError: terminated`**, which was NOT in `SH_TRANSIENT`. It therefore fell through to the non-transient branch and raised a card on **every occurrence**, so the whole escalation policy did nothing for the failure that was actually happening. `terminated` is what **undici** throws when the connection dies mid-response, and undici is what `fetch` runs on — the list had been written in generic Node vocabulary (`ECONNRESET`, `socket hang up`) rather than undici’s own. It now also covers `UND_ERR_*`, `HeadersTimeoutError` / `BodyTimeoutError` / `ConnectTimeoutError`, `SocketError`, `ECONNREFUSED`, `EPIPE` and `socket disconnected`. Replaying that night’s exact four messages: **4 cards before, 0 after**; an hour of continuous `terminated` gives 2. A genuine bug (`Cannot read properties of undefined`, `… is not a function`) is still non-transient and still loud immediately. ⏱️ **A run also now has a DEADLINE** (`SH_RUN_MS`, default 100s, under the 120s tick) — the cards carried durations of **150.5s, 177.1s, 305.1s and 500.9s**, i.e. a run blocking four ticks behind it. ⚠ The deadline stops the tick **WAITING**; it does NOT cancel the work, so `_shRunning` is cleared by the run itself finishing and **never** by the tick giving up — the obvious `finally { _shRunning = false }` would let the next tick start while the abandoned run was still holding orders, which is the exact overlap the guard exists to prevent. The abandonment error is marked `transient = true` **by flag, not by wording**, because a message-shaped test for our own error is a string we could rename by accident. ⚠ The selftest now **evals `shTransient` straight out of `server.js`** instead of re-typing it: the hand-copied version silently missed the new flag and passed against its own copy of the rule — caught only because the assertion disagreed with the real code. |
| `0 */2 * * *` | RapidShyp sync — last 7 days (skips 16:00 slot). Fetches only AWBs with **no row yet** in `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom`, so anything that fails is simply picked up by the next run. **Hardened 2026-08-17** after a card read `❌ Cron failed — RS Sync · 827s · timeout of 8000ms exceeded` ×3: (a) the 8s timeout is now **`RS_TIMEOUT_MS` (25s)** — the same API is given 25s by `fetchRsLive()` in `warehouse_slack_report.js`, which never had these timeouts; (b) **one retry** after a 2s backoff on transient errors (timeout / 429 / 5xx), since a first-attempt timeout usually means RapidShyp is briefly slow; (c) per-AWB `console.error` is gone — it made three isolated timeouts a red cron-failure card for work that self-heals two hours later. Failures are now summarised in **one** line, at WARN, escalating to ERROR (which raises the card) only when **≥25% of a batch of ≥5 fails**; (d) **`RS_ABORT_AFTER` (6 consecutive failures)** stops the run when RapidShyp is down rather than walking hundreds of AWBs at ~27s each — one error line, remaining AWBs retry next run |
| `0 16 * * *` | RapidShyp sync — full month-to-date |
| — (not a cron) | **RapidShyp "unknown AWB" handling (2026-08-03)** — `enrichAWBsBackground()` in `fulfillment_ops.js` asks RapidShyp about every AWB found on a Shopify fulfillment, but the catalogue is multi-courier, so shipments booked with **Delhivery direct / GoKwik(Kwikship) / DocPharma** come back **HTTP 400 "unknown AWB"**. That is a normal fact, not a failure (nothing was ever going to be written for them), yet it was `console.error`'d — which under cron reporting raised false ❌ cards. Now a **400** logs at `warn` and the AWB is remembered in `notRapidshypAwbs` so later runs skip it entirely; any other status (5xx / timeout / auth) still `console.error`s and still alerts. ⚠️ Keyed on **RapidShyp's own verdict, never the courier name** — measured: **13 of 181 GoKwik AWBs and 28 non-RapidShyp-journey AWBs ARE tracked by RapidShyp**, so filtering by aggregator/courier would have wrongly skipped real shipments and lost their tracking |
| `0 9 * * *` (`CRON_DIGEST_SCHEDULE`) | **Cron digest → Teams "Cron Response"** (2026-08-01). Every job runs through `cronJob()` → `runCron()` in **`app/api/cron_report.js`**, which times it, catches what it throws, **and also intercepts `console.error` for the duration** so errors a job swallowed itself (`.catch(e => console.error(…))` — most of them do) are still reported. **Attribution is by LOG TAG (2026-08-03):** a captured line counts only when its own `[Tag]` matches the job name (names are derived from that same tag, so `ShopifyHold (*/2 * * * *)` accepts only `[ShopifyHold…]` lines). Without this a long job absorbs whatever overlaps it — `ShopifyHold` runs ~43s and was reported FAILED because of `[RS Sync] … 400` lines that were never its own. Lines from another job, or untagged, are ignored (that job reports its own); a **thrown** error is always attributed regardless of logging, so a genuine crash can't be missed. **A FAILURE posts an immediate ❌ card** (job · time · duration · reason); **successes are counted and rolled into this one digest** (runs · ok · failed + last error per job). Why not a card per success: the 26 jobs fire **~1,800×/day** (two `*/2` jobs alone are 1,440), which would bury the failures. Modes via `CRON_REPORT_MODE`: `digest` (default) / `all` (a card per run) / **`failures`** (failures only, no digest — silence means healthy; **this is what live runs**) / `off`. **`all` is impractical in practice — measured 1,748 cards/day, one every 49s** — because the two `*/2` jobs alone are 1,440 of them and both are usually instant no-ops (they return the moment their feature flag is off), so real failures get buried; `CRON_REPORT_SKIP` (comma-separated, case-insensitive substrings of the job name) mutes the SUCCESS card for named jobs under `all` only — **failures are never muted**, a skipped job still posts the instant it fails. Suggested if using `all`: `CRON_REPORT_SKIP=TallyBatch,ShopifyHold,EE Session,EscMail` → ~66 cards/day. Needs `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_CRON` (a Workflows *incoming webhook* created **on that channel** — a channel link is not a webhook); unset = reporting silently off and the crons run untouched. Reporting never throws, so it cannot take a cron down |
| `30 8-20/2 * * *` | Warehouse Ops report → Teams, **every 2 hours 08:30–20:30 IST** (cutoff −1; override with `WH_REPORT_CRON`). Was 3×/day until 2026-08-17. Overnight is skipped deliberately — nobody acts on a 02:30 card, and every run live-verifies each pending AWB with RapidShyp. Highlights **orders stuck in Ready-for-Pickup over `WH_STUCK_HOURS` (default 48h)** at the top of the card, oldest first with an age. That age comes from EasyEcom `raw_data.manifest_date`, falling back to `shipping_last_update_date` for parcels still at shipping_status "Shipment Created" — a label exists but the parcel was never manifested, which IS the stuck condition (one such order had sat 5 days). Both strings are **IST with no zone**: parsing them as UTC shifts every age by 5.5h, enough to flip an order across the threshold, so `parseIst()` subtracts the offset explicitly. ⚠️ Deliberately NOT aged from the order date (an order placed 3 days ago and labelled an hour ago is not stuck) nor from `shipment_journey_ecom.awb_assigned_at` (only ~26% populated, 0% on Kwikship/DocPharma). An order with no usable timestamp is never guessed at. **Movement comes from the COURIERS, never EasyEcom (2026-08-01):** `fetchCourierMoved()` reads `shipment_journey_ecom` — the one table **RapidShyp + DocPharma + Kwikship** all write — matched on `awb` OR `order_name`, and drops an order only on a real movement EVENT (`dispatched_at` / `out_for_delivery_at` / `delivered_at` / `rto_at` set, or `is_final`). Previously "has it moved?" asked **only RapidShyp**, so a parcel on any other carrier had no record → read as "not moved" → fell through to Shopify's `displayStatus=CONFIRMED` and sat in the report forever (**45 of 53 "Confirmed" orders had already shipped**; 70 reported vs 27 real). ⚠️ **Do NOT use `b2c_order_easycom.order_status` for movement** — EasyEcom flips an order to `Shipped` when the AWB is merely BOOKED, so that route hid 12 genuinely stuck parcels whose RapidShyp status was still `Shipment Booked` / `Pick up Exception`, 7 of them unpicked since 24–25 Jul. EasyEcom is used ONLY for the fallback AWB + the `Cancelled` drop. Deliberately NOT "a journey row exists": a label can be generated (row created, `outcome='in_transit'`) while the parcel is still on the shelf — those must stay in Ready-for-Pickup. **GROUPED BY “Platform · Courier” (2026-08-20, user ask):** the Stuck and Ready-for-Pickup sections group orders under the courier whose van hasn't come (▸ *RapidShyp · Delhivery (11)* …), largest group first, “No courier assigned yet” last — that IS who to chase, which is the report's job. Source: `shipment_journey_ecom` source+courier joined on the normalized order name (`fetchPlatformCourier`), **falling back to EasyEcom's own allocation** (`b2c_order_easycom.courier_name` + the `raw_data.courier_aggregator_name` marker → RapidShyp/KwikShip) for orders with no journey row — ⚠️ the journey is written at the FIRST COURIER SCAN, and Ready-for-Pickup is precisely the pre-scan population, so two Shree Maruti orders manifested at 03:35 read “No courier assigned yet” on the 06:30 card until this fallback (2026-08-20); courier names normalised without losing identity (“Blue Dart Air”→Bluedart, but “Speed Post” stays whole — first-word truncation made it a meaningless “Speed” on the first dry run). **Confirmed is grouped too** (user, 2026-08-20 — and rightly: 34 of 36 Confirmed orders were already KwikShip-allocated on the first dry run, which the “no label yet” assumption would have hidden); only Unfulfillable stays flat — nothing is allocated on a dead order. `sendWarehouseOpsReport(days, { dry: true })` builds the payload and posts nothing — how changes to this report get previewed |
| `0 20 * * *` | Full **forced** RapidShyp cache refresh — standalone since 2026-08-17, timed 30 min before the day's last report so the evening card is built on the freshest courier status |
| `47 8-19 * * *` | DocPharma-rejected **detection** (at :47, deduped via handled-list) → Teams report + records to `dp_rejected_handled_ecom` |
| `56 8-19 * * *` | Warehouse **auto-route** pass (at :56, 9 min after detection) — gently moves not-yet-routed rejections to Shifupro (cookie-based, ~1 order/1.5s, `routed_at` marks done). Kept separate + slow so it never bursts/crashes |
| `0 11,18 * * *` | On-Hold report → Teams — **twice daily, 11 AM & 6 PM IST** (2026-07-29). **Covers BOTH sources**: EasyEcom holds (live-verified) **and** Shopify holds (`shopify_hold` marks). **Shopify holds are GROUPED BY REASON** (2026-07-29) — each distinct reason from the mark's note (e.g. "short address (<60 chars)", "no delivery in last 3, another live order") is a heading, with its order IDs listed **horizontally as chips** beneath it (biggest group first); EasyEcom holds are one group (no per-order reason). **Shopify marks are cross-checked against the order's live state** and stale ones dropped (cancelled / fulfilled / already-shipped orders whose mark was never cleared — e.g. 68 marks → 37 genuinely held), matching how the dashboard decides the hold chip. "all clear" only when both are empty |
| `*/2 * * * *` | **Shopify auto-hold backstop** — holds repeat COD orders (same criteria as the Call Queue Repeat tab) on Shopify before EasyEcom imports them. Backstop to the instant `orders/create` webhook. OFF unless `SHOPIFY_AUTOHOLD_ENABLED=true`; skips already-held / manually-released |
| `*/20 * * * *` | EasyEcom panel-session **freshness watch** — warns in the server log if the extension-synced routing cookie goes stale (>45 min = extension offline). The VPS can't ping/keep it warm (WAF); the browser extension does |
| `30 9 * * 1` | **Silent-RTO weekly claim email → RapidShyp** (Mon, last **30 days**, freight + invoice value) |
| `45 9 1,16 * *` | **Late-deliveries email** — **every 15 days (1st & 16th)** 9:45 AM, last 30 days, delivered-only; one email per platform |
| `30 9 * * *` | **First-OFD-late email** — **DAILY** 9:30 AM (RTO-only; last 30 days; first OFD after promise EDD; per platform → RapidShyp/DocPharma) |
| `35 9 * * *` | **In-transit-overdue email** — **DAILY** 9:35 AM (still in transit past promised EDD; last 30 days; per platform) |
| `*/10 * * * *` | **Escalation reply poll** — IMAP inbox scan, match replies to sent threads (whole-chain In-Reply-To + subject), AI-score inbound replies, retry unscored |
| `20 */3 * * *` | RapidShyp cache sync for EasyEcom orders + once ~15s after boot |
| `0 10 * * *` | Amazon auto-review — finds eligible orders and **sends immediately** (`AUTO_REVIEW_CRON`, in `amazon_auto_review.js`) |
| `30 6 * * *` | FBA locations sync (`amazon_fba.js`) |
| every 20 s | Teams keyword listener poll (`teams_listener.js`) — not a cron, an interval |

---

### Finance → Tally nightly push (added 2026-07-30)

| Cron (IST) | Job |
|---|---|
| `45 23 * * *` | Warm the Tally masters so the 23:50 validation reflects Tally *now* (a ledger renamed during the day must not let a push silently create it under Suspense) |
| `50 23 * * *` | **Build the batch** — take every `draft`, re-validate against live masters, group the valid ones, post the Teams approval card. **Nothing is sent to Tally.** |
| `*/2 * * * *` | **Watcher** — once every voucher in a `pushing` batch is terminal, post the result card. A cron rather than a hook inside `/bridge/ack`, so a batch is still reported if the agent dies mid-run. Also flags a batch stalled >1 h. |
| `5 * * * *` | Expire batches unapproved for `TALLY_BATCH_TTL_HOURS` (24) — vouchers return to `draft` for the next run, so nothing is lost and nothing is double-posted |

All four are no-ops unless `TALLY_BATCH_CRON_ENABLED=true`.

## 10. External Integrations

| Service | Usage | Module |
|---|---|---|
| **Shopify** | Orders, fulfillments, customers, tags (GraphQL/REST) | `orders.js`, sync scripts |
| **RapidShyp** | `track_order` (scan timelines), `shipment_details` (freight/`final_freights`, invoice value, EDD — lookup by AWB), serviceability; inbound webhook (Supabase edge function) | `delivery_journey.js`, `shipping.js`, `serviceability.js` |
| **DocPharma** | `POST /fetch-details` (status milestones only — **no scan log**), partner portal ingest (auto-login), invoices/ledger recon. Heavily rate-limited — all checks are cached | `helpers.js`, `docpharma_*.js` |
| **Kwikship (GoKwik)** | `GET /api/v1/shipments/:awb` → `status_history[]` (a real scan timeline). Auth = `gk-app-id`+`gk-app-secret` headers. **Real-time `kwikship-webhook` edge fn** (GoKwik Merchant Webhooks) + nightly 2 AM cron backstop → `shipment_journey_ecom` source=`kwikship` (ONLY Kwikship-allocated orders, see below). Live-tracking modals fall back to Kwikship when RapidShyp/DocPharma 400 (the Fulfillment-Ops `/track`,`/track-order` handlers + the Delivery-Perf `/delivery-performance/shipment/:awb` branch, which **caches the scan log on first view** into `raw.status_history` — repeat views cost 0 API, mirroring RapidShyp's `raw.scans` cache; read back via `ksScans()`) | `kwikship_sync.js`, `fulfillment_ops.js`, `delivery_reports.js`, `functions/kwikship-webhook` |
| **Tally Prime (XML gateway)** | Finance → Data Entry. Plain-HTTP XML on **port 9000** (Gateway of Tally → F1 → Advanced Config → *Tally acts as: Both*). Writes = `TALLYREQUEST: Import Data` / `REPORTNAME: Vouchers`; reads = `Export`/`Collection` for ledgers, groups, voucher types, stock items, company list. **Four traps the code defends against:** (1) Tally answers **HTTP 200 even on total failure** — success is `CREATED>=1` **and** no `LINEERROR`, decided only in `parseImportResponse`; (2) Dr/Cr is expressed by **sign** — Dr = `ISDEEMEDPOSITIVE=Yes` + **negative** `AMOUNT`, Cr = `No` + positive (inverted vouchers are accepted silently); (3) dates are `YYYYMMDD` in the company calendar — deriving them via `toISOString()` books IST entries to the **previous day**; (4) an unknown `LEDGERNAME` **auto-creates** that ledger under Suspense instead of erroring, so every name is validated against `tally_masters_ecom` first. **Reads** add two more: (5) the stock `TYPE=Data / ID=DayBook` export **ignores `SVFROMDATE`/`SVTODATE`** (returns only the current date, at ~43KB per voucher) — a `Collection` of `Voucher` with `<FETCH>` honours the range and costs ~3KB each; (6) every reply opens with a `<CMPINFO>` block containing a literal **`<VOUCHER>0</VOUCHER>` counter element** that matches a voucher regex and parses into a phantom null row, so `parseVouchers` requires a `<DATE>` to accept a block. **Connectivity:** the gateway only listens on the finance PC's localhost, and it has **no authentication whatsoever** — never expose port 9000 to the internet or a tunnel. `TALLY_MODE=direct` (app running on the Tally machine) posts synchronously; `TALLY_MODE=bridge` (the LIVE/VPS case) queues the voucher and the agent in `tally-bridge/` pulls it over key-authenticated `/api/tally/bridge/*`. **Multiple companies.** As soon as two sets of books are open, several things stop having a safe default and are handled explicitly: `resolveCompany()` **refuses to guess** when more than one company is open and `TALLY_COMPANY` is unset (picking "whichever Tally listed first" would silently decide which financial year an entry lands in); `parseCompanyInfo(xml, want)` must be told which company, because Tally's reply lists them all and the first block's `BooksFrom` would otherwise mis-decide whether a voucher is in period; the masters mirror is keyed `(kind, name, company)` and pruned per company, since a single-company prune deleted the other company's whole chart of accounts and a `(kind,name)` unique index made `HDFC BANK` collide across both. `/tally/status` returns `openCompanies` + `ambiguousCompany` so the UI can force a choice.

**The nightly batch only ever takes `source='manual'`** and is capped at `TALLY_BATCH_MAX` (250). A bank import can leave ~1000 drafts; sweeping those into one Teams card would be unreadable and unapprovable, so imported vouchers are posted deliberately from the import screen instead. Any overflow is reported on the card rather than silently dropped.

**Deleting in Tally is deliberately NOT exposed.** `tally_xml.js` has a tested `buildDeleteVoucherXml`/`parseDeleteResponse` pair (Tally needs `REMOTEID`+`VCHKEY`, not just `MASTERID`, and reports `<DELETED>1</DELETED>`), but no route calls it — the operator removes vouchers inside Tally with Alt+D, which keeps the irreversible action where the audit trail and GST period locks live. The dashboard's Delete only removes our own row. `TALLY_POST_ENABLED` is a master kill switch — while it is not exactly `true`, vouchers can be drafted, validated and previewed but nothing is sent | `tally.js`, `tally_xml.js` |
| **EasyEcom** | B2C order sync, on-hold report, JWT-auth API | `easyecom.js` |
| **Amazon SP-API** | Orders, review-request eligibility ("Request a Review"), FBA stock/inbound | `amazon*.js` |
| **Meta (Facebook) Ads** | Ad set / ad performance metrics | `adset_performance.js`, `ad_performance.js` |
| **Microsoft Teams (outbound)** | 4 Power Automate Workflow webhooks post **native Adaptive Cards** (warehouse-ops, dp-to-mwh, easyecom-hold, amazon-review). Payload: `{ card: JSON.stringify(adaptiveCard) }`. The **EasyEcom On-Hold** and **Warehouse Ops** reports additionally send `{ text: <html>, attachments: [<card>] }` so their flows can post as a **reply into a specific thread** via "Reply with a message in a channel" (Adaptive Cards can't be channel replies). `postTeams(url, payload, {text})`: `text` is a verbatim HTML string, or `true` to auto-generate HTML from the Slack blocks via `slackToHtml()` (header→bold, section→text, fields→stacked lines, order IDs→`<code>` chips). On-Hold uses a hand-built string → `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_WAREHOUSE_HOLD` (workflow `fdac450e`, thread `1784287243826`); Warehouse uses `text:true` → `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_WAREHOUSE` (thread `1784291816658`). The single-element `attachments` array makes each flow's `For each` (over `attachments`) run exactly once → one reply. Other reports omit `text`/`attachments`. | `teams.js`, `warehouse_slack_report.js` |
| **Microsoft Teams — EcomBot (bot, BOTH directions)** | 🤖 **The current answer, and the only one that does both directions under one identity (2026-08-14).** Azure Bot Service, **Single Tenant**, Free (F0) tier, resource `ecom-central-bot` in RG `ecom-central`. Code: **`app/api/teams_bot.js`** → `POST /api/bot/messages` (public in `PUBLIC_API`; it carries a MICROSOFT JWT which the module verifies against Microsoft's published signing keys with **audience pinned to our App ID** — without that anyone could POST a fake "approve the Tally push"). Outbound uses a client-credentials token for `https://api.botframework.com/.default` against the **tenant** endpoint (single-tenant bots cannot use `common`). No Bot Framework SDK — three REST calls, same reasoning as the base64 upload path. **Why it exists:** Graph polling is protected-API blocked, incoming webhooks post as "Workflows" and can't receive, outgoing webhooks are reply-only and **don't work in private channels**. Env: `TEAMS_BOT_APP_ID`, `TEAMS_BOT_APP_PASSWORD`, `TEAMS_TENANT_ID`. Diagnostics: `GET /api/bot/health` (admin-gated) shows whether Azure issues a token and which channels are reachable. App package + icon generator live in **`teams-app/`** (`node teams-app/build.js`, then zip the three files **at the zip ROOT**); `botId` is read from `.env` so it cannot drift from the real Azure app. ⚠️ **Azure warns icon changes take up to 24 h to propagate** — a "wrong logo" right after an update is almost always cache. ⚠️ **`serviceUrl` is TENANT-scoped** (`https://smba.trafficmanager.net/in/<tenant>`), learned from any inbound activity and stored in `teams_bot_channels_ecom`; because it is tenant-wide, one learned value serves every channel, so a new report channel only needs the bot INSTALLED in its team — nobody has to @mention it there first | `teams_bot.js`, `teams-app/` |
| **Microsoft Teams — app updater (Settings → "Teams bot app")** | 🔁 **One click keeps the bot's name/icon current in every team — no remove → re-add → re-map (2026-08-27).** `app/api/teams_app.js`, admin-only: `GET /api/teams/app/status` (package version from `teams-app/manifest.json`, org-catalog version via `GET /appCatalogs/teamsApps?$filter=externalId eq '<TEAMS_BOT_APP_ID>'&$expand=appDefinitions`, per-team installed version via `/me/joinedTeams` → `/teams/{id}/installedApps?$expand=teamsApp,teamsAppDefinition`), `POST /api/teams/app/update` (publishes `teams-app/Pravidhi.zip` as a new app definition when the catalog is behind the package, then `POST /teams/{team}/installedApps/{id}/upgrade` in every team with an older copy; per-step results returned + logged as `[TeamsApp]`). Same App ID throughout, so channel history and the bot's stored serviceUrl are untouched. **Auth = the listener's delegated Graph identity (`TEAMS_CLIENT_ID` + rotating `TEAMS_REFRESH_TOKEN`) but with 3 extra delegated scopes** — `Team.ReadBasic.All`, `TeamsAppInstallation.ReadWriteForTeam` (admin must be a team OWNER), `AppCatalog.ReadWrite.All` — which must be **added to the app registration with admin consent** (Azure → App registrations → API permissions) or the token refresh fails with AADSTS65001; the card then shows **Sign in to Microsoft** = a device-code flow (`POST /api/teams/app/signin` → code + link; `GET /api/teams/app/signin/:handle` polled by the browser — the device_code never leaves the server) that re-mints `TEAMS_REFRESH_TOKEN` via the listener's `persistRefreshToken`. ⚠️ Same tenant caveat as the message API: if Microsoft moves the catalog/installation APIs behind protected-API approval, status returns 403 and the card says so — it never claims an update it did not make. The catalog must contain the app once (first upload is manual in Teams admin center); after that the button owns every later version. Prebuilt-Tailwind trap: `sm:grid-cols-3`, `disabled:opacity-50`, `list-disc`, `text-[11px]` are absent → inline styles / a scoped `<style>` rule. |
| **Microsoft Teams — card rendering** | 📐 **`msteams: { width: 'Full' }` is on every card (2026-08-14).** Without it Teams renders cards at a narrow fixed width and leaves most of the message area blank — which is what squeezed the reorder table into `16,8…` / `TE-BD…` no matter how few columns it had. It is a Teams-specific extension the Adaptive Card schema does not describe. Any card carrying a table wants it. 🧱 **Tables are rendered TWO ways on purpose.** The card is built twice and only when needed: **EcomBot sends schema 1.5** and gets the native `Table` element with `showGridLines` (real borders, cell padding, header styling); the **webhook fallback stays on 1.4**, where `Table` does not exist, and gets the column-major `ColumnSet` imitation. Sending 1.5 markup down the 1.4 Workflows connector renders the table as **nothing at all**, which is why one card cannot serve both. The long-standing comment saying native tables "aren't available to us" was true of the Workflows connector and stopped being true the moment a bot was sending. 🚫 **No action buttons on Teams cards** — "Open Inventory Dashboard" / "Open Voucher Register" / "Review & approve in dashboard" removed by request; suppressed inside `buildAdaptiveCard` (callers may still pass `actionUrl`, it is ignored) so a newly-added report cannot reintroduce one. 📱 **A PHONE IS THE BINDING WIDTH CONSTRAINT, and `msteams.width: Full` does NOT apply there (2026-08-19).** The card is ~360px on Teams mobile whatever the desktop shows, and **Adaptive Cards cannot scroll horizontally** — the user asked for a scrollable table and the platform simply has no such thing. The reorder table proved it twice in one day: TEN columns gave one-letter headers (its own comment still said seven — comment drift), and even a five-column regrouping still clipped every cell on a phone. **So the Recommended Order is now a PNG** — rendered by the `inventory-doi-image-teams` edge fn (v12) in the same dark style as the stock table, with the FULL ten-column detail restored (SKU · Stock · DRR · DOI · Recommend · Raised PO · Final Qty · Case Size · Cases · Order Units + TOTAL). The ROWS ARE COMPUTED IN NODE and posted to the fn as `{ reorder: [...] }` — the open-PO subtraction lives in `inventory.js` (EasyEcom lookups) and rendering caller-supplied rows means no second copy of that rule to drift; no payload → the fn behaves exactly as before. Uploaded to `reports/inventory-reorder/<date>.png`; a render failure degrades to the five-column card table (SKU · Stock·DOI · Need · Cases · Order Units), never drops the report. ⚠️ Satori glyphs: the Roboto latin subset has no ✓/≤ (tofu) — covered rows read "on PO". 🔍 **Report images expand IN TEAMS** — every image block gets `msteams: { allowExpand: true }`, which opens Teams' own full-screen viewer with pinch-zoom. ⚠️ **NOT `selectAction: Action.OpenUrl`** — that was shipped first and it opens the raw PNG in the BROWSER, which the user explicitly rejected; the selftest fails a card that carries a selectAction on an image for exactly that reason. A card `Image` with neither does nothing on tap (Teams never opens its viewer for card images unaided). Both constraints are enforced in `tests/selftest.js` **through the real renderer** (builds the 1.4 and 1.5 cards; asserts allowExpand + no selectAction + the ≤5-column fallback), not by grepping source — the seven-vs-ten drift is exactly what a grep-style check misses | `teams.js`, `inventory.js`, edge fn `inventory-doi-image-teams` |
| **Microsoft Teams (outbound reports)** | `postTeams()` **prefers EcomBot and falls back to the Workflow webhook** — it can only add a delivery path, never remove one. The channel is resolved from the WEBHOOK URL (`TEAMS_WEBHOOK_<X>` ↔ `TEAMS_CHANNEL_<X>`), so every existing report switched sender with **no call-site changes**; a report whose channel id is unset simply stays on its webhook. ⭐ **`TEAMS_CHANNEL_<X>` may carry a THREAD ROOT**: `19:…@thread.tacv2;messageid=1784787628987` posts as a **reply inside that thread** instead of a new channel post. That matters because Hold, Warehouse and Inventory all share **Ops › Daily Reports** and would otherwise interleave as top-level posts, burying the history each thread has built. The `messageid` is the `parentMessageId=` from a Teams "copy link to message" URL. **This is a capability only the bot has** — Adaptive Cards cannot be posted as channel replies through a Workflow. ⚠️ Amazon/Finance still point at a **private** channel, where bots are limited, so those two remain on Workflows until moved to standard channels | `teams.js` |
| **Microsoft Teams (inbound, superseded)** | ⚠️ **Graph polling is DEAD on this tenant — use the webhook (2026-08-14).** Microsoft gates the Teams MESSAGE APIs behind a separate **protected-API approval**: without it `GET /teams/{id}/channels/{id}/messages` returns a bare **403 / UnknownError** even though `ChannelMessage.Read.All` IS granted, `/me` returns 200, and the account genuinely IS in the team — all three verified here. Nothing in the code can fix it, and it starts failing with no change on our side because it turns on by tenant rollout. **The direction is now reversed** — Teams pushes to **`POST /api/webhook/teams`**; no Graph permission, no token rotation, instant instead of a 20 s poll. Two callers are accepted, whichever authenticates: **(1) a Teams OUTGOING WEBHOOK** (the one actually in use — free; Power Automate's generic HTTP action turned out to be a **Premium** connector) which signs the raw body `Authorization: HMAC <base64>` with the token shown ONCE at creation (`TEAMS_OUTGOING_TOKEN`; ⚠️ the HMAC key is that token **base64-DECODED**, not the literal string), and **(2)** a Power Automate flow sending `x-teams-token: <TEAMS_INBOUND_SECRET>`. ⚠️⚠️ **Two traps in the Outgoing-Webhook payload, both silent:** its `channelId` is the literal string **`"msteams"`**, NOT the Teams channel — the real one is `channelData.teamsChannelId` (falls back to `conversation.id` before the `;`); and the text arrives as **`<at>EcomBot</at> rejected`**, so plain tag-stripping leaves `"EcomBot rejected"` and `parseApproval` — which requires the command to be the WHOLE message — would reject **every** command. The mention element is removed before stripping. Commands are @mentioned: `@EcomBot rejected` / `@EcomBot yes`. The webhook is **team-wide**, so a mention in any Ops channel reaches us; unconfigured channels are ignored by `handleInboundMessage`. **Reply behaviour:** Teams posts our HTTP response into the channel, so we stay **silent when an action fired** (the existing flow already posts its own 🔄 ack + result — a second line would duplicate it) and **also silent on a duplicate id** (Teams retries on a slow reply; "ignored, nothing pending" would misinform someone who just watched it work), speaking up only when a mention matched nothing. Same actions as before: `"rejected"` in DP → DP report; `"yes"/"no"` in Amazon/Finance → approve/cancel the pending batch; posts a "🔄 Got it" ack. **Both paths call the SAME `handleInboundMessage()`** (dedup by message id inside), so they cannot disagree about what a message means, and a message arriving on both acts once. Set **`TEAMS_POLL=0`** to stop the futile Graph reads. ⚠️ The route is PUBLIC (like every webhook), so `TEAMS_INBOUND_SECRET` is the only thing between the internet and "approve the Tally push" — compared in constant time, and the route **503s rather than opening** if the secret is unset or under 16 chars. `teams_listener.js` also self-diagnoses on the first failed read (`/me`, `/me/joinedTeams`, `/teams/{id}/channels` + a verdict); note its team/channel probes need `Team.ReadBasic.All`/`Channel.ReadBasic.All` which the token does NOT carry, so a 403 on those two is inconclusive by design and labelled as such | `teams_listener.js`, `webhook_handler.js` |
| **Slack** | **Decommissioned (2026-07).** Posting + keyword trigger hard-disabled in code unless `SLACK_ENABLED=true` | `warehouse_slack_report.js` |

| **SMTP (Gmail)** | All report emails via shared `sendMail()` — portal settings override `.env` defaults | `email_settings.js` |
| **IMAP (same mailbox)** | Reads the inbox every 10 min for replies to sent escalations (imapflow + mailparser; two-phase fetch — never download inside an active fetch stream). Replies matched via In-Reply-To across the whole thread chain, then AI-scored | `email_replies.js` |
| **RapidShyp NDR action** | `POST /ndr/action` — reattempt (with corrected phone/address) or return. Vendor docs contradict themselves on the action value (`REATTEMPT` vs `RE_ATTEMPT`); the wrapper tries the curl-example spelling first and falls back only on an invalid-action rejection. **Never live-test** — it acts on real shipments | `ops_control.js` |
| **EasyEcom hold/unhold** | `PUT /orders/holdOrders` / `unholdOrders` keyed by **invoice_id** (from `b2c_order_easycom.raw_data`) — endpoint discovered from their official Postman collection (not in public docs). API JWT refused (needs panel session) → we **replay the admin's browser session cookie** (`laravel_session`+`PHPSESSID`, stored AES-GCM in `easyecom_panel_session`). ⚠️ The VPS's datacenter IP is **AWS-WAF-blocked** from `app.easyecom.io`, so UpdateVendor is executed by the **browser extension** (residential IP), not the server (see the `/ee-routes` executor above). Modal session badge = **cookie freshness** (extension re-syncs ~20 min), not a server ping. Only before shipment/AWB assigned | `easyecom.js` |

| **Apify** (Instagram scraping) | Influencer CRM: profile/reels/tagged-post scrapers — key lives as a Supabase edge-function secret (`APIFY_API_KEY`), invoked via `analyze-influencer` / `fetch-reel-metrics` / `refresh-recent-video-metrics` / `scan-brand-mentions` | Supabase edge functions |
| **Lovable AI Gateway** (Gemini) | Influencer AI vetting inside `analyze-influencer` — key is the `LOVABLE_API_KEY` edge-function secret | Supabase edge functions |

## 11. Reports & Notifications

### Teams channel reports (Adaptive Cards)

**Tabular data — the `table` block (added 2026-08-04).** Neither obvious way of showing a table in Teams works: a ```` ``` ```` code fence renders as **literal backticks with the whitespace collapsed** (so hand-padded columns arrive as a ragged single-spaced list), and Adaptive Card 1.5's native `Table` element is unavailable because the Workflows connector pins **1.4**. So `teams.js` gained a pseudo-block `{ type: 'table', columns: [{title, width?, align?}], rows: [[...]], total?: [...] }` handled by `tableToCard()` / `tableToHtml()`. The card path lays it out **column-major** — one `ColumnSet` whose N columns each hold a stack of TextBlocks (header + every value for that column) — because separate per-row ColumnSets do not align to each other, while columns sharing one container width do. Cells default to **`align: 'Center'`** (override per column with `'Left'`/`'Right'`). ⚠️ **In the HTML twin, alignment must go on the legacy `align` attribute** — Teams' sanitizer strips `text-align` out of an inline `style` on `<td>`, which is why an early version had only the header row centred (that was Teams' own default `<th>` styling, not ours).

1. **Warehouse Ops** — pending Confirmed / Ready-for-Pickup / Unfulfillable orders, grouped by category with order IDs, **every 2 hours 08:30–20:30 IST**. Opens with a 🚨 **stuck over 48h** block (oldest first, with each order's age) above every other list — the one section that is someone's job right now.
2. **DocPharma Rejected → Warehouse Action** — orders with no RapidShyp tracking that DocPharma cancelled/rejected (hourly 8-19). **Dedup:** each reported order is written to `dp_rejected_handled_ecom` and never re-reported; if that ledger can't be read the run **aborts** (fail-safe, no re-spam).
3. **On-Hold (EasyEcom + Shopify)** — daily 11:00. EasyEcom holds (live-verified) + Shopify `shopify_hold` marks shown with their reason.
4. **Amazon Review** — daily 10:00 IST. **No approval step (removed 2026-08-17).** The cron finds eligible orders (delivered 10–30 days ago, not already sent, not permanently 403/ineligible), posts a card saying what it is about to do, sends at one request per 1.2s, then posts a result card with sent/failed grouped by reason. `POST /api/amazon/auto-review/trigger` re-runs it by hand — retry-failed by default, `?mode=full` for the complete check — and `GET /auto-review/status` reports `running` plus the last run.

   ⚠️ **The eligibility reads are PAGED (`pageAll()`) — never collapse them back to one `.select()`.** PostgREST caps every response at 1000 rows silently, and `amazon_review_requests` passed that long ago (1,833 rows on 2026-08-17). Reading it unpaged built the already-sent / ineligible / permanently-failed sets from barely half the table, so orders solicited weeks earlier read as eligible again: the 2026-08-17 card announced **375 to send — every order in the window** — when the true figure was **63**. What saved it was `runBulkSend()`'s send-time re-check, which queries by explicit order-id chunks where the cap cannot apply; it skipped 312 and Amazon never saw them. The orders query is paged for the same reason though it sits at 375 today — a 20-day delivery window will cross 1000 in a busy month, and that truncation fails the *other* way: eligible orders silently never solicited, no error, nothing missing to notice.

   **Why the approval went away.** The pending batch lived in an in-memory variable, so a restart, redeploy or crash wiped it while the card still read "expires in 24 hours"; the reply then got "nothing is waiting for approval right now" and the day's batch was lost (hit 2026-08-17, four minutes after a deploy). Approval that cannot survive a restart is worse than no approval, because it looks reliable. The safety that matters is elsewhere and still in place: `runBulkSend()` re-checks every order's status immediately before sending, so an order already solicited or permanently rejected is never hit at Amazon twice, and a `running` flag stops the cron and the manual button overlapping.

**Who sends a card: EcomBot or Workflows.** `postTeams()` prefers the bot and falls back to the Workflows webhook. It picks the bot only when `channelForWebhook(url)` resolves a channel id, and that lookup finds the webhook's env var **by scanning every `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_*` key** for a URL match, then reads the matching `TEAMS_CHANNEL_<suffix>`. It used to check a hard-coded list of seven suffixes instead, and **any webhook var outside that list silently posted via Workflows forever** — which is what happened to the On-Hold report (2026-08-14 → fixed 2026-08-17): it posts to `TEAMS_WEBHOOK_WAREHOUSE_HOLD` (its own thread inside the Ops channel), a name the list never contained. Compound names resolve through an explicit `CHANNEL_ALIAS` map (`WAREHOUSE_HOLD → HOLD`, `FINANCE_RESULT → FINANCE`) — **never** by trimming the trailing word, which would drop the hold card into the warehouse thread.

Only the sender name on a posted card reveals which path was taken, so **`GET /api/teams/routing`** reports `bot` / `webhook` / `not configured` for every target plus the reason, without posting anything. `POST /api/teams/test?target=<name>` also returns the `via` it used. Check routing after adding any report or channel — a fallback is otherwise invisible.

### Emails (via portal-configured SMTP)
| Email | Recipient | Trigger |
|---|---|---|
| Silent-RTO claim (order/AWB/courier/date/**shipping cost**/**invoice value** + totals) | RapidShyp address from Settings | Mon 09:30 weekly + dashboard button |
| Late deliveries (promised vs delivered vs days late) | Per-platform recipients (RapidShyp / DocPharma, one email each) | Mon 09:45 weekly + dashboard button |
| In-transit overdue chase list | Per-platform recipients (one email each) | Dashboard button |
| First-OFD late **RTO** (order/AWB/courier/promise EDD/**first OFD**/RTO date/days late) — RTO whose first delivery attempt was after the promise EDD | Per-platform recipients → RapidShyp in practice | Mon 09:50 weekly + dashboard button |
| Critical escalation (AI-polished; single order or all marked-fake; shipment table auto-attached) | RapidShyp (or typed recipient) | Per-order / batch buttons (admin) |
| Adset performance PDFs (MTD + last month) | Default recipients | `cron_job.js` (external scheduler) |

**Reply tracking:** every sent critical escalation is logged as a thread (`escalation_emails_ecom`); the inbox is IMAP-polled every 10 minutes, replies are matched to their thread (In-Reply-To across the whole chain, subject fallback), stored, and **AI-scored** (0–100 resolution + status + suggested next action — inbound replies only). Threads render inside the order's expanded row on Delivery Performance in sender/receiver format (THE ELEMENT ↔ RAPIDSHYP).

## 12. Environment Variables

Defined in `.env` (git-ignored — **never commit**) and, since 2026-08-27, **sealed into `.env.vault` (AES-256-GCM) — see §17**; read exclusively through `config.js`, which loads them via `app/secrets.js`.

| Group | Variables |
|---|---|
| Server | `PORT` (5002), `JWT_SECRET` (≥32 chars, enforced), `DASHBOARD_URL` |
| Bootstrap admin | `APP_USER_EMAIL`, `APP_USER_PASSWORD`, `APP_USER_NAME` (optional — welcome-splash name for the .env admin; if unset it falls back to the name on the admin's Users-page row). 2FA OTP — all accounts — is emailed to the login address; no extra env vars, activates whenever SMTP is usable. Sessions last 6 hours |
| Supabase | `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` |
| Shopify | `SHOPIFY_TOKEN`, `SHOPIFY_SHOP_URL` |
| RapidShyp | `RAPIDSHYP_API_KEY`, `RAPIDSHYP_API_URL` |
| DocPharma | `DOCPHARMA_API_KEY`, `DP_PORTAL_EMAIL`, `DP_PORTAL_PASSWORD`, `DP_PORTAL_TOKEN`, `DP_TRIGGER_WORD` |
| Kwikship (GoKwik) | Node `.env`: `KWIKSHIP_APP_ID`, `KWIKSHIP_APP_SECRET` (→ `gk-app-id`/`gk-app-secret` headers), `KWIKSHIP_BASE_URL` (default `https://api.gokwik.co/kwikship`). **Supabase Edge secrets** (for `kwikship-webhook`): same `KWIKSHIP_APP_ID`/`KWIKSHIP_APP_SECRET` + `KWIKSHIP_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (the Bearer token pasted into GoKwik's webhook Token field) |
| EasyEcom | `EASYECOM_BASE_URL`, `EASYECOM_API_KEY`, `EASYECOM_WH_KEY` (Shifupro location key), `EASYECOM_WH2_KEY` (DocPharma/DP Bangalore location key — for change-warehouse routing), `EASYECOM_JWT`, `EASYECOM_EMAIL`, `EASYECOM_PASSWORD`, `EASYECOM_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` |
| Amazon SP-API | `AWS_ACCESS_KEY`, `AWS_SECRET_KEY`, `AWS_REGION`, `LWA_CLIENT_ID`, `LWA_CLIENT_SECRET`, `REFRESH_TOKEN`, `MARKETPLACE_ID`, `BASE_URL` |
| Meta Ads | `FACEBOOK_AD_ACCOUNT_ID`, `FACEBOOK_ACCESS_TOKEN` |
| Email | `EMAIL_HOST`, `EMAIL_PORT`, `EMAIL_USER`, `EMAIL_PASSWORD`, `RECIPIENT_EMAIL`, `EMAIL_ENC_KEY` (optional 64-hex AES key; falls back to key derived from `JWT_SECRET` — **don't change after passwords are stored**) |
| AI | `AI_API_KEY`, `AI_API_URL` (`https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com` for Gemini), `AI_MODEL` (`gemini-flash-latest`), `AI_MODEL_FALLBACK` (optional, default `gemini-flash-lite-latest` — used automatically on 503/429) |

| Teams inbound | **`TEAMS_OUTGOING_TOKEN`** (the token Teams shows ONCE when creating the outgoing webhook — HMAC key, base64-decoded), **`TEAMS_INBOUND_SECRET`** (only for the Power Automate alternative; ≥16 chars or the route 503s), **`TEAMS_POLL`** (`0` = stop the dead Graph polling), `TEAMS_TENANT_ID`, `TEAMS_CLIENT_ID`, `TEAMS_REFRESH_TOKEN` (auto-rotated — run the listener in ONE process only), `TEAMS_TEAM_ID`, `TEAMS_CHANNEL_DP`, `TEAMS_CHANNEL_AMAZON`, `TEAMS_CHANNEL_FINANCE`, `TEAMS_LISTENER_DRYRUN` |

| Misc | `PICKUP_PINCODE`, `AUTO_REVIEW_CRON`, `CACHE_DIR`, `GOOGLE_CREDENTIALS` |

## 13. Frontend Build

Tailwind is **statically compiled** (no runtime CDN — CSP forbids it):

```bash
node_modules/.bin/tailwindcss -i tw-input.css -o app/static/tailwind.css --minify
```

**Run this after ANY change that adds/removes CSS utility classes** in `app/templates/index.html` or `app/static/app.js`, and commit the rebuilt `app/static/tailwind.css`. The scan config lives in `tailwind.config.js` (no safelist — the content scan is sufficient).

**Dashboard content width (2026-08-17):** every view's inner container was `p-8 max-w-7xl mx-auto w-full` — 1280px, which left ~600px of empty margin either side on a 1900px screen while the tables squeezed. The cap is now **1720px**, set in `smooth.css` as `.p-8.mx-auto.w-full{max-width:1720px}` with wider padding above 1600px. ⚠️ **Real CSS on purpose:** `tailwind.css` is PREBUILT, so `max-w-[1720px]` and `max-w-screen-2xl` are not compiled and would have silently done nothing. Still capped rather than edge-to-edge — an unbounded row of eight KPI cards on an ultrawide stretches into unreadable strips. **Bump the `?v=` on the `smooth.css` link whenever you edit it**, or browsers serve the cached copy and the change appears not to have worked.

⚠️ **The prebuilt-Tailwind trap, again (2026-08-17):** `lg:grid-cols-8`, `text-violet-500` and `text-teal-600` were written into Order Insights and silently did nothing — `lg:grid-cols-8` is absent even though `md:`/`xl:grid-cols-8` exist. **Grep `app/static/tailwind.css` for the exact escaped selector (`grep -F 'lg\:grid-cols-8'`) before using any class you have not seen elsewhere in this codebase**, or rebuild Tailwind per the command above.

Shared UI conventions: `.filter-select` / `.filter-input` / `.filter-btn` (38px, custom chevron, indigo focus ring) for all filter rows; DD-MM-YYYY (IST) date display on newer dashboards; progressive/chunked table rendering with `requestAnimationFrame` for large lists; `escapeHtml`/`ecEsc` on all user-content sinks; **every loading state uses the branded logo loader** — `brandLoader(label)` (large, tables/KPIs/modals) or `brandLoaderSm(label)` (compact panels), never plain "Loading…" text.

## 14. Deployment & Operations

### Standard deploy
```bash
# on the server
cd <app-dir>
git pull
npm install            # required when package.json changed (2026-07: added imapflow + mailparser)
node_modules/.bin/tailwindcss -i tw-input.css -o app/static/tailwind.css --minify   # only if not committed
pm2 restart <app-name>
pm2 logs <app-name> --lines 50   # verify clean boot
```

### Boot checklist (log lines to expect)
- `Server running on port 5002`
- `[TeamsListener] started — watching Amazon + DP channels every 20s`
- `[AutoReview] Cron scheduled`
- `[DP Trigger] Slack keyword trigger disabled (Teams-only)` ← expected; Slack is off

### Which table tells the truth about order status (2026-08-17)
**`shipment_journey_ecom` — it is the only table RapidShyp, DocPharma AND Kwikship all write, from webhooks.** Measured over 30 days on the 5,887 orders that have an AWB: journey **99.9%** coverage · `order_tracking` 87.8% · `rapidshyp_tracking_ecom` 67.2% (RapidShyp-only, and a polled cache) · `b2c_order_easycom` present for all but ⚠️ flips to `Shipped` when an AWB is merely *booked* · `enriched_orders_ecom` present for all but **mixes Shopify fulfillment states with courier states**.

That last one was costing real money. The Ad Set Breakdown classified status from `enriched_orders_ecom`, where a Kwikship parcel's `raw_rapidshyp_status` is Shopify's **`FULFILLED`** — a value matching none of the courier keywords in `normalizeStatus()`, so a **delivered** Kwikship order fell through to **Processing** permanently. Nothing ever writes a courier status back to that table for Kwikship. Over 30 days it missed **805 delivered orders (674 Kwikship) ≈ ₹6.5 lakh of delivered revenue**, and **221 RTOs**. Delivered revenue drives ROAS, so every ad set shipping Kwikship looked worse than it was. Month-to-date before → after: delivered **802 → 1,532**, delivered revenue **₹6.35L → ₹12.17L**, RTO **240 → 459**.

⚠️ **An UNDELIVERED scan is NOT an RTO (2026-08-17).** `normalizeStatus` read `UNDELIVERED || RTO || RETURN` → `'RTO'`, but "undelivered" is a **failed delivery attempt** — the parcel is still with the courier and most reattempts succeed. It now returns `'In-Transit'`, and RTO requires an actual **RTO or RETURN**, i.e. a return that has been initiated. Month to date this moved **49 orders** out of RTO (rate **23.6% → 21.8%**), which had been inflating RTO% and depressing Eff. ROAS on every ad set carrying an open NDR. A courier verdict of `rto` still wins over the raw text.

`normalizeStatus(order, rawStatus, docpharmaData, journeyOutcome)` now takes the courier outcome first. ⚠️ **Only the FINAL outcomes override** — `delivered` / `rto` / `lost` are settled facts. `in_transit` and `ndr_pending` deliberately do NOT, because the older fields often carry a more specific live state (Out for Delivery, Delivery Delayed) and overriding would coarsen what already worked. Wired into `adset_performance.js` **and** `excel_report.js` (that export is this breakdown's per-order drill-down; leaving it behind would have made the two contradict each other on the same orders). ⚠️ `awb` must stay in the adset `COLS` select — it is the join key, and without it every lookup misses **silently** and the numbers simply stay wrong. Both lookups are chunked 200 at a time, since PostgREST caps a response at 1000 rows without saying so. Covered by `npm run selftest`.

### Engineering rule — every cache needs a signal or a TTL (2026-08-17)
**"Re-fetch only when the cache is empty" is not a cache policy — it is a permanent freeze.** The shipment scan log was cached in `shipment_journey_ecom.raw` and the live fetch ran only when that cache was empty, so **the first person to open a shipment froze its timeline forever**. TE25-40754 showed *"Out for delivery, 13 Aug 11:32"* for four days while the parcel went RTO that same afternoon and kept scanning (22 cached events vs 31 live). Delivery Performance and the Support tracker read the same endpoint (`/api/delivery-performance/shipment/:awb`), so both showed the frozen copy, and **79 of the 189 cached logs were stale** — stale precisely on the shipments someone had opened, i.e. the ones being chased. Nothing errored; a stale-but-plausible payload never does.

Use **`isCacheStale({capturedAt, signalAt, ttlMs, frozen})`** from `helpers.js` for any cached upstream payload. Prefer a **signal** — a timestamp some other job already maintains (here `shipment_journey_ecom.last_scan_at`, written by the webhook/cron) — because comparing it costs nothing and *proves* the cache is behind, where a TTL only guesses. TTL is the fallback when no signal exists. `frozen` (e.g. a delivered shipment) suppresses TTL expiry only: a newer signal still wins, because "can't change" is an assumption and the signal is evidence. Two things that must go with it: **write the refreshed payload back** (otherwise the stale copy stays and every view re-fetches), and **fall back to the stale copy if the upstream call fails** — a stale timeline beats an empty panel.

Audited at the time: the DocPharma not-found cache (`DP_NOT_FOUND_TTL_MS`), the RapidShyp status cache (`RS_CACHE_TTL_HOURS` + a nightly forced sweep), the PO cache (`PO_TTL`) and the Tally books cache all have a policy; `/track-order` never caches. The scan log was the only write-once one.

### Self-tests — `npm run selftest`
`tests/selftest.js` — no framework, no network, no DB. It covers logic that failed **silently** in production: the cache-freshness rule above, the shape of the scan-log fix (that the only-when-empty guard has not returned), and the RS Sync alerting behaviour (a transient timeout must not raise a cron-failure card, an outage must abort early, an HTTP 400 verdict must survive a restart). Verified against the pre-fix commit: two of those checks fail on it. **Add a case here whenever a silent-failure bug is fixed** — the ones no amount of watching the dashboard would reveal.

### Engineering gotcha — router-level response caches
`ops_control.js` and `amazon_fba.js` keep 5-minute GET response caches via `router.use(...)`. Because those routers are mounted at `/api`, an **unscoped** `use()` runs for every `/api` request that falls through them and silently caches *other routers'* GET responses (this once froze all `/support/*` reads for 5 minutes — "DB changed but dashboard shows old data"). Both middlewares are now path-guarded (`/ops-control`, `/fba`) — never widen those guards, and scope any future `router.use()` middleware to its own path prefix.

### `.env` sync
`.env` is not in git. When new variables are introduced (see §12 — most recently `AI_*`, `EMAIL_ENC_KEY`, `TEAMS_*`), copy them to the server's `.env` manually before restart.

### HTTPS & the proxy chain (VPS infra — **not in this repo**)
Served at **https://dashboard.theelement.skin**. Requests reach Node (`127.0.0.1:5001`) through two proxies: Traefik (owns `:443`, shared with n8n — never restart it casually) → a small `nginx:alpine` sidecar container `dashboard-proxy` (compose `/root/dashboard-proxy.yml`, config `/root/dashboard-proxy.conf`). A separate host nginx on `:8443` still proxies `/api/` directly, because Shopify and EasyEcom webhooks are hardcoded to that port. `Caddyfile.example` is a leftover from an abandoned plan — ignore it.

**Proxy read timeout — any request slower than 60s dies at the proxy, not in the app.** nginx defaults `proxy_read_timeout` to 60s, so a long request returns HTTP 504 to the browser *while Node happily keeps working and completes it*. This bit Influencer → Discover (2026-08-14): `analyze-influencer` drifted from ~35s to 60–85s as Apify slowed down, and every run past the line showed a red `HTTP 504` even though the analysis finished and landed in `analysis_queue` as `completed`. Users then retried, each retry paying for another Apify scrape — one handle was analyzed six times in 45 minutes. Both nginx layers are now set to `proxy_read_timeout`/`proxy_send_timeout` **300s**.

The tell for this class of bug: the UI shows a bare `HTTP <code>` with no message (the proxy's HTML error body fails `JSON.parse`, so the frontend falls back to the status code), and the work turns out to have succeeded server-side. Before blaming the feature, compare the edge function's `execution_time_ms` against the proxy timeout. The durable fix for anything routinely over ~30s is to return immediately and poll, not to keep raising the ceiling.
